# 2 Chronicles

# ANNOTATIONS ON THE SECOND BOOK OF THE CHRONICLES

## THE ARGUMENT

This book contains a history of four hundred seventy-two years, from the beginning of Solomon’s reign to the Jews’ return from captivity. It registers the state of the kingdom of Judah under nineteen kings of David’s posterity, and Athaliah the usurper. And sometimes the affairs of the kingdom of Israel, wherein the two kingdoms had to do one with another, are intermingled. See the notes on the title of the first book of the Chronicles.

## CHAPTER 1

**Verse 1. ***{And Solomon}* From hence unto ch. 10 those general points of Solomon’s excellency noted 1 Chr. 29:23, 24, 25 are particularly and distinctly exemplified. Of this name Solomon see 1 Chr. 22:9.

*{the son of David}* This is added for honor’s sake. See 1 Kings 8:15.

*{was strengthened in his kingdom}* This has relation to that submission which the princes, mighty men, and sons of David yielded to Solomon, 1 Chr. 29:24. See 1 Chr. 11:10. See also ch. 11:17. It may have also relation to Adonijah’s conspiracy, which was now clean quashed. See 1 Kings 2:12, 46.

*{and the LORD his God was with him}* See 2 Kings 18:7; 1 Chr. 22:11; 2 Sam. 5:10.

*{and magnified him exceedingly}* See 1 Chr. 29:25.

**Verse 2. ***{Then Solomon spake unto all Israel}* See 1 Chr. 11:1. As David had done before, ch. 28:1 and 29:1, so did Solomon now.

*{to the captains of thousands, and of hundreds}* See ch. 28:1.

*{and to the judges}* See 1 Chr. 13:4 and 26:29. Both Levites and men of other tribes also were judges, ch. 19:5, 8.

*{and to every governor in all Israel}* There were governors of tribes, of families, over many people, over few; all here comprised under this particle every.

*{the chief of the fathers}* See 1 Kings 8:1; 1 Chr. 29:6.

**Verse 3. ***{So Solomon and all the congregation with him}* See 1 Chr. 29:1.

*{went to the high place that was at Gibeon}* See 1 Kings 3:4.

*{for there was the tabernacle of the congregation of God}* See 1 Kings 8:4.

*{which Moses the servant of the LORD}* Of this style see 1 Kings 3:6.

*{had made}* God first gave the pattern of the tabernacle to Moses, Exod. 25:40, and Bezaleel with others were the workmen that made it; yet because Moses was the visible minister that set the men on work, and gave directions to them how to make it, he is said to make it. Thus Solomon is said to *make and build the temple*, 1 Kings 6:2, 4.

*{in the wilderness}* Where the Israelites sojourned forty years.

**Verse 4. ***{But the ark of God}* See 1 Kings 6:19.

*{had David brought up from Kirjath-jearim}* See 1 Chr. 13:5 and 15:15.

*{to the place which David had prepared for it}* See 1 Chr. 15:1 and 16:1.

*{for he had pitched a tent for it at Jerusalem}* The place where David pitched a tent for the ark was the *city of David* which was Zion, 1 Chr. 11:5 and 15:1. But Zion was within the circuit of Jerusalem, and therefore without any contradiction it may be said to be *in Zion* and *at Jerusalem*.

**Verse 5. ***{Moreover the brasen altar that Bezaleel the son of Uri, etc.}* Of the parentage of Bezaleel see Exod. 35:30. Of *the brazen altar* see Exod. 38:1, etc. The inward matter of that altar was shittim wood, but it was laid all over with plates of brass, and in that respect called *the brazen altar*.

*{he put}* Or, *was there*, i.e., it was there placed.

*{before the tabernacle of the LORD}* There was a court round about the tabernacle called *the priests’ court*: there was the brazen altar for burnt offerings placed. See 1 Kings 6:36.

*{and Solomon and the congregation sought unto it}* There they offered their sacrifices, and worshiped God, who there manifested his presence; and so they are said to *seek unto it*.

**Verse 6. ***{And Solomon went up thither}* Here that which in general was said of seeking unto the altar is particularly expounded.

*{to the brasen altar}* See v. 5.

*{before the LORD}* This is said in regard of the manifestation of God’s presence in the tabernacle: so as what was done before the tabernacle was done before the Lord.

*{which was at the tabernacle of the congregation}* The altar was there. See v. 3.

*{and offered a thousand burnt offerings upon it}* See 1 Kings 3:4.

**Verse 7. ***{In that night did God appear, etc.}* See 1 Kings 3:5.

**Verse 8. ***{And Solomon said unto God, Thou hast, etc.}* See 1 Kings 3:6.

*{and hast made me to reign in his stead}* See 1 Chr. 28:5 and 29:23.

**Verse 9. ***{Now, O LORD God, let thy promise unto David my father be established}* Solomon here prays for the extent of God’s promise, which was, that David’s kingdom should be established not only for his time, but forever; and that Solomon should build God’s house, 1 Chr. 17:11, 12. The beginning of that promise was already accomplished; for Solomon was made king and settled upon the throne, v. 1. Therefore it is somewhat more that he here prays for.

*{for thou hast made me king}* Solomon ascribes to God that honor which he had.

*{over a people like the dust of the earth in multitude}* Heb. *much as the dust of the earth*. Nothing more innumerable. Gen. 13:16 and 28:14; Num. 23:10. See this phrase explained 1 Kings 3:8.

**Verse 10. ***{Give me now wisdom and knowledge}* See 1 Kings 3:9.

*{that I may go out and come in before this people}* See 1 Kings 3:7.

*{for who can judge this thy people that is so great}* See 1 Kings 3:9.

**Verse 11. ***{And God said to Solomon}* See 1 Kings 3:11.

*{Because this was in thine heart}* This implies that Solomon’s desire was not rashly and suddenly conceived, but pondered in his heart before; which God well knew, Ps. 139:2.

*{and thou hast not asked riches, etc.}* See 1 Kings 13:11.

*{but hast asked wisdom and knowledge for thyself}* He means not that Solomon asked them for himself alone, but that being endued with those graces he might use them for the good of his people.

*{that thou mayest judge my people}* *To judge* in general signifies well to govern; in particular, to order to every one his own.

*{over whom I have made thee king}* God makes kings, Prov. 8:15.

**Verse 12. ***{Wisdom and knowledge is granted unto thee}* See 1 Kings 3:12.

*{and I will give thee riches, and wealth, and honour}* This God added as an advantage. See 1 Kings 3:13.

*{such as none of the kings have had}* This has relation to all the things before promised.

*{that have been before thee}* See 1 Chr. 29:25.

*{neither shall there any after thee have the like}* See 1 Kings 3:12.

**Verse 13. ***{Then Solomon came from his journey to the high place that was at Gibeon}* The journey here meant was his going to Gibeon v. 3, where he lay that night wherein God appeared to him.

*{to Jerusalem}* i.e., He came from Gibeon to Jerusalem.

*{from before the tabernacle of the congregation}* At Gibeon, v. 3.

*{and reigned over Israel}* He continued to reign and govern the people, being settled on his throne, 1 Chr. 29:23.

**Verse 14. ***{And Solomon gathered chariots and horsemen}* See 1 Kings 10:26.

*{and he had a thousand and four hundred chariots, and twelve thousand horsemen, which, etc.}* See 1 Kings 4:26 and 10:26.

**Verse 15. ***{And the king made}* Heb. *gave*. By giving of it so liberally as he did he made it to be accounted as is here set down.

*{silver and gold at Jerusalem}* That was the royal city, and there was the king’s palace, and thither was the greatest part of his treasure brought.

*{as plenteous as stones, etc.}* See 1 Kings 10:27.

**Verse 17. ***{And they fetched up, etc.}* See 1 Kings 10:27.

## CHAPTER 2

**Verse 1. ***{And Solomon determined}* Heb. *said*. He said in his heart, or with himself. After David had informed Solomon and others that it was God’s mind that Solomon should *build him an house*, 1 Chr. 28:6, it was presently in the heart of Solomon so to do, and answerably he determined it. See 1 Kings 5:5.

*{to build an house for the name of the LORD}* See v. 4; 1 Kings 5:3.

*{and an house for his kingdom}* This was his royal palace, 1 Kings 9:1. It is said to be *for his kingdom*, because it made much for the glory thereof, and for the use of the subjects of his kingdom.

**Verse 2. ***{And Solomon told out, etc.}* See 1 Kings 5:15, 16.

**Verse 3. ***{And Solomon sent to Huram}* Or, *Hiram*.

*{the king of Tyre}* 1 Kings 5:1. It is said that Hiram first sent to congratulate Solomon’s setting upon the throne.

*{saying, As thou didst deal with David my father}* 1 Kings 5:1 it is said that *Hiram was ever a lover of David*: Solomon therefore presses that argument to move him to show kindness to him, who was David’s best beloved son.

*{and didst send him cedars}* See 1 Chr. 14:1.

*{to build him an house to dwell therein}* This did Hiram in the beginning of David’s reign, before he thought of building an house to the Lord. 2 Sam. 5:11.

*{even so deal with me}* These words be not in the original, yet are fitly supplied to make up the sense, and to add an application to that note of comparison, *As thou didst deal*, etc. The Hebrew ofttimes by such necessary consequences is to be understood.

**Verse 4. ***{Behold}* It is a very remarkable matter that follows, and Solomon would have Hiram well observe it.

*{I build an house to the name of the LORD}* Or, *to his honor*. See 1 Kings 8:16.

*{my God}* See 1 Kings 3:7 and 8:28.

*{to dedicate it to him}* To set it apart for his worship. See 1 Kings 8:63.

*{and to burn before him sweet incense}* Heb. *incense of spices*. See 1 Kings 9:25.

*{and for the continual shewbread}* See 1 Kings 7:48.

*{and for the burnt offerings morning and evening}* See 1 Kings 3:15.

*{morning and evening}* See Exod. 29:38, etc.; Num. 28:3, etc.

*{on the sabbaths}* See Num. 28:9, etc.

*{and on the new moons}* See Num. 28:11, etc.

*{and on the solemn feasts of the LORD our God}* See Num. 28:16 and 29:1.

*{This is an ordinance for ever to Israel}* See 1 Kings 8:13.

**Verse 5. ***{And the house which I build is great}* See 1 Chr. 22:5.

*{for great is our God}* See 1 Chr. 16:25. We must endeavor to order the things wherein we have to do with God answerably (so far as we can) to his excellency and greatness.

*{above all gods}* i.e., Such as are counted gods by ignorant and idolatrous persons. There is but one true God, who is infinitely above all that are falsely counted gods. In this respect he is styled *the God of gods*, Deut. 10:17; Josh. 22:22.

**Verse 6. ***{But who is able}* Heb. *who hath retained*, or, *obtained strength*. It is by some strength or other that any is able to do what he does.

*{to build him an house}* See 1 Kings 8:27.

*{seeing the heaven and heaven of heavens cannot contain him}* See ch. 6:18.

*{who am I then that I should build him an house}* If any man might do it, surely Solomon might, who far excelled all others in wisdom, knowledge, riches, honor, and other excellencies, ch. 1:12.

*{save only to burn sacrifice before him}* That which was before said might seem to import that it was a needless thing to build an house for him whom no house could contain; but this clause shows the reason of building an house to God. It was not to contain him, but to perform service to him in. See v. 4.

**Verse 7. ***{Send me now therefore a man}* There were more curious workmen in Tyre than in Israel; therefore Solomon sends to Hiram for one. This has a reference to v. 3. It may be Solomon had heard of Hiram described 1 Kings 7:13, 14, and sent for him.

*{cunning to work in gold, and in silver, and in brass, and in iron}* There were curious works to be wrought in all these metals. See 1 Kings 7:14, 45, etc. Of things of iron see 1 Chr. 22:3.

*{and in purple, and crimson, and blue}* These were for the veil, ch. 3:14, other hangings, priests’ vestments, and sundry other like things.

*{and that can skill to grave}* Heb. *grave gravings*. This Hebraism carries an emphasis with it. See 1 Kings 8:13.

*{with the cunning men that are with me in Judah and in Jerusalem}* Though there might be one man or two or three in Tyre that had extraordinary skill in curious works, yet there were also very cunning men among the Jews. There were such in Moses’ time, Exod. 36:1, and so afterwards in other generations, Isa. 3:3.

*{whom David my father did provide}* See 1 Chr. 22:15.

**Verse 8. ***{Send me also cedar trees}* See 1 Chr. 5:6.

*{fir trees}* See 1 Kings 5:8.

*{and algum trees}* Or, *almug trees*. See 1 Kings 10:11.

*{for I know that thy servants can skill to cut timber in Lebanon}* It was everywhere known abroad that the Sidonians were expert therein. Therefore Solomon writing to the king of Tyre says *thou knowest*, 1 Kings 5:6, and here, *I know*.

*{and behold}* This word Solomon added to assure Hiram that he would not fail to perform his promise.

*{my servants shall be with thy servants}* See 1 Kings 5:6.

**Verse 9. ***{Even to prepare me timber in abundance}* This is added as a reason why Solomon would send his servants to be assistant in the work, lest the burden might be thought too heavy for the Sidonians alone.

*{for the house which I am about to build shall be wonderful great}* See v. 5.

**Verse 10. ***{And behold, I will give to thy servants, etc.}* That which is here promised to be given to Hiram’s servants is promised to Hiram himself, and said also to be given unto him, 1 Kings 5:6, 11. It was given to Hiram to distribute to his servants as he pleased.

**Verse 11. ***{Then Huram the king of Tyre answered in writing}* See 1 Kings 5:7.

*{which he sent to Solomon}* The king of Tyre and the king of Israel were each in their several dominions, and so absent one from another, so as they were forced to declare their mind one to another by letters and messengers. This proved every way as effectual as if they had spoken together.

*{Because the LORD hath loved his people, he hath made thee king over them}* Hiram took notice of Solomon’s prudence and piety, and in that respect judged him fit to be a king: and he knew that Israel was God’s peculiar people, and thereupon inferred that it was God’s love to Israel that moved him to provide such a king for them.

**Verse 12. ***{Huram said moreover}* Apprehension of God’s mercies quickly raises heavenly ejaculations in the hearts of such as are piously affected.

*{Blessed be the LORD God of Israel}* See 1 Kings 5:7 and 8:15.

*{that made heaven and earth}* Particular blessings bring to mind God’s general and common blessings. See 2 Kings 19:15.

*{who hath given to David the king a wise son}* Huram loved David, 1 Kings 5:1, and therefore for honors’ sake styles this wise king *the son of David*.

*{endued with prudence and understanding}* Heb. *knowing prudence and understanding*, i.e., one that well understood what was best to be done; and answerably could order the same to the best advantage; which is an especial point of prudence.

*{that might build an house for the LORD, and an house for his kingdom}* See v. 1.

**Verse 13. ***{And now I have sent a cunning man}* As was desired v. 7.

*{endued with understanding}* By this his skill and cunning might be the better improved.

*{of Huram my father’s}* One that was servant to my father Huram. The present king of Tyre and his father were both called Huram, or Hiram. So was the workman which was sent, and his father also, called Huram, or Hiram, ch. 4:16.

**Verse 14. ***{The son of a woman, the daughter of Dan, etc.}* Of the difference betwixt this description and that 1 Kings 7:14 see on that place.

*{skilful to work in gold, etc.}* Such an one in particular is sent for v. 7. But here is further added that he was skillful to work *in stone, and in timber, and in fine linen*. All the particulars show that he was skillful in any work whatsoever.

*{also to grave any manner of graving}* See v. 7.

*{and to find out every device which shall be put to him}* He could not only work exactly according to a pattern set before him, but could also invent other curious works fit for that he undertook.

*{with thy cunning men}* See v. 7. As Solomon had many materials more than his father left him, so had he also many cunning workmen.

*{and with the cunning men of my lord David thy father}* See 1 Chr. 22:15. This title *my lord* shows that Huram was a tributary king to David, at least had an honorable esteem of him.

**Verse 15. ***{Now therefore the wheat and the barley, etc.}* See v. 10.

*{let him send unto his servants}* Huram acknowledged Solomon as David before, to be his lord, and his subjects to be Solomon’s servants: which was an evidence if not of fealty and homage, yet surely of a very high esteem of Solomon.

**Verse 16. ***{And we will cut wood out of Lebanon as much as thou shalt need}* Heb. *according to all thy need*. In Lebanon there was enough to satisfy his desire to the full.

*{and we will bring it to thee in floats by sea to Joppa}* Or, *Japho*. See 1 Kings 5:9.

*{and thou shalt carry it up to Jerusalem}* From Joppa, which was an haven town of the tribe of Dan, such things as were brought by sea might conveniently be conveyed to Jerusalem.

**Verse 17. ***{And Solomon numbered all the strangers}* Heb. *the men the strangers*.

*{that were in the land of Israel}* That dwelt amongst the Israelites, and submitted to their laws.

*{after the numbering wherewith David his father had numbered them}* This has relation to that which is noted 1 Chr. 22:2.

*{and they were found an hundred and fifty thousand and three thousand and six hundred}* This makes up the sum set down v. 2.

**Verse 18. ***{And he set threescore and ten thousand, etc.}* See v. 2.

## CHAPTER 3

**Verse 1. ***{Then Solomon began to build}* In the former chapter is set down the care that Solomon had to provide materials and workmen; in this and the next how he employed all, what he built and made.

*{the house of the LORD}* See 1 Kings 3:1 and 6:1.

*{at Jerusalem in mount Moriah}* That mount whereon Abraham was about to offer up his son, Gen. 22:2, etc. Jerusalem was built thereon. Of Jerusalem see 1 Chr. 11:4.

*{where the Lord appeared unto David his father}* Or, *which was seen of David his father*. Lord is not in the original, and that makes the ambiguity. This has relation to 1 Chr. 21:15, etc. In this place the angel that destroyed the Israelites upon David’s numbering the people stood, and there God commanded him to cease. Thereupon David built an altar, and offered offerings thereon; and the Lord answered him by fire from heaven upon that altar. On these grounds David appointed that place for the house of the Lord.

*{in the place that David had prepared}* See 1 Chr. 22:1.

*{in the threshingfloor of Ornan the Jebusite}* See 1 Chr. 21:15, 20.

**Verse 2. ***{And he began to build, etc.}* See 1 Kings 6:1.

**Verse 3. ***{Now these are the things}* *The things* is not in the original, whereupon some thus supply it, *these are the measures*, or, *these are the divisions*.

*{wherein Solomon was instructed, etc.}* Heb. *founded*. He that is well instructed in a thing has a good foundation laid for a further progress therein. Some thus turn it, *there was a foundation*, namely, those measures or dimensions which Solomon used in building the temple. If we take it as here translated, *Solomon was instructed*, it may have relation to that wisdom which God gave him, ch. 1:12. Or to some inward inspiration of the Spirit, or to the direction which his father gave him, 1 Chr. 28:11, etc., or to that counsel which the skillful workmen gave him about building the temple.

*{The length by cubits after the first measure}* This was the sacred measure, which was the greatest, and which David left as a pattern, 1 Chr. 28:11.

*{was threescore cubits, and the breadth twenty cubits}* The height also which was *thirty cubits*, is set down 1 Kings 6:2.

**Verse 4. ***{And the porch, etc.}* See 1 Kings 6:3.

*{and he overlaid it within with pure gold}* This was counted a part of the temple, and thereupon was thus overlaid with gold. Of pure gold see 1 Kings 6:2.

**Verse 5. ***{And the greater house}* i.e., the middle part of the temple betwixt the oracle and the porch, called *the holy place*. It was as big again as the most holy place, 1 Kings 6:17, 20, and four times as big as the porch, 1 Kings 6:3, and in that respect here called *the greater house*.

*{he cieled with fir tree}* Thin boards of fir were for such an use, as boards of cedar, Jer. 22:14. See 1 Kings 5:8.

*{which he overlaid with fine gold}* See 1 Kings 28 and 10:21.

*{and set thereon palm trees and chains}* Figures of these were engraven on the ceiling and covered with plates of gold: yea it was also carved with *knops and open flowers*, 1 Kings 6:18.

**Verse 6. ***{And he garnished}* Heb. *covered*, i.e., set here and there.

*{the house with precious stones for beauty}* See 1 Kings 10:10, 11 and 1 Chr. 29:2, 8. Precious stones must needs add great ornament to the temple, and make it appear beautiful.

*{and the gold was gold of Parvaim}* See 1 Kings 9:28.

**Verse 7. ***{He overlaid also the house}* i.e., the middle part of the temple, v. 5.

*{the beams, the posts, and the walls thereof}* All that was seen on the inside of the house.

*{and the doors thereof with gold}* By these they passed from one place to another; yet because they belonged to the house, even they, as well as other things, were overlaid with plates of gold.

*{and graved cherubims on the walls}* Of cherubim see 1 Kings 6:23. These were graved as the palm trees, v. 5.

**Verse 8. ***{And he made the most holy house}* Heb. *the house of holy of holies*. The innermost part of the temple, called *the oracle*. See 1 Kings 6:5.

*{the length whereof, etc.}* See 1 Kings 6:20.

*{and he overlaid it with fine gold}* See 1 Kings 9:28.

*{amounting to six hundred talents}* This sum according to our account is two millions and two hundred and fifty thousand pounds. See 1 Chr. 22:14.

**Verse 9. ***{And the weight of the nails}* Though for the doors and gates of the courts there were nails of iron, 1 Chr. 22:3, yet about the most holy place all the nails were of gold.

*{was fifty shekels of gold}* This comes to twenty ounces, which is threescore and two pounds and ten shillings. See 1 Chr. 22:14.

*{and he overlaid the upper chambers with gold}* Of these see 1 Kings 6:5.

**Verse 10. ***{And in the most holy place he made two cherubims of image work}* Or, as some think, *of movable work*, i.e., he made them of many parts that were set together, and might be taken asunder: which was so ordered by reason of the greatness and heaviness of them. Others take it to be a work that carried the fashion of boys. See 1 Kings 6:23.

*{and overlaid them with gold}* With plates of gold.

**Verse 11, 12. ***{And the wings of the cherubims, etc.}* Of the wings of the cherubim, and their stretching forth from one side of the house to the other, see 1 Kings 6:24, etc., where also the height of them is noted.

**Verse 13. ***{And they stood on their feet}* Their feet rested upon the floor, and so they stood bolt upright.

*{and their faces were inward}* Heb. *toward the house*. They did not look one towards another, as the two little cherubim fastened to the mercy seat, Exod. 25:20, but both of them one way, towards the holy place, which is called *the greater house*, v. 5.

**Verse 14. ***{And he made the vail}* This distinguished the most holy place from the holy, Exod. 26:31, etc. It rent at Christ’s death, Matt. 27:51.

*{of blue, and purple, and crimson}* It was adorned with these beautiful colors.

*{and fine linen}* This was the matter whereof it was made.

*{and wrought}* Heb. *caused to ascend*, or, *to be put upon*.

*{cherubims thereon}* See v. 7.

**Verse 15. ***{Also he made before the house}* Namely, in the porch, that stood before the house, 1 Kings 7:21.

*{two pillars}* See 1 Kings 7:15, etc., where the differences betwixt this and that place are reconciled. See also Jer. 52:21.

**Verse 17. ***{And he reared up the pillars, etc.}* See on 1 Kings 7:21.

## CHAPTER 4

**Verse 1. ***{Moreover he made an altar of brass}* Frequent mention is made of this altar in Solomon’s time, and of the offerings offered thereon, as 1 Kings 8:22, 31, 54, 64 and 9:25, but in the book of Kings there is no mention of making it. This altar of burnt offerings far exceeded that Exod. 27:1, for the temple far exceeded the tabernacle. See 1 Chr. 22:5.

*{twenty cubits the length thereof, and twenty cubits the breadth thereof}* It was ten yards at least square. It was so large, because they used to offer many oxen and other great beasts upon it.

*{and ten cubits the height thereof}* It was at least fifteen foot high: and there was a plain ascent like an hill to go up and offer on it. In this respect the priest is said to *come down from the altar*, Lev. 9:22. By steps they might not go up to an altar; unless that inhibition were temporary. See on Exod. 20:26. It was so high to the end that the people in the courts might all see the offerings laid upon the altar, and by the sight of the smoke and flame ascending up be moved to raise up their hearts unto heaven, and by sight of the burnt offering be put in mind of their sins, and of the sacrifice of Christ to expiate the same. This altar was set in the priests’ court; whereof see 1 Kings 6:36.

**Verse 2. ***{Also he made a molten sea, etc.}* See 1 Kings 7:23.

*{from brim to brim}* Heb. *from his brim to his brim*, i.e., from two parts of the brim diametrically opposite one to the other.

**Verse 3. ***{And under it was the similitude of oxen}* These oxen were cast with the sea on the sides thereof, and so were different from the twelve oxen on which it stood, v. 4. Either these are called *knops*, 1 Kings 7:24, or *knops* were joined with them.

**Verse 4. ***{It stood upon twelve oxen}* See v. 3; 1 Kings 7:25.

**Verse 5. ***{with flowers of lilies}* Or, *like a lily-flower*.

*{and it received and held three thousand baths}* See 1 Kings 7:26.

**Verse 6. ***{He made also ten lavers}* These were vessels to hold water, but much less than the sea before mentioned. See 1 Kings 7:38. The bases whereon these lavers stood are largely described 1 Kings 7:27, etc.

*{such things as they offered for the burnt offering}* Heb. *the work of burnt offering*.

*{they washed in them}* This was the principal use of those many lavers, and of the water in them.

*{but the sea was for the priests to wash in}* See 1 Kings 7:26.

**Verse 7. ***{and he made ten candlesticks of gold}* See 1 Kings 7:49.

*{according to their form}* That prescribed Exod. 25:31, etc., and after which David gave his son a pattern, 1 Chr. 28:11, 15.

*{and set them in the temple}* In the middle part thereof called *the holy place*, before the oracle.

*{five on the right hand, and five on the left}* See 1 Kings 7:49.

**Verse 8. ***{He made also ten tables}* One for the showbread, 1 Kings 7:48, the other for other uses.

*{and placed them, etc.}* See v. 7.

*{and he made an hundred basons of gold}* Or, *bowls*. See 1 Kings 7:50.

**Verse 9. ***{Furthermore, he made the court of the priests, and the great court}* Of the sundry courts about the temple see 1 Kings 6:36.

*{and doors for the court}* The courts were compassed about with walls, and there were gates and doors whereby people passed in and out.

*{and overlaid the doors of them with brass}* Gold was used to overlay the doors appertaining to the temple; but to show a difference betwixt the courts and the main house itself, the things in the courts were of brass, a sea of brass, lavers of brass, pots and shovels of brass, 1 Kings 7:38, 45, etc. But this brass was very fine brass, and bright. And with plates of such brass the doors were overlaid.

**Verse 10. ***{And he set the sea on the right side of the east end}* See 1 Kings 7:39.

*{over against the south}* For the right side of the east end was north, and that was over against the south.

**Verse 11. ***{And Huram made the pots, and the shovels, and the basons}* Or, *bowls*. See 1 Kings 7:15.

*{and Huram finished the work that he was to make}* Heb. *finished to make*. See 1 Kings 7:40.

*{for king Solomon for the house of God}* Solomon appointed him to do what he did, and in that respect he made all for Solomon; but the use whereunto all that he made was put was the service of God’s house.

**Verse 12. ***{To wit, the two pillars}* Mentioned ch. 3:15, etc.

*{and the pommels}* Or, *bowls*. 1 Kings 7:41.

*{and the chapiters which were on the top of the two pillars}* See 1 Kings 7:16.

*{and the two wreaths to cover the two pommels, etc.}* These are called *net works*, or *wreaths of chain work*, 1 Kings 7:17.

**Verse 13. ***{And four hundred pomegranates on the two wreaths}* See the difference about this number 1 Kings 7:20.

*{two rows of pomegranates on each wreath, to cover, etc.}* See 1 Kings 7:18, 20.

*{which were upon the pillars}* Heb. *upon the face of the pillars*; i.e., upon the outside and uppermost part of the pillars.

**Verse 14. ***{He made also bases}* See 1 Kings 7:27, etc.

*{and lavers}* Or, *cauldrons*. Either of the words signify vessels to hold water.

*{made he upon the bases}* He set the lavers upon the bases, that they might the better be moved up and down. See 1 Kings 7:32.

**Verse 15. ***{One sea, and twelve oxen under it}* See v. 2, 4.

**Verse 16. ***{The pots also and the shovels}* See 1 Kings 7:45.

*{and the fleshhooks}* See Exod. 27:3; 1 Chr. 28:17.

*{and all their instruments}* i.e., Such utensils as were fit to be used by the priests and Levites in the courts of the Lord’s house. See 1 Chr. 28:14.

*{did Huram his father}* Some apply this relative his to Solomon. As if Solomon had called him for honor’s sake *his father*. Others apply it to Hiram the younger, as if hereby were meant Hiram’s father; so as both Hiram the father and Hiram the son wrought to King Solomon.

*{make to king Solomon for the house of the LORD}* See v. 11.

*{of bright brass}* Heb. *made bright*, or, *scoured*. See 1 Kings 7:45.

**Verse 17. ***{In the clay ground, etc.}* See 1 Kings 7:46.

**Verse 18. ***{Thus Solomon made all these vessels in great abundance, etc.}* See 1 Kings 7:47; 1 Chr. 22:3.

**Verse 19. ***{And Solomon made all the vessels that were for the house of God}* The particulars mentioned v. 11, etc. were for the priests’ court; these that follow were for the holy place.

*{the golden altar also}* *The altar of incense*, ch. 26:16, 18.

*{and the tables whereon the shewbread was set}* See v. 8.

**Verse 20. ***{Moreover the candlesticks with their lamps}* See 1 Kings 7:49.

*{that they should burn after the manner}* That as the lamps in the tabernacle burnt and gave light, so these should do in the temple.

*{before the oracle}* i.e., The most holy place. See 1 Kings 6:5.

*{of pure gold}* This has reference to the candlesticks, which were made of pure gold. See 1 Kings 9:28.

**Verse 21. ***{And the flowers, and the lamps, and the tongs made of gold}* See 1 Kings 7:49.

*{and that perfect gold}* Heb. *perfections of gold*. See 1 Kings 9:28.

**Verse 22. ***{And the snuffers, and the basons}* Or, *bowls*.

*{and the spoons, and the censers, of pure gold}* See 1 Kings 7:50.

*{And the entry of the house, the inner doors thereof for the most holy place}* Or, *the inward doors of the gates of the house whereby they entered into the most holy place*.

*{and the doors of the house of the temple}* i.e., Of the holy place.

*{were of pure gold}* i.e., Covered with plates of gold. For the doors were made of wood, 1 Kings 6:31, etc.

## CHAPTER 5

**Verse 1. ***{Thus all the work that Solomon made, etc.}* See 1 Kings 7:51. The gold and silver put into the treasures was over and above that which was laid out upon the temple.

**Verse 2. ***{Then Solomon assembled the elders of Israel}* From hence to the end of the chapter, is set down the solemn bringing of the ark into the most holy place, and setting it there. And this story not only for matter and substance, but also for words and phrases is in 1 Kings 8:1–11.

**Verse 3. ***{In the seventh month}* Called *Ethanim* 1 Kings 8:2.

**Verse 4. ***{The Levites took up the ark}* They were such Levites as were also priests, v. 5. See 1 Chr. 15:2.

**Verse 5. ***{These did the priests and the Levites bring up}* The priests only did bear the ark; the Levites carried the other things, Num. 4:15.

**Verse 6. ***{Sacrificed sheep and oxen}* This was an usual manner of testifying holy joy and thankfulness unto God, 1 Chr. 29:21.

**Verse 7. ***{unto his place}* The place appointed for it.

**Verse 9. ***{And there it is unto this day}* Or, *they are there*, 1 Kings 8:8. If Ezra was the penman of this book, this phrase cannot be taken of the time wherein he lived. For we meet with no mention of the ark after the burning of the temple. Therefore it must here be taken as a common proverbial speech. See 1 Kings 8:8.

**Verse 10. ***{when the LORD}* Or, *where the Lord*. For this may have reference both to the place and also to the time.

**Verse 11. ***{for all the priests}* That which we have from hence to v. 14 is not at all mentioned where the former story is set down, 1 Kings 8. This with that that follows is added as a reason why all sorts of priests went into the most holy place.

*{that were present}* See 1 Chr. 29:17.

*{were sanctified}* See 1 Chr. 15:14.

*{and did not then wait by course}* There were certain courses appointed by David, which the priests in their order observed, 1 Chr. 24:3, etc. But now all of them being employed together about that extraordinary work, observed not those distinct courses.

**Verse 12. ***{Also the Levites which were the singers}* i.e., Chief and principal musicians, masters of music.

*{all of them of Asaph, of Heman, of Jeduthun}* See 1 Chr. 25:1, etc.

*{with their sons and their brethren}* i.e., Their posterity and kindred. These are by name set down 1 Chr. 25:2, etc.

*{being arrayed in white linen}* With such garments were such as served in the house of the Lord clad, to put them in mind of that righteousness and integrity which becomes such as appear before the Lord, and serve him, Rev. 7:13 and 15:6.

*{having cymbals, and psalteries, and harps}* See 1 Chr. 13:8.

*{stood at the east end of the altar}* i.e., The altar of burnt offering. The east end was at the entrance into the priests’ court.

*{and with them an hundred and twenty priests}* It must needs be a loud music that was made by so many, though they sounded in course.

*{sounding with trumpets}* This belonged to the priests, 1 Chr. 15:24 and 16:6.

**Verse 13. ***{It came to pass as the trumpeters and singers were as one}* They were as one in the matter which they sung, in their melodious harmony, and in the time wherein they continued.

*{to make one sound to be heard in praising and thanking the LORD}* This is one particular wherein they were as one.

*{and when they lift up their voice with the trumpets and cymbals}* i.e., When they sounded aloud with the trumpets and cymbals.

*{and instruments of musick}* These might be other wind instruments, as cornets, and sackbuts; or else stringed instruments, whereon some played while other sounded.

*{and praised the LORD, saying, For he is good, etc.}* See 1 Chr. 16:34.

*{that then the house was filled with a cloud}* See 1 Kings 8:10.

*{even the house of the LORD}* i.e., The temple.

**Verse 14. ***{So that the priests, etc.}* See 1 Kings 8:11.

## CHAPTER 6

**Verse 1. ***{Then said Solomon}* There are two parts of this chapter: I. Solomon’s solemn blessing of God for the house that was built, to v. 12. II. Solomon’s prayer to God for the house, v. 12–42. Both are set down almost word for word as here 1 Kings 8:12, etc.

**Verse 2. ***{I have built}* This word is doubled 1 Kings 8:13, which adds much emphasis.

*{an house of habitation for thee}* Or, *I have built thee an house to dwell in*, 1 Kings 8:13.

*{and a place for thy dwelling for ever}* Or, *a settled place for thee to abide in forever*, 1 Kings 8:13.

**Verse 3. ***{turned his face}* Or, *turned his face about*, 1 Kings 8:14.

**Verse 4. ***{who hath with his hands fulfilled that which he spake with his mouth to my father David}* Or, *which spake with his mouth unto David my father, and hath with his hand fulfilled it*, 1 Kings 8:15.

**Verse 5. ***{my people}* Or, *my people Israel*, 1 Kings 8:16.

*{neither chose I any man to be a ruler over my people Israel}* This is not to be taken simply, (for God chose Saul, 1 Sam. 9:16, and judges, Judg. 2:16, 17) but respectively to David. God never chose such a ruler as David, who was an especial type of Christ, and in whose seed a royal succession was established till Christ, Gen. 49:10; 2 Sam. 7:16.

**Verse 6. ***{But I have chosen Jerusalem, that my name might be there}* This is not mentioned 1 Kings 8:16. See 1 Kings 11:13. God’s name is said to be in Jerusalem, because the temple and other types of his presence were there.

**Verse 10. ***{And am set on the throne}* Or, *sit on the throne*. The former phrase shows that he was set on his throne by God: the latter implies an establishment thereof.

**Verse 11. ***{In it have I put the ark}* Or, *I have set there a place for the ark*, 1 Kings 8:21. This latter phrase notes his preparation for the ark; the other, the accomplishment of what he had prepared.

*{that he made with the children of Israel}* Or, *which he made with our fathers, when he brought them out of the land of Egypt*, 1 Kings 8:21. This shows of which of the children of Israel he speaks, and to what time he has reference.

**Verse 12. ***{and spread forth his hands}* *Toward heaven*, 1 Kings 8:22. So v. 13.

**Verse 13. ***{For Solomon had made a brasen scaffold}* This verse is here inserted by way of parenthesis, but not mentioned 1 Kings 8. That whereon he stood is called a scaffold, because it was erected higher than the ground whereon the people stood.

*{of five cubits long, and five cubits broad}* Heb. *the length thereof five cubits, etc*. Two yards and a half square.

*{and three cubits high}* A yard and half from the ground. Thus he might the more easily be seen of all the people, being so much higher than they.

*{and had set it in the midst of the court}* The great court mentioned ch. 4:9. This remained there long after this, for kings to stand on before the people. See 2 Kings 11:14.

*{and upon it he stood, and kneeled upon his knees}* After he had given praise unto God, which he did standing, then he kneeled to make the supplication following. See 1 Kings 8:22, 54.

*{before all the congregation of Israel}* The great congregation that was then assembled. See 1 Kings 8:5.

*{and spread forth his hands toward heaven}* See 1 Kings 8:22.

**Verse 14. ***{And said, O LORD God of Israel, etc.}* See 1 Kings 8:23.

*{in the heaven}* *Above*.

*{nor in the earth}* *Beneath*. These two words *above* and *beneath* are added 1 Kings 8:23. And they are oft added, where mention is made of heaven and earth, as Exod. 20:4; Jer. 31:37; Acts 2:19.

*{which keepest covenant, etc.}* See 1 Kings 8:23, 24.

**Verse 16. ***{There shall not fail thee a man}* There shall not be a man cut off. The meaning is, All his posterity should not be clean cut off, but there should be an heir left him.

*{to walk in my law}* This is an interpretation of the phrase *walk before me*, 1 Kings 8:25. For they which set God before them, make God’s law their rule; and they who make God’s law their rule, walk before God, and so carry themselves as they who know they are ever in God’s presence.

**Verse 17. ***{O LORD God}* This title *Lord* is left out, 1 Kings 8:26.

*{let thy word be verified}* *I pray thee* is inserted 1 Kings 8:26.

*{thy servant David}* *my father* is added 1 Kings 8:26.

**Verse 18. ***{dwell with men on the earth}* *with men* is left out, 1 Kings 8:27.

*{behold, heaven, etc.}* Ch. 2:6; Isa. 66:1; Acts 7:49.

**Verse 19. ***{prayer before thee}* *to day* is added 1 Kings 8:28.

**Verse 20. ***{which thy servant prayeth towards this place}* Or, *which thy servant shall make in this place*, 1 Kings 8:29.

**Verse 21. ***{which they shall make}* Heb. *pray*.

**Verse 22. ***{And an oath be laid upon him}* Heb. *and he require an oath of him*. Namely, if his neighbor require an oath.

**Verse 23. ***{from heaven}* Or, *in heaven*, 1 Kings 8:32.

*{by requiting the wicked}* Or, *condemning the wicked*, 1 Kings 8:32.

**Verse 24. ***{be put to the worse}* Or, *smitten*, 1 Kings 8:33.

*{before thee in this house}* Or, *unto thee in this house*, 1 Kings 8:33.

**Verse 25. ***{from the heavens}* Or, *in heaven*, 1 Kings 8:34.

*{which thou gavest to them and to their fathers}* *to them* is left out 1 Kings 8:34.

**Verse 26. ***{when the heaven is shut up}* 1 Kings 17:1.

**Verse 27. ***{from heaven}* Or, *in heaven*, 1 Kings 8:36.

*{when thou hast taught them the good way}* Or, *that thou teach them*, 1 Kings 8:36.

**Verse 28. ***{in the cities of their land}* Heb. *in the land of their gates*. Gates by a synecdoche are put for cities. See 1 Kings 8:37.

**Verse 29. ***{when every one shall know his own sore}* Or, *the plague of his own heart*, 1 Kings 8:38.

*{and his own grief}* Grief is here put for that which does or should cause grief, namely, sin.

*{in this house}* Or, *towards this house*, 1 Kings 8:38.

**Verse 30. ***{And forgive}* 1 Kings 8:39 is added *and do*, namely, what was prayed for; grant that to him that prays.

*{unto all his ways}* *all* is left out 1 Kings 8:39. The plural number is equal to a general.

*{for thou only}* *thou* is doubled 1 Kings 8:39.

*{of the children of men}* *all* is inserted 1 Kings 8:39. See 1 Chr. 28:9.

**Verse 31. ***{So long as they live}* Heb. *all the days which they live*.

*{in the land}* Heb. *upon the face of the land*. For men tread upon the uppermost part of the earth, which is the face of it.

**Verse 32. ***{which is not of thy people Israel}* John 12:20; Acts 8:27.

*{for thy great name’s sake}* *great* is left out 1 Kings 8:41.

*{and thy mighty hand, and thy stretched out arm}* Or, *for they shall hear of thy great name, and of thy strong hand, and of thy stretched out arm*. These words are inserted in a parenthesis 1 Kings 8:42, and show the reason why a stranger comes to, or prays towards the house of the Lord.

*{if they come and pray in this house}* Or, *when he shall come and pray towards this house*, 1 Kings 8:42.

**Verse 33. ***{Then hear thou from the heavens}* Or, *hear thou in heaven*, 1 Kings 8:43.

*{and may know that this house which I have built is called by thy name}* Heb. *thy name is called upon this house*.

**Verse 35. ***{from the heavens}* Or, *in heaven*, 1 Kings 8:45.

*{maintain their cause}* Or, *their right*, i.e., their righteous cause.

**Verse 36. ***{There is no man which sinneth not}* Prov. 20:9; Eccles. 7:20; James 3:2; 1 John 1:8.

*{and deliver them over before their enemies}* Or, *and deliver them to the enemy*, 1 Kings 8:46.

*{and they carry them away captives}* Heb. *they that take them captives, carry them away*.

*{unto a land far off or near}* Or, *unto the land of the enemy, far or near*, 1 Kings 8:46.

**Verse 37. ***{Yet if they bethink themselves}* Heb. *bring back to their heart*. When men consider their sin, and repent, they are said to bring it back to their heart.

*{in the land of their captivity}* Or, *in the land whither they were carried captives*, 1 Kings 8:47.

**Verse 38. ***{In the land of their captivity}* Or, *in the land of their enemy*, 1 Kings 8:48.

**Verse 39. ***{Their cause}* Or, *right*. See v. 35.

*{which have sinned against thee}* See what is added 1 Kings 8:50, 51, etc.

**Verse 40. ***{Now, my God, let, I beseech thee, thine eyes be open, and let thine ears be attent}* See 1 Kings 8:29.

*{unto the prayer that is made in this place}* The sense of the Hebraism is well given in our translation.

**Verse 41. ***{Now therefore arise}* Ps. 132:8. This is spoken to God after the manner of man. For when we would have any do a thing readily and cheerfully, we use to say, Arise. See 1 Kings 14:2.

*{O LORD God}* See 1 Kings 8:15, 53.

*{into thy resting place}* i.e., The temple, where the ark was settled. See 1 Chr. 28:2. Solomon hereby entreats God to take possession of that house which he had built for him, and to dwell and abide therein forever.

*{thou, and the ark}* Because God was wont to manifest his presence over the ark, God and the ark are joined together. See 2 Kings 19:15.

*{of thy strength}* This epithet is attributed to the ark, because by it God did manifest his mighty power at Jordan, Josh. 3:15, 16, at Jericho, Josh. 6:4, and among the Philistines, 1 Sam. 5:4, 6. See Ps. 78:61.

*{Let thy priests, O LORD, be clothed}* Let them be adorned as with a garment, Isa. 61:10.

*{with salvation}* Under salvation such graces are comprised as might bring salvation to the priests themselves and to others, 1 Tim. 4:16.

*{and let thy saints rejoice in goodness}* By goodness is meant that favor and kindness which God showed to them.

**Verse 42. ***{O LORD God, turn not away the face}* Reject not the prayer, see 1 Kings 2:16, 17, 20. Of oft using the titles *Lord God* see 1 Chr. 17:27.

*{of thine anointed}* He means this of himself, whom God ordained to be king, and who was thereupon anointed twice. See 1 Chr. 29:22.

*{Remember the mercies of David thy servant}* i.e., The promises which God’s mercy moved him to make to David, yea, and under him to his posterity. See 1 Chr. 17:11, etc.

## CHAPTER 7

**Verse 1. ***{Now when Solomon had made an end of praying}* From hence to v. 4 a visible approbation of Solomon’s prayer is set down. But 1 Kings 8:55, etc. Solomon’s solemn praising of God and blessing the people are inserted before this.

*{the fire came down from heaven}* This was one evidence of God’s accepting Solomon’s prayer. See 1 Chr. 21:26.

*{and consumed the burnt offering and the sacrifices}* See 1 Kings 3:15. The consuming of these showed that God accepted them, Lev. 9:24; 1 Kings 18:38.

*{and the glory of the LORD filled the house}* By the glory of the Lord is meant a thick cloud, which set out the incomprehensible glory of God, which if it had shined forth in its brightness would have utterly dazzled and confounded the beholders. It was therefore covered with a cloud, to show that it was there present, but could not by man’s mortal eye be discerned. After such a manner God dealt with Moses, Exod. 33:22, when he covered with his hand Moses’ face. See 1 Kings 8:10.

**Verse 2. ***{And the priests could not enter into the house of the LORD, etc.}* See 1 Kings 8:11. This cloud was another evidence of God’s approbation.

**Verse 3. ***{And when all the children of Israel saw how the fire came down}* This visible manifestation of God’s good pleasure wonderfully wrought upon the people. We read the like 1 Kings 18:39.

*{and the glory of the LORD upon the house}* The cloud which filled the house within did also cover it without, whereby it came to pass that not only the priests but also the people discerned it. We read the like Exod. 40:34.

*{they bowed themselves with their faces to the ground}* See 1 Chr. 29:20.

*{upon the pavement}* i.e., The floor of the great court. See 1 Kings 6:36.

*{and worshipped}* As they testified their reverence by bowing themselves, so they did also inwardly adore and worship God, head and heart moved together.

*{and praised the LORD}* They did also with their tongues set forth the glory of God.

*{saying, For he is good, for his mercy endureth for ever}* See ch. 5:13.

**Verse 4. ***{Then the king and all the people offered sacrifices before the LORD}* 1 Kings 8:62, instead of *all the people* is put *all Israel*.

**Verse 5. ***{And king Solomon offered a sacrifice}* The kind of sacrifice, namely, *peace offerings*, is set down 1 Kings 8:63, and there is added *which he offered unto the Lord*.

*{of twenty and two thousand oxen, and an hundred and twenty thousand sheep}* The greatest offering that we read of. See 1 Kings 8:63.

*{So the king and all the people}* Or, *the children of Israel*, 1 Kings 8:63.

*{dedicated the house of God}* 1 Kings 8:63, *the Lord*.

**Verse 6. ***{And the priests waited on their offices}* Every one in this solemn sacrifice did what belonged to his course, as they were in general appointed by the law, Lev. 1:2, etc., and particularly by David, 1 Chr. 24:3.

*{the Levites also with instruments of musick}* Such as are expressed 1 Chr. 15:16, etc.

*{of the LORD}* Composed to his honor. Or, *whole instruments*.

*{which David the king had made}* Or, *caused to be made*.

*{to praise the LORD}* David had ordained them to sing and set forth the praise of God.

*{because his mercy endureth for ever}* By this clause the whole 136 Psalm is meant: for this is the cadence, staff, or latter part of every verse in that Psalm: and that Psalm was wont to be sung at all solemn praisings of God. See ch. 5:13.

*{when David praised by their ministry}* Heb. *by their hand*. See 1 Kings 8:53, 56.

*{and the priests sounded trumpets before them}* The priests here meant were those hundred and twenty mentioned ch. 5:12.

*{and all Israel stood}* This is an evidence of their reverent attention to what they saw and heard.

**Verse 7. ***{Moreover, Solomon hallowed}* This has reference to v. 5, and may resolve a doubt about the multitude of sacrifices there mentioned. They were too many to offer on one altar, but this place shows what other means Solomon used.

*{the middle of the court, etc.}* See 1 Kings 8:64.

*{burnt offerings, and the fat of the peace offerings}* Betwixt these *meat offerings* are inserted 1 Kings 8:64, which are also mentioned in the end of this verse.

*{because the brasen altar which Solomon had made}* Or, *that was before the Lord*, 1 Kings 8:64.

*{was not able}* Or, *was too little*, 1 Kings 8:64.

*{and the fat}* *Of the peace offerings*, 1 Kings 8:64.

**Verse 8. ***{Also at the same time}* When he offered solemn a sacrifice, as is noted v. 5. For he had great store of provision.

*{Solomon kept the feast seven days, etc.}* This is meant of that extraordinary feast made at the dedication of the temple, which began on the eighth day of the seventh month, which in part answered to our September. On the fifteenth day of that month began the feast of tabernacles, Lev. 23:34, and thereof mention is made in the latter end of the next verse. In relation to these two feasts it is said that they kept the feast *seven days and seven days, even fourteen days*, 1 Kings 8:65. It is there also said that they held this feast *before the Lord their God*.

**Verse 9. ***{And in the eighth day they made a solemn assembly}* Heb. *a restraint*, namely from servile works. This was the eighth day from the beginning of the first feast, mentioned v. 8, and the first day of the other feast, the feast of tabernacles, and the fifteenth day of the month, Lev. 23:34.

*{for they kept the dedication of the altar}* Because the altar was the principal thing about the temple, and the service of God, being that whereon all their offerings were laid, it is here synecdochically put for the whole house of God and for all things appertaining thereunto: therefore it is said that *they dedicated the house of the Lord*, v. 5 and 1 Kings 8:63; so also concerning the second temple, that *they kept the dedication of the house*, Ezra 6:16, 17. This dedication of the house and of the altar set out one and the same thing. Unless it should be imagined that there were two dedications, one of the temple, another of the altar. And we read of a solemn dedication of the altar that Moses made, Num. 7:10, 11, 84, 88, and of a continuance of a feast styled *the feast of the dedication*, John 10:22, which is supposed to have had relation to the dedication of an altar, 1 Macc. 4:59.

*{seven days}* See v. 8.

*{and the feast seven days}* These were other seven days immediately following upon the former. In these latter seven the feast of tabernacles was kept.

**Verse 10. ***{And on the three and twentieth day of the seventh month}* This is said to be *the eighth day*, 1 Kings 8:66, namely the eighth from the beginning of the feast of tabernacles, which began on the fifteenth of the seventh month, Lev. 23:34. And thus it falls out just on the three and twentieth day.

*{he sent the people away into their tents}* i.e., Their several habitations. See 1 Kings 8:66 and 12:16.

*{glad and merry in heart}* *they went unto their tents joyful and glad of heart*, 1 Kings 8:66.

*{for the goodness}* Or, *all the goodness*. See 1 Kings 8:66.

*{that the LORD had shewed unto David}* Or, *had done for David his servant*, 1 Kings 8:66.

*{and to Solomon}* This is left out 1 Kings 8:66, but is comprised under the former clause. For the special kindness here intended unto David had relation to Solomon; for it was a kindness to David that his son prospered so as he did. And herein the good promise made to David, 1 Chr. 17:11, etc., is comprised.

*{and to Israel his people}* The whole land had a benefit by God’s blessing of Solomon.

**Verse 11. ***{Thus Solomon finished the house of the LORD}* Or, *it came to pass, when Solomon had finished the building of the house of the Lord*, 1 Kings 9:1.

*{and the king’s house}* i.e., The royal palace in Jerusalem, wherein the kings of Judah had their chief residence, generation after generation. It seems to have been a very stately house; for Solomon was thirteen years in building it, 1 Kings 7:1.

*{and all that came into Solomon’s heart}* Or, *all Solomon’s desire which he was pleased to do*, 1 Kings 9:1.

*{to make in the house of the LORD}* This has reference to all manner of things appertaining to the temple and to the courts thereof, movable or immovable.

*{and in his own house}* This has as large an extent as former.

*{he prosperously effected}* He finished with very good success, according to his father’s charge, 1 Chr. 22:11, and his own desire.

**Verse 12. ***{And the LORD appeared unto Solomon by night}* This was a second time, 1 Kings 9:2. God first appeared unto him in the beginning of his reign, before he began to build the temple, ch. 1:7. And now again, after all is finished. This God did to give testimony of his accepting his prayer, and to return a gracious answer thereunto.

*{and said unto him, I have heard thy prayer}* *and thy supplication that thou hast made before me*, 1 Kings 9:3. Of the difference betwixt prayer and supplication see 1 Kings 8:28.

*{and have chosen this place}* Or, *I have hallowed this house which thou hast built*, 1 Kings 9:3.

*{to myself}* Or, *to put my name there for ever*, 1 Kings 9:3.

*{for an house of sacrifice}* Therein alone sacrifices were to be offered up, which were then a principal part of God’s service.

**Verse 13. ***{If I shut up heaven that there be no rain}* This has reference to that petition ch. 6:26, whereof see 1 Kings 8:35.

*{or if I command the locusts to devour the land}* This has reference to ch. 6:28. See 1 Kings 8:37. Under locusts are comprised caterpillars and all other like hurtful creatures; and under land, whatsoever fruit grows out of the earth.

*{or if I send pestilence among my people}* This also has reference to ch. 6:28. See 1 Kings 8:37.

**Verse 14. ***{If my people which are called by my name}* Heb. *upon whom my name is called*. i.e., Such as God chose to be of his household and family, i.e., his church, whose God and Lord he professed himself to be, and who professed God’s name, acknowledging thereby that he was their Lord and they his people, Deut. 28:10; Amos 9:12.

*{shall humble themselves}* This is very acceptable in the sight of the high God, Isa. 57:15; 1 Pet. 5:6.

*{and pray}* This is the means which God has sanctified both for obtaining blessings, Matt. 7:7, and removing judgments, Ps. 50:15.

*{and seek my face}* i.e., My favor and grace. See 1 Kings 13:6. This is the ground of all true confidence in them that pray.

*{and turn from their wicked ways}* All the other means without this are nothing at all. Repentance is a grace necessary for such as desire God’s favor, Lam. 3:40.

*{then will I hear from heaven}* See ch. 6:21. When men are once rightly qualified and duly prepared by their afflictions for mercy, God will soon hear them. Now because heaven is the throne of God, and petitioners use to come to a king sitting on his throne when they prefer their petitions unto him, God is said to hear from heaven.

*{and will forgive their sin}* Sin makes us so odious in God’s sight as till that be forgiven he will grant no true mercy. See 1 Kings 8:30, 34; Matt. 9:2.

*{and will heal their land}* This is a metaphor taken from the bodies of living creatures. When sores or wounds therein are so dressed as that all putrefaction and anguish is removed, and the flesh brought to be as it was before, they are said to be healed: so when judgments, which are the sores or wounds of a land, are removed, the land is said to be healed.

**Verse 15. ***{Now mine eyes shall be open}* This phrase is also metaphorical. When men will not see or regard a thing, they use to shut their eyes; but when they have a mind to behold it, and to do good to it, they will open their eyes upon it. See 1 Kings 8:29.

*{and mine ears attent unto the prayer that is made in this place}* Heb. *to the prayer of this place*. See ch. 6:40. They who have a mind to grant what is desired, will with their ears diligently attend to it.

**Verse 16. ***{For now have I chosen and sanctified this house, etc.}* See 1 Kings 9:3.

**Verse 17. ***{And as for thee}* This phrase imports a special and particular direction to Solomon himself, and is used to make him the more attentive thereunto.

*{if thou wilt walk before me as David thy father walked}* 1 Kings 9:4 is further added, *in integrity of heart and in uprightness*.

*{and do according to all, etc.}* See 1 Kings 9:4.

**Verse 18. ***{Then will I stablish the throne of thy kingdom}* 1 Kings 9:5, *upon Israel for ever*, is added.

*{according as I have covenanted with David thy father}* Or, *as I promised to David*, 1 Kings 9:5. See 2 Sam. 7:12; 1 Chr. 22:10.

*{saying, There shall not fail thee a man}* Heb. *there shall not be cut off to thee*. See ch. 6:16.

*{to be ruler in Israel}* Or, *upon the throne of Israel*, 1 Kings 9:5. This shows that he means by ruler a sovereign king.

**Verse 19. ***{But if you turn away}* Or, *if you shall at all turn from following me*, 1 Kings 9:6. And there is added, *you*, or *your children*.

*{and forsake my statutes and my commandments which I have set before you}* Or, *will not keep my commandments and my statutes, etc*., 1 Kings 9:6; Lev. 26:14; Deut. 28:15.

*{and shall go and serve other gods, and worship them}* See 1 Kings 9:6.

**Verse 20. ***{Then will I pluck them up by the roots out of my land}* This metaphor is taken from briers, thorns, and all manner of noisome weeds, which men use to root out of their grounds; and it signifies an utter destruction of them. It is thus expressed 1 Kings 9:7, *then will I cut off Israel out of the land*.

*{which I have given them}* See 1 Kings 8:36.

*{and this house which I have sanctified for my name, will I cast out of my sight}* See 1 Kings 9:7.

*{and will make it to be a proverb}* Or, *Israel shall be a proverb*, 1 Kings 9:7.

*{and a byword among all nations}* Or, *among all people*, 1 Kings 9:7.

**Verse 21. ***{And this house which is high, shall be an astonishment to every one that passeth by it}* Or, *at this house which is high, every one that passeth by it shall be astonished, and shall hiss*, 1 Kings 9:8.

*{so that he shall say}* Namely, every one, or any one. Or, *and they shall say*, 1 Kings 9:8.

*{Why hath the LORD done thus unto this land, and unto this house?}* See 1 Kings 9:8; Deut. 19:24; Jer. 22:8, 9.

**Verse 22. ***{And it shall be answered}* Or, *they shall answer*, 1 Kings 9:9.

*{Because they forsook the LORD God of their fathers}* Or, *Because they forsook the Lord their God*, 1 Kings 9:9. For he who was the God of their fathers was also their God. See ch. 11:16.

*{which brought them forth out of the land of Egypt}* Or, *who brought forth their fathers out of the land of Egypt*, 1 Kings 9:9. For if their fathers had not been brought out of Egypt, their children, generation after generation, might have been bond slaves there. So as by bringing their fathers out of Egypt they who are here spoken of were brought thence.

*{and laid hold on other gods, and worshipped them, and served them}* See 1 Kings 9:9.

*{therefore hath he brought all this evil upon them}* Or, *therefore hath the Lord brought upon them all this evil*, 1 Kings 9:9.

## CHAPTER 8

**Verse 1. ***{And it came to pass}* Hitherto has been declared how Solomon settled the matters of the house of God, and his service. The affairs of the state are set down in this chapter.

*{at the end of twenty years, etc.}* See 1 Kings 9:10.

**Verse 2. ***{That the cities which Huram had restored to Solomon}* This Huram did because the cities given him by Solomon pleased him not, 1 Kings 9:12.

*{Solomon built them}* He caused them to be repaired and fortified. See 1 Kings 9:17.

*{and caused the children of Israel to dwell there}* Solomon made them useful for his people, though the king of Tyre regarded them not.

**Verse 3. ***{And Solomon went to Hamath-zobah}* This appertained to the king of Zobah. See 1 Chr. 18:3.

*{and prevailed against it}* David had overcome the king of Zobah, but it may be Hamath was not then taken by him: or if it were, it revolted after David’s death, and thereupon Solomon fought against it, and got it by force.

**Verse 4. ***{And he built Tadmor in the wilderness}* So 1 Kings 9:18.

*{and all the store cities}* See 1 Kings 9:19.

*{which he built in Hamath}* Hamath being taken from the enemy, Solomon built therein places for ammunition, for the better defense thereof.

**Verse 5. ***{Also he built Beth-horon the upper, and Beth-horon the nether}* See 1 Kings 9:17; 1 Chr. 7:24.

*{fenced cities, with walls, gates, and bars}* Like a prudent prince, in the time of peace he provides against enemies.

**Verse 6. ***{And Baalath, and all the store cities, etc.}* See 1 Kings 9:18, 19.

*{and all that Solomon desired to build}* Heb. *All the desire of Solomon which he desired to build*.

*{in Jerusalem, and in Lebanon, etc.}* See 1 Kings 9:19.

**Verse 7. ***{As for the people that were left, etc.}* Or, *All the people that were left*, 1 Kings 9:20.

*{which were not of Israel}* Or, *which were not of the children of Israel*, 1 Kings 9:20.

**Verse 8. ***{But of their children who were left after them in the land}* 1 Kings 9:21, *but* is left out; and in the original it is not expressed in this place.

*{whom the children of Israel consumed not}* Or, *whom the children of Israel also were not able utterly to destroy*, 1 Kings 9:21.

*{them did Solomon make to pay tribute}* Or, *upon those did Solomon levy a tribute of bondservice*.

*{until this day}* See ch. 5:9.

**Verse 9. ***{But of the children of Israel did Solomon make no servants for his work}* He means hereby bondmen, 1 Kings 9:22.

*{but they were men of war}* See 1 Kings 9:22.

*{and chief of his captains, and captains of his chariots and horsemen}* Or, *and his servants, and his princes, and his captains, and rulers of his chariots, and his horsemen*, 1 Kings 9:22.

**Verse 10. ***{And these were the chief of king Solomon’s officers}* Or, *these were the chief of the officers that were over Solomon’s work*, 1 Kings 9:23.

*{even two hundred and fifty}* 1 Kings 9:23, *five hundred and fifty*. See on that place.

*{that bare rule over the people}* 1 Kings 9:23 is added *that wrought in the work*.

**Verse 11. ***{And Solomon brought up the daughter of Pharaoh out of the city of David}* Or, *but Pharaoh’s daughter came up out of the city of David*, 1 Kings 9:24.

*{unto the house that he had built for her}* Solomon at first placed his wife in the city of David, 1 Kings 3:1, upon necessity, because he had not elsewhere a place fit for such a princess: but after he had built the Lord’s house and his own, he built a fair palace for her, 1 Kings 7:8, into which he removed her from the city of David.

*{for he said, My wife shall not dwell in the house of David king of Israel, because the places are holy}* Heb. *holiness*. This reason was ceremonial. Hereby it appears that he reserved the place where the ark formerly stood for some holy use, or at least left it void. But if Pharaoh’s daughter had still continued in the place where she was before, Solomon must have taken in the place where the ark was, to make a palace for his wife.

*{whereunto the ark of the LORD hath come}* This shows the reason why that place was counted holy. The ark was counted the most holy type that the Jews had, and the principal evidence of God’s presence. Solomon therefore sanctified that place, where God was pleased to manifest his presence so solemnly.

**Verse 12. ***{Then Solomon offered burnt offerings unto the LORD}* Then has reference to a continual custom which Solomon constantly used, as is evident 1 Kings 9:25. *And three times in a year did Solomon offer burnt offerings, and peace offerings*.

*{on the altar of the LORD}* Or, *upon the altar which he built unto the Lord*, 1 Kings 9:25.

*{which he had built before the porch}* The porch was at the east end of the temple, 1 Kings 6:3, and there were wide doors at the entering into or out of the priests’ court, and at the entering out of it into the holy place, which being opened, the priests and people might see the most holy place. Now this altar is said to be *built before the porch*, to show 1. that it was in the priests’ court; 2. that it was placed directly before the ark; for the porch was before the holy place and the most holy place.

**Verse 13. ***{Even after a certain rate}* Of this phrase see on 1 Kings 8:59.

*{every day}* Both morning and evening, Num. 28:3, 4.

*{offering according to the commandment of Moses, on the sabbaths}* The offerings on the sabbaths were to be *beside the continual daily burnt offering*, Num. 28:10.

*{and on the new moons}* See ch. 1:4; 1 Chr. 23:31.

*{and on the solemn feasts}* These are set down Lev. 23.

*{three times in a year}* See Exod. 23:14; Deut. 16:16.

*{even in the feast of unleavened bread}* Of the offering at this feast see Num. 28:19. The Passover was called *the feast of the unleavened bread*, Exod. 12:17; Lev. 23:6; Deut. 16:16, because then no leaven was to be eaten, Exod. 12:15.

*{and in the feast of weeks}* This was the feast of firstfruits, Lev. 23:10. Of the offerings therein see Num. 28:27.

*{and in the feast of tabernacles}* See Lev. 23:34, etc. Of the offerings to be offered on every several day of this feast see Num. 29:13, etc.

**Verse 14. ***{And he appointed according to the order of David his father}* This has reference to 1 Chr. 24:1, etc.

*{the courses of the priests to their service}* These services of the priests are distinctly set down Lev. 1:2, 3, etc. See 1 Chr. 16:40.

*{and the Levites to their charges}* See 1 Chr. 25, 26.

*{to praise and minister before the priests}* To praise has reference to such as were appointed to sing and play on instruments; to minister, to such as were to attend the priests about sacrifices and other services.

*{as the duty of every day required}* See 1 Chr. 16:37.

*{the porters also by their courses}* See 1 Chr. 9:17, etc. and 26:1, etc.

*{at every gate}* Heb. *at the door and door*. See 1 Chr. 9:19, 22 and 26:13, 16.

*{for so had David the man of God commanded}* Heb. *so was the commandment of David the man of God*. The commandment which David gave hereabouts was from the Lord, 1 Chr. 23:25; 2 Chr. 29:25, and therefore the more carefully to be observed. David is called *a man of God*, because he was chosen of God, Ps. 78:70, and inspired by God, 2 Sam. 23:2, and *a man after God’s own heart*, 1 Sam. 13:14; Acts 13:22.

**Verse 15. ***{And they departed not from the commandment of the king unto the priests and Levites}* i.e., the priests and Levites did carefully and conscionably observe those things which Solomon had given them in charge.

*{concerning any matter}* i.e., all such things as belonged unto them in their several courses and services.

*{or concerning the treasures}* About safekeeping and just disposing the treasures committed to their charge. See 1 Chr. 26:20, etc.

**Verse 16. ***{Now all the work of Solomon was prepared}* Whatsoever was requisite for the effecting of anything that Solomon appointed to be done, was provided and well ordered.

*{unto the day of the foundation of the house of the LORD}* Till he began to build, and to do what he intended to do about the house of the Lord.

*{and until it was finished}* From time to time till all was ended. Some read it thus, *Whatsoever was requisite for beginning and perfecting all, from laying the foundation even until all was finished, was prepared*.

*{So the house of God was perfected}* See 1 Kings 9:25.

**Verse 17. ***{Then went Solomon to Ezion-geber, and to Eloth}* Or, *to Elath*, Deut. 2:8. See 1 Kings 9:26. In these two places Solomon had a navy of ships made, in which respect he is said to go thither, that he might take the better notice of them.

*{at the sea side in the land of Edom}* See 1 Kings 9:26.

**Verse 18. ***{And Huram sent him}* Or, *conducted*, or, *guided for him*. So is this word used Gen. 18:16. See 1 Kings 9:27.

*{by the hands of his servants ships}* What these servants were is a little after declared, namely mariners. The things which by their skill and pains were conducted or guided were ships.

*{and servants}* Or, *even servants*.

*{that had knowledge of the sea}* Skillful seamen.

*{and they went with the servants of Solomon}* See 1 Kings 9:27.

*{to Ophir}* See 1 Kings 9:28.

*{and took thence four hundred and fifty talents of gold}* i.e., one million, six hundred eighty-seven thousand and five hundred pounds sterling. See 1 Chr. 22:14. In 1 Kings 9:28 it is but four hundred and twenty talents. See the reason of the difference there.

*{and brought them to king Solomon}* See 1 Kings 9:28.

## CHAPTER 9

**Verse 1. ***{And when the queen of Sheba}* In the twelve first verses of this chapter an especial evidence of the spreading of Solomon’s fame far and near is given, by the coming of a queen from the uttermost parts of the earth, Matt. 12:42, to be an eye and ear witness of the things reported of Solomon in her own and other dominions.

*{heard of the fame of Solomon}* *Concerning the name of the Lord*, 1 Kings 10:1.

*{at Jerusalem}* Or, *to Jerusalem; and so it has reference to her coming thither*, 1 Kings 10:2.

*{with a very great company}* Or, *train*, 1 Kings 10:2.

*{gold in abundance}* Or, *very much gold*, 1 Kings 10:2.

*{and when she was come, etc.}* See 1 Kings 10:2.

**Verse 2. ***{And Solomon told her, etc.}* See 1 Kings 10:3.

*{from Solomon}* *The king*, 1 Kings 10:3.

**Verse 3. ***{And when the queen of Sheba had seen the wisdom of Solomon, etc.}* Or, *all Solomon’s wisdom*, 1 Kings 10:4.

**Verse 4. ***{And the meat, etc.}* See 1 Kings 10:5.

*{his cupbearers also}* Or, *butlers*.

*{and their apparel}* The apparel of Solomon’s ministers is expressed 1 Kings 10:5, but this phrase is not there applied to his cupbearers, yet here expressed, because several officers had their several apparel fit for their functions. The ministers had their distinct apparel, and the cupbearers theirs.

**Verse 5. ***{And she said unto the king, It was a true report}* Heb. *word*. See 1 Kings 10:6.

*{which I heard in mine own land, of thy acts, etc.}* Or, *sayings*. See 1 Kings 10:6.

**Verse 6. ***{Howbeit, I believed not their words}* Or, *the words*. See 1 Kings 10:7.

*{for the one half of the greatness of thy wisdom was not told me}* *The greatness of thy wisdom* is not set down 1 Kings 10:7, for mention is made of Solomon’s wisdom in the next clause of that text.

*{for thou exceedest the fame that I heard}* Or, *thy wisdom and prosperity exceedeth the fame which I heard*, 1 Kings 10:7.

**Verse 7. ***{Happy are thy men, etc.}* See 1 Kings 10:8.

**Verse 8. ***{Blessed be the LORD thy God, which delighted in thee, to set thee on his throne}* Or, *on the throne of Israel*. Why it is called *the throne of Israel*, see 1 Kings 10:9, and why *the throne of the Lord*, 1 Chr. 29:23.

*{to be king for the LORD thy God}* This shows one reason why it was called the throne of the Lord. Solomon is said to be *king for the Lord*, as in general because God is the high supreme king, and kings on earth bear his image and are his vicegerents; so in particular because Israel, over whom Solomon reigned, was God’s peculiar people, Exod. 19:5. Of *thy God* see 1 Kings 13:6 and 17:12 and 18:10.

*{because thy God loved Israel}* Or, *because the Lord loved Israel for ever*, 1 Kings 10:9.

*{therefore made he thee king over them}* *Over them* is here added to show that herein the love of God to Israel was manifested, not simply in that Solomon was a king, but in that he was a king over Israel.

**Verse 9. ***{And she gave the king an hundred and twenty talents of gold}* See 1 Kings 9:14.

*{and of spices great abundance}* Or, *of spices very great store*, 1 Kings 10:10.

*{Neither was there any such spice as the queen, etc.}* Or, *there came no more such abundance of spices as these*, 1 Kings 10:10.

**Verse 10. ***{And the servants also of Huram, and the servants of Solomon}* Or, *and the navy also of Hiram*, 1 Kings 10:11. By servants are meant mariners, ch. 8:18. And Solomon’s and Huram’s servants went together in a navy: therefore under servants the navy is comprised; and under navy, servants.

*{which brought gold from Ophir, brought algum trees}* Or, *the navy that brought gold from Ophir, brought in from Ophir great plenty of almug trees*, 1 Kings 10:11. See there.

**Verse 11. ***{And the king made of the algum trees terraces, etc.}* Or, *stairs*. Heb. *high ways*. See 1 Kings 10:12.

*{And there were none such seen before in the land of Judah}* Or, *there came no such algum trees, nor were seen unto this day*, 1 Kings 10:12.

**Verse 12. ***{And king Solomon gave to the queen, etc.}* See 1 Kings 10:13.

*{Besides that which she had brought unto the king}* It is said v. 1 that the queen of Sheba brought *spices, gold in abundance, and precious stones*: these she gave to the king, v. 9. He therefore either in such or some other kinds of gifts returned to her full as much in value; and this by way of recompense. But besides, he gave her also of his royal bounty other rare and precious presents: and if she liked anything in his kingdom that she saw, upon manifestation of her desire he gave her it also.

*{So she turned and went away, etc.}* See 1 Kings 10:13.

**Verse 13. ***{Now the weight of gold that came, etc.}* See 1 Kings 10:14.

**Verse 14. ***{Besides that which chapmen and merchants brought}* Or, *besides that he had of the merchantmen, and of the traffick of the spice merchants*, 1 Kings 10:15.

*{and all the kings of Arabia, and governors of the country}* Or, *captains of the country*. See 1 Kings 10:15.

*{brought gold and silver to Solomon}* This was an especial means of that abundance that Solomon had.

**Verse 15. ***{And king Solomon made two hundred targets, etc.}* See 1 Kings 10:16.

**Verse 16. ***{And three hundred shields}* See 1 Kings 10:17.

*{three hundred shekels of gold went to one shield}* *Shekels* is not in the original, neither can it be properly here understood. Indefinitely here are meant pieces of gold, and that of such a quantity as an hundred of them made a pound. And thus three hundred pieces of gold here agree with three pound of gold, 1 Kings 10:17.

*{and the king put them, etc.}* See 1 Kings 10:17.

**Verse 17. ***{Moreover the king made a great throne of ivory and overlaid it with pure gold}* Or, *the best gold*, 1 Kings 10:18.

**Verse 18. ***{And there were six steps to the throne}* Or, *the throne had six steps*, 1 Kings 10:19, but the Hebrew in both places is the same.

*{with a footstool of gold}* There is a little difference betwixt the description of this throne here and 1 Kings 10:19. Here mention is made of a footstool; there, of a top: and the throne had both a cover and a footstool; and the one is mentioned in the one place, the other in the other.

*{which were fastened to the throne}* This has reference to the steps, for the word is of the plural number.

*{and stays on each side}* Heb. *hands on this and that side*. They were such stays as a man might rest his hands upon; or, by which a man might be helped up, as by an hand.

*{of the sitting place}* Heb. *on the place of the seat*.

**Verse 19. ***{And twelve lions stood there, etc.}* See 1 Kings 10:20.

**Verse 20. ***{And all the drinking vessels, etc.}* See 1 Kings 10:21.

**Verse 21. ***{For the king’s ships went to Tarshish with the servants of Huram}* Or, *the king had at sea a navy of Tarshish, with the navy of Hiram*, 1 Kings 10:22. See v. 10.

**Verse 22. ***{And king Solomon passed all the kings of the earth in riches and wisdom}* See 1 Kings 10:23. There is more difference in the translation betwixt these two places than in the original.

**Verse 23. ***{And all the kings of the earth}* Or, *and all the earth*, 1 Kings 10:24.

*{sought the presence, etc.}* Heb. *face*.

**Verse 24. ***{And they brought every man, etc.}* See 1 Kings 10:25.

*{and raiment, harness}* Or, *garments, and armour*, 1 Kings 10:25. The same Hebrew words are in both places.

*{and spices, etc.}* See 1 Kings 10:25.

**Verse 25. ***{And Solomon had}* Or, *Solomon gathered together chariots and horsemen*, 1 Kings 10:26.

*{four thousand stalls for horses and chariots}* See on 1 Kings 10:26.

**Verse 26. ***{And he reigned over all the kings}* Or, *over all the kingdoms*, 1 Kings 4:21.

*{from the river}* i.e., Euphrates, Gen. 15:18.

*{even unto the land of the Philistines, and to the border of Egypt}* See 1 Kings 4:21.

**Verse 27. ***{And the king made}* Heb. *gave*.

*{silver in Jerusalem as stones, etc.}* See 1 Kings 10:27.

*{that are in the low plains in abundance}* Or, *that are in the vale for abundance*, 1 Kings 10:27. There is the very same Hebrew in both places.

**Verse 28. ***{And they brought unto Solomon horses out of Egypt}* Or, *Solomon had horses brought out of Egypt*, 1 Kings 10:28. This also is there added *and fine linen*.

*{and out of all lands}* Hereby are meant the nations that were round about him, and had store of good horses.

**Verse 29. ***{Now the rest of the acts of Solomon}* See 1 Kings 11:41.

*{first and last}* See 1 Chr. 29:29. By the first acts of Solomon may be meant such as he did before his apostasy; by the last, such as after.

*{are they not written in the book}* Heb. *words*. A book consists of words. See on the title of the first book of Chronicles.

*{of Nathan the prophet}* See 1 Chr. 29:29.

*{and in the prophecy of Ahijah the Shilonite}* See 1 Kings 11:29.

*{and in the visions}* Such visions and revelations as were registered, being by God’s Spirit manifested to Iddo.

*{of Iddo the seer}* See ch. 12:15 and 13:22. Some take this man to be the same that is called Obed, 1 Kings 15:1.

*{against Jeroboam the son of Nebat}* There is an express prophecy of Ahijah against Jeroboam, 1 Kings 14:7, etc. And it is to be supposed that Iddo, who wrote the history of Rehoboam, ch. 12:15, did also join the story of Jeroboam therewith. Part of the first book of Kings is here meant, or some civil records penned by these prophets; which without prejudice to canonical Scripture might be lost.

**Verse 30. ***{And Solomon reigned in Jerusalem, etc.}* See 1 Kings 11:42.

**Verse 31. ***{And Solomon slept with his fathers, etc.}* See 1 Kings 11:43.

## CHAPTER 10
