**Verse 1. ***{And Rehoboam went to Shechem, etc.}* In this chapter the revolt of ten tribes from the house of David is set down. This foolish King Rehoboam was the occasion thereof.

**Verse 2. ***{And it came to pass, etc.}* See 1 Kings 11:2.

*{who was in Egypt}* Or, *who was yet in Egypt*, 1 Kings 12:2.

*{whither he had fled from the presence of Solomon the king}* Or, *for he was fled from the presence of king Solomon*, 1 Kings 12:2. There is further added, *and Jeroboam dwelt in Egypt*. See 1 Kings 11:40.

*{heard it}* This has reference to the beginning of the verse, *q.d.*, *When Jeroboam heard it*. In the Hebrew they are so joined together.

*{that Jeroboam returned out of Egypt}* This is not mentioned 1 Kings 12:2, but is necessarily implied by that which follows.

**Verse 3. ***{And they sent and called him}* See 1 Kings 12:3.

*{so Jeroboam and all Israel came, etc.}* Or, *and Jeroboam and all the congregation of Israel came*, 1 Kings 12:3.

**Verse 4. ***{Thy father made our yoke grievous, etc.}* See 1 Kings 12:4.

*{Now therefore ease thou somewhat the grievous servitude of thy father, etc.}* There is a little difference betwixt this place and 1 Kings 12:4 in the translation, but not in the original.

**Verse 5. ***{And he said unto them, Come again unto me after three days, etc.}* Or, *Depart yet for three days, then come again to me, etc*., 1 Kings 12:5. Here where he says, *come again*, he implies they should depart for that time, that so he might take advice in their absence.

**Verse 6. ***{And king Rehoboam took counsel with the old men}* Or, *consulted*, 1 Kings 12:6.

*{that had stood before Solomon, etc.}* There is greater difference in the translation of this verse and of 1 Kings 12:6 than in the Hebrew.

**Verse 7. ***{And they spake unto him, saying, If thou be kind to this people}* Or, *If thou wilt be a servant unto this people this day, and wilt serve them*, 1 Kings 12:7. These two places expound one another. For he that seeks to satisfy the desire of others after a kind manner does therein seem to be a servant unto them.

*{and please them, and speak good words unto them, etc.}* By speaking good words to them he might please them. See 1 Kings 12:7.

**Verse 8. ***{But he forsook the counsel which the old men gave him, etc.}* Here also is a greater difference in the translation betwixt this verse and 1 Kings 12:8 than in the Hebrew.

**Verse 9. ***{And he said unto them, What advice give ye, etc.}* This verse in the Hebrew agrees with 1 Kings 12:9. word for word.

**Verse 10. ***{And the young men that were brought up with him, etc.}* This verse also agrees with 1 Kings 12:10 in the Hebrew; only there are two different words which signify one and the same thing.

**Verse 11. ***{For whereas my father put an heavy yoke upon you, etc.}* Heb. *laded you*. This also in Hebrew agrees with 1 Kings 12:11 in sense and words.

*{but I will chastise you with scorpions}* *I will chastise you* is not in the Hebrew, but well supplied out of 1 Kings 12:11.

**Verse 12. ***{So Jeroboam and all the people came, etc.}* See 1 Kings 10:12.

**Verse 13. ***{And the king answered them roughly}* Or, *and the king answered the people roughly*, 1 Kings 12:13.

*{and king Rehoboam forsook the counsel of the old men}* Or, *forsook the old men’s counsel that they gave him*, 1 Kings 12:13.

**Verse 14. ***{And answered them after the advice of the young men, etc.}* See v. 11.

**Verse 15. ***{So the king hearkened not unto the people, etc.}* See 1 Kings 12:15.

*{for the cause was of God}* Or, *for the cause was from the Lord*, 1 Kings 12:15.

*{that the LORD might perform his word}* Or, *that he might perform his saying*, 1 Kings 12:15.

*{which he spake by the hand of Ahijah, etc.}* Or, *which the Lord spake by the hand of Ahijah*, 1 Kings 12:15. In the Hebrew both places are alike. See 1 Kings 8:53.

**Verse 16. ***{And when all Israel saw, etc.}* See 1 Kings 12:16.

*{every man to your tents, O Israel}* Or, *to your tents, O Israel*, 1 Kings 12:16.

**Verse 17. ***{But as for the children of Israel, etc.}* See 1 Kings 12:17.

**Verse 18. ***{Rehoboam sent Hadoram, etc.}* Or, *Adoram*, 1 Kings 12:18.

*{and the children of Israel stoned him, etc.}* Or, *all Israel stoned him*, 1 Kings 12:18.

**Verse 19. ***{And Israel rebelled, etc.}* See 1 Kings 12:19. The people’s making Jeroboam king over Israel is further added 1 Kings 12:20.

## CHAPTER 11

**Verse 1. ***{And when Rehoboam was come to Jerusalem}* Here is set down the Lord’s hindering Rehoboam’s attempt to reduce the ten tribes.

*{he gathered of the house of Judah and Benjamin, etc.}* Or, *he assembled all the house of Judah with the tribe of Benjamin*, 1 Kings 12:21.

*{to fight against Israel}* Or, *against the house of Israel*, 1 Kings 12:21.

*{that he might bring the kingdom again to Rehoboam}* 1 Kings 12:21, *to Rehoboam the son of Solomon*.

**Verse 2. ***{But the word of the LORD, etc.}* Or, *of God*, 1 Kings 12:22.

**Verse 3. ***{Speak unto Rehoboam, etc.}* 1 Kings 12:23.

*{and to all Israel in Judah and Benjamin}* Or, *unto all the house of Judah and Benjamin, and to the remnant of the people*, 1 Kings 12:23.

**Verse 4. ***{Thus saith the LORD, Ye shall not go up}* See 1 Kings 12:24.

*{nor fight against your brethren}* *The children of Israel*, 1 Kings 12:24.

*{Return every man to his house: for this thing is done of me}* Or, *for this thing is from me*, 1 Kings 12:24.

*{And they obeyed the words of the LORD}* Or, *They hearkened therefore to the words of the Lord*, 1 Kings 12:24.

*{and returned from going against Jeroboam}* Or, *and returned to depart, according to the word of the Lord*, 1 Kings 12:24.

**Verse 5. ***{And Rehoboam dwelt in Jerusalem}* Jerusalem was the fairest and strongest city in all Judah and Benjamin, and there Solomon had built a royal palace; therefore Rehoboam there settled his habitation. See 1 Kings 12:18.

*{and built cities for defence in Judah}* Especially in the uttermost coasts, bordering upon the kingdom of Israel.

**Verse 6. ***{He built}* Here are fifteen cities reckoned up, most of which were built before; so as by building is here meant repairing, fencing, fortifying. So 1 Kings 12:25 and 15:17.

*{even Beth-lehem}* There was one Bethlehem in Zebulun, Josh. 19:15; another in Judah, where Christ was born, Ruth 1:2; Matt. 2:1, 5, 6, and where David was first anointed king, 1 Sam. 16:1, 13 and 17:12. This latter is here meant.

*{and Etam}* See 1 Chr. 4:32.

*{and Tekoa}* See 1 Chr. 2:24.

**Verse 7. ***{And Beth-zur}* See 1 Chr. 2:45.

*{and Shoco}* This was also a city of Judah. It was one of those which the Philistines took from Ahaz, ch. 28:18.

*{and Adullam}* The chief city of one of those kings which Joshua took, Josh. 12:15. It was in Judah, Josh. 15:35. See on Josh. 10:3.

**Verse 8. ***{And Gath}* See 1 Chr. 18:1.

*{and Mareshah}* See 1 Chr. 4:21.

*{and Ziph}* A city of Judah, Josh. 15:24, 55. See 1 Chr. 2:42.

**Verse 9. ***{And Adoraim}* This city is nowhere else mentioned; but it seems it was within the compass of Judah or Benjamin.

*{and Lachish}* See 2 Kings 14:19.

*{and Azekah}* This was in Judah also, Josh. 15:35. See Jer. 34:7.

**Verse 10. ***{And Zorah}* A city of Dan, Josh. 19:41, made famous by the birth of Samson, Judg. 13:2, etc., and also by his burial, Judg. 16:31.

*{and Aijalon}* See 1 Chr. 6:69.

*{and Hebron}* See 1 Kings 2:11.

**Verse 11. ***{And he fortified the strong holds}* i.e., Castles and other forts.

*{and put captains in them}* Under captains are comprised their soldiers also. He placed strong garrisons in those cities, to keep them safe from attempts of enemies. These captains are supposed to be his sons; for it is said that *he dispersed his children unto every fenced city*, v. 23. Herein he dealt prudently, for in his own sons he might securely confide.

*{and store of victual}* That he might keep together his garrison soldiers, and the better encourage them to stand out against their enemies, if they were beleaguered.

*{and of oil and wine}* These were not only for necessity, but also for delight. He did the rather furnish these places with these refreshments, because his sons and other great men were there. Of the use of oil see 1 Kings 17:12; 2 Kings 4:2. Wine is known to be a generous drink, whereby the heart of man is much refreshed and cheered, Judg. 9:13; Ps. 104:15; Prov. 31:6. Wine and oil among other sorts of provision were prepared for David and such as took part with him, 1 Chr. 12:40, and these did Solomon send as a present to Hiram, ch. 2:10.

**Verse 12. ***{And in every several city}* Heb. *city and city*.

*{he put shields and spears}* Under these two kinds all manner of warlike ammunition is comprised. He prepared these beforehand, that they might not be to seek in time of need.

*{and made them exceeding strong}* i.e., The places which he fortified.

*{having Judah and Benjamin on his side}* For they revolted not with the other tribes from the house of David.

**Verse 13. ***{And the priests and the Levites that were in all Israel}* Here is declared how the kingdom of Judah was strengthened, to wit, by such as out of other tribes came to them.

*{resorted to him}* Heb. *presented themselves to him*. Herein they showed much courage and zeal in God’s cause; like their predecessors, Exod. 32:26.

*{out of all their coasts}* Out of such cities as belonged to the priests and Levites in any of the tribes of Israel.

**Verse 14. ***{For the Levites left their suburbs and their possession}* They left both their houses and hereditaments that belonged to them.

*{and came to Judah and Jerusalem}* To dwell in such places throughout Judah and Benjamin as should be allotted them, and to do the services of the Lord in the temple at Jerusalem.

*{For Jeroboam}* He was the chief and principal.

*{and his sons}* They were governors in sundry places, (as the sons of Rehoboam, v. 23) and were forward to put their father’s design against the priests in execution.

*{had cast them off}* See ch. 14:9.

*{from executing the priest’s office unto the LORD}* The priests’ and Levites’ office was to instruct the people in the law of the Lord, and to go in their accustomed courses unto the temple to do the services thereof. See a main reason of casting them off inferred hence, 1 Kings 12:27. And surely another reason was, to possess their cities and lands. This appears to be so, in that Jeroboam did not settle other priests and Levites in the cities of those that were cast out, but made priests of the meanest of the people, 1 Kings 12:31 and 13:33.

**Verse 15. ***{And he ordained him priests}* Such as are mentioned 1 Kings 13:33.

*{for the high places}* Such as himself built, 1 Kings 12:31, idolatrous high places. Of high places see 1 Kings 3:4.

*{and for the devils}* The word signifies *hairy, horrid things*. As *satyrs*, Isa. 13:21. In such shapes many idols were wont to be set out as affrighted the beholders. And devils for the most part use to appear in horrible shapes. The word שרים *Sharim*, Deut. 32:17, which sets out the same things, signifies *wasters* or *destroyers*; as Rev. 9:11. Idolaters are said to do service to devils, because they do what they do by the instigation of devils, and because such piety and devotion as is not done to the true God is done to devils; there is no mean betwixt them, 1 Cor. 10:20, 21; Rev. 9:20. See on Lev. 17:7.

*{and for the calves which he had made}* See 1 Kings 12:28.

**Verse 16. ***{And after them}* After their example. This has reference to v. 13.

*{out of all the tribes of Israel}* Even such as had revolted from the house of David. See ch. 10:19.

*{such as set their hearts to seek the LORD}* That abhorred Jeroboam’s idolatry, and being instructed in the law, and thereby knowing Jehovah to be the only true God, heartily desired to serve him according to his own direction.

*{God of Israel}* See 1 Kings 8:15.

*{came to Jerusalem}* Where the house of God was.

*{to sacrifice}* This was an especial part of divine service, and might nowhere be done but in the temple.

*{unto the LORD God of their fathers}* i.e., Abraham, Isaac, and Jacob, to whom God made special promises for the good of their posterity. See 1 Chr. 29:18. This God is opposed to Jeroboam’s idols, to whom the Israelites sacrificed.

**Verse 17. ***{So they strengthened the kingdom of Judah}* i.e., The priests, Levites, and others who left their habitations in other tribes, and came to Judah, added their power to the kingdom of Judah, being ready to do what they could for defense thereof; yea, and added their prayers also.

*{and made Rehoboam the son of Solomon strong}* The strength of a king consists in the power of his kingdom.

*{three years}* So long as God is pleased to bless, means will be prevalent, but no longer.

*{for three years they walked in the way of David}* David all his days remained faithful, and maintained the true worship of God.

*{and Solomon}* This must be meant of Solomon’s former times, before his apostasy, and of the time of his repentance and returning unto the Lord afterwards.

**Verse 18. ***{And Rehoboam took him Mahalath}* Rehoboam’s domestic affairs are here set down, and that about his wives and children. Ishmael’s daughter whom Esau married was also called Mahalath, Gen. 28:9.

*{the daughter of Jerimoth the son of David to wife}* None of David’s sons 1 Chr. 3 are called by this name. But it is said 1 Chr. 3:9 that the nineteen sons before mentioned were *all the sons of David, beside the sons of the concubines*: so as this Jerimoth might be a son of David by a concubine.

*{and Abihail}* *and* is not in the original; therefore some read, *Rehoboam, besides Mahalath, took Abihail*. Both readings agree in sense. Abihail was also a man’s name, 1 Chr. 5:14; Esther 2:15.

*{the daughter of Eliab the son of Jesse}* 1 Sam. 16:6; 1 Chr. 2:13.

**Verse 19. ***{Which bare him children}* Which has reference to Abihail, the second wife.

*{Jeush, and Shamariah, and Zaham}* We read nothing else of these three but this, that they were Rehoboam’s sons.

**Verse 20. ***{And after her he took Maachah}* This was his third wife.

*{the daughter of Absalom}* See 1 Kings 15:2.

*{which bare him Abijah}* Or, *Abijam*. This son succeeded his father Rehoboam in the throne, 1 Kings 15:1.

*{and Attai, and Ziza, and Shelomith}* Neither do we read any more of these three.

**Verse 21. ***{And Rehoboam loved Maachah the daughter of Absalom}* It may be her beauty or some other outward matter moved him so to dote on her. Absalom himself was very beautiful; and so was his daughter Tamar, and his sister Tamar also, 2 Sam. 13:1 and 14:25, 27. Certainly it was not piety nor any such endowment that moved him to love her; for she was a detestable idolater, 1 Kings 15:13.

*{above all his wives and his concubines}* That whereupon a man sets his heart uses to be preferred before all other things.

*{for he took eighteen wives and threescore concubines}* When reins are let loose to lust, it grows into excess. See 1 Kings 11:3.

*{and he begat twenty and eight sons and threescore daughters}* He might well have so many children of the multitude of wives and concubines he had.

**Verse 22. ***{And Rehoboam made Abijah the son of Maachah the chief}* The love that he bore to the mother above his other wives made him love her son above his other children.

*{to be ruler among his brethren}* He was not only preferred in dignity above them, but also had authority over them.

*{for he thought to make him king}* That authority before mentioned Rehoboam in his lifetime gave him, that he might be the better fitted to reign after him.

**Verse 23. ***{And he dealt wisely}* The prudence here intended was his disposing of his children in his lifetime.

*{and dispersed of all his children throughout all the countries, etc.}* See on v. 11. He might have two ends in thus dispersing his children: 1. to keep such subjects as yet stood with him from revolt; 2. to keep his children from falling out among themselves, Prov. 18:19. So did Abraham, Gen. 25:6, and Jehoshaphat, ch. 21:3.

*{unto every fenced city}* i.e., Those cities named, v. 6, etc., and others like them.

*{and he gave them victual in abundance}* See v. 11.

*{and he desired}* Heb. *asked for*. i.e., With desire he inquired after; or, sought to take.

*{many wives}* Heb. *a multitude of wives*. It is not expressed whether his desire of many wives were for himself or for his sons. The Hebrew word *asked for* implies that the many wives were for his sons. But it may be without restraint taken, that he sought many wives both for himself and for his sons.

## CHAPTER 12

**Verse 1. ***{And it came to pass}* In this chapter is set down Rehoboam’s revolt from the Lord, and his return unto him.

*{when Rehoboam had established the kingdom}* He was overconfident in his power, and did not duly consider that it was the Lord that settled his kingdom, and gave him that peace that he had.

*{and had strengthened himself}* See ch. 11:17.

*{he forsook the law of the LORD}* Of forsaking the Lord, see 1 Kings 9:9. The Lord by his law declares his will unto us; and they who order not their life, especially their course of piety and divine service, according to the law of God, but follow either their own or other men’s inventions, forsake God’s law. See 1 Kings 9:9 and 18:18.

*{and all Israel with him}* By Israel are meant in this place no other than those who usually are intended under this title Judah; as 2 Chr. 15:15. They are here called Israel, not in opposition to Judah, as ch. 13:15, 16, but in relation to the common father of all the twelve tribes, which was Israel, Gen. 49:2, 28. This instance shows that subjects are prone to follow their sovereign, especially in evil courses.

**Verse 2. ***{And it came to pass that in the fifth year of Rehoboam}* Three years Rehoboam and his subjects walked in the way of David, ch. 11:17; in the fourth year they revolted from the law of the Lord, whereby they provoked his wrath against them. The Lord therefore stirred up the spirit of the king of Egypt against them, and he making preparations in that fourth year, in the fifth comes against Judah.

*{Shishak king of Egypt came up against Jerusalem}* See 1 Kings 14:25.

*{because they had transgressed against the LORD}* Judah’s particular transgressions are set down 1 Kings 14:22, 23, 34.

**Verse 3. ***{With twelve hundred chariots}* Of the use of chariots see 1 Chr. 18:4 and 1 Kings 9:19.

*{and threescore thousand horsemen}* These also are of great use in war, for in regard of the strength and swiftness of their horses they have a great advantage over footmen.

*{and the people were without number that came with him out of Egypt}* This innumerable company it may be were footmen of all sorts, and those out of all countries that came with him, or in particular those mentioned in the words following.

*{the Lubims}* These bordered on Egypt. They are joined with the Cushites, ch. 16:8; Nah. 3:9.

*{the Sukkiims}* So called because they dwelt in tents, as the notation of the word imports. They were also called *Nomades*. Some say they are they who are called *Troglodytes*, and supposed to be wild men living in caves.

*{and the Ethiopians}* Called *Cushites*. They used to gather together in great multitudes. See ch. 14:9; 2 Kings 19:9.

**Verse 4. ***{And he took the fenced cities which pertained to Judah}* Those mentioned ch. 11:5, 8, which stood in his way as he was marching to Jerusalem.

*{and came to Jerusalem}* With that great host v. 3.

**Verse 5. ***{Then came Shemaiah the prophet}* See 1 Kings 12:12.

*{to Rehoboam, and to the princes of Judah}* They all had sinned against the Lord, and therefore both the fault and judgment is declared to them all.

*{that were gathered together to Jerusalem}* Jerusalem was their strongest city, and they took it to be their safest refuge.

*{because of Shishak}* They gathered themselves together to consult and prepare as well as they could against the king of Egypt’s army that he had brought into the kingdom as far as Jerusalem.

*{and said unto them, Thus saith the LORD}* See 1 Kings 12:24.

*{Ye have forsaken me}* See v. 1.

*{and therefore have I also left you in the hand of Shishak}* Had God been with them, Shishak could no more have prevailed against Rehoboam than Zerah did against Asa, ch. 14:9. But the Lord leaving them, they soon fell into Shishak’s power. This shows how dangerous it is to forsake the Lord.

**Verse 6. ***{Whereupon the princes of Israel}* Israel is here taken as v. 1, and by princes of Israel are meant such as were within Rehoboam’s dominions.

*{and the king humbled themselves}* Whether this humiliation were hearty or no is questionable; for at the close of this story it is said of Rehoboam, that *he did evil, because he prepared not his heart to seek the Lord*, v. 14. Yet God might have respect to their outward humiliation, as he had to Ahab’s. See on 1 Kings 21:29.

*{the LORD is righteous}* Not only the upright and such as fear God may by judgments be brought to acknowledge God’s just and righteous proceeding with them, as Dan. 9:14, but also wicked ones, as Pharaoh Exod. 9:27. So as this acknowledgment is no infallible evidence of an entire respect to God, seeing judgments may outwardly work upon hypocrites and obstinate sinners.

**Verse 7. ***{And when the LORD saw that they humbled themselves}* The Lord takes notice both of men’s inward dispositions and also of their outward carriage.

*{the word of the LORD came to Shemaiah}* God uses his ministers to carry joyful tidings as well as doleful.

*{saying, They have humbled themselves, therefore I will not destroy them}* Humiliation for sin and acknowledgment of God’s just dealing is very prevalent with God to remove a judgment, ch. 33:12, or to prevent a judgment, Jonah 3:10, or to delay a judgment, ch. 32:26. Yea, external humiliation is of force to remove an outward judgment, Exod. 9:27, etc.; 1 Kings 21:29.

*{but I will grant them some deliverance}* Or, *for a little while deliverance*. The word translated *a little while* is the same that is used Ps. 2:12. This limitation of deliverance has relation to v. 8, where is implied that the land of Judah and the inhabitants thereof were in a kind of subjection to the king of Egypt.

*{and my wrath shall not be poured out upon Jerusalem by the hand of Shishak}* God would not suffer this enemy utterly to destroy Jerusalem, as he threatened Deut. 28:62, and executed ch. 36:17, etc. The metaphor of pouring out wrath is taken for the executing of full vengeance, no part thereof being kept in. The utter destruction of the Jews by the Babylonians is set forth under this phrase of pouring out God’s wrath, ch. 34:21, 25. So we read of pouring out of God’s fury, Ezek. 7:8, *his indignation*, Ezek. 21:31; *the fury of his anger*, Isa. 42:25, and *his fierce anger*, Lam. 4:11, and of pouring out a curse, Dan. 9:11. This is further set out by another metaphor, of a vial full of wrath. Such a vial is said to be poured out when there is nothing left in it. Commonly the dregs of liquor lie in the bottoms of vessels: therefore when they will show that all is poured out they use to say, the dregs are poured out. Thus mention is made of *the dregs of the cup of God’s fury*, Isa. 51:22. Here is implied that God would restrain his wrath, and not deal according to the uttermost extremity.

**Verse 8. ***{Nevertheless they shall be his servants}* i.e., The men of Judah shall be forced to yield fealty and tribute unto Shishak, and accept what conditions he shall put upon them.

*{that they may know my service, and the service of the kingdoms of the countries}* By that hard tax which the king of Egypt would lay upon them they might easily discern what difference there was betwixt serving God and serving enemies, Deut. 28:47, 48; Isa. 26:13. God blesses with all manner of blessing such as faithfully serve him, but enemies spoil and oppress whom they get under their subjection. By the kingdoms of the countries he means the heathenish nations, which had neither knowledge nor fear of God.

**Verse 9. ***{So Shishak king of Egypt came up against Jerusalem}* See v. 2.

*{and took away the treasures of the house of the LORD}* We are not to think that he had those vessels of gold and silver that were used in the house of the Lord, but rather such gold and silver and other precious things as were laid up for repairing the temple, or other sacred uses. Neither are we to think that Shishak came into God’s house to take these, but that laying a great sum of money upon the king and people of Judah, they were forced to take the treasures of the house of the Lord to make it up; which Shishak took and carried away. The like is noted of Hezekiah, 2 Kings 18:14, 15. Of the treasures of the house of God see 1 Chr. 26:28.

*{and the treasures of the king’s house}* That which is said of the treasures of the house of the Lord in the former note may be applied to the treasures of the king’s house also. Kings of Judah had their peculiar treasures; and these used to be rich ones, 2 Kings 16:8 and 18:15 and 20:13, 15. In this respect David says that *of his own proper goods* he gave towards the house of God. Those goods were laid up in his own treasury, and amounted even to many millions, 1 Chr. 29:4.

*{he took all}* The king of Egypt took of the king of Judah all that he gave him: and to satisfy him, the king of Judah gave him most if not all the treasures that were in his own and in the Lord’s house.

*{he carried away also the shields of gold which Solomon had made}* 1 Kings 10:16, 17. See 1 Kings 14:26.

**Verse 10. ***{Instead of which king Rehoboam made shields of brass, etc.}* Or, *and king Rehoboam made in their stead brasen shields*, 1 Kings 14:27.

**Verse 11. ***{And when the king, etc.}* See 1 Kings 14:28.

**Verse 12. ***{And when he humbled himself}* See v. 7.

*{the wrath of the LORD turned from him}* The Lord’s wrath was the cause of Shishak’s coming against him; and his departure was an evidence that the wrath of the Lord was turned from him.

*{that he would not destroy him altogether}* Heb. *to consumption*. Though the king of Egypt made great spoil in sundry cities of Judah, and had the treasures of the Lord’s house and the king’s house, yet Judah remained still to be a kingdom, and the king and people thereof abode still therein; which was not so in Zedekiah’s time, 2 Kings 25:21.

*{and also in Judah things went well}* Or, *and yet in Judah there were good things*. According to the first reading this may be the sense, Notwithstanding all the evil that Shishak had done, the state of Judah continued as before: they enjoyed their liberty of serving God, the benefit of their own laws, with peace and prosperity. According to the other reading this may be the sense, God’s ordinances were duly observed, judgment and justice was executed, and there were many of the princes and people in whom *good things* were found, as is said of Jeroboam’s son, 1 Kings 14:13. Besides, there was in Judah the law of God, the seals of his covenant, the ministry of his prophets, priests and Levites, ordinary and extraordinary sacrifices, and many persons that had not conformed to the sins of the kingdom: these and many other like were the *good things* in Judah.

**Verse 13. ***{So king Rehoboam strengthened himself in Jerusalem}* Though he were much weakened by the spoil which Shishak had made in his kingdom, yet by the Lord’s freeing of him from his enemy he recovered strength again, and repaired and fortified the cities of his kingdom, as ch. 11:5, 11, and thus he strengthened himself.

*{and reigned}* As king. He continued to be sovereign in his kingdom, notwithstanding his service to the king of Egypt, mentioned v. 8.

*{for Rehoboam was one and forty years old when he began to reign}* See 1 Kings 14:21.

**Verse 14. ***{And he did evil}* This may have reference both to the fourth and fifth years of his reign, after he was freed from Shishak. That which is here noted of the king of Judah is indefinitely applied to Judah, namely, to the princes and people thereof, 1 Kings 14:22.

*{because he prepared not}* Or, *fixed not*. Preparation is an especial means to bring a man on to that which is good, but want of it makes one run into evil: and a thorough settled resolution to do that which is good is a great preparation thereunto: but when such a resolution is wanting, evil will quickly creep on a man.

*{his heart}* The heart is the spring out of which flows both good and evil.

*{to seek the LORD}* See 1 Chr. 16:10 and 28:9.

**Verse 15. ***{Now the acts of Rehoboam first and last}* Such as were done in his first three good years, ch. 11:17, in his two next evil years, v. 1, 2, and in his other years following them. See ch. 9:29.

*{are they not written in the book}* Heb. *words*. See ch. 9:29.

*{of Shemaiah the prophet}* See 1 Kings 12:22.

*{and of Iddo the seer}* See ch. 9:29.

*{concerning genealogies}* Or, *pedigrees*, or, *histories of kings and others who were famous in the church of God*.

*{And there were wars between Jeroboam and Rehoboam continually}* See 1 Kings 14:30.

**Verse 16. ***{And Rehoboam, etc.}* See 1 Kings 2:10.

*{and Abijah}* Or, *Abijam*. See 1 Kings 14:31.

*{reigned in his stead}* Thus Rehoboam’s purpose and endeavor was accomplished, ch. 11:22.

## CHAPTER 13

**Verse 1. ***{Now in the eighteenth year of king Jeroboam}* See 1 Kings 15:1.

*{began Abijah to reign over Judah}* In this chapter Abijah’s reign and his great success against Jeroboam is set down.

**Verse 2. ***{He reigned three years in Jerusalem}* See 1 Kings 15:2.

*{his mother’s name also was Michaiah the daughter of Uriel of Gibeah}* Or, *his mother’s name was Maachah the daughter of Abishalom*. See on 1 Kings 15:2.

*{and there was war between Abijah and Jeroboam}* Thus much is noted of his father Rehoboam and Jeroboam. See 1 Kings 14:30.

**Verse 3. ***{And Abijah set}* Heb. *bound together*. For by good order in an army particular soldiers and several companies are as it were bound together.

*{the battle in array}* Prepared his soldiers, and set them in good order, that they might be ready to fight with the enemy.

*{with an army of valiant men of war}* Not only the commanders but all the soldiers are comprised under the word *army*. This epithet *valiant* sets out the courage of their minds and strength of their bodies. This phrase *men of war* shows that they were exercised in military discipline.

*{even four hundred thousand}* This was a very great army, considered in itself. We count fifty thousand a royal army, but here were eight times so many. Yet this great army was but half so big as Jeroboam’s.

*{chosen men}* Not vagrants, or pressed in the street, but chosen for that purpose.

*{Jeroboam also set the battle in array against him with eight hundred thousand chosen men}* Jeroboam had two to one.

*{being mighty men of valour}* See 1 Chr. 5:24. These two armies, being all of the children of Israel, amounted to twelve hundred thousand, all valiant men. Had they been, as brethren, united, they had been the greatest host that we have read or heard of by express number to be assembled together in the field. See ch. 14:9. Indeed there is in David’s time mention made of fifteen hundred thousand, and threescore and three thousand that drew sword, besides Levi and Benjamin, which were not counted among them, 1 Chr. 21:5, 6, but these were not all drawn out into the field together. See ch. 17:14.

**Verse 4. ***{And Abijah stood up}* It is to be supposed, that while the armies were set one against the other, before the fight, Abijah desired and obtained a parley or treaty, and thereupon chose a fit place where he might best be heard. It was ever usual with prudent commanders before the battle to desire a treaty, thereby to show the justness of their cause, and persuade the enemy to desist, if it might be. This was enjoined by the law, Deut. 20:10. So did Jephthah, Judg. 11:12, and so did Abijah here.

*{upon mount Zemaraim, which is in mount Ephraim}* A city of this name was in Benjamin, Josh. 18:22. The city and mount might both border on Mount Ephraim, by the outcoasts of Benjamin. Some conceive the name to be taken from Zemari, Gen. 10:18.

*{and said, Hear me thou Jeroboam, and all Israel}* He directs his speech, as to Jeroboam, who had the chief command of the army, so to other commanders under him, who questionless were now upon the foresaid desire of treaty assembled together to hear what Abijah would say. And these especially are comprised under that phrase *all Israel*. Yet withal it was Abijah’s desire that as many of the ordinary officers and soldiers as could might hear his voice, as Rabshakeh, 2 Kings 18:28.

**Verse 5. ***{Ought you not to know}* So clear was the case he propounded as he supposed they could not be ignorant thereof; at least they might and ought to have known it.

*{that the LORD God of Israel}* See 1 Kings 8:15. He uses this phrase to show that the Lord was not only the God of Judah, but of all the twelve tribes; and to move them the rather to observe that which their God had covenanted.

*{gave the kingdom over Israel to David for ever}* Under this word *David*, David himself and his posterity is comprised. See 2 Sam. 7:12, 13, 16.

*{even to him and to his sons by a covenant of salt}* By this metaphor of salt a perpetuity is set forth. For salt makes things last. In this respect Lot’s wife was turned into *a pillar of salt*, Gen. 19:26, that she might remain a perpetual spectacle. The covenant therefore here is declared to be a perpetual covenant, not to be abrogated or nulled. In this respect these two phrases *a covenant of salt* and *for ever* are joined together, Num. 18:19. Some take this metaphor to be used in relation to their manner of making their covenant, with a sacrifice, on which salt was always sprinkled, Lev. 2:13. And thereby is implied that this was a most solemn covenant, not to be violated. Both senses tend to the same purpose.

**Verse 6. ***{Yet Jeroboam the son of Nebat}* See 1 Kings 11:26.

*{the servant of Solomon}* This title is here used to aggravate Jeroboam’s rebellion, in that being a servant he would presume to usurp his master’s throne.

*{the son of David}* This shows the right that Solomon and his son had to the kingdom by reason of the forementioned covenant v. 5.

*{is risen up}* This also implies an usurpation above that which was meet.

*{and hath rebelled}* Notwithstanding God foretold that Jeroboam should be king, 1 Kings 11:35, and the people chose him, 1 Kings 12:20. Yet he came to the kingdom with a proud, aspiring, rebellious disposition. It is therefore said that *he lift up his hand against the king*, 1 Kings 11:26.

*{against his lord}* He rebelled both against Solomon, whose peculiar servant he was, 1 Kings 11:26, and also against Rehoboam, whose subject he was, 1 Kings 12:16, etc.

**Verse 7. ***{And there are gathered unto him vain men}* i.e., inconstant, rash and heady, not well understanding what they did.

*{the children of Belial}* See 1 Kings 21:10. Certainly they were such men as put on Jeroboam to do what he did; and in that particular act of setting him up to be king, they showed themselves to be such men. But they among the ten tribes which set their hearts to seek the Lord would not consent thereto, ch. 11:16.

*{and have strengthened themselves}* By mutual consent and joint conspiracy, and also by building cities and castles of defense, 1 Kings 12:20, 25.

*{against Rehoboam the son of Solomon}* This is said to show the right that Rehoboam had to the kingdom, and to aggravate their rebellion. In this respect Solomon is styled *the son of David*, v. 6.

*{when Rehoboam was young}* Not in age, (for he was *one and forty years old* when he came to the crown, ch. 12:13) but in understanding and discretion, 1 Cor. 12:20. He was as a young man that wanted experience, and in that respect foolish. Thus is this word taken, Eccles. 10:16; Isa. 3:4. See Eccles. 4:13. Or he may be called young in regard of his breeding: he was trained up in a delicate manner under a prince of peace, and had no experience of war, nor was exercised in military discipline: and in this respect he was as a young tree that might easily be plucked up. He was no old beaten soldier, but a young and fresh one.

*{and tenderhearted}* i.e., soon daunted. This is opposed to courage and magnanimity. He wanted stoutness of spirit.

*{and could not withstand them}* This is added as the effect of his folly and fearfulness. Besides it implies that the conspiracy made against him was very great.

**Verse 8. ***{And now ye think}* Heb. *speak*, or, *say*. They did speak in themselves, and this is to think. See 1 Kings 5:5 and Gen. 20:11.

*{to withstand the kingdom}* Heb. *to strengthen yourselves in the face of the kingdom*. i.e., with a strong and mighty power to oppose it.

*{of the LORD}* Why the kingdom of Judah was called *the kingdom of the Lord*, see ch. 9:8; 1 Chr. 28:5. This epithet is here added to aggravate Jeroboam’s folly in opposing against that which was the Lord’s. See Acts 5:39.

*{in the hand of the sons of David}* For God had given the kingdom unto them. See 1 Chr. 17:14 and 28:4, etc.

*{and ye be a great multitude}* They were ten tribes to two, and their army as big again as Abijah’s, v. 3. This he alleges as the ground of their confidence, but a vain ground. See Isa. 30:1, 2, 3 and 31:1, 2, 3.

*{and there are with you golden calves}* This also is mentioned as another ground of their confidence, but to aggravate their folly therein. For what help could calves, without life, made of metals, afford them? Some conceive they brought the golden calves with them into the camp, because it is said, *there are with you*. So did the Israelites in superstition carry the ark into the camp, 1 Sam. 4:5, and the Philistines their gods, 1 Chr. 14:12.

*{which Jeroboam made you for gods}* 1 Kings 12:28. This is a greater aggravation of their folly, in that they placed confidence in such things as a mortal man had made to be gods.

**Verse 9. ***{Have you not cast out the priests of the LORD}* Ch. 11:13, 14. They did not only trust to false gods, but also rejected the true God, in his ministers and priests, by casting them out.

*{the sons of Aaron}* This has reference to God’s choosing them only to be his priests, Exod. 29:9; Num. 3:10, and is here alleged in opposition to Jeroboam’s practice, who chose any sorts of persons to be priests, 1 Kings 12:31 and 13:33.

*{and the Levites}* These also were ministers of the Lord, though under the priests, Num. 3:9, 12. And by adding these he discovers the extent of Jeroboam’s impiety. It extended against all the ministers of God.

*{and have made you priests after the manner of the nations of other lands}* Not after God’s prescript, according to which none but Aaron’s seed should be priests; but as other nations that knew not God, nor had the light of his word for their guide. These had no stock or family or succession of men, from fathers to sons, to be priests; neither had the Israelites any, but chose whom they pleased.

*{so that whosoever cometh to consecrate himself}* Heb. *to fill his hand*. See 1 Kings 13:33.

*{with a young bullock}* This was enjoined under the law for the manner of consecrating a true priest, Exod. 29:1. Hypocrites can observe some outward rites prescribed by God.

*{and seven rams}* The law required but two rams at the most, Exod. 29:1, 19. But idolaters and hypocrites in external rites oft exceed God’s prescript, Num. 23:1; Mic. 6:7; Mark 7:4.

**Verse 10. ***{But as for us, the LORD is our God}* Heb. *and we, the Lord our God*. The sense of the Hebraism is well expressed in our translation. This is to be taken of their outward profession of the true God. For Abijah’s heart was not upright, 1 Kings 15:3.

*{and we have not forsaken him}* As the ten tribes did, in forbearing to go to the temple, and setting up the calves for their gods, 1 Kings 12:28, 29, 30.

*{and the priests which minister unto the LORD are the sons of Aaron}* This was according to God’s word, and contrary to the Israelites’ practice, v. 9.

*{and the Levites wait upon their business}* They observe those courses and do those services which in special belong to them.

**Verse 11. ***{And they burn unto the LORD}* To the honor of God, to do that service to him which he has appointed, to make atonement with him, and to seek his favor.

*{every morning and every evening}* Heb. *morning and morning, evening and evening*. This has relation to Exod. 29:41, 42.

*{burnt sacrifices}* One in the morning, and another in the evening, Exod. 29:39. These were constantly offered up. There were also other sacrifices upon occasion offered up mornings and evenings, Num. 28:10, 15, 23, 24.

*{and sweet incense}* See ch. 2:4; 1 Kings 9:25.

*{the shewbread also set they in order upon the pure table}* Of this also see ch. 2:4. The table was covered with gold, and consecrated for that purpose, and in that respect called *pure*.

*{and the candlestick of gold}* This and the other phrase may have relation to the tabernacle, wherein was but one table, and one candlestick, Exod. 25:23, 31. Or by a synecdoche the singular number may be used for the plural. For there were ten tables and ten candlesticks in the temple, ch. 4:7, 8.

*{with the lamps thereof, to burn every evening}* See ch. 4:20; Lev. 24:3. Evening is here synecdochically put for morning and evening.

*{for we keep the charge of the LORD our God}* We observe those ordinances which the Lord has prescribed and given us in charge.

*{but ye have forsaken him}* See ch. 12:5.

**Verse 12. ***{And behold, God himself is with us}* See 1 Kings 8:57. He produces this as the ground of their confidence: and it is a sure ground, Rom. 8:31.

*{for our captain}* Herein he may have relation to the Lord’s appearing unto Joshua, Josh. 5:13, 14. And he implies that the Lord would go before them, and fight for them; and in that respect they might have assurance of victory.

*{and his priests with sounding trumpets to cry alarm against you}* This has express relation to an ordinance which God made for his people when they went out to battle, and to his promise thereupon, Num. 10:9 and 13:6.

*{O children of Israel}* This title Abijah gives to his enemies by way of insinuation, to put them in mind of the mutual brotherhood betwixt the two parties: they were all children of the same father. Such an argument used Abraham, Gen. 13:8.

*{fight ye not against the LORD}* A strong motive to stir them up to desist: for who ever prevailed against the Lord? See 2 Kings 19:22.

*{God of your fathers}* See ch. 11:16.

*{for you shall not prosper}* Hereby he shows that they who are against the Lord cannot look for any good success, Job 9:4. This oration which Abijah made is a very pithy one, every sentence comprising a strong argument to dissuade Jeroboam and such as took part with him from fighting against the men of Judah. All the arguments may be drawn to two heads; 1. the dishonor that Jeroboam’s party had done to God; 2. the honor which the men of Judah had done to him.

**Verse 13. ***{But Jeroboam caused an ambushment}* While Abijah was dealing fairly and friendly with Jeroboam, Jeroboam deals treacherously with him, and takes advantage from his treating to surprise him at unawares. The Hebrew word translated *ambushment* is put for *a lurking place*, Ps. 10:8. Our English word *ambushment* implies *a lying in wait*, or *a sudden and unexpected coming upon men*. See Josh. 8:2, etc.; Judg. 20:33, 34.

*{to come about behind them}* This implies a secret conveying of the stratagem.

*{so they were before Judah}* The main battalia of the Israelites stood face to face before the men of Judah.

*{and the ambushment was behind them}* Such as lay in wait to fall upon the rear while the other were in fight.

**Verse 14. ***{And when Judah looked back, behold, the battle was before and behind them}* They were compassed about by the multitude of their enemies, and that unawares; for they saw not them that were behind them till they looked back.

*{and they cried unto the LORD}* Some understand this of an outward clamor and great shout which the men of Judah gave as it were with one voice to affright the enemy. We read of the like done 1 Sam. 4:5. But this is afterwards noted to be done by them, v. 15. Others understand a strong fervency in prayer to the Lord for succor in that dangerous case. Thus is this phrase used ch. 14:11; Exod. 14:15; 1 Kings 22:32.

*{and the priests sounded with the trumpets}* To strengthen their faith in the Lord’s promise, Num. 10:9, and to encourage the soldiers.

**Verse 15. ***{Then the men of Judah gave shout}* The whole army together hollowed out aloud, and made a great acclamation.

*{and as the men of Judah shouted}* Questionless their hearts were lift up to God together with their loud voice.

*{it came to pass that God smote Jeroboam}* This word *smote* is oft put for *slew*, 1 Kings 15:29; 2 Kings 3:24 and ch. 9:24. But as it has relation to Jeroboam, it implies only an affrighting of him, so as to make him flee.

*{and all Israel}* i.e., the army of the Israelites gathered together in the field. They were affrighted as well as their king, and overthrown.

*{before Abijah and Judah}* Abijah and his army, here called Judah, might pursue the Israelites, but it was God that affrighted them and made them flee.

**Verse 16. ***{And the children of Israel fled before Judah, etc.}* This is an exposition of what went before.

**Verse 17. ***{So there fell down slain of Israel five hundred thousand}* So as the men of Judah slew more than every one his man. See v. 3.

*{chosen men}* See v. 3. As the number, so the quality of those that were slain much amplifies the victory.

**Verse 18. ***{Thus the children of Israel were brought under at that time}* He says *at that time*, because they gathered strength afterwards, so as they maintained war against Asa, 1 Kings 15:16.

*{and the children of Judah prevailed, because they relied upon the LORD}* God fails not those who trust in him.

*{God of their fathers}* See ch. 11:16.

**Verse 19. ***{And Abijah pursued after Jeroboam, and took cities from him}* This was a point of wisdom, to follow the advantage he had gotten, and having slain many of them that stood against him, and put others to flight, to surprise their cities. See Josh. 10:19.

*{Bethel}* There was one of the golden calves set up, 1 Kings 12:29.

*{with the towns thereof}* Bethel was a fair city, and such used to have towns belonging to them. See 1 Chr. 2:23 and 7:28.

*{and Jeshanah, etc.}* This city was not far from Bethel.

*{and Ephrain, etc.}* Called *Ophrah*, Josh. 18:13. It was in Benjamin. Some take it to be that place whereof mention is made John 11:54.

**Verse 20. ***{Neither did Jeroboam recover strength, etc.}* See on v. 18.

*{and the LORD struck him, and he died}* i.e., Jeroboam. Though he lived a year or two after Abijah, 1 Kings 15:9, yet his death is here set down, to conclude his story. This phrase, *the Lord struck him*, implies that he died by a special hand of God, not by an ordinary death, as 1 Sam. 25:38; Ezek. 24:16.

**Verse 21. ***{But Abijah waxed mighty}* The rather by reason of that great conquest he got over Jeroboam.

*{and married fourteen wives}* This is not to be restrained to the time after he got that victory, nor to the time of his reign (for he reigned but three years, v. 2) but to be extended to the whole time of his life wherein he was marriageable. For he married some of these wives, if not all of them, in his father’s days.

*{and begat twenty and two sons, and sixteen daughters}* See ch. 11:21.

**Verse 22. ***{And the rest of the acts of Abijah}* See 1 Kings 11:41.

*{and his ways}* Or *course of life*.

*{and his sayings}* This may have reference to his oration v. 5, etc. Or it may be he uttered many sentences that were thought worthy the registering, as his grandfather’s proverbs were, 1 Kings 4:31, 33 and 11:41.

*{are written in the story}* Or *commentary*, or *chronicle*.

*{of the prophet Iddo}* See ch. 12:15.

## CHAPTER 14

**Verse 1. ***{So Abijah slept with his fathers, etc.}* See 1 Kings 2:10.

*{And Asa his son reigned in his stead}* 1 Kings 15:8, etc. Asa was the third king of Judah after the division. His history is recorded in this and the two next chapters. In this two things are noted; 1. the prosperous continuing of his reign ten years, to v. 9. 2. a memorable victory against a mighty enemy, v. 9, etc.

*{in his days the land was quiet}* Free from outward invasions, and inward insurrections.

*{ten years}* From hence it may be inferred that after ten years there were open wars between him and his enemies. It is not expressed what enemies troubled the land before the Cushites, v. 9. Yet this limitation of peace to ten years implies there was some other enemy that disquieted them: for it may be gathered from that which is noted of their offering unto the Lord of the spoil they had taken from their enemies in the fifteenth year of Asa, ch. 15:10, 11, that the Cushites were overthrown in that year. There might before that be war betwixt Judah and Israel, or betwixt Judah and other nations. Whereas it is said 1 Kings 15:16, *there was war betwixt Asa and Baasha all their days*, it is to be taken of the skirmishes on the borders of the kingdoms, as 1 Kings 14:30.

**Verse 2. ***{And Asa did that which was good, etc.}* See 1 Kings 15:11.

**Verse 3. ***{For he took away the altars of the strange gods}* Such haply as remained in the cities which his father took from Jeroboam, ch. 13:19. This was according to the law, Deut. 7:5.

*{and the high places}* i.e., idolatrous high places. For others were not put down. See 1 Kings 15:14. Of high places see 1 Kings 3:4.

*{and brake down the images}* Heb. *statues*. See 1 Kings 14:23.

*{and cut down the groves}* See 1 Kings 16:33.

**Verse 4. ***{And commanded Judah}* Heb. *said to Judah*. The matter being about religion, he lays a command upon his subjects, 2 Kings 10:26.

*{to seek the LORD}* See 1 Chr. 16:10.

*{God of their fathers}* See ch. 11:16; 1 Chr. 29:18, 20.

*{and to do the law and the commandment}* See 1 Kings 2:2.

**Verse 5. ***{Also he took away out of all the cities of Judah}* This implies that the reformation v. 3 was of cities of Israel.

*{the high places}* See v. 3.

*{and the images}* Heb. *sun-images*. Some conceive these were made in the likeness of an orb or round compass with rays about it, to represent the sun, which some accounted to be a god. Others say they were made in the shape of a man with rays about his head, to represent the sun, as before. From the word חמן *Chaman* it is inferred that it was an image of Jupiter Hammon. It is used in the plural number; as ch. 34:4, 7; Lev. 26; Isa. 17:8 and 27:9; Ezek. 6:4, 6. By this it appears how the people of God followed the fashions of the heathen.

*{and the kingdom was quiet}* This was the fruit of establishing true religion. It may be some suggested unto Asa that the destroying of those things which his predecessors had set up would cause tumults and wars: but contrary to that conceit it is here said, *the kingdom was quiet*.

*{before him}* He living and seeing it.

**Verse 6. ***{And he built}* i.e., repaired. See ch. 11:6.

*{fenced cities}* Heb. *cities of fence*. Such as are set down ch. 11:6, etc.

*{in Judah}* And Benjamin.

*{for the land had rest}* See v. 1. He made the right use of his peace, preparing against future danger.

*{and he had no war in those years}* This shows in what respect the land is said to have rest.

*{because the LORD had given him rest}* The peace which kingdoms enjoy is from the Lord, Ps. 46:9.

**Verse 7. ***{Therefore he said unto Judah}* He stirs up his subjects to do what he thought meet to be done.

*{Let us build these cities}* These have reference to cities then standing; so as building must be taken for repairing and fortifying.

*{and make about them walls}* Being unwalled before, and so open to enemies, for their safety he compasses them about with walls.

*{and towers}* Towers used to be in the walls of cities, to set watchmen thereon to descry dangers. See 2 Kings 19:17. They used also therein to put warlike ammunition to keep off and annoy enemies.

*{gates and bars}* Gates were for passage, and to shut upon all occasions of fear. Bars kept the gates being shut so fast as they could not easily be broken open.

*{while the land is yet before us}* While it is quiet under our government, not surprised or disturbed by enemies, but possessed by ourselves in peace.

*{because we have sought the LORD our God}* Prudently and piously he ascribes to God the peace they had.

*{we have sought him, and he hath given us rest}* The assurance of what he said before, and his zealous desire of giving all glory to God, makes him repeat the same again.

*{on every side}* In every part of the land, from all sorts of enemies.

*{so they built and prospered}* With good success they accomplished what they undertook. See 1 Chr. 22:11.

**Verse 8. ***{And Asa had an army of men}* Ready prepared on all occasions to be used.

*{that bare targets and spears, etc.}* These two particulars are synecdochically put for all kind of armor and weapons, offensive and defensive, as ch. 11:12.

*{that bare shields}* Of the difference between targets and shields see 1 Kings 10:16, 17.

*{and drew bows, etc.}* Heb. *which trod the bow*. See 1 Chr. 8:40.

*{two hundred and fourscore thousand}* There were as many out of Benjamin as out of Judah within twenty thousand.

*{all these were mighty men of valour}* See ch. 13:3.

**Verse 9. ***{And there came out against them}* The cause of this invasion is not set down. Some suppose it was Asa’s denying to yield that homage and tribute to the king of Egypt which his grandfather covenanted. See ch. 12:8.

*{Zerah the Ethiopian}* Or, *Cushite*. This Zerah was king of the Cushites, who bordered upon Egypt. And either he was at that time also king of the Egyptians, or so favored them as to undertake their quarrel.

*{with an host of a thousand thousand}* This is the greatest army we read of in Scripture. See ch. 13:3. They had questionless auxiliaries out of other nations, as the Lubims, ch. 16:8, and it may be also the Philistines. See v. 14.

*{and three hundred chariots}* Some here supply *a thousand*, *q.d.*, *three hundred thousand chariots*, because they are said to have had *very many chariots and horsemen*, ch. 16:8.

*{and came to Mareshah}* A city in Judah, Josh. 15:44. See ch. 11:8; 1 Chr. 4:21. The enemy came to the very land of Judah.

**Verse 10. ***{And Asa went out against him}* Prudently did Asa go out to the confines of his own country, to prevent the plundering and spoiling of his subjects.

*{and they set the battle in array}* i.e., the men of Judah. See ch. 13:3.

*{in the valley of Zephathah}* See Judg. 1:17. It is supposed this valley was sufficient for Asa’s army, but so set with banks and hills on each side as the enemy with his exceeding great number could not compass him in. Great advantage may be gotten by a fit place to pitch an army in.

*{at Mareshah}* See v. 9.

**Verse 11. ***{And Asa cried unto the LORD}* See ch. 13:14.

*{his God}* This relation was the ground of his fervency.

*{and said, LORD}* He directs his prayer as it were face to face to God, which implies an holy familiarity with him.

*{it is nothing with thee to help, whether with many, or with them that have no power}* Heb. *it is not with thee to help betwixt the mighty and him that hath no power*. i.e., There is no difference with God, but to him it is all one to help those that are many in multitude and every way well furnished, and to help such as are but few and weak. God is able to strengthen the weak, and to give victory to a small army, as well as to a great one. See 1 Sam. 14:6.

*{help us}* Upon the forementioned ground he craves God’s succor.

*{O LORD our God}* He mentions this relation to strengthen his faith.

*{for we rest on thee}* God uses not to fail those that place their whole trust in him: this therefore Asa here pleads. See ch. 16:8.

*{and in thy name}* Some hence gather that God being consulted did encourage Asa and the men of Judah to go against that great army, as in a like case he encouraged David, 1 Chr. 14:10. This phrase *in thy name* further implies that they went forth for God’s glory, calling upon him, and trusting in his aid, as 1 Sam. 17:45. See 2 Kings 2:24.

*{we go against this multitude}* Faith in God encourages against the greatest armies.

*{O LORD thou art our God}* His faith rises from degree to degree. First he used this title *Lord*; then, with addition of a particular relation, *O Lord our God*; lastly, with a confident asseveration of the same, *O Lord thou art our God*. See 1 Chr. 17:27.

*{let not man prevail against thee}* The word translated *man* signifies *a mortal, weak, miserable man*. Thus he speaks to strengthen his own faith and the faith of his army against the enemy, and withal to imply that it would be a dishonor to God for such an one to prevail.

**Verse 12. ***{So the LORD smote the Ethiopians before Asa and before Judah}* As he smote Jeroboam and all Israel, ch. 13:15.

*{and the Ethiopians fled}* They who are affrighted by God cannot stand. See ch. 13:16.

**Verse 13. ***{And Asa and the people that were with him}* i.e., his army.

*{pursued them}* See ch. 13:19.

*{unto Gerar}* A city of the Philistines, Gen. 20:1.

*{and the Ethiopians were overthrown, that they could not recover themselves}* Heb. *there was no reviving to them*. So many were slain, and the rest so routed, as they could not rally again nor make a head to stand against their enemies.

*{for they were destroyed}* Heb. *broken*. They came in a great body with all their forces united, but they were as it were pulled asunder limb from limb.

*{before the LORD}* God was with the men of Judah for their captain, as ch. 13:12, and in that respect the Ethiopians were destroyed before him.

*{and before his host}* Being God’s people, and going out in God’s name, v. 11, they are counted God’s host.

*{and they carried away very much spoil}* i.e., Asa and his people. Their enemies brought much treasure with them, as Jehoshaphat’s did, 2 Chr. 20:25, but they were forced to leave it behind them, so as the men of Judah took it away.

**Verse 14. ***{And they smote all the cities round about Gerar}* It is very probable that the men of Gerar and the Philistines round about aided the Ethiopians; and so Judah might well account them their enemies, and deal with their cities accordingly. By smiting the cities is meant a destruction of their inhabitants.

*{for the fear of the LORD came upon them}* i.e., a great terror which the Lord cast into their minds, wherewith they were exceedingly affrighted, so as they durst no longer stand against the men of Judah. So ch. 17:10 and 20:29; Gen. 35:5.

*{and they spoiled all the cities}* Took what was worth carrying away.

*{for there was exceeding much spoil in them}* It is probable that the Ethiopians laid up their treasures in Gerar and the cities thereabouts: and so the goods that the Philistines themselves had in their cities, and that which the Ethiopians brought thither, must needs afford very much spoil to the conqueror.

**Verse 15. ***{They smote also the tents of cattle}* The Arabians bordered on the Philistines, and so might be moved to join with them in aiding the Ethiopians. They dwelt in tents, which they might remove from place to place, and find out good pasture for their cattle whereof they had great store: for they lived upon breeding and fattening cattle. See 1 Chr. 4:41. These affording aid to Judah’s enemies, Judah had just cause to spoil their tents, and take away their cattle.

*{and carried away sheep and camels in abundance}* Under these two kinds all other cattle that use to be kept on pasture are comprised, as oxen, kine, calves, goats, kids, etc.

*{and returned to Jerusalem}* There to divide the spoil, and to render praise to God. See ch. 20:27.

## CHAPTER 15

**Verse 1. ***{And the Spirit of God came upon Azariah}* This phrase is used of such as have some extraordinary inspiration, revelation, gift or grace conferred upon them, and that for some special employment: and it is oft spoken of wicked men and hypocrites as well as of pious men. See Num. 24:2; 1 Sam. 10:10; Judg. 14:6, 19. See ch. 20:14; ch. 24:20.

*{the son of Oded}* See v. 8 and ch. 9:29.

**Verse 2. ***{And he went out to meet Asa}* Heb. *before Asa*. As Asa was coming to Jerusalem, ch. 14:15, with a great assembly of his subjects, this prophet came before his face, and thereupon may well be said to meet him.

*{and said unto him}* God having given a great evidence of his fatherly care over Judah, not only by long peace, ch. 14:1, but also by a great victory, ch. 4:12, a prophet by divine inspiration stirs them up to make a right use of those mercies by cleaving to the Lord, and renewing their covenant with him.

*{Hear ye me}* By this preface he stirs up their attention to what he should say.

*{Asa, and all Judah and Benjamin}* This phrase implies that there was a very great assembly, partly of those who returned from the victory, and partly of those who went out to congratulate them. The prophet speaks to them all.

*{the LORD is with you}* Present among you, to take care of you, to provide for you, to protect you, and every way to do you good. See v. 9; ch. 13:12 and 32:8 and 36:23; 1 Kings 8:57; 1 Chr. 22:18.

*{while you be with him}* While you carry yourselves as in his presence, fear him, serve him, trust in him.

*{and if ye seek him, he will be found of you}* See v. 15 and 1 Chr. 28:9.

*{but if ye forsake him}* See 1 Chr. 28:9.

*{he will forsake you}* See 2 Kings 21:14.

**Verse 3. ***{Now for a long season}* This has reference to the ten tribes’ first revolt from the house of David, and from the house of God and all his ordinances. And compare ch. 12:13 and 13:2 and 14:1, and you find thirty years from the division till this time.

*{Israel}* the ten tribes that revolted, 1 Kings 12:16.

*{hath been without the true God}* They set up idols instead of the true God, 1 Kings 12:28. See Judg. 5:8.

*{and without a teaching priest}* The sons of Aaron, who were the only true priests, were so trained up in the law of God as they could well teach and instruct the people therein. But these were cast off by the ten tribes, ch. 11:14, and in their stead they chose the meanest of the people, very *ignaros*, 1 Kings 12:31 and 13:33.

*{and without law}* They also cast out the Levites that should have instructed them in the law, ch. 11:14, neither regarded they any of the ordinances of the law.

**Verse 4. ***{But when they in their trouble did turn unto the LORD}* We do not read that the Israelites after their revolt did ever turn unto the Lord: but this is here to be taken by way of supposition, *If they had turned*, as Deut. 4:29, or it must be referred to the times before their apostasy. In the book of the Judges we have many instances of Israel’s turning to the Lord in their trouble, Judg. 3:9, 15 and 10:10, 16; 1 Sam. 7:4, etc.

*{God of Israel}* See 1 Kings 8:15.

*{and sought him, he was found of them}* See v. 2.

**Verse 5. ***{And in those times there was no peace}* For besides the war that the ten tribes had with other nations there was war betwixt Jeroboam and Rehoboam all their days, 1 Kings 14:30, so also betwixt Jeroboam and Abijah, ch. 13:2, etc., and betwixt Asa and Baasha, 2 Kings 15:16. See Judg. 5:6, 7.

*{to him that went out, nor to him that came in}* Which way soever a man went, either abroad or homewards, he was in great danger, and subject to many troubles.

*{but great vexations were upon all the inhabitants of the countries}* Many troubles befell the Israelites wheresoever they were in any of their tribes, and those such as much vexed and perplexed their spirits.

**Verse 6. ***{And nation was destroyed}* Heb. *beaten to pieces*. For in wars and all manner of dissensions men do what they can to pull one another in pieces.

*{of nation}* Nation is put for kingdom; kingdom did rise against kingdom. Israel rose against Judah in Rehoboam’s time, ch. 10:16, and Judah against Israel in Abijah’s time, ch. 13:2, etc. Besides foreign enemies might annoy them.

*{and city of city}* When there was no general war against the nation, the bordering cities of each nation annoyed one another.

*{for God did vex them with all adversity}* God is the principal author of judgments, though men be the instruments, Isa. 10:5, 6; Amos 3:6.

**Verse 7. ***{Be ye strong therefore}* Judgments on those that forsake the Lord should move us to be courageous in removing corruptions, and renewing our covenant with God. See of this phrase *be ye strong* 1 Chr. 22:13.

*{and let not your hands be weak}* The hands are the chief instruments whereby men do this or that; and if they be weak, nothing can be well done. But this is metaphorically to be taken and applied to the mind. Hereby the prophet dehorts them from being fearful, or negligent, or too much dejected, 2 Sam. 4:1; Heb. 12:12.

*{for your work shall be rewarded}* It is a great encouragement to stir up a man’s spirit and make him diligent, to know that his labor shall not be lost, 1 Cor. 15:58. The reward here promised is not upon desert or merit, but upon God’s free grace and mercy.

**Verse 8. ***{And when Asa heard these words}* It appears he diligently heeded them, in that he was careful to make a right use of them.

*{and the prophecy of Oded the prophet}* This questionless has reference to the prophecy immediately going before. Indeed it is v. 1 that Azariah the son of Oded was the man who uttered that prophecy. But he might have two names, Azariah and Oded. The same name might be given to the father and the son, and both called Oded. See the like 1 Chr. 18:16. Or some former prophecy might be uttered by Oded the father, and this have relation thereunto. Or it may refer to Ahijah’s prophecy, 1 Kings 14:10, etc., which Oded wrote and put into the Chronicles; in which respect it may be called *the prophecy of Oded*.

*{he took courage}* He gathered his spirits together, and laid aside all fear of danger that might follow upon reformation.

*{and put away the abominable idols}* Heb. *abominations*. We read of great reformation before made by Asa, ch. 14:3, 4, 5. But it appears by this, that all abominations were not taken away. He now searched more narrowly from house to house, and redressed many things that lay hid before. So did Jacob, Gen. 35:2, 3, 5. Of abominations see 1 Kings 11:5; 2 Kings 23:24.

*{out of all the land of Judah and Benjamin}* Of these he had an especial care, because they had never revolted.

*{and out of the cities which he had taken from mount Ephraim}* i.e., the cities which his father took, ch. 13:19 and 17:2, or other cities which himself had taken.

*{and renewed the altar of the LORD}* i.e., the great brazen altar in the priests’ court; whereon fire being kept continually, Lev. 6:12, and every day sacrifices offered, could not but in time decay, and need reparation. Asa therefore renewing his covenant with God solemnly, and intending to offer a great sacrifice, v. 11, repaired and beautified that altar. And herein he showed a mind seasoned with piety and inflamed with zeal.

*{that was before the porch of the LORD}* See ch. 8:12; 1 Kings 8:22.

**Verse 9. ***{And he gathered all Judah and Benjamin}* Besides those comprised under this phrase v. 2, he sent up and down throughout these two tribes to assemble his subjects unto that solemn covenant which he intended, v. 12.

*{and the strangers with them}* Such of the ten tribes as were in Judah after the revolt were counted strangers: for they separated themselves from the house of David and kingdom of Judah, yea, they forsook the true religion, and served idols, and subjected themselves to other kings and other laws. See ch. 30:25.

*{out of Ephraim and Manasseh}* Under these two tribes are comprised the other tribes that revolted.

*{and out of Simeon}* The greater part of Simeon was within the compass of Judah, Josh. 19, and held with the house of David. See 1 Kings 11:13. But that part which bordered on Israel revolted with the rest. Of these there came many, and joined themselves to Judah, and these are here called *strangers out of Simeon*.

*{for they fell to him out of Israel in abundance}* They voluntarily left their own habitations, and came to Judah, as others before them had done at the first revolt, ch. 11:16.

*{when they saw that the LORD his God was with him}* They saw this by the continuing of the kingdom of Judah in David’s line, by the great victory his father had got over the Israelites, ch. 13:17, and by that which himself had got over the Ethiopians, and by that long peace he had enjoyed; hereby they knew that the true God whom he worshiped did prosper and bless him. On the other side they observed how Jeroboam, that forsook the Lord, never prospered, but that he and his whole posterity was at this time clean rooted out. This reason shows that they were not forced to come to Judah, but that piety and the fear of God brought them.

**Verse 10. ***{So they gathered themselves together at Jerusalem}* i.e., those mentioned v. 9. The king having sent for them, they readily came, and assembled together at the great city where the temple was.

*{in the third month}* Answering in part to our May, and in part to June. In it the great feast of weeks, commonly called Pentecost, was celebrated, wherein all the males were to appear before the Lord, Exod. 23:14, 16; Deut. 16:16. This moved the king the rather to send for them, and them to come to Jerusalem.

*{in the fifteenth year of the reign of Asa}* This was anon after the great victory over the Ethiopians. So as they came long after those ten years mentioned ch. 14:1.

**Verse 11. ***{And they offered unto the LORD the same time}* Heb. *in that day*. Whereon they were assembled together, the day of Pentecost.

*{of the spoil which they had brought}* There was a law, that when God’s people had obtained a victory over their enemies, part of the spoil should be given to the Lord for offerings, Num. 31:28, etc. See 1 Chr. 26:27, 28. Saul makes this a pretense of his keeping the cattle which he brought from the Amalekites, 1 Sam. 15:15.

*{seven hundred oxen, and seven thousand sheep}* This considered in itself was a very great offering, but a very small one, if compared unto that 1 Kings 8:63. This gave great evidence of their pious and zealous and thankful spirits.

**Verse 12. ***{And they entered into a covenant}* To bind themselves more firmly to a due serving of the Lord. See 2 Kings 11:4.

*{to seek the LORD}* To call upon his name, to worship and serve him, to depend on him, to cleave close unto him. All duties of piety are comprised under this phrase. See 1 Chr. 16:10; Isa. 55:6.

*{God of their fathers}* See ch. 11:16.

*{with all their heart, and with all their soul}* See 1 Kings 2:4.

**Verse 13. ***{That whosoever would not seek}* See v. 12.

*{the LORD God of Israel}* See 1 Kings 8:15.

*{should be put to death}* This is not to be taken of every failing in that duty of seeking the Lord, but of an obstinate refusal to be subject to him, and of seeking to other gods. This was according to the law, Deut. 13:9.

*{whether small or great, whether man or woman}* There must be no partiality in executing judgment, Deut. 13:6 and 33:9, nor respect of persons in his cause who respects no person, Deut. 10:7.

**Verse 14. ***{And they sware unto the LORD}* To make the covenant more inviolable on their part. For an oath is a curb to conscience. This oath was an imprecation against themselves if they should break the covenant, as Neh. 5:13. The Jews were wont, when they made a solemn covenant, to cut a beast in sunder, and one part being laid on one side and the other on the other, to pass betwixt those parts; implying thereby that they desired to be dealt with as that beast was if they did not keep their covenant. See Gen. 15:10; Jer. 34:18.

*{with a loud voice}* Not whisperingly, as if they feared, or were ashamed of the matter, and would not have others to know it; but audibly and boldly, so as they would have all to take notice of it.

*{and with shouting}* This was a loud joining of their voices together, which soldiers use in war to affright their enemies. See ch. 13:15; 1 Chr. 15:28.

*{and with trumpets, and with cornets}* Of trumpets see 1 Chr. 13:8; of cornets, 1 Chr. 15:28. These were expressions of much cheerfulness and joyfulness in what they did, v. 15; 2 Sam. 6:15; Ezra 3:11.

**Verse 15. ***{And all Judah rejoiced at the oath}* They were so far from backwardness in binding themselves to God as they rejoiced therein.

*{for they had sworn with all their heart}* Without any dissimulation or by-respect. That which comes from the heart is willingly and cheerfully done.

*{and sought him}* See v. 12.

*{with their whole desire}* It was not other men’s persuasions, nor outward motives, but the inward motion of their own spirit, and desire of their own hearts that stirred them up to that which is here set down.

*{and he was found of them}* The Lord heard their prayers, granted their desires, accepted what they did, and prospered their endeavors. See v. 2; Isa. 55:6.

*{and the LORD gave them rest round about}* i.e., peace. After the Ethiopians were overthrown they had no more war, neither with the Israelites, nor with any other nation.

**Verse 16. ***{And also concerning Maachah, etc.}* See 1 Kings 15:13.

*{and Asa cut down her idol, and stamped it, etc.}* This he did out of his great indignation against such an abomination. See 1 Kings 15:13.

**Verse 17. ***{But the high places were not taken away out of Israel}* Israel is here put for Judah, as ch. 12:12. See ch. 15:3, 5.

*{nevertheless the heart of Asa was perfect, etc.}* See 1 Kings 15:14.

**Verse 18. ***{And he brought into the house of God, etc.}* See 1 Kings 15:15.

**Verse 19. ***{And there was no more war}* Or, *there had been no war*. i.e., betwixt Judah and Israel in Asa’s time. *q.d.*, *Till this year the king of Israel made no war against Judah*. For this has relation to ch. 16:1, where it is said that *the king of Israel came up against Judah*.

*{unto the five and thirtieth year}* This account must begin from the division betwixt Israel and Judah, and so this be taken not of the five and thirtieth but sixteenth year of Asa’s reign. See ch. 16:1. That this was the five and thirtieth year from the division, is thus evident; Rehoboam, at the beginning of whose reign the division began, ch. 10:1, etc., reigned seventeen years, ch. 12:13. Abijah three years, ch. 13:2. To these add fifteen years of Asa’s reign, and the five and thirty years will be made up. There is a like computation from the beginning of the division, ch. 22:2.

*{of the reign of Asa}* Or, *in the reign of Asa*. This acception of the preposition helps the former exposition.

## CHAPTER 16

**Verse 1. ***{In the six and thirtieth year}* This account must needs have beginning before Asa’s reign, because Baasha died in the seven and twentieth of Asa, 1 Kings 15:33. See ch. 15:19.

*{of the reign of Asa}* Or, *in the reign of Asa*. See ch. 15:19.

*{Baasha king of Israel}* See 1 Kings 15:27.

*{came up against Judah, etc.}* This history is set down and expounded 1 Kings 15:17, etc. It shall be therefore sufficient to note only the additions and variations.

**Verse 2. ***{Then Asa brought out silver and gold}* Or, *then Asa took all the silver and the gold*, 1 Kings 15:18.

*{out of the treasures of the house of the LORD, and of the king’s house}* Or, *that were left in the treasures of the house of the Lord, and the treasures of the king’s house*, 1 Kings 15:18. Of these treasures see 1 Chr. 26:20 and 29:3.

*{and sent to Ben-hadad}* Or, *and delivered them into the hand of his servants: and king Asa sent them to Ben-hadad*, 1 Kings 15:18.

*{king of Syria}* Or, *the son of Tabrimon the son of Hezion king of Syria*, 1 Kings 15:18.

*{that dwelt at Damascus, saying}* Heb. *Darmesek*.

**Verse 3. ***{There is a league between me and thee, as there was between my father and thy father}* Or, *and between my father and thy father*.

*{behold, I have sent thee silver and gold}* Or, *a present of silver and gold*, 1 Kings 15:19.

*{go, break thy league with Baasha, etc.}* See 1 Kings 15:19.

**Verse 4. ***{And Ben-hadad hearkened, etc.}* See 1 Kings 15:20.

*{and Abel-maim}* Or, *Abel-beth-maachah*, 1 Kings 15:20.

*{and all the store cities of Naphtali}* Or, *and all Cinneroth, with all the land of Naphtali*. See 1 Kings 15:20.

**Verse 5. ***{And it came to pass when Baasha, etc.}* See 1 Kings 15:21.

*{and let his work cease}* Because he was afraid of Ben-hadad’s surprising him. It is added 1 Kings 15:21 that *Baasha dwelt in Tirzah*.

**Verse 6. ***{Then Asa the king took all Judah, etc.}* Or, *made a proclamation throughout all Judah; none was exempted, etc*., 1 Kings 15:22.

*{and he built therewith Geba, etc.}* *Geba of Benjamin*, 1 Kings 15:22.

**Verse 7. ***{And at that time Hanani the seer}* He was the father of the prophet Jehu, 1 Kings 16:1. Of seer see 2 Kings 17:13.

*{came to Asa king of Judah, and said unto him}* Faithful prophets dare come even to kings to tell them the Lord’s mind.

*{Because thou hast relied on the king of Syria}* For children of God to place their confidence on men, on those especially who are out of the church, is highly displeasing to God.

*{and not relied on the LORD}* Confidence on man draws away our confidence on God, Isa. 31:1.

*{thy God}* This special relation betwixt God and Asa aggravated his diffidence.

*{therefore is the host of the king of Syria escaped out of thine hand}* This may have reference to that which might have fallen out. If the king of Syria had come against Judah, being in league with Baasha, God would have delivered both those kings into his hands, as he did Zerah the Ethiopian, ch. 14:14. But Asa by making an agreement with Ben-hadad prevented that occasion, and so suffered Ben-hadad to escape out of his hands. Or it may have reference to Ben-hadad’s false play and deceitful dealing with Asa. For howsoever at this time he hearkened to Asa, yet after this, when Baasha annoyed Asa, he afforded him no succor at all. In this sense this phrase, *the king of Syria escaped out of thine hand*, implies thus much, He is so gone as in future distress he shall afford you no help. The Syrians were such implacable enemies of Israel and Judah as it had been far better for Asa to have stood out at defiance than to have sought friendship with them. For this therefore is Asa justly reproved.

**Verse 8. ***{Were not the Ethiopians}* Or, *Cushites*, ch. 14:9.

*{and the Lubims}* A people bordering on Ethiopia. See ch. 12:3.

*{a huge host}* Heb. *an army in multitude*. See ch. 14:9.

*{with very many chariots and horsemen}* The chariots are expressed ch. 14:9. The horsemen necessarily understood.

*{yet because thou didst rely on the LORD}* That Asa placed his confidence in God alone, is evident by this phrase ch. 14:11.

*{he delivered them into thine hand}* See ch. 14:12.

**Verse 9. ***{For the eyes of the LORD}* The providence of God is hereby set forth, which as an eye sees and observes things.

*{run to and fro throughout the whole earth}* God by his providence takes special notice of all things in all countries and places, particularly of the very disposition of people, Prov. 5:21 and 15:3; Job 34:21; Jer. 16:17.
