*{to shew himself strong in the behalf of them}* Or, *strongly to hold with them, etc*. To manifest his almighty power in protecting, preserving and delivering his, and in confounding their enemies.

*{whose heart is perfect towards him}* Who are upright and sincere in heart. See 1 Kings 8:61. Hereby the prophet implies that the heart of Asa was not perfect, at least in that for which he is reproved. See 1 Kings 15:14.

*{Herein hast thou done foolishly}* Like a fool, that considers not what may afterwards fall out, but only dotes upon the present. See 1 Chr. 21:8.

*{therefore from henceforth thou shalt have wars}* That which is noted 1 Kings 15:32, that there was war between Asa and Baasha all their days, verifies this threatening.

**Verse 10. ***{Then Asa was wroth with the seer}* This manifests the great corruption in Asa’s heart. See ch. 26:19. His son Jehoshaphat made a better use of Jehu’s reproof, ch. 19:2, etc. This epithet *seer* much aggravates Asa’s sin. See 2 Kings 17:13.

*{and put him in a prison house}* Heb. *gave him to the house of subversion*. Some expound it a noisome prison, a prison wherein such were laid as could expect nothing but death. Others take it to be a prison wherein he was manacled and fettered. He was dealt with as Jer. 20:2 and 29:26. This Hebrew word is used in those two places. The king dealt with him as with a traitor. So dealt Ahab with Micaiah, ch. 18:26.

*{for he was in a rage with him because of this thing}* The reason why Hanani the seer was so dealt withal, was not any desert on his part, but the undue wrath and fury of the king. Great men will not endure to be told of their faults.

*{And Asa oppressed}* Heb. *crushed*, or *trampled upon*.

*{some of the people the same time}* His cruelty extended to all sorts; he was impious against God, and injurious to men. This was a great fall.

**Verse 11. ***{And behold, the acts of Asa, first and last}* Before this fall and after. See ch. 12:15.

*{lo, they are written in the book of the kings of Judah and Israel}* They are briefly set down in sacred Scripture, as 1 Kings 15:9, etc., and largely in civil chronicles; in the chronicles of the kings of Judah, because he was a king thereof; and of the kings of Israel, because he had much to do with the kings of Israel.

**Verse 12. ***{And Asa in the thirty and ninth year of his reign}* This was many years after his making a league with Ben-hadad, v. 2, 3.

*{was diseased in his feet}* He was troubled with the gout, which in the extremity thereof is very painful.

*{until his disease was exceeding great}* Heb. *till it ascended upwards*. Till it went through his whole body, to his heart and head. This made it the more dangerous and deadly.

*{yet in his disease he sought not to the LORD}* He neither turned to the Lord by unfeigned repentance, nor called upon him for help, nor sought to know his will by any of his prophets. See 1 Chr. 10:14.

*{but to the physicians}* It is lawful to use the skill of physicians, and to seek for help by their means; but so as we depend on the Lord for his blessing on the means. Asa committed two sins; he was too careless and negligent of the Lord, and too confident in man. These he committed before, v. 2, 7. As in war, so in sickness, he put too little confidence in God, too much in man.

**Verse 13. ***{And Asa slept with his fathers}* See 1 Kings 2:10.

*{and died in the one and fortieth year of his reign}* This was the longest time that any king hitherto had reigned; only two after him reigned longer than he, namely Uzziah, whose end was much like Asa’s, ch. 26:26, and Manasseh, ch. 33:1.

**Verse 14. ***{And they buried him in his own sepulchres}* See on 2 Kings 22:29.

*{which he had made}* Heb. *digged*.

*{for himself}* Kings then used to provide graves for themselves.

*{in the city of David}* See 1 Kings 2:10.

*{and laid him in the bed which was filled}* Or, *which he filled*. Either he prepared this for himself in his lifetime, or one that he left in trust did it. For the word may be taken impersonally.

*{with sweet odours and divers kinds of spices}* Such as were used in embalming dead corpses.

*{prepared by the apothecaries’ art}* Great care was used about choice of spices and other things, and great skill in ordering them, Eccles. 10:1.

*{and they made a very great burning for him}* To wit, of sweet and fragrant things. This was counted an honorable funeral solemnity. It was denied to wicked Jehoram, ch. 21:19, but afforded to Zedekiah, Jer. 34:5, though he died in a strange land: for he was their last king. It may be this was done by those that survived Asa, in memorial of his former acts, and the good things that he had done for Judah. Or it may be that at length he repented, and out of a good respect to him thereupon was thus honored. Or this might be done by some special friends and favorites of his; or by his own appointment before his death.

## CHAPTER 17

**Verse 1. ***{And Jehoshaphat his son reigned in his stead}* 1 Kings 15:24. Jehoshaphat was the fourth king of Judah from the division, the fifth from David, the best since the division. His history is related in this and the three next chapters. In this his care in reforming religion and settling his kingdom is set down.

*{and strengthened himself against Israel}* Ahab reigned in Israel when Jehoshaphat began his reign, 1 Kings 22:41, and he was a mighty prince; yet we read not that he did any hostile act against Judah, but yielded to join affinity with Jehoshaphat, ch. 18:1. It is probable that he saw the kingdom of Judah too strong for him to oppose. Jehoshaphat is here said to strengthen himself, by preparing warlike provision against all attempts that the men of Israel might make against him. Some apply this word Israel to such as out of the ten tribes had subjected themselves to the kingdom of Judah, ch. 15:9, yea, and to other of his own subjects: he settled his dominion so strongly over them as he held them firm unto him.

**Verse 2. ***{And he placed forces in all the fenced cities of Judah}* He put soldiers and needful ammunition in them. See v. 19; ch. 11:6, etc.

*{and set garrisons in the land of Judah}* i.e., governors with companies of soldiers, throughout the whole land; in other places as well as in fenced cities.

*{and in the cities of Ephraim which Asa his father had taken}* See ch. 15:8.

**Verse 3. ***{And the LORD was with Jehoshaphat}* See ch. 15:2.

*{because he walked in the first ways of his father David}* Or, *in the ways of David, and in the first ways of his father*. The former reading has relation to those times of David wherein he kept himself free from such scandalous crimes as he fell into in his latter time, as his adultery with Uriah’s wife, his murdering of Uriah, and his numbering of the people. In the latter reading relation is had to Asa’s former times, before he made a league with Ben-hadad, ch. 16:2, etc.

*{and sought not unto Baalim}* Of Baal properly taken see 1 Kings 16:32. Here it is indefinitely taken for any manner of idol. Jehoshaphat gave not himself to any manner of idolatry.

**Verse 4. ***{But sought to the LORD}* See ch. 15:4.

*{God of his fathers}* fathers may have reference to David, mentioned v. 3, or to Asa, who in his former time did that which was good and right in the eyes of the Lord his God, ch. 14:2. And it is an amplification of his piety, that he was a pious son of a pious father.

*{and walked in his commandments}* He made God’s commandments the rule of his life. See 1 Kings 2:3.

*{and not after the doings of Israel}* As this has relation to the idolatry of Israel in worshiping the golden calves, 1 Kings 12:28, etc., so also to other evil practices of theirs, 1 Kings 12:31, etc. and 14:9.

**Verse 5. ***{Therefore the LORD stablished the kingdom}* See 1 Kings 2:12.

*{in his hand}* Under his government, while he sat upon the throne and swayed the scepter.

*{and all Judah}* i.e., all the subjects of Jehoshaphat.

*{brought}* Heb. *gave*. What they did they did freely.

*{to Jehoshaphat presents}* As strangers used to send presents to such kings as they honored, 1 Kings 4:21, so also did natives and proper subjects, in testimony of their grateful, cheerful and joyful subjection. See the contrary 1 Sam. 10:27.

*{and he had riches and honour in abundance}* These were blessings which God conferred on Solomon, as an abundant remuneration of his pious petition, 1 Kings 3:9, 13.

**Verse 6. ***{And his heart was lift up}* i.e., was encouraged. It was not puffed up with vainglory and self-conceit by reason of his honor and riches, as the heart of Hezekiah was, ch. 32:25, but it was made free, cheerful, undaunted.

*{in the ways of the LORD}* In doing such things as God required, and were pleasing and acceptable to him. This he counted his highest honor, and in that respect may be said to be lift up.

*{moreover he took away the high places and groves out of Judah}* We read how Asa twice made such a reformation, ch. 14:5 and 15:8, 16: therefore there were some high places and groves that he overslipped, or else the people being too much addicted unto them set up such things again in his latter years, especially after he fell into his disease, ch. 16:12. Of high places see 1 Kings 3:4; of groves, 1 Kings 16:33; of the different kinds of high places, ch. 14:1 and 15:17.

**Verse 7. ***{Also in the third year of his reign}* It seems the reformation before mentioned was before his third year, so as he began betimes.

*{he sent to his princes}* Or, *with his princes*. According to the former reading the princes were such as were governors in several places of the land. According to the latter they were such as were about the court whom the king judged to be able and faithful in that whereabout he sent them. Hereupon some read it thus, *He sent his princes*.

*{even to Ben-hail, etc.}* Here are five princes reckoned by name.

*{to teach in the cities of Judah}* i.e., to command and admonish in the king’s name all the people that were up and down in the cities of Judah, to hearken to those instructions that the priests and Levites should give them concerning the law of God, and answerably carry themselves: so as these princes were sent, or sent unto, to make way for the priests and Levites by their authority.

**Verse 8. ***{And with them he sent Levites}* Levites and priests were in their turns among the people in cities and countries, to instruct and teach them, as well as do the services of the temple. Levites were to read and expound the law, Neh. 8:7.

*{even Shemaiah, etc.}* Here are nine Levites set down by name, as there were five princes, v. 7.

*{and with them Elishama, and Jehoram, priests}* Priests also were sent to resolve doubts, and to decide controversies, Deut. 17:8, 9; Mal. 2:7.

**Verse 9. ***{And they taught in Judah}* In those several places whither they were sent, and where there was need.

*{and had the book of the law of the LORD with them}* That they might hereby demonstrate the truth of what they taught, and show the very text to such as made any question about anything. See Acts 17:11.

*{and went about throughout all the cities of Judah, and taught the people}* This explains the general noted in the beginning of the verse.

**Verse 10. ***{And the fear of the LORD}* See ch. 14:14.

*{fell}* Heb. *was*.

*{upon all the kingdoms of the lands that were round about Judah}* For they might best hear of the goodness of the Lord towards Jehoshaphat, and of his mighty power in protecting him; yea, and of that great respect which all his subjects showed him, and of the strong defenses that he had made in his land.

*{so that they made no war against Jehoshaphat}* Where God strikes men’s hearts with terror, they dare not stir nor make any opposition. See Gen. 35:5.

**Verse 11. ***{Also some of the Philistines}* These were most deadly enemies of the Israelites.

*{brought Jehoshaphat presents}* See 1 Kings 4:21. This they did out of desire of his friendship, and in testimony of their homage.

*{and tribute silver}* For Asa had subdued a great part of them, ch. 14:14.

*{and the Arabians brought him flocks}* These also were subdued by Asa, ch. 14:15. Their chief calling being to feed cattle, they brought such presents and tribute to him.

*{seven thousand and seven hundred rams, and seven thousand and seven hundred he goats}* These were fit both for meat and for sacrifices; for they were all clean cattle. See the like of Moab, 2 Kings 3:4.

**Verse 12. ***{And Jehoshaphat waxed great exceedingly}* Heb. *he was going and increasing*. He increased more and more. This word *great* is here to be taken in a large extent, great in riches and honor, v. 5, great in power, v. 13, etc., great in name and esteem, v. 5, 11.

*{and he built in Judah castles}* Or, *palaces*. Fair, strong places.

*{and cities of store}* Places for ammunition and provision.

**Verse 13. ***{And he had much business in the cities of Judah}* He took great care himself and set others on work about such things as might be fit for a kingdom in times of peace and war.

*{and the men of war}* See 1 Kings 9:22; 1 Chr. 12:8.

*{mighty men of valour}* See 1 Chr. 5:24 and 7:7.

*{were in Jerusalem}* This was the greatest city of the kingdom, and the safest place for the king: here was the temple, here was the king’s court, where he and his chief princes did most reside, and therefore here were such men placed.

**Verse 14. ***{And these are the numbers of them}* All made up eleven hundred and threescore thousand. See v. 18 and ch. 13:3.

*{according to the house of their fathers}* See 1 Chr. 5:13, 14.

*{of Judah, the captains of thousands; Adnah the chief}* Or, *in Judah, of the captains of thousands, Adnah the chief*. He was above all those that are hereafter named.

*{and with him mighty men of valour three hundred thousand}* All these were under his command, whether officers or soldiers.

**Verse 15. ***{And next to him}* Heb. *at his hand*. He that is next to one is at his hand.

*{was Jehohanan the captain, and with him two hundred and fourscore thousand}* These were such as in the general are described v. 13, 14, mighty men of valour.

**Verse 16. ***{And next him was Amasiah the son of Zichri}* Zichri was a man of note: for honor therefore is he set down.

*{who willingly offered himself unto the LORD}* Of his own accord, cheerfully and readily he put himself on to fight the battles of the Lord, or to do service for the honor of God and safety of God’s people. Some expound it that he devoted himself a Nazarite to the Lord; and the Hebrew word may bear as much.

*{and with him two hundred thousand mighty men of valour}* See v. 13, 14.

**Verse 17. ***{And of Benjamin}* The former commanders and companies were of Judah; those that follow, of Benjamin; in which two tribes stood Jerusalem, wherein all these companies were, v. 13.

*{Eliada a mighty man of valour, and with him armed men with bow and shield, etc.}* With offensive and defensive weapons. The Benjamites were very well exercised in the bow, ch. 14:8; 1 Chr. 12:2.

**Verse 18. ***{And next him was Jehozabad, and with him, etc.}* These were the last company. All the number of Benjamin was three hundred and fourscore thousand; of Judah seven hundred and fourscore thousand. See v. 14.

*{ready prepared for the war}* As they were trained up in military discipline and fit for war, so were they always well furnished to be sent forth to the war upon any occasion.

*{These waited on the king}* It is not probable that so many thousands should continually attend on the king, or be together at Jerusalem; but in courses they came thither, some at one time, some at another.

*{besides those whom the king put in the fenced cities throughout all Judah}* V. 2.

## CHAPTER 18

**Verse 1. ***{Now Jehoshaphat had riches and honour in abundance}* See ch. 17:5, 12. In this chapter a great fault of Jehoshaphat, who was much commended ch. 17, is set down, namely his helping of the ungodly, ch. 19:2.

*{and joined affinity with Ahab}* i.e., Jehoram Jehoshaphat’s son married Athaliah Ahab’s daughter, 2 Kings 8:18.

**Verse 2. ***{And after certain years}* Heb. *at the end of years*. i.e., such as were passed over from the beginning of the league betwixt Jehoshaphat and Ahab. Now this was the last year of Ahab’s reign and life, v. 34, and about the seventh of Jehoshaphat’s reign. See 1 Kings 22:51.

*{he went down to Ahab to Samaria}* Upon complement to testify his respect to him, and to continue the league and amity. Hereupon Ahab took occasion to require his assistance in the following war.

*{and Ahab killed sheep and oxen for him in abundance}* This he did to show how welcome Jehoshaphat was, and the more to endear himself unto him. Friends use to testify their mutual good respect by feasting one another.

*{and for the people that he had with him}* Jehoshaphat came as a king, in royal estate and great pomp, as the queen of Sheba did, 1 Kings 10:2. And by that which follows it appears that he came accompanied with bands of soldiers. Therefore that all his train might be liberally entertained, it was requisite that Ahab should kill abundance of sheep and oxen.

*{and persuaded him to go up with him}* Ahab by this royal entertaining of Jehoshaphat and his fair speech wrought on him, and prevailed with him to do what he desired.

*{to Ramoth-gilead}* See 1 Kings 22:3, where this story is set down almost word for word as it is here.

**Verse 3. ***{And Ahab king of Israel said unto Jehoshaphat king of Judah}* As their names so their kingdoms are distinguished: which at this time differed in religion, though they were all the posterity of Jacob.

*{Wilt thou go with me to Ramoth-gilead?}* This question he propounds to try Jehoshaphat’s mind to him.

*{And he answered him, I am as thou art, and my people as thy people}* 1 Kings 22:4 is added, *my horses as thy horses*.

*{and we will be with thee in the war}* We will be assistant unto you, and as helpful as if we were your own subjects.

**Verse 4. ***{And Jehoshaphat said, etc.}* See 1 Kings 22:5.

**Verse 5. ***{four hundred men}* Or, *about four hundred men*, 1 Kings 22:6.

*{Shall we go to Ramoth, etc.}* Or, *Shall I go against Ramoth*, 1 Kings 22:6.

**Verse 6. ***{Is there not here a prophet of the LORD besides}* Heb. *yet*, or *more*. Though there were many prophets, yet Jehoshaphat asked if there were not any more; not simply, as if he thought four hundred not enough, but because he discerned they were idolatrous prophets, and thereupon suspected their answer, and would therefore be informed by another kind of prophet, even a prophet of the true God Jehovah.

*{that we might enquire of him}* See 1 Kings 22:7.

**Verse 7. ***{There is yet one man, etc.}* His name and parentage is set down 1 Kings 22:8, *Micaiah the son of Imlah*.

*{but always evil}* Heb. *all his days*.

*{the same is Micaiah the son of Imlah}* Here is supplied his name and parentage, which was before omitted.

*{And Jehoshaphat said, Let not the king say so}* See 1 Kings 22:8.

**Verse 8. ***{called for one of his officers}* Or, *eunuchs*.

*{and said, Fetch quickly, etc.}* Heb. *Hasten*. See 1 Kings 22:9.

**Verse 9. ***{And the king of Israel, etc.}* See 1 Kings 22:10.

**Verse 10. ***{With these thou shalt push Syria}* Or, *the Syrians*, 1 Kings 22:11.

*{until they be consumed}* Heb. *until thou consume them*. Or, *until thou have consumed them*.

**Verse 12. ***{And the messenger that went, etc.}* See 1 Kings 22:13.

**Verse 13. ***{Even what my God saith, etc.}* Or, *what the Lord saith*, 1 Kings 22:14.

**Verse 14. ***{And when he was come to the king, etc.}* See 1 Kings 22:15.

*{Shall we go to Ramoth-gilead, etc.}* Or, *shall we go against Ramoth-gilead*, 1 Kings 22:15.

*{or shall I forbear?}* Or, *shall we forbear*?

*{And he said, Go ye up, and prosper}* Or, *Go, and prosper, in the singular number*, 1 Kings 22:15.

*{and they shall be delivered into your hand}* Or, *for the Lord shall deliver it into the hand of the king*, 1 Kings 22:15.

**Verse 15, 16. ***{And the king said, etc.}* See 1 Kings 22:16, 17.

**Verse 17. ***{but evil}* Or, *but for evil*.

**Verse 18. ***{Again he said, etc.}* i.e., Micaiah.

*{standing, etc.}* Or, *standing by him, etc*., 1 Kings 22:19.

**Verse 19. ***{Who shall entice}* Or, *Who shall persuade*, or *deceive*, 1 Kings 22:20. The same Hebrew word is in both places. So v. 20, 21.

**Verse 21. ***{Then there came out, etc.}* See 1 Kings 22:21, 22.

**Verse 22. ***{Of these thy prophets, etc.}* *of all these thy prophets*, 1 Kings 22:22.

*{against thee}* Or, *concerning thee*, 1 Kings 22:23.

**Verse 23, 24, 25. ***{Then Zedekiah, etc.}* See 1 Kings 22:24, 25, 26.

**Verse 26. ***{Until I return in peace}* *until I come in peace*, 1 Kings 22:27.

**Verse 27. ***{If thou certainly return in peace, etc.}* Or, *If thou return at all in peace*, 1 Kings 22:28. The Hebrew word is doubled, *if returning thou return*. See 1 Kings 8:13.

*{Hearken all ye people}* Or, *Hearken, O people, every one of you*, 1 Kings 22:28. The Hebrew is the same.

**Verse 28. ***{So the king of Israel, etc.}* See 1 Kings 22:29.

**Verse 29. ***{And the king of Israel said, etc.}* See 1 Kings 22:30.

*{they went to the battle}* Or, *he went into the battle, in the singular number*, 1 Kings 22:30.

**Verse 30. ***{Now the king of Syria had commanded the captains of the chariots that were with him, etc.}* Or, *But the king of Syria commanded his thirty and two captains, that had rule over his chariots, etc*., 1 Kings 22:32.

**Verse 31. ***{It is the king of Israel}* Or, *Surely it is the king of Israel*, 1 Kings 22:23.

*{therefore they compassed about him to fight}* Or, *they turned aside to fight against him*.

*{but Jehoshaphat cried out}* See 1 Kings 22:32.

*{and the LORD helped him, and God moved them to depart from him}* This is not mentioned 1 Kings 22:32. The first clause shows how ready the Lord is to help them that call upon him; the latter, that the hearts of enemies are in his power to turn them as it pleases him, Prov. 21:1.

**Verse 32. ***{For it came to pass that when, etc.}* See 1 Kings 22:33.

*{from pursuing him}* Heb. *from after him*.

**Verse 33. ***{And a certain man drew a bow, etc.}* See 1 Kings 22:34.

*{therefore he said to his chariot man, etc.}* See 1 Kings 22:34.

**Verse 34. ***{And the battle increased that day}* See 1 Kings 22:35.

*{howbeit the king of Israel stayed himself up in his chariot against the Syrians}* Or, *the king was stayed up, etc*.

*{until the even}* Or, *and died at even*, 1 Kings 22:35.

*{and about the time of the sun going down he died}* This expressly shows what time of the even is meant. There follows more about Ahab 1 Kings 22:35, etc., but the main scope of the penman of this history being to set forth so much as concerned Jehoshaphat, he here ceases.

## CHAPTER 19

**Verse 1. ***{And Jehoshaphat the king of Judah returned to his house in peace to Jerusalem}* Though he were in great danger, and the king with whom he went to battle were slain, ch. 18:30–34, yet he returned in peace, not slain, not wounded, no nor the enemy stirred up against him.

**Verse 2. ***{And Jehu the son of Hanani the seer}* Of this Jehu see 1 Kings 16:1. Of Hanani the seer see ch. 16:7.

*{went out to meet him}* When Jehoshaphat returned from the war in which Ahab was slain, then this prophet met him. See ch. 15:2.

*{and said to king Jehoshaphat}* Though his message were very sad, and that to a king, yet boldly he declares the mind of the Lord.

*{Shouldest thou help the ungodly}* i.e., Ahab, and the Israelites, who were idolaters, and in that respect very ungodly. And this was the more unlawful because the Israelites were apostates and revolters from the true religion, and Ahab himself was one of the worst kings, if not simply the worst of all the kings of Israel. See 1 Kings 16:30. By the help he afforded him (whereof see ch. 18:3, etc.) he seemed to give countenance to his idolatry. It is not simply unlawful to afford aid to idolaters of other nations in external politic mutual defense against enemies; but revolters are to be detested.

*{and love them that hate the LORD}* Here two circumstances plainly demonstrate Jehoshaphat’s fact to be a sin, and also aggravate the same. 1. He loved the ungodly, and that not only in a politic respect, but with an inward entire affection. 2. The ungodly are so detestable to the Lord, as they who love them profess a hatred of him.

*{therefore is wrath upon thee from before the LORD}* This may be taken 1. of Jehoshaphat’s desert: he had deserved wrath. 2. of the danger wherein he was, ch. 18:31. God manifested his wrath upon him in bringing him to that danger, because he would help and love the ungodly. 3. of some judgment that hung over his head, as that conspiracy of Moab, Ammon and others, ch. 20:1, etc. See on that place.

**Verse 3. ***{Nevertheless, there are good things found in thee}* This has reference to ch. 17:3, etc. God takes notice of that grace that is in his children, so as to spare them, out of his own grace, when they trespass. These are said to be found in Jehoshaphat, because they were evidently manifested to be in him, Neh. 9:8.

*{in that thou hast taken away the groves out of the land}* See ch. 17:6.

*{and hast prepared thine heart to seek God}* In the best manner he could, he did consult and advise with himself how he might best serve the Lord. See Ezra 7:10. The contrary is noted of Rehoboam, ch. 12:14.

**Verse 4. ***{And Jehoshaphat dwelt at Jerusalem}* i.e., quietly continued there. Some apply this to his giving over the government to his son, 2 Kings 1:57, and so remaining quietly in his palace.

*{and he went out again}* Heb. *he returned and went out*. As he had done before, ch. 17:7, so now he did again.

*{through the people}* Throughout the whole nation. In testimony of his true repentance for his sin he endeavored to bring all his people to the true worship of God.

*{from Beer-sheba to mount Ephraim}* From one end of his kingdom to the other. Beer-sheba was the south border, Mount Ephraim the north border of Judah in Asa’s time. See 1 Kings 19:3; ch. 15:8, 9.

*{and brought them back to the LORD}* It seems divers of the kingdom of Judah, especially in the outcoasts, had revolted from the true religion. In this respect Jehoshaphat is said to bring them back to the Lord.

*{God of their fathers}* See 1 Chr. 29:18.

**Verse 5. ***{And he set judges in the land}* What these were, see v. 8, etc.

*{throughout all the fenced cities of Judah}* Lest soldiers should abuse their power unto violence and wrong, he sets judges in the cities where they were, to see justice and right done.

*{city by city}* In every city.

**Verse 6. ***{And said to the judges}* He does not only appoint judges, but also incite and direct them to do justice.

*{Take heed what you do}* Judges, that are in place to right others’ wrongs, had need to be very circumspect.

*{for ye judge not for man}* Not simply in the name, or by the authority of man. Though all civil public affairs were done in the name of the king, yet the king tells them that, comparatively to God’s, their king’s name was not to sway them this way or that way.

*{but for the LORD}* The Lord is that supreme king to whom they must give an account, and therefore they ought to have an eye especially unto him.

*{who is with you in the judgment}* Heb. *in the matter of judgment*. Judges sit in the place of God, and bear his image, and therefore must be very circumspect about the judgment they give.

**Verse 7. ***{Wherefore now let the fear of the LORD be upon you}* Stand in awe of God and be afraid to do anything that may offend him.

*{take heed, and do it}* Do that which is right. Unless men be very observant over themselves, they will soon be brought to do wrong.

*{for there is no iniquity with the LORD our God}* He will no way do that which is unjust, but render to every one what is just and equal.

*{nor respect of persons}* Deut. 10:17; Job 34:19; Acts 10:34; Rom. 2:11; Eph. 6:9; Col. 3:25; 1 Pet. 1:17. God regards not any outward thing in those which come before him, to move him to do this or that.

*{nor taking of gifts}* God will not be bribed to do anything.

**Verse 8. ***{Moreover in Jerusalem did Jehoshaphat set}* He had an especial care over this his chief city, to keep it in good order, as well as the garrison towns where soldiers were.

*{of the Levites}* To instruct the people.

*{and of the priests}* To resolve cases, ch. 17:8.

*{and of the chief of the fathers of Israel}* To see justice and good judgment executed, Deut. 16:18. At Jerusalem there was a great and high court, consisting of priests and Levites and elders of the people. Before these were great and weighty causes brought, and appeals from other courts were made to them.

*{for the judgment of the LORD}* For causes ecclesiastical.

*{and for controversies}* Doubtful and disputable cases about civil matters, as bargains, contracts, etc.

*{when they returned to Jerusalem}* Heb. *and they returned*. They who were sent abroad, returned; or they who had causes to be judged, as v. 10. Some take this to be spoken of such judges in the country and cities abroad as by reason of the obscurity and difficulty of causes came to Jerusalem to advise with the court there. See Exod. 18:26; Deut. 1:17 and 17:8.

**Verse 9. ***{And he charged them, saying}* He thought it not enough to exhort them to duty, but in the name of the Lord he lays an express charge upon them.

*{Thus shall ye do in the fear of the LORD}* Setting God before your eyes, take heed of provoking him.

*{faithfully}* Doing no wrong, nor seeking advantage to yourselves.

*{and with a perfect heart}* Uprightly. See 1 Kings 8:61.

**Verse 10. ***{And what cause soever shall come to you}* The ears of judges must be open to every cause of any person whatsoever.

*{of your brethren}* Judges and common people came all from the same stock, and in that respect were all brethren. This is spoken to move judges readily to hear them.

*{that dwell in their cities}* These were other than Jerusalem.

*{between blood and blood}* i.e., The kinds of murder, whether willful or no.

*{between law and commandments, statutes and judgments}* See 1 Kings 2:3. The meaning is, In case one take a law, or commandment, or statute, or judgment one way, another, another way, the judges’ endeavor should be to inform and resolve those who mistake, and to declare the true sense thereof.

*{ye shall even warn them that they trespass not against the LORD}* By misinterpreting the law, and doing otherwise than it intends.

*{and so wrath come upon you}* i.e., Some judgment that is an effect of God’s wrath, Num. 16:46. This might fall upon them for not warning or directing the people, Ezek. 3:18.

*{this do, and you shall not trespass}* If those that have a charge over others be conscionable in directing them aright, and warning them of their sins, they shall neither trespass against God, whose charge they keep; nor against their brethren, whose good they seek.

**Verse 11. ***{And behold, Amariah the chief priest}* There were divers high priests of this name, 1 Chr. 6:7, 11.

*{is over you in all matters of the LORD}* i.e., Ecclesiastical matters, concerning religion and the service of God, which were to be decided by the law of the Lord.

*{and Zebadiah the son of Ishmael, the ruler of the house of Judah}* This man was a prince, and governor of state.

*{for all the king’s matters}* i.e., Civil affairs, concerning peace and justice betwixt man and man, depending on the king’s laws, and to be decided by the statutes of the kingdom.

*{also the Levites shall be officers before you}* Such as are mentioned 1 Chr. 26:29, who were up and down in several cities, to see laws put in execution, according to the directions that should be given them from the great court at Jerusalem.

*{Deal courageously}* Heb. *Take courage and do*. Judges have need of courage that they be not daunted with people’s murmurings and gainsayings.

*{and the LORD shall be with the good}* He will be with such as are careful and conscionable in doing that which is just and right, to assist them, to protect them, and every way to bless both their persons and endeavors.

## CHAPTER 20

**Verse 1. ***{It came to pass after this also}* From hence to v. 21 is set down a very great deliverance which God gave Jehoshaphat from many and mighty enemies. This first clause is a transition from that which was noted ch. 19 to that which follows here. It may have a particular reference to the judgment denounced ch. 19:2, and so it gives instance of God’s wrath upon Jehoshaphat, in suffering enemies to invade him; and also of God’s respect to the good things found in him, ch. 19:3, in preserving and delivering him. In relation to his care of reformation noted ch. 19:4, etc., it shows that notwithstanding the good things found in God’s children he suffers them to be tried, but so as their trial turns to their greater glory.

*{that the children of Moab, and the children of Ammon}* These two nations were of the posterity of Lot, Gen. 19:37, 38, yet always bitter and mortal enemies to the children of Israel.

*{and with them other beside the Ammonites}* These other are supposed to be the Syrians, v. 2, and Edomites, v. 10, 22. Some take them to be Amalekites, who in habit and language feigned themselves to be Ammonites. Others read, *and with them of the Hammonims*; a people distinct from the Ammonites.

*{came against Jehoshaphat to battle}* The cause of this invasion is not set down, but imagined to be that aid which Jehoshaphat afforded Ahab against the Syrians, ch. 18:3, etc. If so, then is further confirmed what is said of God’s wrath upon Jehoshaphat for helping Ahab, ch. 19:2. Kings oft bring judgments upon their whole lands. See v. 22.

**Verse 2. ***{Then there came some that told Jehoshaphat, saying}* These were either some scouts that he had abroad, or some of his own subjects that occasionally were out of their country, or messengers from some neighboring princes that wished well unto him.

*{There cometh a great multitude against thee}* Had it not been a great multitude indeed, Jehoshaphat, that was so well prepared, ch. 17:19, would not have been cast into so great a fear as is intimated v. 3, 12.

*{from beyond the sea on this side Syria}* This is that sea called *Asphaltites*, or the Dead Sea, or Salt Sea. The people here mentioned came from thereabouts. See Gen. 14:3, 7. This sea was the east border of Judah, Josh. 15:5.

*{and behold, they be in Hazazon-tamar}* See Gen. 14:7.

*{which is En-gedi}* See Josh. 15:62.

**Verse 3. ***{And Jehoshaphat feared}* This was both a natural fear in regard of his many enemies, and also a pious fear in relation to God.

*{and set himself}* Heb. *set his face*. See 2 Kings 12:17.

*{to seek the LORD}* This phrase in general implies a care to serve God, and to obtain reconciliation with him, ch. 11:16, and in particular, prayer to God for protection and assistance, Isa. 55:6.

*{and proclaimed a fast}* This was an ordinary means used by God’s people, and always joined with extraordinary prayer, when they either would obtain some special blessing, or being in any great danger desired deliverance from it. See Judg. 20:26; 1 Sam. 7:6; Ezra 8:21, 23; Neh. 1:4 and 9:1; Esther 4:3. Hereby they did the better fit themselves for prayer, and gave evidence of the earnestness of their desire, together with an acknowledgment of their unworthiness; for they did voluntarily forbear the use of ordinary food and all manner of refreshing their bodies, to testify that they thought themselves altogether unworthy of any mercy.

*{throughout all Judah}* The danger was public and concerned the whole kingdom, and therefore he sent proclamations abroad into all parts, that they might all join in supplication to God, and so their prayers prove the more prevalent.

**Verse 4. ***{And Judah}* Benjamin also, and all that were under Jehoshaphat’s jurisdiction are here comprised under Judah.

*{gathered themselves together}* Such as used to come to public assemblies at Jerusalem are here intended.

*{to ask help of the LORD}* This showed their faith in God.

*{even out of the cities of Judah}* Cities are here put for towns and villages and houses in the country.

*{they came to seek the LORD}* To pray unto him. For that end they came to Jerusalem and the temple, where the evidence of God’s presence was. See v. 3.

**Verse 5. ***{And Jehoshaphat stood in the congregation of Judah and Jerusalem}* As Solomon did before the congregation of Israel, 1 Kings 8:22.

*{in the house of the LORD}* i.e., That great court, ch. 4:9, where the people used to assemble, and pious kings to pray, 2 Kings 19:14.

*{before the new court}* This was the court of the priests, which was before the house of the Lord, 1 Kings 8:64. It is styled *new*, in that it was newly repaired and beautified. We read ch. 15:8 that Asa *renewed the altar of the Lord*, which stood in this court: he might then also repair the whole court, and thereupon this title *new* be given it. Or it may be Jehoshaphat himself did it. Now the king stood before this court, namely before the entrance into it, both because the great altar stood there, and also because through it he might see into the holy place, yea, and unto the most holy place where the ark was. See 1 Kings 8:22.

**Verse 6. ***{And said, O LORD God of our fathers}* See ch. 11:16.

*{art not thou God in heaven?}* This place of God’s glory puts him in mind of his sovereignty and almighty power. See 1 Kings 8:23, 30.

*{and rulest thou not over all the kingdoms of the heathen?}* Though God be in heaven, yet his jurisdiction extends to the earth; and though by his word, Spirit and special grace he governs the church, yet his power manifests itself throughout the whole world.

*{and in thine hand is there not power and might}* Meditation on God’s power much strengthens faith in the greatest strait.

*{so that none is able to withstand thee}* He opposes God’s power to the power of his enemies, that he might be the less affrighted with their great preparations.

**Verse 7. ***{Art not thou our God}* This has reference to God’s promise and covenant, Gen. 17:7. As in the former verse he pleaded God’s power against his enemies, so here he pleads his covenant and mercy to his people.

*{who didst}* Heb. *thou didst*.

*{drive out the inhabitants of this land before thy people Israel}* Hereby he justifies Israel’s holding that land, by God’s casting out the Canaanites to make way for his people, Num. 35:51, 52, 53.

*{and gavest it to the seed of Abraham}* By seed is meant posterity. And here is the right they had to enjoy their land, God’s gift. Gen. 15:7, 18.

*{thy friend}* This title is three times given to Abraham; here, Isa. 41:8 and James 2:23, and that in regard of the mutual relation betwixt God and him. God did really favor Abraham as his friend, and Abraham entirely loved God as one friend loves another. Thus Christ styled Lazarus *friend*, John 11:11, and all his disciples, John 15:15.

*{for ever}* This has relation to the land which God gave to Abraham’s seed for *an everlasting inheritance*, Gen. 17:8 and 48:4. See 1 Kings 8:13. The many negative interrogatives in these two verses add much emphasis, and are equivalent to strong affirmations.

**Verse 8. ***{And they dwelt therein}* Israel dwelt in Canaan.

*{and have built thee a sanctuary therein}* By sanctuary is meant the holy temple. See 1 Chr. 9:29 and 22:19 and 28:20.

*{for thy name, saying}* See 1 Kings 5:3 and 8:16, 17.

**Verse 9. ***{If when evil cometh upon us}* 1 Kings 8:37, etc.

*{as the sword}* Or, *war*. For the sword is a special instrument in war.

*{judgment}* Judgment may here indefinitely be taken for any effect of God’s wrath. But because there is no disjunctive particle betwixt sword and judgment, some thus translate it, *the sword of judgment*, such a sword as God in just judgment does send.

*{or pestilence, or famine}* These three, war, plague, and famine, are arrows of God’s wrath, which he uses to shoot at those with whom he is offended.

*{we stand before this house}* The people came not into the very temple, but stood in the great court before it when they made any public solemn prayer to God.

*{and in thy presence}* God did after a special manner manifest his presence in the temple, especially in the most holy place, by the ark.

*{for thy name is in this house}* See 1 Kings 8:29.

*{and cry unto thee in our affliction}* Earnestly pray unto you for help. See ch. 14:11.

*{then thou wilt hear and help}* See 1 Kings 8:29, 30.

**Verse 10. ***{And now behold, the children of Ammon, and Moab, and mount Seir}* See v. 1, 2. Of Mount Seir see Deut. 2:4. Hereby are meant the inhabitants of Mount Seir, i.e., the Edomites, Gen. 32:3.

*{whom thou wouldest not let Israel invade}* See Deut. 2:5, 9, 19.

*{when they came out of the land of Egypt}* And were in the wilderness.

*{but they turned from them, and destroyed them not}* Num. 20:21.

**Verse 11. ***{Behold, I say, how they reward us}* Jehoshaphat pleads the ingratitude of his enemies, to move the Lord the rather to help him.

*{to come to cast us out of thy possession}* He shows that the enemy wronged God, by seeking to dispossess those whom he had put in possession.

*{which thou hast given us to inherit}* This shows the reason of that phrase, *thy possession*.

**Verse 12. ***{O our God}* See ch. 14:11. Before he pleaded the relation betwixt God and their fathers, v. 6, here he comes nearer to their own interest in him.

*{wilt thou not judge them?}* He refers the whole case to God, and leaves it to him to recompense their evil. To judge is to revenge or punish, Ezek. 24:14.

*{for we have no might}* This is to be taken comparatively, in regard of the mighty power of their enemies; and in reference to God, without whom indeed they had no might at all.

*{against this great company that cometh against us}* See v. 2.

*{neither know we what to do}* In regard of human means.

*{but our eyes are upon thee}* We rest only on you and expect help from you, Ps. 123:1. See 1 Kings 1:20.

**Verse 13. ***{And all Judah stood before the LORD}* i.e., Before the temple and ark. Or, *round about the king, praying unto God*. Some from every place of Judah were there present.

*{with their little ones, their wives, and their children}* This in those days was usual in common calamities, to move God to pity them the more, or rather to stir up their own spirits more earnestly to call upon him, Jonah 3:5. This was according to the law, Deut. 29:11 and 31:12; Joel 2:16.

**Verse 14. ***{Then upon Jahaziel the son of Zechariah, etc.}* The parentage of Jahaziel is distinctly set forth by sundry generations, both for honor to him and his predecessors, and also for distinction from another Jahaziel, 1 Chr. 23:19.

*{a Levite of the sons of Asaph}* See 1 Chr. 15:19 and 16:5.

*{came the Spirit of the LORD}* On a sudden he was endued with an extraordinary gift of prophecy. See ch. 15:1; Num. 11:25, 26.

*{in the midst of the congregation}* There he stood, that he might be the better heard of all.

**Verse 15. ***{And he said, Hearken ye all Judah, and ye inhabitants of Jerusalem, and thou king Jehoshaphat}* See ch. 15:2.

*{Thus saith the LORD unto you}* See 1 Kings 12:24.

*{Be not afraid, nor dismayed}* See 1 Chr. 22:13 and 28:20.

*{by reason of this great multitude}* Many and few are all one to God, ch. 14:11.

*{for the battle is not yours, but God’s}* This is spoken in regard of God’s undertaking it. God would not so much as use them for instruments in fighting that battle.

**Verse 16. ***{To morrow go ye down against them}* God would have them thus to do, both to try their faith and courage, and also to make them eyewitnesses of that which he intended to do.

*{behold, they come up by the cliff of Ziz}* Heb. *ascent of Ziz*. This was a place where they might well see all their enemies.

*{and ye shall find them at the end of the brook}* Or, *valley*.

*{before the wilderness of Jeruel}* Or, *in the front*, or *entrance*. These places were well known to Jehoshaphat and the rest of the Israelites.

**Verse 17. ***{Ye shall not need to fight in this battle}* For they were all slain one of another, before Jehoshaphat with his army could come at them. See v. 22, 23, 24.

*{set yourselves}* Compose yourselves quietly to expect the deliverance which God will provide for you.

*{stand ye still}* Neither fly upon your enemies, nor flee from them.

*{and see the salvation of the LORD with you}* Well mark that kind of deliverance which the Lord will give you. See Exod. 14:13.

*{O Judah and Jerusalem}* He means the inhabitants of those places.

*{fear not, nor be dismayed}* See v. 15. They were prone to fear; therefore he does again and again inculcate this dehortation.

*{to morrow go out against them}* He would have them rest quiet that night, and expect the time of God’s deliverance.

*{for the LORD will be with you}* See ch. 15:2.

**Verse 18. ***{And Jehoshaphat bowed his head with his face to the ground}* See ch. 7:3; 1 Chr. 29:20.

*{and all Judah, and the inhabitants of Jerusalem}* See v. 15.

*{fell before the LORD}* Prostrated themselves with their faces towards the most holy place, where the ark was.

*{worshipping the LORD}* See 1 Chr. 29:20.

**Verse 19. ***{And the Levites of the children of the Kohathites}* These descended from Kohath by Korah, and were singers, 1 Chr. 6:33.

*{and of the children of the Korhites}* These came from Kore, and were porters in the temple, 1 Chr. 26:1, etc. There were also of these that could sing.

*{stood up}* To testify their reverence, to stir up their spirits, and to be heard the better.

*{to praise}* This was the end of their singing. See 1 Chr. 23:5.

*{the LORD God of Israel}* See 1 Kings 8:15.

*{with a loud}* Heb. *a great*.

*{voice on high}* With a very high and loud voice. See ch. 15:14 and the title of Ps. 120.

**Verse 20. ***{And they rose early in the morning}* This shows their faith in God’s promise, v. 16, and their earnest desire to see the accomplishment thereof. For this was the morning of the morrow, v. 16.

*{and went forth into the wilderness of Tekoa}* The cliff of Ziz v. 16 was betwixt this Tekoa and Jeruel.

*{and as they went forth, Jehoshaphat stood, and said, etc.}* This good king believing the word of the Lord stirred up his whole army to do so too, that they might more cheerfully and courageously go on.

*{Believe in the LORD your God}* Faith is an especial means of obtaining God’s promises, Isa. 7:9; Heb. 6:12.

*{so shall you be established}* By faith the mind is settled, and kept from fear and doubting, Isa. 28:16.

*{believe his prophets}* Give credit to that word which they deliver from the Lord. Believing in relation to God implies trust and confidence in him; in relation to his ministers, a giving credit to what they deliver.

*{so shall you prosper}* You shall find the word of God true, and shall have good success in your going forth. See ch. 13:12 and 1 Chr. 22:11, 13.

**Verse 21. ***{And when he had consulted with the people}* With the army then present, especially with the commanders. See 1 Chr. 13:1.

*{he appointed singers unto the LORD}* Levites, who should be ready by singing to advance the glory of the Lord.

*{and that should praise}* Heb. *praisers*.

*{the beauty of holiness}* By beauty is meant majesty, or glory: beauty of holiness is most holy majesty. This transcendent description of God, or of the ark, did the more quicken up their spirits in the duty they performed. See Ps. 29:2 and 26:9.

*{as they went out before the army}* They believed the truth of what God had promised as steadfastly as if they had seen it accomplished, therefore they triumphed therein before they saw the issue.

*{and to say, Praise the LORD, for his mercy endureth for ever}* This is the beginning of the 136 Psalm, and the close of every verse in it. And that Psalm was sung on all occasions of public rejoicing. See ch. 7:3 and 1 Chr. 16:34.

**Verse 22. ***{And when they began}* Heb. *in the time that they, etc*. This circumstance is noted to show that God by his providence ordered that which fell out, and accepted that duty which they so cheerfully performed.

*{to sing and praise}* Heb. *in singing and praise*, or, *by singing to praise*.

*{the LORD set ambushments}* See ch. 13:13. It is probable that some among the enemies being incensed against others, did secretly lie in wait, and took their advantage to rise up and destroy the army on a sudden; whereupon there was a confusion amongst them, so as they fought one against another, and destroyed one another, as is set down v. 23. Some take this metaphorically, of God’s stirring up their minds one against another, as if an ambushment had been laid on a sudden to rise upon them. Others understand it of angels, who came upon them unexpectedly, and slew them; and then one nation of these enemies supposing one of the other nations had slain them, fell upon that nation: and so they destroyed one another.

*{against the children of Ammon, Moab, and mount Seir}* See v. 10.

*{which were come against Judah}* Verse 1 it is said that *they came against Jehoshaphat*; but this place shows that their quarrel was not a personal quarrel against Jehoshaphat alone, but a common public quarrel, against Jehoshaphat and his whole kingdom. See v. 1.

*{and they were smitten}* Or, *they smote one another*. They destroyed one another. See v. 23; Judg. 7:22; 1 Sam. 14:20. Of smiting in this sense see 2 Kings 9:7, 27.

**Verse 23. ***{For the children of Ammon and Moab}* Ammon and Moab, the progenitors of these nations, were brethren, both children of Lot, Gen. 19:37, 38.

*{stood up against the inhabitants of mount Seir}* These descended from Esau, Gen. 32:3. See v. 19.

*{utterly to slay and destroy them}* God put such a spirit into them as made them executioners of his vengeance to the uttermost.

*{and when they had made an end of the inhabitants of Seir}* This implies that that part of the army was utterly destroyed.

*{every one helped to destroy another}* Heb. *for the destruction of another*. They were carried with such a spirit of rage and fury that no man spared his neighbor, but each one destroyed him that was next him.

**Verse 24. ***{And when Judah came toward the watch tower}* This was on the cliff of Ziz, v. 16.

*{in the wilderness}* Of Jeruel, v. 16.

*{they looked unto the multitude}* The great formidable army before mentioned is here meant. See v. 2.

*{and behold, they were dead bodies fallen to the earth, and none escaped}* Heb. *there was not an escaping*. They were all slain before they could think of flying.

**Verse 25. ***{And when Jehoshaphat and his people came to take away the spoil of them}* It is usual with armies for the conqueror to spoil the slain.

*{they found among them in abundance}* See 1 Chr. 18:7, 11 and 20:2.

*{both riches with the dead bodies, and precious jewels}* As rings on their fingers, chains about their necks, jewels in their ears, or in some other place about them. Heb. *things of desire*. Such things as were much desired and esteemed, Prov. 3:15.

*{which they stripped off for themselves, more than they could carry away}* For there were many more of the enemies than of the Israelites. Besides, they brought much wealth on their beasts, and in carts or wagons.

*{and they were three days in gathering of the spoil, it was so much}* Thus God did not only free them from their enemies, but exceedingly enriched them by their enemies’ spoil.

**Verse 26. ***{And on the fourth day they assembled themselves in the valley of Berachah}* That is, blessing. This was in the wilderness of Jeruel, v. 16.

*{for there they blessed the LORD: therefore the name of the same place was called the valley of Berachah}* This is thought to be that place which is called *the valley of Jehoshaphat*, Joel 3:2, 12. By giving this name to the place the memorial of that wonderful great deliverance was continued.

*{unto this day}* See 1 Kings 9:13 and 12:19.

**Verse 27. ***{Then they returned every man of Judah and Jerusalem}* All that went to that valley.

*{and Jehoshaphat in the forefront of them}* Heb. *head of them*. Jehoshaphat herein did as David did at the removing of the ark, 2 Sam. 6:14, 15.

*{to go again to Jerusalem with joy}* At Jerusalem they made their prayer, v. 5, and in Jerusalem they would render their praise.

*{for the LORD had made them to rejoice over their enemies}* They ascribe their success to God. See Neh. 12:43.

**Verse 28. ***{And they came to Jerusalem with psalteries, and harps, and trumpets}* Of these musical instruments see 1 Chr. 13:8.

*{unto the house of the LORD}* That there they may offer up the more solemn praise and sacrifices.

**Verse 29. ***{And the fear of God was on all the kingdoms, etc.}* See ch. 14:14.

*{when they had heard that the LORD fought against the enemies of Israel}* Report of God’s mighty works makes all sorts of people far and near to fear, Josh. 2:10, 11.

**Verse 30. ***{So the realm of Jehoshaphat was quiet}* See ch. 14:1.

*{for his God gave him rest round about}* The Lord being in an especial manner the God of Jehoshaphat, who was his faithful servant, made all nations round about him forbear all opposition against him; in which respect he had rest and freedom from war.

**Verse 31. ***{And Jehoshaphat reigned, etc.}* See 1 Kings 22:41, 42.

**Verse 32. ***{And he walked in the way of Asa, etc.}* Asa’s first course of life is here meant. See ch. 16:11 and 17:3 and 1 Kings 22:43.

**Verse 33. ***{Howbeit the high places were not taken away}* See 1 Kings 22:43.

*{for as yet the people had not prepared their hearts, etc.}* Some outward reformation they had yielded unto, but yet their hearts lingered after their old superstitions. See ch. 15:17; 1 Chr. 29:18.

**Verse 34. ***{Now the rest of the acts of Jehoshaphat first and last}* See ch. 16:11.

*{behold, they are written in the book}* Heb. *words*. See ch. 12:15.

*{of Jehu the son of Hanani}* See ch. 19:2.

*{who is mentioned}* Heb. *was made to ascend*, or *registered*.

*{in the book of the kings of Israel}* 1 Kings 16:1.

**Verse 35. ***{And after this}* After that great deliverance, v. 22, etc.

*{did Jehoshaphat king of Judah join himself with Ahaziah king of Israel}* See 1 Kings 22:51.

*{who did very wickedly}* Jehoshaphat’s sin was the greater because he had been reproved for the like, ch. 19:2.

**Verse 36. ***{And he joined himself with him to make ships to go to Tarshish}* It is said 1 Kings 22:49 that Jehoshaphat would not go with the ships of Ahaziah: therefore it is probable that though at first he did indeed join with Ahaziah, yet after being reproved for it, v. 37, he refused to go.

*{and they made the ships in Ezion-geber}* See 1 Kings 22:48.

**Verse 37. ***{Then Eliezer the son of Dodavah of Mareshah}* There were more of this name, as Abraham’s servant, Gen. 15:2, Moses his son, Exod. 18:4, and sundry others.

*{prophesied against Jehoshaphat, saying, Because thou hast joined thyself with Ahaziah}* We see how good men may fall again into the same fault. See ch. 19:2. Compare also Gen. 12:13 with 20:2.

*{the LORD hath broken thy works: and the ships were broken that they were not able to go to Tarshish}* God will not prosper the evil undertakings of his children. See 1 Kings 22:48.

## CHAPTER 21

**Verse 1. ***{Now Jehoshaphat slept with his fathers, and was buried, etc.}* See 1 Kings 2:10 and 22:50.

*{and Jehoram his son reigned in his stead}* An impious son succeeded a pious father. Grace is not propagated by nature.

**Verse 2. ***{And he had brethren the sons of Jehoshaphat, Azariah, etc.}* Here are six expressed by name, and it may be they were all he had living. Our English translators give the same name to the first and the fourth, but in the Hebrew there is a little difference: for the name of the fourth is *Azariahu*.

*{All these were the sons of Jehoshaphat}* This is repeated, that the unnaturalness of Jehoram v. 4 might be the more manifested.

*{king of Israel}* See ch. 12:1 and 15:17. Jehoshaphat coming from David by lineal descent had a right to the whole kingdom of Israel, but he held only a part thereof: so that here is a synecdoche. So v. 4.

**Verse 3. ***{And their father gave them great gifts}* He showed a fatherly respect towards them, in providing bountiful gifts for them, but he would not impair his eldest son’s inheritance by giving them part thereof.

*{of silver and of gold, and of precious things}* Under precious things are comprised jewels, pearls, precious stones, silks, fine linen, with the like.

*{with fenced cities}* Jehoshaphat placed his sons in those cities, because he most confided in them for defense of the land, and that they also might be the better secured against their enemies. Rehoboam did the like, and it is said that *he did wisely* therein, ch. 11:23.

*{in Judah}* Under Judah Benjamin also is comprised. See ch. 11:1.

*{but the kingdom gave he to Jehoram}* It is probable that Jehoram carried himself fairly while his father lived: for he committed the government of the kingdom to him when he went out to war with Ahab. See 2 Kings 1:17 and 8:16.

*{because he was the firstborn}* This he did according to the law, Deut. 21:17. This privilege God of old gave the firstborn, Gen. 4:7.

**Verse 4. ***{Now when Jehoram was risen up to the kingdom of his father}* This is not to be taken of his governing as viceroy in his father’s life, but of his sitting upon the throne when his father was dead, and the kingdom settled in him alone.

*{he strengthened himself}* See ch. 12:1, 13. He sought to make himself strong (as Jeroboam did, ch. 13:7) that he might the better effect his mischievous intents.

*{and slew all his brethren with the sword}* It might be his brethren were pious men, and set themselves to maintain the true worship of God; Jehoram therefore, being a great idolater, took them out of the way, that they might be no eyesore nor obstacle unto him. See v. 13; Judg. 9:5.

*{and divers also of the princes}* The same reason might move him to slay the princes that moved him to slay his brethren.

*{of Israel}* Israel is here put for Judah, as v. 2.

**Verse 5. ***{Jehoram was thirty and two years old, etc.}* See 2 Kings 8:17.

**Verse 6. ***{And he walked in the way of the kings of Israel, etc.}* See 2 Kings 8:18.

**Verse 7. ***{Howbeit the LORD would not destroy the house of David}* Or, *Judah*, 2 Kings 8:19.

*{because of the covenant that he had made with David}* Or, *for David his servant’s sake*, 2 Kings 8:19.

*{and as he promised to give a light to him}* Heb. *lamp*, or *candle*.

*{and to his sons for ever}* See 2 Kings 8:19; 1 Kings 11:36; Ps. 132:11, etc.; 1 Chr. 17:12, 14.

**Verse 8. ***{From under the dominion of Judah}* Heb. *hand of Judah*.

*{and made themselves a king}* See 2 Kings 8:20.

**Verse 9. ***{Then Jehoram went forth with his princes}* Or, *Joram went over to Zair*, 2 Kings 8:21. By that which is here noted of his princes, it appears that some of them yielded to idolatry, and did as he would have them.

*{and all his chariots with him, etc.}* See 2 Kings 8:21. There it is added that *the people fled into their tents*.

**Verse 10. ***{So the Edomites revolted, etc.}* See 2 Kings 8:22.

*{because he had forsaken the LORD God of his fathers}* See ch. 13:10 and 15:2. This is added to show that the Lord stirred up those Edomites, as his scourge, to punish this idolatrous king. This was not the reason that moved the Edomites to revolt: it was their own desire of freedom from being subject to a foreign prince that moved them thereto: yet by his idolatrous and wicked courses and by his cruelty to his brethren and princes they might gather that the Lord would forsake him, and thereupon take advantage and occasion of revolting.

**Verse 11. ***{Moreover he made high places in the mountains of Judah}* See 1 Kings 3:2, 4. Thus this wicked king restored those impieties and idolatries which his father and grandfather had destroyed, ch. 14:15 and 19:3.

*{and caused the inhabitants of Jerusalem to commit fornication}* Spiritual fornication is here meant, i.e., idolatry, v. 13. See 2 Kings 9:22.

*{and compelled Judah thereto}* He contented not himself to draw the inhabitants of that city where himself dwelt unto idolatry, but he drew also his whole kingdom thereunto, and that by force, raising persecution against such as refused.

**Verse 12. ***{And there came a writing to him from Elijah the prophet, saying}* Without all question Elijah was before this translated. For while Jehoshaphat was living, inquiry being made about a prophet of the Lord in Israel, answer was returned that there was *Elisha who poured water on the hands of Elijah*, 2 Kings 3:11, by which it appears that Elisha was then famous for a prophet; but this was not till after Elijah was taken away, 2 Kings 2:10. Hereupon a great doubt arises, How a writing should come from Elijah, who was now in heaven, unto Jehoram, who was on earth. Some rather cut than untie the knot, denying that this was that Elijah whose story is recorded 1 Kings 17:1, etc., and saying that another man might bear this name; or that Elisha might be called Elijah as John the Baptist was, Matt. 17:12, 13, because the spirit of Elijah was in him, Luke 1:17. But a more ready and probable resolution of the doubt is this, That the true and famous Elijah had written that which is here set down before his rapture, even while he was on earth, and left it behind him to be delivered to Jehoram after he should have committed the forementioned abominations. We read 1 Kings 13:2 that a man of God prophesied of Josiah by name long before his birth. So did Isaiah of Cyrus, Isa. 45:1. The like might Elijah do of Jehoram. This might the rather be done, because this impious king would not endure a living prophet to declare the truth unto him; and that he might be the more convinced and confounded of and for his wickedness, when he should see a writing brought to him from one that was then in heaven.

*{Thus saith the LORD God of David thy father}* This description of God is a great aggravation of his sin and his ingratitude against him.

*{Because thou hast not walked in the ways of Jehoshaphat thy father}* See ch. 20:32.

*{nor in the ways of Asa king of Judah}* See ch. 14:2. To degenerate from the good pattern of holy progenitors is a great aggravation.

**Verse 13. ***{But hast walked in the way of the kings of Israel}* All the kings of Israel from the revolt were impious and idolatrous.

*{and hast made Judah and the inhabitants of Jerusalem to go a whoring}* See v. 11.

*{like to the whoredoms of the house of Ahab}* Ahab’s idolatries were more and greater than his predecessors, 1 Kings 16:31, 33.

*{and also hast slain thy brethren of thy father’s house}* V. 4. Cruelty and impiety much aggravate each other.

**Verse 14. ***{Behold, with a great plague}* Heb. *stroke*. See 1 Kings 8:37.

*{will the LORD smite thy people}* He himself also was smitten, v. 18, so as it is here to be understood, *q.d.*, *The Lord will smite thee and thy people*, etc. But the people and the other that follow are here named to aggravate the judgment, which he brought not upon himself only but upon them also. The people must needs be smitten when an enemy entered into the land, v. 17.

*{and thy children, and thy wives}* These were smitten in that they were carried away by the enemy, v. 17.

*{and all thy goods}* The greater part of his substance is here meant.

**Verse 15. ***{And thou shalt have great sickness, etc.}* See v. 19.

*{by reason of the sickness day by day}* It seems he had no intermission of pain, but day after day was tormented therewith.

**Verse 16. ***{Moreover the LORD stirred up against Jehoram}* The Lord has the spirits even of enemies in his power, to order them as he will.

*{the spirit of the Philistines and of the Arabians}* These had been tributaries to the king of Judah, ch. 17:11, but now they take advantage to subdue their lord and free themselves.

*{that were near the Ethiopians}* Heb. *Cushites*. The Arabians that bordered upon the Ethiopians assisted them in their war against Asa, and thereupon were smitten and spoiled by the men of Judah, ch. 14:15. And now they take their advantage to execute revenge.

**Verse 17. ***{And they came up into Judah and brake into it}* That which follows shows that they ran through the land so far as to enter into the royal city and plunder it.

*{and carried away}* Heb. *carried captives*.

*{all the substance that was found in the king’s house}* The king’s house was in Jerusalem: so that the enemy entered the chief city.

*{and his sons also}* Here we see God’s just revenge: Jehoram slew his father’s sons v. 4, and the enemy slew his sons. See ch. 22:1.

*{and his wives}* Kings then used to have many wives. All Jehoram’s were not taken from him; for Athaliah remained to usurp the crown, ch. 22:10.

*{so that there was never a son left him save Jehoahaz}* Or, *Ahaziah*, ch. 22:1, or, Azariah, ch. 22:6. So as he had three names.

*{the youngest of his sons}* No reason is rendered why this should be spared. It may be the enemy could not find him. But herein appeared the divine providence about the accomplishment of his promise to David. See v. 7.

**Verse 18. ***{And after all this}* Impenitents bring upon themselves one judgment after another, till they be even clean destroyed. See Lev. 26:18, 21, 24, 28.

*{the LORD smote him in his bowels}* These being within a man are oft the subject of the greater torment.

*{with an incurable disease}* His malady was intolerable for the present, and incurable for the future.

**Verse 19. ***{And it came to pass that in process of time, after the end of two years}* This was a long time for a man to lie under a torturing malady.

*{his bowels fell out by reason of his sickness}* The disease was very great, that it forced his bowels out of him.

*{so he died of sore diseases}* He had no cure for his body, nor yet for his soul; but died under God’s heavy hand and great indignation.

*{And his people made no burning for him, like the burning of his fathers}* See ch. 16:14. The great sins he committed against God, the wrongs he did to man, and the judgments he brought upon the land turned the hearts of his subjects from him; so that though he had been a king, yet little respect was showed him when he was dead.

**Verse 20. ***{Thirty and two years old, etc.}* See v. 5.

*{and departed without being desired}* Heb. *without desire*. He lived and died so miserable and ignoble, as living he took no content in himself, nor in life and death did any other care for him.

*{howbeit they buried him in the city of David, etc.}* See 1 Kings 2:10. Because he was the son of David, and a king, they buried him in the city of David; but to show that he deserved neither the honor of a king nor of a son of David, they would not lay his corpse in that place where his predecessors lay. And this is added as an evidence of his subjects’ disesteem of him.

## CHAPTER 22

**Verse 1. ***{And the inhabitants of Jerusalem made Ahaziah his youngest son king in his stead}* It seems Jehoram being for two years together miserably perplexed with a tormenting disease, took little care about his successor: therefore the inhabitants of Jerusalem took that care upon them, it being the chief city and strongest hold of the kingdom, and the rest of the land having been extremely spoiled by the enemy, ch. 21:17. By the inhabitants of Jerusalem the Sanhedrin or great council of the kingdom may be meant. Yet others also gave their consent.

*{for the band of men that came with the Arabians to the camp had slain all the eldest}* Chap. 21:17. This was the reason why they made the youngest son king; he was the only son of Jehoram then living.

*{So Ahaziah the son of Jehoram king of Judah reigned}* 2 Kings 8:24. In this chapter is set down the short reign and miserable end of Ahaziah, and the evil consequence thereof; namely, Athaliah, a woman and a stranger, to be an usurper of the crown, v. 10.

**Verse 2. ***{Forty and two years old was Ahaziah when he began to reign}* This place seems to contradict two others, one whereof says that Jehoram, the father of this Ahaziah, was *thirty and two years old when he began to reign*, and that *he reigned eight years*, ch. 21:5, the other, that this very Ahaziah was *two and twenty years old when he began to reign*, 2 Kings 8:26. By the former, Ahaziah’s father was but forty years old when he died; how then could his son be two and forty? Can a son be elder than his father at the same time? By the latter, one and the same man should be two and twenty and two and forty years old at once. To reconcile these places some say that Jehoram being two and thirty years old when he began to reign, lived eight and twenty after that; of which, Jehoram himself reigned but eight, and Ahaziah governed the kingdom twenty years in his father’s lifetime: so as they make Ahaziah two and twenty years old when he began to govern as viceroy, and two and forty when he began to reign alone after his father’s death. But besides that there is no ground of Scripture for this reconciliation, it crosses all the circumstances of the history; in particular, it interrupts the series of the genealogy of kings, and especially the chronology of the reigns of the kings of Judah and of Israel. There is therefore a fairer way of solving the forementioned doubts; namely, That the two and twenty years, 2 Kings 8:26, be taken properly of Ahaziah’s age at that time, but the two and forty years here of the continuance of Omri’s pedigree, from whom Ahaziah descended by his mother Athaliah, 2 Kings 8:18, 26. For Omri, great-grandfather to Ahaziah, reigned six years as sole king, 1 Kings 16:23, Ahab, his grandfather, two and twenty, 1 Kings 16:29, Ahaziah his uncle, two, 1 Kings 22:51, Joram, another uncle, twelve, 2 Kings 3:1. These make up just forty-two years. We have a like computation from the beginning of the division of the two kingdoms, ch. 15:19 and 16:1. And this may the better be borne, because the Hebrew thus expresses it, *A son of forty and two years was Ahaziah in his reigning*. I will not deny but that Hebraism is oft used of a king’s age when he began to reign, but it may also be taken of the continuance of a stock. To make this interpretation the more clear, some putting the last clause of this verse in the beginning (which is called a trajection) thus read it with part of the former verse, *So Ahaziah the son of Jehoram king of Judah reigned: his mother’s name also was Athaliah the daughter of Omri. He was forty and two years old when Ahaziah began to reign, and he reigned one year in Jerusalem*, etc. See 1 Kings 8:26.

**Verse 3. ***{He also walked in the ways of the house of Ahab}* See 2 Kings 8:27. Also has reference to Jehoram; Ahaziah as well as Jehoram, did so. See ch. 21:6.

*{for his mother}* She was grandchild to Omri, and daughter of Jezebel a most wicked woman. See 1 Kings 16:31 and 18:4 and 19:2 and 21:5, etc. and 2 Kings 9:22. And this daughter was no better than her mother, if not worse, v. 10.

*{was his counsellor to do wickedly}* Mothers’ counsel is very prevalent with children, either for good or evil. See 1 Kings 15:10.

**Verse 4. ***{wherefore he did evil in the sight of the LORD, like the house of Ahab}* See 2 Kings 8:27.

*{for they were his counsellors}* It seems Athaliah brought divers of Ahab’s stock to Jerusalem with her, where they corrupted the king her son.

*{after the death of his father, to his destruction}* They persuaded him after his father’s death to join with Joram the son of Ahab in war against Hazael, 2 Kings 8:28, and this was the occasion of his death, 2 Kings 9:27.

**Verse 5. ***{He walked also after their counsel}* He did not only hear their counsel, but also follow it.

*{and went with Jehoram the son of Ahab, etc.}* See 2 Kings 8:28.

*{And the Syrians smote Joram}* Or, *wounded*, 2 Kings 8:28. The Hebrew is the same in both places.

**Verse 6. ***{And he returned to be healed, etc.}* See 2 Kings 8:29.

*{because of the wounds which were given him}* Heb. *wherewith they wounded him*.

*{And Azariah}* Called *Ahaziah*, v. 1, and *Jehoahaz*, ch. 21:17.

**Verse 7. ***{And the destruction}* Heb. *treading down*. Both himself and his whole kingdom was indeed trodden down.

*{of Ahaziah was of God}* Ordered by divine providence. See 1 Kings 12:15.

*{by coming to Joram}* His going to Joram was the occasion of his destruction, 2 Kings 9:21, etc. Had he tarried at Jerusalem, Jehu would not have meddled with him.

*{For when he was come, he went out with Jehoram against Jehu}* This shows how his coming to Joram was his destruction.

*{the son of Nimshi}* See 2 Kings 9:2.

*{whom the LORD hath anointed to cut off, etc.}* See 2 Kings 9:6, etc.

**Verse 8. ***{And it came to pass that when Jehu was executing judgment upon the house of Ahab}* 2 Kings 9:22, etc.

*{and found the princes of Judah}* i.e., Such as accompanied Ahaziah when he went to Joram, 2 Kings 8:28.

*{and the sons of the brethren of Ahaziah}* 2 Kings 10:13, etc.

*{that ministered to Ahaziah}* They were his subjects, and attended upon him.

*{he slew them}* Caused them to be slain, 2 Kings 10:14.

**Verse 9. ***{And he sought Ahaziah}* For when he saw Joram slain by Jehu, he fled, 2 Kings 9:27.

*{and they caught him}* i.e., Jehu’s soldiers.

*{for he was hid in Samaria}* See 2 Kings 9:27.

*{and brought him to Jehu}* To see what judgment he would pronounce against him.

*{and when they had slain him}* At Jehu’s command, 2 Kings 9:27.

*{they buried him}* They who slew Ahaziah gave his servants leave to carry his dead corpse away and to bury it.

*{because (said they) he is the son of Jehoshaphat, who sought the LORD with all his heart}* Piety, sincerity, and integrity (which are here noted to have been in Jehoshaphat) are reverenced and had in esteem even by those that have not their own hearts possessed therewith. But this is also to be taken as a special motion of the Spirit in them, to testify God’s respect to his servant Jehoshaphat.

*{So the house of Ahaziah had no power to keep still the kingdom}* The king himself, his kindred and princes being destroyed.

**Verse 10. ***{But when Athaliah, etc.}* See 2 Kings 11:1.

*{of the house of Judah}* The ground of her cruelty was that she might make way to the crown.

**Verse 11, 12. ***{But Jehoshabeath, etc.}* See 2 Kings 11:2, 3.

## CHAPTER 23

**Verse 1. ***{And in the seventh year}* This has relation to the six years ch. 22:12. Here is set down the rescuing of this kingdom from Athaliah the usurper, to the house of David.

*{Jehoiada strengthened himself}* Athaliah usurping the crown had got so many on her side, and so destroyed and weakened those that stood for the house of David, as that Jehoiada (of whom see 2 Kings 11:4) durst not oppose himself openly and publicly: yet from time to time he got as many as he could to take part with him, and laid what weapons and ammunition he could within the courts and chambers of the house of God, and so strengthened himself.

*{and took the captains of hundreds}* Or, *sent and fet the rulers over hundreds, with the captains and the guard*, 2 Kings 11:4. The same word is here translated *captains*, and there *rulers*.

*{Azariah, etc.}* The parentage of these captains here named declares that they were Levites.

*{into covenant with him}* 2 Kings 11:4 it is added, that *he brought them to him into the house of the Lord*, and also, that *he took an oath of them in the house of the Lord, and shewed them the king’s son*.

**Verse 2. ***{And they went about in Judah}* Wheresoever any Levites had their abode.

*{and gathered the Levites out of all the cities of Judah}* Not only those whose course it was at that time to serve in the house of the Lord, but others also.

*{and the chief of the fathers of Israel}* These might be other than Levites, namely, governors in the commonwealth of Judah and Benjamin: for so is Israel here to be taken, as ch. 12:1 and 21:2.

*{and they came to Jerusalem}* That there they might combine together, and aid Jehoiada in that great work which he intended.

**Verse 3. ***{And all the congregation made a covenant with the king}* This was a mutual stipulation betwixt the king and them, That the king should maintain the true worship of God, the peace of the kingdom, and privileges of the subjects; and that the people should maintain the king, and yield unto him his due.

*{in the house of God}* The courts wherein the priests and Levites offered sacrifices and did other services, and wherein the people stood to behold and hear them, are oft styled *the house of God*; so in this place.

*{and he said unto them, Behold, the king’s son shall reign}* This he speaks in regard of the right the king had to the crown, being the former king’s son, ch. 22:11, and of God’s promise to David and his posterity forever, 1 Chr. 17:14, and of his confidence that God would prosper their endeavors.

*{as the LORD hath said of the sons of David}* 2 Sam. 17:12; 1 Kings 2:4 and 9:5; ch. 6:16 and 7:18.

**Verse 4. ***{This is the thing that ye shall do, a third part, etc.}* See 2 Kings 11:5.

*{of the priests and of the Levites}* This has reference to the third part, implying that it was taken out of such and such priests and Levites.

*{shall be porters of the doors}* Heb. *thresholds*. See 2 Kings 12:9. Some of them being by their ordinary office porters of the doors of the house of God, had at this time a special charge for the gate behind the guard, 2 Kings 11:6.

**Verse 5. ***{And a third part at the king’s house}* i.e., The gate that opened to the king’s house: and they are said to be *keepers of the watch of the king’s house*, 2 Kings 11:5.

*{and a third part at the gate of the foundation}* This was also called *the gate of Sur*, 2 Kings 11:6.

*{and all the people shall be in the courts of the house of the LORD}* The great court, ch. 4:9, where the people stood, was divided into sundry parts, and thereupon the plural number, courts, is here used. See 1 Kings 6:36.

**Verse 6. ***{But let none come into the house of the LORD}* i.e., The court of the priests, ch. 4:9. See v. 3.

*{save the priests}* For they were to offer sacrifices there.

*{and they that minister of the Levites}* Such Levites as were attendant upon priests, and therein were distinguished from singers and porters, 1 Chr. 24:20 and 25:1.

*{they shall go in, for they are holy}* Heb. *holiness*. They were not merely holy by profession, as all God’s people were, but also by special function, being set apart to holy services.

*{but all the people shall keep the watch of the LORD}* They shall stand in the great court, and there abide, without thrusting themselves into the priests’ courts; as the Lord of old enjoined. Or, *they shall keep all but priests and Levites from entering into the priests’ courts*. Or, *they shall observe that ordinance which God has appointed to them in their own court*.

**Verse 7. ***{And the Levites shall compass, etc.}* See 2 Kings 11:8.

*{and whosoever else cometh into the house he shall be put to death, etc.}* Or, *he that cometh within the ranges let him be slain*, 2 Kings 11:8.

**Verse 8. ***{So the Levites and all Judah}* Or, *the captains over hundreds*, 2 Kings 11:9. These captains were Levites. See v. 1. By Judah others of the people may be meant. They are called *all the people*, v. 6.

*{did according to all things, etc.}* See 2 Kings 11:9.
