*{for Jehoiada the priest dismissed not the courses}* Priests and Levites had their courses to serve, which when they had executed, they were usually permitted to go to their houses; but at this time they were not permitted so to do.

**Verse 9. ***{Moreover, Jehoiada the priest delivered to the captains of hundreds spears, and bucklers, and shields}* These three are put for all kind of warlike instruments: for without question he gave them swords also.

*{that had been king David’s}* See 2 Kings 11:10.

*{which were in the house of God}* Or, *in the temple*, 2 Kings 11:10. See v. 3.

**Verse 10. ***{And he set all the people}* By all the people are here meant that multitude of Levites which were now brought together, and had weapons given them.

*{every man having his weapon in his hand}* Prepared and ready to defend the king, and oppose all that should hinder his coronation.

*{from the right side}* Heb. *shoulder*. For as a man’s body has two shoulders, so an edifice has two sides. This was the north side.

*{of the temple}* Heb. *house*.

*{to the left side of the temple}* That was the south side: for they went into the temple at the east.

*{along by the altar and the temple}* See 2 Kings 11:11.

*{by the king round about}* The king standing at the entrance into the priests’ court, the priests and Levites might guard him in that court, and others of the people in the great court.

**Verse 11. ***{Then they brought out the king’s son, etc.}* See 2 Kings 11:12.

*{and Jehoiada and his sons anointed him}* Jehoiada himself might pour oil upon his head, and his sons stand close by him, and give him the vessel wherein the oil was, and also take it away again. It is said that Zadok and Nathan anointed Solomon, 1 Kings 1:34. There see more of anointing kings. It is very probable that Zechariah the son of Jehoiada was one of those that are here said to anoint Joash; which does much aggravate the ingratitude of Joash in stoning him, ch. 24:21, 22.

*{and said, God save the king}* Heb. *Let the king live*. See 1 Kings 1:39. Not only the priests who anointed Joash, but also all the people present made that acclamation.

**Verse 12. ***{Now when Athaliah heard the noise of the people}* Or, *the noise of the guard, and of the people*, 2 Kings 11:13.

*{running}* It being an unexpected and very joyful sight, they ran up and down rejoicing thereat.

*{and praising the king}* They much rejoiced that one of the house of David was anointed king, and praised God for the same.

*{she came to the people into the house of the LORD}* Or, *into the temple of the Lord*, 2 Kings 11:13.

**Verse 13. ***{And she looked, and behold, the king stood at his pillar}* It is called *his pillar* because it was a pillar where kings used to stand. And therefore it is added 2 Kings 11:14, *as the manner was*. See on 1 Kings 6:36.

*{at the entering in}* i.e., The entering into the priests’ court.

*{and the princes and the trumpets by the king, etc.}* See 2 Kings 11:14.

*{and all the people of the land rejoiced, and sounded with trumpets}* This latter act of sounding with trumpets, is to be applied to the priests, whose office it was to sound therewith.

*{also the singers with instruments of musick}* See 1 Chr. 25:1, etc.

*{and such as taught to sing praise}* Music masters, 1 Chr. 25:1, etc.

*{Then Athaliah rent her clothes, and said, Treason, treason}* Heb. *conspiracy, conspiracy*. See 2 Kings 11:14.

**Verse 14. ***{Brought out}* Or, *commanded*, 2 Kings 11:15.

*{that were set over the host, etc.}* *the officers of the host*, 2 Kings 11:15.

**Verse 15. ***{So they laid hands on her, etc.}* See 2 Kings 11:16.

**Verse 16. ***{And Jehoiada made a covenant between him, etc.}* Or, *between the Lord and the king*, 2 Kings 11:17.

**Verse 17. ***{Then all the people went to the house of Baal, etc.}* See 2 Kings 11:18.

**Verse 18. ***{Also Jehoiada appointed the officers of the house of the LORD}* Or, *the priest appointed officers over the house of the Lord*, 2 Kings 11:18.

*{by the hand of the priests the Levites}* These he committed to the priests the Levites.

*{whom David had distributed in the house of the LORD}* See 1 Chr. 23 and 24 and 25 and 26.

*{to offer the burnt offerings of the LORD}* Namely, in their several courses.

*{as it is written in the law of Moses}* Num. 28:2, etc.

*{with rejoicing and with singing}* Singing was both an evidence of their rejoicing, and also a means of quickening their spirits the more.

*{as it was ordained by David}* Heb. *by the hands of David*. See 1 Kings 8:53, 56.

**V. 19. ***{And he set the Porters at the gates of the house of the LORD}* Of these porters and their courses see 1 Chron. 26:1, etc.

*{that none which was unclean in any thing should enter in}* Namely, into the great court, among the people that were assembled to worship God. This was one principal end of the porters’ function.

**V. 20. ***{And he took the captains of hundreds}* Such as are mentioned v. 2.

*{and the nobles}* Such as are called *princes* v. 13.

*{and the governors of the people}* Of these some were of cities and towns; others of tribes.

*{and all the people of the land}* By the enumeration of all sorts it appears that it was a very great assembly.

*{and brought down the King from the house of the LORD}* See 2 King. 11:19.

*{and they came through the high gate to the King’s house}* Or, *by the way of the gate of the guard*, 2 King. 11:19.

*{and set the King upon the throne of the Kingdom}* Or, *he sat on the throne of the Kings*, 2 King. 11:19.

**V. 21. ***{And all the people of the land rejoiced, etc.}* 2 King. 11:20, this clause is added, *beside the Kings house*.

## CHAPTER 24

**Verse 1. ***{Joash was seven years old when he began to reign}* See 2 King. 11:21. In this chapter are two remarkable points; I. The good government of Joash while Jehoiada lived, together with the death and burial of Jehoiada, from v. 1 to v. 17. II. The apostasy of King Joash, together with the ill consequences thereof, v. 17, etc.

*{and he reigned forty years, etc.}* See 2 King. 12:1.

**V. 2. ***{And Joash did that which was right, etc.}* The ground hereof is added 2 King. 12:2. *Jehoiada the Priest instructed him*.

**V. 3. ***{And Jehoiada took for him}* This is a father’s part, Gen. 24:4 and 28:2. And Jehoiada showed himself as a father to Joash even from his infancy, ch. 22:11, 12 and 23:1, etc. King Joash also carried himself towards Jehoiada as a son to his father.

*{two wives}* An evil custom made good men yield to it. From the beginning it was unlawful to have more wives than one, Matt. 19:4.

*{and he begat sons and daughters}* This was in itself a blessing, especially to the kings of Judah, in regard of God’s promise to David, 1 Chr. 17:11.

**V. 4. ***{And it came to pass after this}* After that Joash was established in the kingdom, and had wives and children.

*{that Joash was minded to repair}* Heb. *it was in the heart of Joash to renew*.

*{the house of the LORD}* All the parts of the temple and several things therein are comprised under this word *house*; as Asa *renewed the Altar of the Lord*, ch. 15:8. The reason of the need there was to repair the house of God is rendered v. 7.

**V. 5. ***{And he gathered together the Priests and the Levites}* To these especially did the care of the house of the Lord belong.

*{and said to them, Go out unto the cities of Judah}* Under Judah Benjamin and so much of other tribes as were under the government of Joash are comprised.

*{and gather of all Israel}* Israel is here to be taken in no larger extent than Judah before was. See ch. 11:1 and 21:2.

*{money to repair the house}* By money all things needful to such a purpose might be prepared.

*{of your God}* The Lord was in special manner the God of the priests and Levites, by reason of their special attendance in his house and upon his service. This relation is here added to make them the more diligent and faithful.

*{from year to year}* From time to time as need should require. This is added, 1. because the work was great and could hardly be perfected in a year. 2. because the manner of collecting the money and providing materials would be long. 3. because the collectors were somewhat negligent. 4. because as one thing was repaired, another thing might need reparation; and that year after year.

*{and see that ye haste the matter}* This he added to show his earnest desire to have the work done, and to prevent such inconveniences as by delay might fall out.

*{howbeit the Levites hasted it not}* For *in the three and twentieth year of King Joash the Priests had not repaired the breaches of the house*, 2 King. 12:6.

**V. 6. ***{And the King called for Jehoiada the chief}* *And for the other Priests*, 2 King. 12:7.

*{and said unto him}* The king’s speech is in special directed to the high priest.

*{Why hast thou not required of the Levites}* The high priest had special command over the Levites.

*{to bring in out of Judah and out of Jerusalem}* Out of country and city.

*{the collection according to the commandment of Moses}* Ex. 30:12, 13, 14. Heb. *collection of Moses*. The other words are inserted to make up the sense. The commandment which Moses gave thereabout was from the Lord. There were two kinds of collections enjoined by Moses; one voluntary, what every one would give of his own good will; see Ex. 25:2, etc. The other imposed on every one as a ransom for his soul, Exod. 30:12, etc. Either of these, or both, may be meant. In the former collection of Moses, gold, silver, brass, blue, purple, scarlet, fine linen, and sundry other things might be contributed, but here only money, v. 5.

*{the servant of the LORD}* A title frequently given to Moses, ch. 1:3. Num. 12:7. Deut. 34:5. Josh. 1:1. See 1 King. 3:6.

*{and of the congregation of Israel}* Or, *even of the congregation of Israel*. Which Moses commanded to receive of the congregation.

*{for the tabernacle of witness}* That which Moses built was styled *the Tabernacle of witness*, Num. 17:7, 8 and 18:2, in regard of the ark and the tables of stone within it, which contained the covenant betwixt God and his people, and was a witness between them. These being in the tabernacle, it was called *the Tabernacle of witness*. The end of mentioning this here is to show that as Moses had a collection for the tabernacle, so in like case a like course was to be taken for the temple.

**V. 7. ***{For the sons of Athaliah that wicked woman had broken up the house of God}* Or, *Athaliah dealing wickedly, her sons had broken up the house of God*. Q. Athaliah had no sons but by Jehoram, and he had no sons left but his youngest, ch. 21:17. How then could the sons of Athaliah do that which is here said to be done by them? A. Her sons might *break up the house of God* before they were taken and slain by the enemy. Or *sons* may by synecdoche be put for *son*, and Ahaziah only be meant; who might break up the house of God; for he was a very wicked king, ch. 22:3, 4.

*{and also all the dedicate things of the house of the LORD}* This was their end of breaking up the house of God, that they might take away such treasures as had been dedicated to God’s service, and laid up there.

*{did they bestow upon Baalim}* Baalim may here properly be taken for that idol which Ahab worshiped, 1 King. 16:31, or indefinitely for all manner of idols, as ch. 17:3. This aggravates their impiety, in that they did not only rob God, but also bestowed that which was his upon idols.

**V. 8. ***{And at the King’s commandment they made a chest}* The king being very careful that all that was gathered for the temple should be reserved for that use, would have a chest made for that end. This was by Jehoiada’s advice, 2 King. 12:9.

*{and set it without at the gate of the house of the LORD}* In the great court, beside the altar on the north side, as they went into the priests’ court. It was here set, because so far the people might come, and no further. See 2 King. 12:9.

**V. 9. ***{And they made a proclamation}* Heb. *and they gave a voice*. By the voice of a crier or herald they caused it to be proclaimed. See 2 King. 15:22.

*{through Judah and Jerusalem}* Country and city, as v. 6.

*{to bring in to the LORD}* i.e. To the house of the Lord, for the Lord’s service.

*{the collection that Moses the servant of God laid upon Israel in the wilderness}* Where and when the tabernacle was building. See v. 6.

**V. 10. ***{And all the Princes and all the people rejoiced}* Even as they did upon a like occasion in David’s time, 1 Chr. 29:9.

*{and brought in}* Willingly and cheerfully.

*{and cast into the chest}* Or, *put in*. For the chest *had a hole in the lid of it*, 2 King. 12:9.

*{until they had made an end}* Of giving what they intended. Or, *they continued their contributions till all that needed repairing was finished*.

**V. 11. ***{Now it came to pass, that at what time the chest was brought}* Or, *at what time he brought the chest*. Namely he to whom the charge thereof was given.

*{unto the King’s office}* A place appointed by the king for taking out and telling the money.

*{by the hand of the Levites}* Who had the charge thereof.

*{and when they saw that there was much money, the King’s Scribe, and the high priest’s officer}* The high priest himself is said to do this, 2 King. 12:10.

*{came and emptied the chest}* It is added 2 King. 12:10, *and they put up in bags, and told the money that was found in the house of the Lord*.

*{and took it and carried it to its place again}* That it might be ready for more money to be put into it.

*{thus they did day by day}* Not that every day the chest was emptied, but time after time, so oft as it was full.

*{and gathered money in abundance}* This shows that the people willingly offered to the house of the Lord.

**V. 12. ***{And the king and Jehoiada}* The king was in such things counseled by Jehoiada, and did what he would have him do.

*{gave it to such as did the work of the service of the house of the LORD}* *They gave it, being told, to such as had the oversight of the house of the Lord*, 2 King. 12:11.

*{and hired Masons, and Carpenters, etc.}* i.e. Such as had the oversight of the house hired these workmen, or laid out the money to them, 2 King. 11:12. See there the difference betwixt masons and carpenters.

*{and also such as wrought iron and brass, etc.}* i.e. Smiths and braziers; for both iron and brass were used about the temple. See 1 Chron. 22:3.

**V. 13. ***{So the workmen wrought}* Every one in that which belonged unto him.

*{and the work was perfected by them}* Heb. *the healing went up upon the work*. This is a metaphor, whereby the ruins of the temple are resembled to the wounds of a body, and the repairs of those ruins to the healing of wounds.

*{and they set the house of God in his state}* Even as it was before it was broken up, v. 7.

*{and strengthened it}* So as they kept it from further decay.

**V. 14. ***{And when they had finished it}* This has reference to the workmen, and imports as much as the metaphor v. 13.

*{they brought the rest of the money, etc.}* i.e. The overseers who had the charge of receiving and disbursing the money. See v. 12.

*{whereof were made vessels for the house of the LORD}* i.e. Such as were made by Solomon, 1 King. 7:50. For Athaliah had taken away such as were in the temple before, v. 7.

*{even vessels to minister}* For such services as were done in the temple.

*{and to offer withal}* Or, *Pestils*, or *Mortars*. Some take these vessels for incense-cups, or censers.

*{and spoons}* See 1 King. 7:50.

*{and vessels of gold and silver}* The vessels before mentioned and also others were made of those metals.

*{and they offered burnt offerings in the house of the LORD}* This is synecdochically put for all the worship and service appertaining to the temple. When they had made the temple fit for service, and done all other things that were requisite, they performed such worship to God therein as was prescribed.

*{continually}* Every day, day after day.

*{all the days of Jehoiada}* After his death *they left the house of the Lord*, v. 18.

**V. 15. ***{But Jehoiada waxed old}* It was a great blessing to that kingdom that he continued till he was old.

*{and was full of days when he died}* See 1 Chron. 23:1. He was ten or eleven years old at Solomon’s death, and lived in the reign of eight kings of Judah.

*{an hundred and thirty years old was he when he died}* Heb. *a son of an hundred and thirty years*. This was an extraordinary old age for those times.

**V. 16. ***{And they buried him in the city of David among the Kings}* This was a great honor. See 1 King. 2:10.

*{because he had done good in Israel}* By Israel is here meant the kingdom of Judah, as ch. 12:1 and 21:4. He restored that kingdom to the house of David, as it is at large set down ch. 23.

*{both towards God}* In restoring his worship, v. 14, and causing his promise to be accomplished, ch. 23 and v. 3.

*{and towards his house}* Because mention is before made both of David and also of God, the relative *his* is by some referred to David, by others to God: *He did good to David’s house*, or stock, by preserving it from utter destruction; or, *he did good towards God’s house*, by causing it to be repaired, and procuring true worship to be performed therein.

**V. 17. ***{Now after the death of Jehoiada}* The death of a wise, godly and zealous governor is a great loss to a kingdom.

*{came the Princes of Judah}* Certainly these princes were hollow-hearted that could conceal themselves while Jehoiada lived, and as soon as he was dead manifested their impious mind.

*{and made obeisance to the King}* They made pretense of doing honor unto him, that so they might persuade him to idolatry.

*{then the King hearkened unto them}* Flatterers much prevail with kings. Though Joash had been well instructed, and thereby addicted to worship the Lord in the temple as he had prescribed, yet the princes by their flatteries alluring him to use his regal authority according to his own mind and lust, yea even in matters of religion, brought him to yield to their counsels.

**V. 18. ***{And they left the house of the LORD}* It seems they were weary of coming to the temple, and so desired that they might every one worship God in such places and after such manner as themselves thought meet.

*{God of their fathers}* See ch. 13:12. This relation betwixt God and their fathers much aggravated their impiety.

*{and served groves and idols}* i.e. Idols in groves. See 1 King. 16:33. Leaving the house of the Lord, they did every one in their several places worship God as they pleased. Yea also they worshiped false gods comprised under this word *idols*.

*{and wrath}* Judgments the effect of God’s wrath. See chap. 28:13.

*{came upon Judah and Jerusalem}* Upon country and city and all the inhabitants therein.

*{for this their trespass}* Sin, especially idolatry, is the cause of judgment.

**V. 19. ***{Yet he sent}* Though they deserved judgment upon their first apostasy, yet the Lord in great patience sent his ministers to declare their sin to their faces, and to foretell them of judgments hanging over their heads, thereby to bring them to repentance. So dealt he with the ten tribes, 2 King. 17:13, and with the men of Judah after this, ch. 36:15.

*{Prophets to them}* Extraordinary messengers, and not one only, but *Prophets*, many of them, time after time, one after another.

*{to bring them again unto the LORD}* This is the main end of God’s sending ministers to his people, either to bring them back from their wicked courses to the Lord, or to hold them close with the Lord.

*{and they testified against them}* They plainly declared that they had deserved judgment, and that God would bring judgment upon them if they did not repent. See 2 King. 17:13, 15. Neh. 9:26, 29.

*{but they would not give ear}* This manifested a most obstinate disposition, which is the forerunner of vengeance. See ch. 36:15, 16, 17. 2 King. 18:14, etc.

**V. 20. ***{And the Spirit of God came upon}* Heb. *clothed*. See ch. 15:1. Judg. 6:34.

*{Zechariah the son of Jehoiada the Priest}* This Zechariah was he of whom Christ speaks Matt. 23:35 and Luke 11:51. The murder there mentioned was after such a manner as the murder of this man, *between the Temple and the Altar*, implied in this phrase, *In the court of the house of the Lord*, v. 21. If it be objected that that Zachary was *the son of Barachias*, I answer, that the father of this man might have two names, Jehoiada, and Barachias; as sundry in Scripture had two, yea three names, ch. 21:17. 1 Chr. 3:15, yea priests also, 1 Chron. 18:16. Or Barachias might be grandfather to this Zechariah, and called his *father*, as grandfathers used to be. Thus Reuel, grandfather to Moses’ wife, was called her *father*, Exod. 2:18. Num. 10:29, and Iddo the grandfather of Zechariah, who prophesied after the captivity, Zech. 1:1, is made the father of Zechariah, Ezra 5:1. Or this name Barachias might be given to Jehoiada for honor’s sake, as Jedidiah was to Solomon, 2 Sam. 12:25. For Barachiah signifies *Blessed of the Lord*, and Jehoiada might well have that title; for he was wonderfully blessed of the Lord. This being so, Christ himself gives evidence of the blood of this man lying upon the Jews generation after generation.

*{which stood above the people}* In a place higher than they, that he might be the better heard.

*{and said unto them, Thus saith God}* He premises this preface to make his message the more regarded; and if not regarded, to aggravate their sin the more. See 1 King. 12:24.

*{Why transgress ye the commandments of the LORD}* God’s *commandments* declare what he would have men observe. *To transgress them* is to oppose the revealed will of God, which is plain rebellion. See ch. 12:2. 1 King. 8:50. 2 King. 18:12. 1 Chr. 5:25.

*{that ye cannot prosper}* Rebellion against God keeps men from all blessing and good success. See ch. 13:12. 1 Chron. 22:11.

*{because ye have forsaken the LORD}* See 1 King. 11:33. Ch. 12:5 and 15:2.

*{he hath also forsaken you}* They renounced God from being their Lord, and served false gods; therefore God renounced them from being his people, and gave them over into the hand of their enemies. God deals with men according to their dealing with him, 2 Sam. 2:30.

**V. 21. ***{And they}* The idolatrous princes, v. 17, and the people that were like them.

*{conspired against him}* See 1 King. 15:27 and 16:9. They consulted together how they might destroy him, as Mark 3:6.

*{and stoned him with stones}* This kind of death they put such as they accounted heinous malefactors unto, as Num. 15:36. John 7:25. 1 King. 21:13.

*{at the commandment of the King}* It is probable that they who conspired and consulted together went to the king to know his mind; and that he being asked, commanded that Zechariah should be stoned.

*{in the court of the house of the LORD}* Zechariah was in the priests’ court; the king, princes and people, in the great court. See 1 King. 6:36. The altar of burnt-offerings being at the entrance of the priests’ court, and Zechariah standing by it, they might there stone him; and in that respect be said *to slay him between the Temple and the Altar*, Matt. 23:35.

**V. 22. ***{Thus Joash the King remembered not the kindness which Jehoiada his father had done to him}* This act of the king’s was more than monstrous ingratitude.

*{but slew his son}* Jehoiada had preserved Joash from death, nourished him in the Lord’s house till he set him upon the throne, instructed him, and been a means of many blessings unto him; and yet he unjustly and cruelly slew his son, only for giving him good counsel, and that in the house of God.

*{and when he died, he said}* That which he said at the time of his death was by special instinct: nor may we think that so holy a man would end his days with a revengeful imprecation in his mouth. For we read the contrary not only of Christ, Luke 23:34, but also of Stephen, Acts 7:60.

*{The LORD look upon it, and require it}* i.e. Take special notice of this cruel act, and require just vengeance of the blood so unjustly shed on the shedders thereof. See Gen. 9:5. Ps. 9:12. Some translate it in the future tense, as a prophecy or prediction, *The Lord will look upon it, and require it*.

**V. 23. ***{And it came to pass}* From hence to v. 27 is set down God’s sore vengeance upon the king and his people for the foresaid sin.

*{at the end of the year}* Heb. *in the revolution of the year*. When kings used to go out to battle, 1 King. 20:22. This may have reference either to the death of Zechariah, that this judgment fell out in the same year; or to a former invasion which Hazael made into the land of Judah, 2 King. 12:17.

*{that the host of Syria came up against him, and they came to Judah and Jerusalem}* Through the whole land of Judah, even to Jerusalem, the chief city, where the king’s palace was. This shows that this was another invasion than that, 2 King. 12:17, 18. For then Hazael only *set his face to go to Jerusalem*, and Joash by a great bribe moved him to return from him.

*{and destroyed all the Princes of the people from among the people}* These princes had first seduced the king, and drawn him to sin against his God; therefore the judgment first comes on them. The enemies were herein directed by God.

*{and sent all the spoil of them}* *Them* has reference to the king and princes. The enemies spoiled them of their goods.

*{unto the king of Damascus}* Heb. *Damasek*. Hazael having slain Benhadad, 2 King. 8:15, and reigned in his stead, sundry kings after him were named Hazael; one whereof made the first invasion against Joash, 2 King. 12:17. And that Hazael might be the king here mentioned. For though upon the great gift given him he *went away* at first *from Jerusalem*, 2 King. 12:18, yet he might be stirred up again to send his men against it. Some hold that that Hazael was now dead, and another king in his room, who regarded not the conditions which his predecessor had made.

**V. 24. ***{For the army of the Syrians came with a small company of men}* The king of Syria was very confident in the prowess and valor of his men, who had before this entered into Judah, 2 King. 12:17, and he had also observed the timorousness of the men of Judah, who gave so great a present to the Syrians to depart out of their country, 2 King. 12:18, or else he would never have been so imprudent as with so small an army to attempt so great a matter. But without question God put spirit into him, and stirred him up hereunto, as the words following imply. See Deut. 32:30.

*{and the LORD delivered a very great host into their hand}* Multitudes of men, where God takes away spirit, can do no good at all. See Lev. 26:37.

*{because they had forsaken the LORD God of their fathers}* God forsakes them who forsake him, v. 20, and they that are forsaken of God cannot stand against their enemies. See v. 18.

*{So they executed judgment against Joash}* This implies that God made the Syrians his instruments to execute his judgment on Joash.

**V. 25. ***{And when they were departed from him}* This was an evidence of God’s patience, who suffered these ministers of his justice to depart from that wretched king, whereby he had a breathing-time and an opportunity of repenting.

*{for they left him in great diseases}* They had wounded him sore, and maladies arising from wounds are called *diseases* or *sicknesses*, 1 King. 22:34. 2 King. 8:29. Ch. 35:23. Some suppose that the enemies had put him to some great tortures.

*{his own servants conspired against him}* Certainly Joash made not that use of the enemies departing from him that he should have done: behold therefore a more heavy judgment, and that by his own servants, which is a great aggravation. Joash consented to those that conspired against Zechariah, v. 21, and God suffers his servants to conspire against him.

*{for the blood}* Blood is a crying sin, Gen. 4:10, and here noted as one of the most proper causes of the judgments on Joash.

*{of the sons of Jehoiada the Priest}* We read but of one son slain, but it may be their rage extended against other sons also. Or, *as they slew Zechariah, so they might afterwards slay his sons (as Naboth’s sons were slain with him*, 2 King. 9:26) and these being grandchildren of Jehoiada might be called his *sons*. Or, *by a synecdoche the plural number may be put for the singular*.

*{and slew him on his bed, and he died}* These servants of the king did not depart from him after they had wounded him, as the Syrians did, but clean deprived him of life. Because other judgments prevailed not with him, God suffered him to be clean cut off.

*{and they buried him}* To wit, such friends as he had about him, or the people of the land.

*{in the city of David}* See 1 King. 2:10.

*{but they buried him not in the sepulchres of the kings}* So they dealt with his wicked grandfather, ch. 21:20.

**V. 26. ***{And these are they that conspired against him}* As men that have done famous acts use to be registered by name for honor’s sake, so are many that commit wicked facts, for their greater infamy and disgrace.

*{Zabad}* Or, *Jozachar*, 2 Kings 12:21.

*{the son of Shimeath an Ammonitess}* Or, *Shimeah*, 2 Kings 12:21.

*{and Jehozabad the son of Shimrith}* Or, *Shomer*, 2 King. 12:21.

*{a Moabitess}* These two though strangers by parentage might be incorporated into the commonwealth of Judah: or though their predecessors came from Ammon and Moab (of whom see ch. 20:1), yet they might be born in the kingdom of Judah, and so become servants to Joash. No particular cause of their anger against their master is set down, but certainly they were ministers of God’s justice, though they acted out of private revenge.

**V. 27. ***{Now concerning his sons}* Namely, how many he had, by what wives, to what age they grew, what offices they had, what was their life, and what their death, with other like circumstances.

*{and the greatness of the burdens laid upon him}* This may be applied to that which he gave to the king of Syria, 2 King. 12:18.

*{and the repairing of the house of God}* Heb. *founding*. It may be the foundation of the temple was in some place decayed, and thereupon repaired by him.

*{behold, they are written in the story}* Or, *Commentary*.

*{of the book of the Kings}* Some of these things are registered in that part of Scripture which is called *The book of the Kings*. But because other things are not mentioned therein, some civil records must here be meant.

*{And Amaziah his son reigned in his stead}* Like his father, he began his reign well, but ended it impiously, and had himself a miserable end.

## CHAPTER 25

**Verse 1. ***{Amaziah was twenty and five years old when he began to reign, etc.}* See 2 King. 14:2. This Amaziah made show of some good things, but at last he showed himself to be a plain hypocrite. From hence to v. 6 are some good things of him noted, but afterwards sins and judgments.

**V. 2. ***{And he did that which was right, etc.}* See 2 King. 14:3.

*{but not with a perfect heart}* This phrase notes his hypocrisy, 2 King. 14:3, it is thus explained, *yet not like David his father*. See 1 King. 11:4. This is added 2 King. 14:3. *He did according to all things as Joash his father did*.

**V. 3. ***{Now it came to pass when the kingdom was established to him, etc.}* Heb. *confirmed to him*. See 2 King. 14:5.

**V. 4. ***{But he slew not their children, etc.}* See 2 Kings 14:6. Jer. 31:30.

**V. 5. ***{Moreover Amaziah gathered Judah together}* i.e. The kingdom of Judah, comprising under it Benjamin as well as Judah.

*{and made them captains over thousands, and captains over hundreds}* See 1 Chron. 13:1. He gathered all sorts of his subjects together, but of some choice ones among them he made captains.

*{according to the houses of their fathers}* He distinguished the companies which he set under captains according to the families in the tribes.

*{throughout all Judah and Benjamin}* Through his whole kingdom, that every part might be secured against enemies.

*{and he numbered them from twenty years old and above}* According to the law, Num. 1:18.

*{and found them three hundred thousand choice men}* i.e. Such as had greater courage, strength and experience than the common sort. 1 Chr. 7:4 and 19:10.

*{able to go forth to war}* These were such as are said to be *skilful in war, and that went out to the war*, 1 Chr. 5:18, and *fit to go out for war*, and *apt to the war*, 1 Chr. 7:11, 40.

*{that could handle spear and shield}* These two by synecdoche are put for all warlike instruments, 1 Chr. 12:8, 33.

**V. 6. ***{He hired also an hundred thousand}* These joined with the three hundred thousand, v. 5, made as great an army as that of Abijah’s, ch. 13:3.

*{mighty men of valour}* See 1 Chr. 5:24.

*{out of Israel}* i.e. The ten tribes revolted from the house of David.

*{for an hundred talents of silver}* i.e. Thirty-seven thousand five hundred pound sterling. See 1 Chr. 22:14. So every regiment consisting of a thousand had a talent of silver, i.e. Three hundred seventy-five pounds.

**V. 7. ***{But there came a man of God to him, saying}* See 1 Kings 13:1.

*{O King}* This man being sent of God fears not to declare his message plainly to the king’s face.

*{let not the army of Israel go with thee}* Help is not to be sought of such as forsake God, and revolt from his worship.

*{for the LORD is not with Israel}* He had been with them before their revolt; but they having forsaken the Lord, the Lord had forsaken them. See ch. 24:20.

*{to wit, with all}* Or, *with any*, Ps. 103:2.

*{the children of Ephraim}* Ephraim is here put for the ten tribes, as Judah for the other two, Hos. 6:4. So Jer. 7:15. For Ephraim had the privilege of the firstborn, Gen. 48:19, and it was the greatest tribe of the ten, Josh. 17:14, 17. Out of it the first king of Israel arose, 1 Kings 21:26. To show that their revolt was the cause of God’s not being with them, mention is here made of Ephraim.

**V. 8. ***{But if thou wilt go}* If you will follow your own mind rather than God’s will, and trust in the arm of flesh. This he added to deter the king of Judah from entertaining the men of Israel.

*{do it}* This is an ironical concession, like that ch. 18:14.

*{be strong for the battle}* This also is spoken ironically, implying that all the preparations he could make would be in vain.

*{God shall make thee fall before the enemy}* Notwithstanding all your preparations the enemy shall prevail against you.

*{for God hath power to help, and to cast down}* Success in war (as in other things) is wholly ordered by God. See ch. 14:11.

**V. 9. ***{And Amaziah said to the man of God}* The king was somewhat wrought upon by the message of the prophet.

*{But what shall we do for the hundred talents}* He knew not well how to recover them from such a numerous company without much bloodshed and great hazard; and this troubled him.

*{which I have given to the army of Israel}* Heb. *band of Israel*. They had the promised reward in hand, and the king well knew they would not easily let it go.

*{And the man of God answered, The LORD is able to give thee much more than this}* It is God’s blessing whereby men get, Prov. 10:22, and without his blessing nothing prospers, Ps. 127:1, 2.

**V. 10. ***{Then Amaziah separated them}* They were set in rank with the men of Judah, but now he severs them.

*{to wit, the army that was come to him out of Ephraim}* Here Ephraim is put for all the ten tribes of Israel, as v. 7.

*{to go home again}* Heb. *to go to their place*. Every one to his home from whence he came.

*{wherefore their anger was greatly kindled against Judah}* For they took themselves to be scorned, as if their aid had not been worth the having: therefore though they had their hire to the full, yet that satisfied them not, by reason of their conceit of scorn. See the like 2 Sam. 19:41, 42, 43.

*{and they returned home in great anger}* Heb. *in heat of anger*. Thus much is manifested by the revenge they took v. 13.

**V. 11. ***{And Amaziah strengthened himself}* With his own men, the three hundred thousand mentioned v. 5.

*{and led forth his people}* He himself as a general went before his army.

*{and went to the valley of salt}* See 2 King. 14:7.

*{and smote of the children of Seir}* Or, *of Edom*. See ch. 20:10.

*{ten thousand}* See 2 King. 14:7.

**V. 12. ***{And other ten thousand left alive}* Namely, in the battle, and not slain in the field.

*{did the children of Judah carry away captive}* For Amaziah routed the whole army of the Edomites, and so took captives such as were not slain outright.

*{and brought them unto the top of the rock}* Some translate it *Petra*, and take it for the proper name of a place, and say it is the same that is called Selah, which Amaziah in this expedition *took by war*, 2 King. 14:7.

*{and cast them down from the top of the rock}* The reason why the children of Judah thus dealt with the Edomites, may be, because having been subdued by David and made tributaries, 1 Chr. 18:13, in the days of Joram son of Jehoshaphat they rebelled, notwithstanding they were then destroyed with a great slaughter, 2 Kings 8:20, 21, 22.

*{that they all were broken in pieces}* Their brains were dashed out, and their bones broken, and it may be their arms and legs violently rent from their body.

**V. 13. ***{But the soldiers of the army which Amaziah sent back}* Heb. *the sons of the band which he sent back*, v. 10.

*{that they should not go with him to battle}* This is added, as the reason why they were sent back.

*{fell upon the cities of Judah}* To wit, such as were on the frontiers, and bordered on the kingdom of Israel.

*{from Samaria even unto Beth-horon}* All along the breadth of Judah. Beth-horon was in the tribe of Benjamin. See 1 Kings 9:17.

*{and smote three thousand of them}* i.e. Of the inhabitants of the cities. The slaughter of so many shows their wrath was great, as is noted v. 10.

*{and took much spoil}* To wit, out of the cities which they invaded, and out of the country of Judah all along.

**V. 14. ***{Now it came to pass, after that Amaziah was come from the slaughter of the Edomites}* This slaughter which he made after he had sent away the hundred thousand hired soldiers, should have raised his heart up to God, of whom the man of God put him in mind, v. 8, 9, but he made no such use of it.

*{that he brought the gods of the children of Seir}* David would not do so, but burnt the gods of his enemies which he took, 1 Chr. 14:12. These *children of Seir* were the Edomites, ch. 20:10.

*{and set them up to be his gods}* This was extreme impiety and monstrous idolatry in itself; but the greater, because done by him who had done *that which was right in the sight of the Lord*, v. 3, and had obeyed the voice of the Lord, v. 7, 10, and had received a great deliverance from the Lord, v. 11, 12.

*{and bowed down himself before them}* This was divine worship which he gave unto idols. See Num. 25:2. 1 Chr. 29:20.

*{and burned incense unto them}* Such services as God enjoined to be performed unto himself, idolaters did to their idols. See 1 King. 13:1.

**V. 15. ***{Wherefore the anger of the LORD was kindled against Amaziah}* See 2 King. 22:13, 17 and 23:26.

*{and he sent unto him a Prophet}* Though God’s anger were justly incensed, and he might have destroyed Amaziah on a sudden, yet through his patience and long-suffering he sent a prophet unto him, to try if he might bring him to repentance.

*{which said unto him, Why hast thou sought after the gods of the people}* By *people* are here meant such nations as had no knowledge of the true God, Ps. 147:20. And this aggravated Amaziah’s idolatry; the true God was made known to him, and yet he imitated those that knew him not.

*{which could not deliver their own people out of thine hand}* This is another aggravation. He left Jehovah that true God who had given him a great victory, and worshiped such gods as could not deliver those that had worshiped them.

**V. 16. ***{And it came to pass as he talked with him, that the King said unto him}* Amaziah was so far from well heeding what the prophet said, as he was wroth with him.

*{Art thou made of the King’s counsel?}* Because himself had not chosen this prophet to be of his counsel, he refused to hearken to that counsel which was sent from God. A very impious disposition. See on Gen. 19:9.

*{forbear}* He would have him to speak no more in God’s name, because it was against his own wicked mind.

*{why shouldest thou be smitten?}* Heb. *why should they smite thee?* This is a menacing speech, implying that his guard or others that attended him would destroy him if he continued to upbraid the king with what he had done.

*{Then the Prophet forbare}* When means of reclaiming men from sin and preventing judgment are despised, God will withhold those means, that they may be no longer abused.

*{and said, I know that God hath determined}* Heb. *counselled*. For God’s determinations are not rash, but upon good advice and counsel.

*{to destroy thee}* Refusing to hear God’s messengers is the ready way to destruction. See ch. 24:19 and 36:16. 1 Sam. 2:25.

*{because thou hast done this}* This has reference to v. 14, where Amaziah’s idolatry is set forth.

*{and hast not hearkened unto my counsel}* He speaks this as a prophet of the Lord, from whom he brought that counsel which he gave to the king.

**V. 17. ***{Then Amaziah King of Judah took advice}* He took advice with such counselors as Rehoboam did, ch. 10:9, who observing the king’s disposition flattered him, and gave such counsel as was most agreeable to his humor.

*{and sent to Joash, etc.}* Or, *he sent messengers to Joash*, 2 Kings 14:8.

**V. 18. ***{And Joash king of Israel sent to Amaziah, etc.}* See 2 Kings 14:9.

*{The thistle, etc.}* Or, *fur-bush*, or *thorn*. He means such low plants as are contemptible, at least in comparison of a tall cedar.

*{and there passed by a wild beast, etc.}* Heb. *a beast of the field*. Such beasts as used continually to abide in the field.

**V. 19. ***{Thou sayest, Lo, thou hast smitten the Edomites}* This is set down 2 Kings 14:10 as a matter granted, *Thou hast indeed smitten Edom*.

*{and thine heart lifteth thee up to boast}* Or, *and thine heart hath lifted thee up*, 2 King. 14:10.

*{abide now at home, etc.}* Or, *glory of this, and tarry at home*, 2 King. 14:10.

**V. 20. ***{But Amaziah would not hear}* See 2 King. 14:11.

*{for it came of God}* See 1 King. 12:24.

*{that he might deliver them into the hand of their enemies}* God oft suffers evil-doers to pull judgments on their own pates.

*{because they sought after the Gods of Edom}* Though God gave victory to Amaziah, yet he abusing the victory unto gross idolatry, he raised up other enemies to be his scourge, and to punish him for it. See v. 14.

**V. 21. ***{So Joash the king of Israel went up, etc.}* See 2 Kings 14:11.

**V. 22. ***{And Judah was put to the worse, etc.}* See 2 King. 14:12.

**V. 23. ***{And Joash the king of Israel took Amaziah, etc.}* See 2 King. 14:13.

*{the son of Joash}* Or, *of Jehoash*.

*{the son of Jehoahaz}* Or, *the son of Ahaziah*. This man had three names. See ch. 21:17.

*{at Beth-shemesh, and brought him to Jerusalem, etc.}* See 2 Kings 14:13.

*{from the gate of Ephraim to the corner gate}* Heb. *the gate of it that looketh*. At a corner-gate men may look divers ways.

**V. 24. ***{And he took all the gold, etc.}* See 2 King. 14:14.

*{with Obed-Edom}* Obed-Edom was a porter of the house of God in David’s time; and he had many sons deputed to that function, 1 Chr. 13:13 and 16:38 and 26:4, 15. So as the posterity of Obed-Edom is here meant.

**V. 25. ***{And Amaziah the son of Joash, etc.}* See 2 King. 14:17.

**V. 26. ***{Now the rest of the acts of Amaziah, first and last}* Both the good things in the beginning of his reign, and his apostasy in the end. See ch. 12:15 and 16:11 and 20:34.

*{behold, are they not written in the book of the Kings of Judah and Israel?}* Both kingdoms had their public records or chronicles. And because there were transactions and wars betwixt Amaziah and the king of Israel, therefore Amaziah’s acts were registered in the chronicles of both kingdoms.

**V. 27. ***{Now after the time that Amaziah did turn away from following the LORD}* Heb. *from after the Lord*. That was, when he set up the gods of the Edomites to be his gods, v. 14.

*{that made a conspiracy}* Heb. *conspired a conspiracy*. It seems the people of the land detested that monstrous idolatry, and thereupon conspired. And though their conspiracy broke not forth before the king of Israel had spoiled Jerusalem, yet it began before.

*{against him in Jerusalem}* See 2 King. 14:19.

**V. 28. ***{They brought him upon horses}* See 2 King. 14:20.

*{and buried him with his fathers in the city of Judah}* i.e. *The city of David*, as it is 2 King. 14:20.

## CHAPTER 26

**Verse 1. ***{Then all the people of Judah took Uzziah, etc.}* Or, *Azariah*, 2 King. 14:21. He was the ninth king of Judah after the division. The former part of his reign was good: whereof there is relation from hence to v. 16. But he fell away. His revolt, together with the punishment thereof, is set down v. 16, etc.

**V. 2. ***{He built Eloth, etc.}* Or, *Elath*. See 2 King. 24:22.

**V. 3. ***{Sixteen years old was Uzziah, etc.}* See 2 King. 15:2.

**V. 4. ***{And he did that which was right, etc.}* See 2 King. 15:3.

**V. 5. ***{And he sought God in the days of Zechariah}* Of *seeking God* see 1 Chr. 16:10, 11. Zechariah was questionless a faithful prophet of the Lord, and careful to instruct and direct the king as long as he lived; and the king was guided by him, as Joash was by Jehoiada, ch. 24:2.

*{who had understanding in the visions of God}* Or, *who made to understand in the fear of God*. He might teach both the king and others such things as belonged to the fear of God. By *visions of God* may be meant such as God had made known. For things revealed are spiritually seen. Zechariah, being a prophet, might see visions of God, as other prophets, being called *Seers*, did. See 2 King. 17:13. Or he might have an especial gift to interpret the visions and revelations of prophets or other men; as Joseph did, Gen. 41:15, and Daniel, Dan. 1:17 and 2:19 and 10:1.

*{and as long as he sought the LORD, God made him to prosper}* See 1 Chr. 22:11, 13. God uses to deal with men according to their dealing with him: while they honor him, he blesses them, 1 Sam. 2:30. See ch. 11:17 and 15:2.

**V. 6. ***{And he went forth and warred against the Philistines}* They were ever deadly enemies to Israel. See 1 Chron. 1:12 and 10:1 and 14:18.

*{and brake down the wall of Gath}* Gath was one of the five principal cities of the Philistines, 1 Sam. 6:17. See on Amos 6:2.

*{and the wall of Jabneh}* This also was a city of the Philistines, but not mentioned elsewhere in Scripture.

*{and the wall of Ashdod}* See on Isa. 20:1 and Zeph. 2:4.

*{and built cities about Ashdod}* Or, *in the country of Ashdod*. Ashdod was the name of a city, and also of a great part of the country round about it.

*{and among the Philistines}* He much prevailed over them, seeing he had power to build cities and place garrisons among them. See 1 King. 10:34.

**V. 7. ***{And God helped him against the Philistines}* This was the true ground of that success he had against his enemies. See 1 Chr. 5:20.

*{and against the Arabians}* See ch. 17:11 and 21:16. 1 King. 10:15.

*{that dwelt in Gur-baal}* Or, *the plain of Gur*. See 2 King. 9:27.

*{and the Mehunims}* Or, *Hammonims*. See ch. 20:1. Some take these to be also people of Arabia, who by reason of their dwelling in tents are called *Scenites*. See 1 Chr. 4:41. These nations all bordered upon Judah; the Philistines, west, the Arabians, south, the Ammonites, or Hamonims, east.

**V. 8. ***{And the Ammonites gave gifts to Uzziah}* See ch. 17:11.

*{and his name spread abroad}* Heb. *went*. Was divulged abroad. See v. 15.

*{even to the entering in of Egypt}* Throughout all countries betwixt Judah and Egypt: so as by God’s blessing on him the very heathen round about feared and honored him, as 1 Chr. 14:17 and ch. 17:10.

*{for he strengthened himself exceedingly}* This he did by those means which are set down in the verses following.

**V. 9. ***{Moreover, Uzziah built towers in Jerusalem}* On or by the city walls which the king of Israel had demolished, ch. 25:23.

*{at the corner gate}* See ch. 25:23.

*{and at the valley gate}* See Neh. 2:13 and 3:13.

*{and at the turning of the wall}* Or, *upon Miktsoa*. This is the Hebrew word: and some take it to be a proper name. Mention is made hereof Neh. 3:19, 24. It is supposed it was a tower or fort in the highest place of Zion.

*{and fortified them}* Or, *repaired them*. This has reference to all the particulars before mentioned.

**V. 10. ***{Also he built towers in the desert}* This desert is taken to be the wilderness of Arabia. And this place Uzziah the rather fortified, that he might keep the Arabians from such invasions as they had made in Jehoram’s time, ch. 21:16, 17.

*{and digged many wells}* Or, *cut out many cisterns*. They had not such store of rivers in the land of Judah as we have in these countries. Thereupon they used what means they could to get and keep fresh water for all needful uses, even for man and beast.

*{for he had much cattle}* By this means plenty of victuals was preserved.

*{both in the low country, and in the plains}* By *the low country* may be meant marsh grounds and meadows; by *plains*, champion grounds that were not hilly.

*{husbandmen also}* This has reference to the verb *he had*.

*{and vine dressers in the mountains}* They used to plant their vines on mountains, for the better ripening of their grapes.

*{and in Carmel}* Or, *in fruitful fields*. See 1 King. 18:19.

*{for he loved husbandry}* Heb. *ground*. He took great delight in seeing fruitful places well tilled and manured, and the various fruits that grew thereon.

**V. 11. ***{Moreover Uzziah had an host of fighting men}* i.e. Such as are called *mighty men of valour*, ch. 17:13. Herein he imitated Jehoshaphat, ch. 17:14, 19.

*{that went out to war by bands}* They were to be ready on all occasions to go forth to battle.

*{according to the number of their account}* He had his distinct captains and soldiers under them divided into regiments and companies, which were registered. See 1 Chr. 27:1.

*{by the hand of Jeiel the Scribe}* Of the office of a scribe see 1 King. 4:3. Scribes used to register such things.

*{and Maaseiah the ruler}* This was an officer that had the care of matters to be well ordered.

*{under the hand of Hananiah, one of the King’s captains}* Or, *under his command*. This has reference to the host before mentioned.

**V. 12. ***{The whole number of the chief of the fathers of the mighty men of valour, were two thousand and six hundred}* He must needs have a very great host that had so many commanders therein.

**V. 13. ***{And under their hand}* Or, *under their command*.

*{was an army}* Heb. *the power of an army*. An army that had great power, or was very valiant.

*{three hundred thousand and seven thousand and five hundred}* This was indeed a very great army, yet not like that which Jehoshaphat had, ch. 17:14, etc. The kings of Judah and Israel trained up many soldiers in their days.

*{that made war with mighty power}* This is to be taken rather of their ability than of any particular act.

*{to help the king against the enemy}* When any enemy should invade the land, or when the king would send them forth against an enemy.

**V. 14. ***{And Uzziah prepared for them throughout all the host}* He took care to have them well furnished with such armor and weapons as were requisite for their places.

*{shields}* For defense.

*{and spears}* Both for defense and offense.

*{and helmets}* To cover their heads.

*{and habergeons}* Armor for back and breast.

*{and bows}* To shoot arrows, see 1 Chr. 5:18 and 12:2.

*{and slings to cast stones}* Heb. *stones of slings*. He provided both slings and stones, as we do muskets and bullets. Slings were much used by the Israelites in war; and the Benjamites were especially skillful therein, Judg. 20:16. 1 Chr. 12:2.

**V. 15. ***{And he made in Jerusalem engines invented by cunning men}* Heb. *inventions by the invention of the inventer*. This is an emphatical Hebraism, the meaning whereof is well set forth by our translators. These were for such uses as our great pieces and cannons, to annoy enemies afar off, or to batter down strong walls and forts.

*{to be on the towers and upon the bulwarks}* So they might do the greater annoyance to an enemy, and that the farther off.

*{to shoot arrows}* These certainly were great and massy arrows, and many of them shot out of an engine together.

*{and great stones withal}* These were other kind of stones than they had v. 14, as great, if not greater, than our great cannon-bullets.

*{and his name spread far abroad}* Heb. *went forth*. He was far and near reported to be a puissant prince, such as enemies dared not oppose. See v. 8.

*{for he was marvellously helped}* Namely, from God. See v. 7.

*{till he was strong}* Till he came to be every way so well furnished and prepared against enemies as is before mentioned, and till he waxed so insolent as is hereafter noted.

**V. 16. ***{But when he was strong}* See v. 15. Here begins his falling away from God.

*{his heart was lifted up}* See ch. 25:19. Men are prone to abuse God’s blessings and that power they have to pride and self-confidence.

*{to his destruction}* Pride is the forerunner of ruin. It makes men attempt such things as provoke God’s wrath against them.

*{for he transgressed against the LORD}* In doing such things as he had expressly forbidden.

*{his God}* This was a great aggravation of his sin, that God having so many ways showed himself *his God*, he notwithstanding should so sin against him.

*{and went into the temple of the LORD}* Whereinto none but priests and Levites might enter, Num. 18:6, 7.

*{to burn incense}* See 1 King. 9:25 and 13:1.

*{upon the altar of incense}* That golden altar which was set close to the entering into the most holy place, Exod. 30:6. This shows the height of Uzziah’s impiety and monstrous presumption. He went through the priests’ court, the high porch, and the holy place, even to the uppermost part thereof; into none of which places he ought at all to have gone. Then he offered incense, which none but priests might do, v. 18. Finally, he offered it upon the altar of incense, which none but the high priest might do, Exod. 30:7, 8. Lev. 16:13.

**V. 17. ***{And Azariah the Priest}* He was then high priest, v. 20.

*{went in after him}* It seems the king had not made known his mind to the priests before he went in, or else they would have opposed him and not suffered him to go in at all, rather than have followed him.

*{and with him fourscore Priests of the LORD}* The spirits of other priests as well as of the high priest were stirred up against his impiety. And the high priest takes so many with him the more to daunt the king.

*{that were valiant men}* Men of great courage and great strength.

**V. 18. ***{And they withstood Uzziah the King}* It seems the king had taken the golden censer into his hand to burn incense thereon, v. 19, but they would not suffer him to go on.

*{and said unto him}* They labor by words to dissuade him from that he was about to do.

*{It appertaineth not unto thee, Uzziah}* Kings must not venture on such things as belong not to them.

*{to burn incense unto the LORD}* See Num. 16:40 and 18:7.

*{but to the Priests the sons of Aaron, that are consecrated to burn incense}* This was one special duty for which priests were set apart and appointed to be for men in things appertaining to God. See Exod. 30:7.

*{Go out of the Sanctuary}* i.e. The holy place. It was in God’s cause and in God’s name that he used so peremptory a charge to a king.

*{for thou hast trespassed}* See v. 16.

*{neither shall it be for thine honour}* No sin, though men have it in high esteem, can be to a man’s honor, but is his shame and dishonor. This is spoken in opposition to Uzziah’s high proud thoughts.

*{from the LORD God}* *God resisteth the proud*, James 4:6. Acts of pride bring therefore shame and disgrace from God, not honor.

**V. 19. ***{Then Uzziah was wroth}* As Asa, ch. 16:10. These two, Asa and Uzziah, seemed to be very good kings for the greatest part of their reign; but both of them fell foully. In both examples we see how much great men scorn to be checked for their sins, and how little God’s servants are regarded by them in their heat of sinning.

*{and had a censer in his hand}* See 1 King. 7:50. It seems he had snatched it up so soon as he came to the altar.

*{to burn incense}* This shows his intent: but withal it implies that he had not done the fact; for he was prevented by the hand of God.

*{and while he was wroth with the Priests}* His wrath against the priests did the more incense God’s wrath against him.

*{the leprosy even rose up in his forehead}* There it suddenly manifested itself. And the sudden rising of the leprosy on his face shows that it was a judgment from the Lord; as Num. 12:10.

*{before the Priests in the house of the LORD}* While they were standing against him, v. 18. This is here noted to show that the Lord took their part, and thereby encouraged them to persist in resisting him.

*{from beside the incense altar}* Or, *from upon the incense-altar*. This being referred to the judgment, implies that some visible evidence of God’s wrath appeared at the altar, whereby he made it appear that the judgment was inflicted by him. See Lev. 10:2. The earthquake mentioned Amos 1:1, Zech. 14:5, to be in Uzziah’s time some suppose was now.

**V. 20. ***{And Azariah the chief Priest}* Or, *high Priest*.

*{and all the Priests looked upon him}* For they stood opposite against him, face to face.

*{and behold, he was leprous in his forehead}* The suddenness of this judgment made it the more remarkable.

*{and they thrust him out}* Their office, their holy zeal, and God’s manifesting his indignation against the king’s fact, v. 19, made them thus courageous.

*{from thence}* From that holy place whereinto he had presumptuously entered.

*{yea, himself also hasted to go out}* As Esth. 6:12. Shame and grief forced him to make the more haste.

*{because the LORD had smitten him}* He now discerned that the Lord himself was displeased with him.

**V. 21. ***{And Uzziah the king was a leper unto the day of his death}* See 2 King. 15:5.

*{and dwelt in a several house}* Heb. *free*.

*{being a leper}* For the law enjoined lepers to live by themselves, Lev. 13:46.

*{for he was cut off from the house of the LORD}* He might not go so much as to the court of the people. He that presumed to go further into the house of the Lord than he should, excluded himself from that liberty that before he had.

*{and Jotham his son was over the king’s house, etc.}* See 2 Kings 16:5.

**V. 22. ***{Now the rest of the acts of Uzziah, first and last}* See ch. 12:15 and 16:11 and 20:34.

*{did Isaiah the Prophet the son of Amoz}* See Isa. 1:1.

*{write}* Isaiah in his prophecy has sundry passages concerning Uzziah and the estate of Judah in Uzziah’s days. So that this may have reference to that prophecy. Or, *Isaiah might be a penman of some part of the second book of Kings*. Or, *Isaiah might write some records of the kings in whose days he lived, which are not now extant*.

**V. 23. ***{So Uzziah slept with his fathers}* See 1 King. 2:10.

*{and they buried him with his fathers}* See 2 King. 15:7.

*{in the field of the burial which belonged to the Kings}* This field was within the city of David; whereof see 1 King. 2:10. It is said *to belong to the Kings* because it was their inheritance. But whether it were that field wherein the sepulchers of David, Solomon and other kings of Judah that were honored in their burials were, is questionable. Some hold that it was the same field, but that Uzziah was buried in a remote place thereof, where none of the other kings’ sepulchers were. So was wicked Jehoram buried, ch. 21:20, and that notorious apostate, Joash, ch. 24:25.

*{for they said, He is a leper}* This is the reason why they afforded him not such honor in his funeral as had been done to many of his predecessors. As in his life, v. 21, so in his death he was separated from others.

*{and Jotham his son reigned in his stead}* See 2 King. 25:7.

## CHAPTER 27

**Verse 1. ***{Jotham was twenty and five years old, etc.}* See 2 King. 15:32, 33. Jotham was the tenth king of Judah from the division. His history is briefly registered in this chapter; and nothing but good is related, except *the peoples doing corruptly in his time*, v. 2.

**V. 2. ***{And he did that which was right, etc.}* See 2 King. 15:34.

*{Howbeit he entered not into the temple of the LORD}* This was his father Uzziah’s great sin, ch. 26:16, etc. And Jotham’s forbearing of that is here expressed to show that he was not like his father in the evil which he did, but in the good only.

*{And the people did yet corruptly}* This general is thus explained 2 King. 15:35. *The people sacrificed and burnt incense still in the high places*. And this is set down as a fault in Jotham’s government. It is thereupon said, *The high places were not removed*: had he removed them, he might have prevented the people’s corruption therein.

**V. 3. ***{He built the high gate, etc.}* See 2 King. 15:35.

*{and on the wall of Ophel}* Or, *tower thereof*. See 2 Kings 5:24.

*{he built much}* Ophel was a place for defense, and might be decayed in Jotham’s time; and so he might bestow much about the repair thereof. For such things as are well repaired are in Scripture-phrase said *to be built*. See ch. 11:5, 6. Or, *he might build fortresses thereabouts*.

**V. 4. ***{Moreover he built cities in the mountains of Judah}* i.e. Such hilly places as were not inhabited. In these he built cities, both for the increase and better defense of his kingdom.

*{and in the forests he built castles and towers}* Forests also used to be places not inhabited; but they were accounted pleasant places, as the forest of Carmel, 1 King. 19:25, the forest of Lebanon, 2 Chr. 9:16. In these he built castles and towers against invasions of enemies. Some translate the word here turned *castles*, *palaces*, fair and sumptuous buildings: but the former is more pertinent to this place. This very word is used ch. 17:12, and there fitly translated, as in this place, *castles*.

**V. 5. ***{He fought also with the king of the Ammonites}* See ch. 20:1. Whether they invaded Judah, or Jotham went out to subdue them in their own country, is not expressed.

*{and prevailed against them}* This is added for commendation of his valor, and demonstration of God’s blessing on him.

*{And the children of Ammon gave him the same year}* He brought the Ammonites so under his jurisdiction as he made them tributaries to him.

*{an hundred talents of silver}* i.e. Thirty-seven thousand five hundred pound sterling. See 1 Chr. 22:14.

*{and ten thousand measures of wheat}* Heb. *Cors*. See 1 Kings 4:22.

*{and ten thousand of barley}* If the quantity of the measure here set down be duly weighed, we shall find the tribute of wheat and barley that the Ammonites paid to the king of Judah to be very great.

*{So much}* Heb. *much*. Or, *this*.

*{did the children of Ammon pay unto him both the second year and the third}* For the three first years Jotham exacted so much yearly of the Ammonites. This time being here expressly set down, it is probable that afterwards he ceased them.

**V. 6. ***{So Jotham became mighty}* See ch. 13:21.

*{because he prepared}* Or, *established*. As he began so he continued.

*{his ways}* i.e. His counsels, his actions, yea, and the whole course of his life.

*{before the LORD his God}* So as he approved himself unto the Lord; the rather because the Lord had given him many evidences that he was in special manner *his God*. See ch. 14:2, 11.

**V. 7. ***{Now the rest of the acts of Jotham}* See 1 King. 11:41.

*{and all his wars}* This implies that he waged more wars, and that with success, than are here set down.

*{and his ways}* See v. 6.

*{lo, they are written in the book of the Kings, etc.}* See ch. 16:11.

**V. 8. ***{He was five and twenty years old, etc.}* See 2 King. 15:33.

**V. 9. ***{And Jotham slept, etc.}* See 1 King. 2:10.

*{and Ahaz his son reigned in his stead}* An impious son succeeded a pious father. He was the eleventh from the division.

## CHAPTER 28

**Verse 1. ***{Ahaz was twenty years old, etc.}* See 2 King. 16:2. In this chapter the history of one of the most impious kings that Judah had is set forth.

**V. 2. ***{For he walked in the ways, etc.}* See 2 King. 16:3.

*{and made also molten images for Baalim}* See 1 King. 16:31 and 18:18. Ch. 24:7. They thought it an honor to their idols to make representations of them, and to consecrate images unto them. They called their idols *Baalim*, because they accounted them their lords.

**V. 3. ***{Moreover he burnt incense}* Or, *offered sacrifice*. See 1 King. 9:25.

*{in the valley of the son of Hinnom}* See 2 King. 16:3.

*{and burnt his children in the fire, etc.}* Lev. 18:21. Or, *made his sons to pass through the fire*, 2 King. 16:3.

**V. 4. ***{He sacrificed also, etc.}* See 2 King. 16:4.

**V. 5. ***{Wherefore the LORD his God}* See ch. 26:16. Though in regard of the people over whom he was king, and the place where his throne was, the Lord is said to be *his God*, yet he severely punishes his iniquity.

*{delivered him into the hand of the king of Syria}* Rezin, 2 Kings 16:5.

*{and they smote him}* To wit, the army or soldiers under Rezin; and that when they took Elath, 2 King. 16:6.

*{and carried away a great multitude of them captives}* This was before Rezin and Pekah had come up to Jerusalem to besiege it; for then they could not overcome Ahaz, 2 King. 16:5. Besides, Rezin was forced to go from Jerusalem to defend Damascus, besieged by the king of Assyria. There Rezin was slain, 2 King. 16:9.

*{and brought them to Damascus}* Heb. *Darmesek*. See on Isa. 7:8.

*{and he was also delivered into the hand of the king of Israel}* To wit, Pekah, v. 6. This also was after the foresaid attempt.

*{who smote him with a great slaughter}* See ch. 13:17.

**V. 6. ***{For Pekah the son of Remaliah}* See 1 King. 15:27.

*{slew in Judah an hundred and twenty thousand in one day}* At one battle, in the fight and pursuit. See 1 King. 20:29.

*{which were all valiant men}* Heb. *sons of valour*. This Hebraism adds much emphasis, and shows that they were full of courage and of great strength from their youth.

*{because they had forsaken the LORD}* See ch. 21:10 and 24:20, 24.

*{God of their fathers}* See 1 Chr. 5:25. Deut. 32:30.

**V. 7. ***{And Zichri a mighty man of Ephraim}* This was a man of more than ordinary strength and courage, and therefore here named.

*{slew Maaseiah the king’s son}* i.e. The son of Ahaz king of Judah. And it may be he was then his eldest son.

*{and Azrikam the governor of the house}* He that had the chief command under the king in the court or royal palace: we call such an one *lord high steward of the king’s house*.

*{and Elkanah that was next to the king}* Heb. *the second to the King*. This was a man in such place and of such authority as Joseph was in Egypt, Gen. 41:40, 43, 44. These three that were slain are thus set down to magnify the valor of Zichri. For questionless these three great personages had many to guard them.

**V. 8. ***{And the children of Israel carried away captive of their brethren}* This title *brethren* has relation to the common stock whence both the children of Judah and the children of Israel came. See ch. 11:4.

*{two hundred thousand women, sons and daughters}* These *sons and daughters* were questionless young children. This is noted to aggravate the cruelty of the Israelites, who spared neither sex nor age. Or, *sons and daughters may imply orphans, whose fathers were slain in the war; which is also an aggravation of their cruelty*.

*{and took also away much spoil from them}* See v. 15.

*{and brought the spoil to Samaria}* See 1 King. 16:24.

**V. 9. ***{But a Prophet of the LORD was there}* God afforded prophets to his people in the worst times, to try if he might reclaim them.

*{whose name was Oded}* There was a prophet of this name in the days of Asa, about two hundred years before this.

*{and he went out before the host that came to Samaria}* This prophet met the host of Israel before they entered into Samaria.

*{and said unto them, Behold, because the LORD God of your fathers}* Though they had cast off the God of their fathers, yet he had not cast off them; therefore his prophet uses this title to the idolatrous Israelites, and that in relation to the ancient patriarchs from whom they came. See ch. 13:12. 1 Chr. 29:18.

*{was wroth with Judah}* Because of their abominable idolatry, v. 2, 3, 4.

*{he hath delivered them into your hand}* This shows that God overthrew the children of Judah, and gave victory to the children of Israel.

*{and ye have slain them in a rage}* Enemies may in envy and malice proceed further against those whom God gives into their hands than he would have them, Zech. 1:15. Isa. 47:6.

*{that reacheth up unto heaven}* This is an hyperbole, implying the uttermost extremity of rage. For heaven is the highest part of the world. See Gen. 11:4. Deut. 9:1. Yea further it implies that the cry of their cruelty came up to heaven, where God was. See Ezra 9:6. Gen. 4:10. Certainly this extreme rage arose from the long disagreement betwixt Israel and Judah, and the many wars that had been betwixt them, and the victories that the men of Judah had oft had over the Israelites.

**V. 10. ***{And now you purpose}* Heb. *you say*. Namely, within yourselves. When men intend a thing, they use to reason with themselves thereabouts. See 1 King. 5:5.

*{to keep under the children of Judah and Jerusalem for bondmen and bondwomen unto you}* The Jews were expressly forbidden to make any of their brethren bond-servants, Lev. 25:39, 42.

*{but are there not with you, even with you}* He ingeminates the point, to make them the more seriously consider their own ways, and to dive into their own hearts.

*{sins against the LORD}* Though God have made you his scourge to punish Judah, yet if you well weigh your own ways, you may find that you have given him just cause to stir up others to punish you.

*{your God}* This he adds to move them the rather to take heed of provoking the Lord against them by their unmercifulness.

**V. 11. ***{Now hear me therefore}* The prophet having declared their sin, gives good advice for preventing judgment.

*{and deliver the captives again}* This was the best evidence of repentance that they could give, to show mercy to those with whom they had dealt unmercifully. See Isa. 58:6, 7.

*{which ye have taken captive of your brethren}* See v. 8.

*{for the fierce wrath of God}* Unmercifulness and cruelty exceedingly provoke God’s anger, James 2:13. See v. 13.

*{is upon you}* Does hang over your head, and will assuredly fall upon you, if you continue to add such cruelty as you have begun to use to the heap of your other sins.

**V. 12. ***{Then certain of the heads of the children of Ephraim}* Either these were commanders in the army, or such governors as remained in Samaria, to which city the army was now marching with their captives. They were for certain such as had a command, and to whom the prophet gave the forementioned advice. *Ephraim* is here put for all the ten tribes. See ch. 25:10.

*{Azariah, etc.}* Here are four of those commanders set down by name, and described by their parentage, because that which they did made much to their honor.

*{stood up against them that came from the war}* They stood against the soldiers that brought the captives of Judah along with them, to keep them from proceeding any further.

**V. 13. ***{And said unto them, Ye shall not bring in the captives hither}* Namely, into Samaria. These commanders stood betwixt that city and the army.

*{for whereas we have offended against the LORD already}* Heb. *it will be for*, or *into, guilt of the Lord upon us*. By *guilt of the Lord* a most grievous or heinous guilt is meant. The Hebrews, when they would set forth the excellency of a thing, use to say it is of God: see 1 Chr. 12:22. So when they would aggravate the heinousness of an evil, they use to affix the title of God unto it. Or, it may be said to be *a guilt of God*, because it was a sin against the law, by which they stood guilty before him.

*{ye intend}* Heb. *ye say*. See v. 10.

*{to add more to our sins}* The idolatry of the whole land, and sundry other notorious sins common among the Israelites were so evident as they could not but know and acknowledge them; which made them afraid of adding other sins to them.

*{and to our trespass}* Heb. *trespasses*. Some apply this word to the guilt that follows upon sin, and so distinguish it from *sins*.

*{for our trespass is great}* They accounted it a very foolish and mad thing, to increase that evil which was very great before.

*{and there is fierce wrath against Israel}* Hereby they imply that they had just cause to fear that some heavy judgment was hanging over their head. By *wrath* is here meant the anger of the Lord: for so great is his wrath as comparatively it alone may be called *Wrath*. So Num. 1:53 and 18:5. Josh. 22:20. 1 Chr. 27:24. Ch. 24:18. *Fierce*, because, as a fire, it may fiercely flame forth and consume such, Ezra 10:14. 2 King. 23:26. 2 Chr. 30:8.

**V. 14. ***{So the armed men left}* Though they were armed with power, yet they were moved by the arguments which were alleged.

*{the captives and the spoil}* See v. 8.

*{before the Princes}* Those that are mentioned v. 12.

*{and all the congregation}* By this it appears that there were many people that accompanied the princes, which are here called *the congregation*.

**V. 15. ***{And the men which were expressed by name}* V. 12.

*{rose up}* This phrase implies a ready and speedy enterprising of a thing. See 1 King. 14:2, 4.

*{and took the captives}* The women, sons and daughters, v. 8.

*{and with the spoil clothed}* Under the word *spoil* all manner of commodities worth carrying away are comprised, as quick cattle, silver, gold, other metals, all kind of household stuff, cloth both linen and woolen, apparel, and other like things. With these latter they clothed their captives.

*{all that were naked among them}* Soldiers use to strip many of those whom they take captives.

*{and arrayed them, and shod them}* They gave apparel and shoes to such as wanted them.

*{and gave them to eat and to drink}* For captives, especially when they are led in a march, use not to be well provided for.

*{and anointed them}* This kind of refreshing was of old used to such as were wearied. Or it may be that this anointing was for such as were wounded. See Luke 10:34.

*{and carried all the feeble of them upon asses}* There being among the captives many women and children, v. 8, some of them must needs be so weak and weary, and others so wounded, as they were not able on foot to go back again. This therefore was a great work of mercy.

*{and brought them to Jericho}* Jericho was not far from Jordan, and it was at the borders of Judah.

*{the city of Palm trees}* See Deut. 34:3. Josh. 2:1.

*{to their brethren}* Namely, the men of Judah. Here the word *brethren* is more strictly used than v. 8, for those that were of the same kingdom and under the same government.

*{then they returned to Samaria}* i.e. Those of Israel which were appointed to conduct the captives to the land of Judah, and there to leave them.

**V. 16. ***{At that time}* When the kings of Syria and Israel distressed Ahaz, 2 King. 18:5, 6, 7.

*{did king Ahaz send unto the kings of Israel to help him}* One king of Assyria to whom Ahaz sent is expressly set down by name, Tiglath-Pileser, 2 King. 16:7, and it may be he only is meant, and the plural number, *Kings*, by synecdoche put for the singular. So ch. 32:4. Or the plural is used for excellency’s sake, because he being a great monarch was instead of many kings. Or there might be many kings of Assyria under Tiglath-Pileser, and Ahaz might send to them all.

**V. 17. ***{For again the Edomites}* The Edomites might before annoy him, though no mention be made thereof. Or *again* may imply as much as *also*, *q.d.* Not the Israelites only but also the Edomites smote him. עוד *Gnod* properly signifies *as yet, hitherto, to this time*.

*{had come and smitten Judah}* Amaziah had destroyed twenty thousand of the Edomites, ch. 25:11, 12. Yet here they make head again, and do much mischief to Ahaz.

*{and carried away captives}* Heb. *a captivity*. The abstract implies a multitude of captives.

**V. 18. ***{The Philistines also had invaded}* Whereas good kings of Judah had brought under all the nations round about them, ch. 17:10, they much annoy this wicked king.

*{the cities of the low country}* Where good pastures were, and store of cattle. See ch. 26:10.

*{and of the south of Judah}* These cities lay towards the country of Edom. See Josh. 15:21.

*{and had taken Bethshemesh}* Of this city see 2 King. 14:11.

*{and Ajalon}* See ch. 11:10 and 1 Chr. 6:69.

*{and Gederoth}* See Josh. 15:33, 41.

*{and Shocho}* 1 Sam. 17:1.

*{with the villages thereof}* Great cities used to have villages appertaining to them, as 1 Chr. 4:32, 33.

*{and Timnah with the villages thereof}* Josh. 5:10, 57.

*{Gimzo also and the villages thereof}* There is no mention made of this city elsewhere, but it seems it was one of the bordering cities of Judah.

*{and they dwelt there}* i.e. In all the six cities here set down. The Philistines much prevailed against Judah, seeing they did not only plunder them, but also continued to dwell in their cities.

**V. 19. ***{For the LORD brought Judah low}* By the multitude of valiant men partly slain, and partly taken captive, v. 5, 6, by the great spoils made, v. 8. Yea, also by reason of the many cities taken, v. 18, and other miseries that befell them.

*{because of Ahaz}* Wicked kings bring judgments on their people.

*{king of Israel}* *Israel* is here put for *Judah*, as ch. 12:1 and 21:2.

*{for he made Judah naked}* This metaphor implies that they were stripped and spoiled of their beauty, glory, wealth, and all manner of commodities; but especially of divine grace, favor and succor, as Exod. 32:25. Others read, *he withdrew in Judah the worship of God*; or, *he drew Judah from the worship of God*, Exod. 5:4.

*{and transgressed sore against the LORD}* Heb. *by transgressing he transgressed*. Of that Hebraism see 1 King. 8:13. Sins against God strip men of that which is precious unto them.

**V. 20. ***{And Tilgath-Pilneser king of Assyria}* See 2 King. 16:10.

*{came unto him}* For Ahaz sent for him, 2 King. 16:7.

*{and distressed him}* I. He laid a heavy tax upon him, to pay which he was forced *to take the silver and gold that was found in the house of the Lord, and in the treasures of the Kings house*, 2 King. 16:8. II. Though he took great gifts to free Ahaz from the kings of Syria and Israel, yet he suffered the Syrian to take away a great city from Judah, namely Elath, 2 King. 16:6. III. He restored not to Ahaz anything that his enemies had taken away.
