In these and other respects Ahaz was much distressed by the king of Assyria, though he took Damascus, and slew the king thereof, and made great spoils in Israel, 2 King. 15:29 and 16:9.

*{but strengthened him not}* Afforded him no aid against his enemies.

**V. 21. ***{For Ahaz took away a portion out of the house of the LORD}* Or, *he took away the silver and gold that was found*, 2 King. 16:8.

*{and out of the house of the King}* Or, *that was in the treasures of the Kings house*, 2 King. 16:8.

*{and of the Princes}* He did not only spoil the Lord’s house and his own, but also the houses of his subjects.

*{and gave it unto the king of Assyria}* Or, *sent it for a present*, 2 King. 16:8.

*{but he helped him not}* See v. 20.

**V. 22. ***{And yet in the time of his distress did he trespass yet more against the LORD}* This argues an obdurate heart and impudent disposition, Isa. 1:5. For judgments are wont to turn men from their sins, and to make them afraid of persisting therein, Hos. 5:14, 15.

*{This is that king Ahaz}* Of all men this man is to be marked for an obstinate sinner, who was no whit wrought upon by afflictions, but even under judgments continued to sin, and thereby showed an irreclaimable disposition. Note him with a black coal. Some read, *he was still the same*. So is this phrase used, Ps. 102:27.

**V. 23. ***{For he sacrificed unto the gods of Damascus}* Heb. *Darmesek*. Idolaters, who forsake the true God, are prone to follow after all sorts of gods.

*{which smote him}* This was Ahaz his opinion of them. Of the Syrians smiting Judah see v. 5 and 2 King. 16:6.

*{and he said}* The wicked pretend reasons of their wickedness, though most absurd.

*{Because the gods of the Kings of Syria help them}* Ahaz accounts the idols of Syria to be gods, and to help those that worshiped them, herein showing himself like the heathen, Judg. 16:23, 24. Hab. 1:11.

*{therefore I will sacrifice to them}* Idolaters give the same worship to false gods that is due to the true God, Exod. 3:18 and 5:3.

*{that they might help me}* Idolatry so blinds men’s eyes as it makes them trust to vain helps. God had helped Ahaz when two kings came against Jerusalem, Isa. 7:3, 4, etc., yet he was not thereby moved to sacrifice to the Lord.

*{but they were the ruin of him and of all Israel}* Thereby they provoked God to give them over to their enemies. See ch. 25:14, 15.

**V. 24. ***{And Ahaz gathered together the vessels of the house of God}* This was sacrilege; for these were consecrated vessels. See 2 King. 16:8.

*{and cut in pieces the vessels of the house of God}* That they might never again be used to God’s service. If they had remained whole, they might again have been returned to God’s house, as Ezra 1:7, etc. This therefore aggravates his impiety.

*{and shut up the doors of the house of the LORD}* That none might enter thereinto to do the services of the Lord. It seems they were thus till the end of his days; for his son in the beginning of his reign *opened the doors of the Lord*, ch. 29:3.

*{and he made him altars in every corner of Jerusalem}* By corners are meant the heads of streets, where divers ways meet. So that which was done might be the more seen of passengers, as Matt. 6:5. He prohibited the true service of God, and exceeded in idolatrous worship.

**V. 25. ***{And in every several city of Judah}* Heb. *in every city and city*. He set up idolatry not only in the royal city, but dispersed it throughout his whole kingdom.

*{he made high places}* See 1 King. 3:2, 4.

*{to burn}* Or, *to offer*.

*{incense}* See 1 King. 9:25.

*{unto other gods}* See 1 King. 11:8 and 12:33.

*{and provoked to anger the LORD}* See 1 King. 26:2, 26.

*{God of his fathers}* See 2 Chr. 21:10.

**V. 26. ***{Now the rest of his acts, and of all his ways}* See ch. 27:7.

*{first and last}* See ch. 12:15.

*{behold, they are written in the book, etc.}* See ch. 16:11.

**V. 27. ***{And Ahaz slept with his fathers}* See 1 King. 2:10.

*{and they buried him in the city}* Namely, of David, 2 King. 16:20.

*{even in Jerusalem}* For the city of David was within the circuit of Jerusalem, 2 Sam. 5:6, 7.

*{but they brought him not into the sepulchres of the kings}* See ch. 24:25.

*{of Israel}* *Israel* is here put for *Judah*. See ch. 12:1, 6.

*{and Hezekiah his son reigned in his stead}* A blessed change. A most pious son succeeded a most impious father.

## CHAPTER 29

**Verse 1. ***{Hezekiah began to reign, etc.}* See 2 King. 18:1, 2. Hezekiah was the twelfth and the best king from the division. His history is recorded in four chapters.

*{and his mother’s name was Abijah}* Or, *Abi*, 2 King. 18:2.

**V. 2. ***{And he did that which was right, etc.}* See 2 King. 18:3.

**V. 3. ***{He in the first year of his reign, in the first month}* Yea, and the first day of that month, v. 17.

*{opened the doors of the house of the LORD}* For Ahaz his father had shut them up, ch. 28:24.

*{and repaired them}* Heb. *strengthened them*. When anything is decayed and out of repair, it is weakened; but when it is repaired, it is strengthened. Some refer *them* to the doors; but by a synecdoche it may be referred to all parts of the temple. Some refer it to the priests and Levites mentioned v. 4, *q.d.* The king strengthened and encouraged them. Howsoever the phrase be taken, it implies that Hezekiah’s heart was seasoned with piety, and that in his father’s days, seeing the very first work he began with after he was crowned was reformation of religion. A worthy pattern.

**V. 4. ***{And he brought in the Priests and the Levites}* Idolatrous Ahaz had forced them to abide in their own cities and suburbs, and thrust them out of the house of God; but now Hezekiah assembles them together for God’s service.

*{and gathered them together in the East street}* i.e. The street before the great door or entrance into the house of God. For that entrance was at the east.

**V. 5. ***{And said unto them, Hear me}* He instructs and directs them before he sets them on work. This was the part of a pious and prudent king.

*{ye Levites}* The priests also are comprised under this title; for they descended from Levi, Deut. 17:9, 18. Josh. 3:3 and 21:4, and they, as well as Levites, observed the direction, v. 16.

*{sanctify now yourselves}* See 1 Chr. 15:12, 14. They who are set apart to sanctify other things must themselves be first sanctified.

*{and sanctify the house of the LORD}* See 1 Chr. 23:13.

*{God of your fathers}* See 1 Chr. 29:18.

*{and carry forth the filthiness}* i.e. All idols, and all those things which idolaters used in their services, yea, and other things that were brought into the temple to pollute the true worship of God. Hereby it appears that *the sanctifying of the house* before mentioned signified especially *cleansing it*, v. 18, though divers rites also were used at the sanctifying of holy things, whereof see Exod. 40:9, etc. Lev. 8:10, 11.

*{out of the holy place}* Of the holy place here meant see v. 7.

**V. 6. ***{For our fathers have trespassed}* This good king looks back to the sins not only of his father, but also of his predecessors.

*{and done that which was evil in the eyes of the LORD our God}* They regarded not that knowledge which God had of their sins. And their such obstinacy lay the more heavy upon Hezekiah’s heart, because of the special relation betwixt God and them, implied in this phrase, *our God*.

*{and have forsaken him}* See ch. 12:5. 1 King. 11:33.

*{and have turned away their faces}* A sign of scorn and disdain. For men use to turn their faces from that which they cannot endure to look upon. See Jer. 2:27. Ezek. 8:16.

*{from the habitation of the LORD}* i.e. The house of the Lord, where he manifested his presence under sundry types; in which respect he is said *to dwell there*. See 1 Kings 8:13. This place is styled *an house of habitation for the Lord, and a place for his dwelling for ever*, ch. 6:2.

*{and turned their backs}* Heb. *given their neck*. Our English phrase of *turning the back* is an aggravation of the former of *turning away the face*. See Jer. 2:27.

**V. 7. ***{Also they have shut up the doors of the porch}* Of entrance into the temple. See 1 King. 6:3.

*{and put out the lamps}* They suffered them not to burn continually, as it was enjoined by the law, Lev. 24:2, 3.

*{and have not burnt incense}* This also was every day to be offered, Exod. 30:7, 8, and was an especial type of the reconciliation betwixt God and his people.

*{nor offered burnt offerings in the holy place}* These were likewise daily services to be performed, Exod. 29:38, etc. Lamps were lighted and incense burnt in the middle part of the temple next to the most holy; but burnt-offerings were offered in the priests’ court: therefore *holy place* here must indefinitely be taken for the temple and the courts appertaining thereto.

*{unto the God of Israel}* See 1 King. 8:15. This is added to aggravate the former impiety. All the foresaid services were withheld from the God of their ancient father from whom they had their name, and from the God of his posterity, of which they themselves were.

**V. 8. ***{Wherefore the wrath of the LORD, etc.}* See ch. 24:18.

*{and he hath delivered them to trouble}* Heb. *commotion*. See ch. 28:5, etc.

*{to astonishment}* See ch. 7:21.

*{and to hissing}* See 1 King. 9:8.

*{as you see with your eyes}* This speech was made by Hezekiah in the very beginning of his reign, v. 3, and the miseries here spoken of were in his father’s days: they therefore to whom he spoke saw them with their eyes.

**V. 9. ***{For lo, our fathers have fallen by the sword}* See ch. 18:6.

*{and our sons, and our daughters, and our wives}* He speaks as the head of the kingdom, and therefore uses the first person, *our*.

*{our captivity}* See ch. 28:5.

*{for this}* *This* may have relation either to the sins of their fathers, v. 6, 7, or to the destruction of their fathers, in the former part of this verse. The men being destroyed by the sword, their wives and children were taken captives.

**V. 10. ***{Now it is in mine heart}* See 1 King. 8:17.

*{to make a covenant}* See 2 King. 11:4.

*{with the LORD God of Israel}* See 1 King. 8:15.

*{that his fierce wrath}* See 2 King. 23:26.

*{may turn away from us}* Reformation is a special means of removing God’s wrath.

**V. 11. ***{My sons}* This title intimates the king’s affection to the priests and Levites, and their subjection to him.

*{be not now negligent}* Or, *be not now deceived*. The Hebrew word most commonly is used for *living quietly and securely*. It is oft translated *to prosper*, Jer. 12:1. Lam. 1:5. When it is put for *deceiving one*, as 2 King. 4:28, it signifies *to make one secure or careless*. Hereby the king implies that the work whereabout he set them was great and weighty, and that therefore they should not be deceived in thinking it might easily be done, and thereupon be careless and negligent.

*{for the LORD hath chosen you}* Num. 8:14 and 18:2, 6. Deut. 10:8.

*{to stand before him}* To remain in the house where he manifested his presence.

*{to serve him, and that you should minister unto him}* By performing the ordinances of his house.

*{and burn incense}* Or, *offer sacrifice*. See 1 King. 3:4, 15 and 9:25.

**V. 12. ***{Then the Levites arose}* This phrase implies a ready and speedy enterprising of a matter, and shows that the Levites were moved with the king’s speech, and were glad of the opportunity they had had to restore the service of the Lord.

*{Mahath the son of Amasai, etc.}* Here are fourteen described by their names, parentage and families, that set themselves to this work.

**V. 15. ***{And they gathered their brethren}* See v. 34. 1 Chr. 5:13. By *brethren* are meant Levites.

*{and sanctified themselves}* Not only the fourteen, v. 12, etc., but also the rest of the Levites that were gathered together, sanctified themselves. Hereof see v. 5.

*{and came according to the commandment of the King}* Not only the thing itself, but the very will of the king moved them to do it.

*{by the words of the LORD}* Or, *in the business of the Lord*. The word properly signifies *words*, yet it is oft put for *things* or *businesses*, Exod. 28:26; it is translated *causes*, Ex. 5:23, *work*. In the former reading it implies that the Levites were moved to do what the king required, the rather because his charge was grounded on the word or law of the Lord; in the latter, that the king’s word prevailed the more with them because it was the business and work of the Lord which he enjoined.

*{to cleanse the house of the LORD}* See v. 5.

**V. 16. ***{And the Priests went into the inner part of the house of the LORD}* i.e. That which was called the *Holy place*, where the golden altar for incense, the golden candlestick, and the golden tables were. Some understand the most holy place, where the ark was. Others, that which is called *the inner court*, 1 King. 6:36, where the great brazen altar stood. Certainly that which they began first to cleanse is here especially intended, to wit, the holy place, if not also the most holy.

*{to cleanse it}* As v. 15.

*{and brought out all the uncleanness that they found in the temple of the LORD}* That which is called *filthiness* v. 5 is here called *uncleanness*. And by *the Temple of the Lord* is meant both the inner part before mentioned, and also the priests’ court.

*{into the court of the house of the LORD}* Some understand the outermost court, where unclean persons and strangers stood.

*{And the Levites took it}* The Levites being to attend upon the priests, and to do the most servile works appertaining to the temple, took such filthiness as the priests had brought into the outward courts.

*{to carry it out abroad into the brook Kidron}* See 1 King. 2:37 and 2 King. 23:6, 12. No doubt but they broke the images and other like things to pieces, yea, and stamped them to dust, before they cast them into that brook; as Exod. 32:20.

**V. 17. ***{Now they began on the first day of the first month}* On this day the king gave his charge to them, v. 3, 5, and on the same day they began the work; which is an evidence of their willingness and readiness to do it.

*{to sanctify}* See on v. 5.

*{and on the eighth day of the month}* Much filthiness in the days of Ahaz was brought into the house of the Lord; which made them so long in cleansing it.

*{came they to the porch of the LORD}* i.e. The entrance into the holy place, described 1 King. 6:3, so as they were eight days in cleansing three parts of the temple, the most holy, the holy place, and the porch.

*{so they sanctified the house of the LORD in eight days}* *The house of the Lord* is here strictly taken for that great building of stone commonly called *the Temple*, consisting of those three parts before mentioned.

*{and in the sixteenth day of the first month}* Besides the forementioned eight days they spent other eight days in cleansing the courts appertaining to the house of the Lord.

*{they made an end}* This has reference not only to the places which they cleansed, but also to the holy things in those places, mentioned v. 18, 19. Yea it further implies both removing all manner of uncleanness from them, and also using such rites as God had appointed for sanctifying holy places and holy things, Exod. 40:9, etc.

**V. 18. ***{Then they went in to Hezekiah the king}* They knew their message would be welcome to the king, and therefore so soon as ever the work was done they give him notice thereof.

*{and said, We have cleansed all the house of the LORD}* Both the temple itself, and the courts appertaining thereunto.

*{and the altar of burnt offering}* This stood in the priests’ court. And thereon was all manner of sacrifice offered.

*{with all the vessels thereof}* Or, *appertaining thereunto*: as the sea and other lavers to wash holy things in, besoms, basins, tongs, with the like. See 1 King. 7:43, etc.

*{and the shewbread table}* See 1 King. 7:48.

*{with all the vessels thereof}* The instruments of gold appertaining to the holy place used to be laid on tables of gold. See 1 King. 7:50. These are the vessels here meant.

**V. 19. ***{Moreover all the vessels which king Ahaz in his reign did cast away}* Ahaz cut some vessels in pieces, and sent them to the king of Assyria, ch. 28:21, 24. Others he reserved whole, and took them out of the temple, and either put them to idolatrous uses, or laid them aside in other places; and in that respect he is said *to cast them away*. These are here meant.

*{in his transgression}* This has especial reference to his idolatry, which God accounts a most grievous transgression.

*{have we prepared and sanctified}* Prepared for use, and sanctified to their proper holy use.

*{and behold, they are before the altar of the LORD}* There all the holy vessels were set together, that they might be seen to be prepared and sanctified; and then removed to their several proper places.

**V. 20. ***{Then Hezekiah the king rose early}* Betimes in the morning of the next day. And hereby he showed his zeal of God’s glory, and his earnest desire of reconciliation betwixt God and his people.

*{and gathered the rulers of the city}* Before he gathered the priests and Levites only, v. 4, because it belonged to their office to sanctify holy places and things. Now he assembles the representative body of the kingdom, which were heads of places and families. For under this word *city* the kingdom of Judah is comprised.

*{and went up to the house of the LORD}* This was the place where reconciliation was to be made.

**V. 21. ***{And they brought seven bullocks and seven rams}* See 1 Chr. 15:26.

*{and seven lambs, and seven he goats}* Thus they brought of all sorts of clean beasts enjoined under the law, because their offering was not only for consecrating of the temple and holy things, but also for all kind of sacrifices, and for all sorts of people, for king and princes, for priests and Levites, and the common sort, even for the whole kingdom.

*{for a sin offering}* See 1 King. 3:15. The end of the sin-offering was to make an atonement for sins committed against God. Sometimes bullocks were offered, sometimes goats, sometimes lambs, Lev. 4:3, 23, 32, sometimes rams, Lev. 5:15.

*{for the kingdom}* i.e. Such as governed the kingdom. And thus this word *kingdom* is distinguished from *Judah* following.

*{and for the sanctuary}* *Sanctuary* is here to be taken in as large a sense as *all the house of the Lord* v. 18.

*{and for Judah}* i.e. The common people therein.

*{and he commanded the Priests the sons of Aaron}* To them only it belonged to offer sacrifices, Lev. 1:5, 7, 8. 1 Chr. 6:49.

*{to offer them on the altar of the LORD}* It is enjoined that offerings be burnt upon the altar, Lev. 1:9, 13, 17. *And the altar sanctified the gift*, Matt. 23:19, and in this respect God is said *to sanctifie the altar*, Exod. 29:44.

**V. 22. ***{So they killed the bullocks}* Others than priests might kill the sacrifices.

*{and the Priests received the blood, and sprinkled it on the altar}* This act was proper to the priests, Levit. 1:5. By it was typified the application of Christ’s sacrifice for the remission of sins. See 2 King. 16:13.

*{likewise when they had killed the rams, they sprinkled, etc.}* The same rite was to be observed, whatsoever kind of beast was offered: for all the sacrifices typified Christ’s blood, and the application thereof for pardon of sin, Heb. 9:21, 22. Lev. 1:5, 11.

**V. 23. ***{And they brought forth}* Heb. *near*. Namely, to the altar. The relative *they* has reference to the priests.

*{the he goats for the sin offering}* Sometimes one bullock, or one goat, male or female, or one lamb, was sufficient for a sin-offering, as Levit. 4:3, 14, 23, 28, 32, but that was for one particular sin; but here a sin-offering was to be made for king, princes, priests, and the whole land, and that for many most heinous sins many years together committed, namely all the days of King Ahaz: and therefore seven goats are offered, and all of them males.

*{before the king and the congregation}* By *congregation* all sorts of subjects great and mean, priests and others are meant.

*{and laid their hands upon them}* This was a rite enjoined Lev. 1:4 and 4:15. The end of it was to testify an acknowledgment of their sins, and that the sacrifice was offered up in their stead; and thus was their faith in Christ nourished.

**V. 24. ***{And the Priests killed them}* After the congregation had laid their hands on them.

*{and they made reconciliation with their blood, etc.}* This was a type of the reconciliation betwixt God and believers by the blood of Christ, Col. 1:20.

*{to make an atonement}* See 1 Chr. 6:49.

*{for all Israel}* *Israel* is here put for *Judah*, as ch. 12:1, 6.

*{for the king commanded that the burnt offering and the sin offering should be made for all Israel}* It is not probable that the king himself had yielded to the idolatry committed in his father’s days; yet he well knew that the whole kingdom had yielded thereto, and therefore his care was that reconciliation might be made for all, that so the plaster might be as large as the sore.

**V. 25. ***{And he set the Levites in the house of the LORD}* No doubt but he placed porters and other officers in those places that properly belonged to them, but his special care was to have the praises of the Lord solemnly set forth: therefore the Levites here meant were such as sung praises to God.

*{with Cymbals, with Psalteries and with harps}* See 1 Chr. 13:8.

*{according to the commandment of David}* See 1 Chr. 16:4 and 25:1, etc.

*{and of Gad the king’s Seer}* See 1 Chr. 21:9.

*{and Nathan the Prophet}* See 1 Chr. 17:1. David, Gad and Nathan were all inspired by God’s Spirit, and joined their counsels together about the worship of God.

*{for so was the commandment of the LORD}* Heb. *by the hand of the Lord*. This is spoken of God after the manner of man. Men do what they do by their hands; so what is done or said by God, is said to be *by his hand*.

*{by his Prophets}* Heb. *by the hand of his Prophets*. See 1 King. 8:53, 56. By this it appears that God sent directions to David by his prophets about the ordering of his worship.

**V. 26. ***{And the Levites stood with instruments of David}* i.e. Such as David by God’s Spirit appointed. See 1 Chr. 13:8 and 23:5.

*{and the Priests with the trumpets}* See Num. 10:8. 1 Chr. 15:24 and 16:6.

**V. 27. ***{And Hezekiah commanded to offer the burnt offering upon the altar}* Good kings of old, being well instructed in the law, gave directions to priests and Levites in sundry circumstances what to do.

*{and when}* Heb. *in the time*.

*{the burnt offering began}* When it was laid on the altar upon the wood, and the flame and smoke began to arise.

*{the song of the LORD began also}* The 136th Psalm, which is a solemn song of praise, every verse whereof ends with setting out the mercy of God, is here meant, and by an excellency called *the song of the Lord*. See ch. 7:3.

*{with the trumpets}* They sounded while the singers sung with their voice.

*{and with the instruments, etc.}* Heb. *hands of instruments*. Parts of a man’s body are metaphorically applied to senseless instruments, because they seemed to act this and that. They had variety of music, voice, trumpets, and other instruments, to quicken up their spirits the more in praising God.

**V. 28. ***{And all the congregation worshipped}* See ch. 7:3.

*{and the singers sang}* Heb. *the song sang*. Some here understand the master of music. But by a metonymy the effect may be put for the actor, *song* for him or them that sang.

*{and the trumpeters sounded}* See v. 26.

*{and all this continued until the burnt offering was finished}* Every one continued to testify his thankful heart and cheerful spirit till the fire had consumed the sacrifice on the altar.

**V. 29. ***{And when they had made an end of offering}* This was their most public service, whereby they were directed when to begin, and when to end.

*{the king and all that were present with him}* Heb. *found with him*. See ch. 5:11 and 1 Chr. 29:17.

*{bowed themselves and worshipped}* See ch. 7:3.

**V. 30. ***{Moreover Hezekiah the king, and the Princes}* In matters of worship expressly prescribed the pious king joins himself with the people; in matters of direction he takes advice with his princes.

*{commanded the Levites}* They did not command that which for matter was not prescribed, but they commanded God’s ministers to do their duty.

*{to sing praise to the LORD}* Besides that praise mentioned v. 27. For this was after the offering was ended.

*{with the words of David and of Asaph the Seer}* See 2 King. 17:13. By this title it appears that Asaph was inspired by God. Some Psalms carry the title, *Of Asaph*, so as if he indited not the matter, yet he put the words into a meet and melodious tune. See 1 Chr. 6:39 and 16:5.

*{and they sang praises with gladness}* Both the singers themselves, and they that heard them, rejoiced.

*{and they bowed their heads and worshipped}* See v. 28, 29. This they did after their second solemn praising of God.

**V. 31. ***{Then Hezekiah answered, and said}* So full of zeal was this good king’s heart, as he contented not himself with all that was done before, though it were very much, but stirred them up to further praising of God.

*{Now you have consecrated, etc.}* Or, *filled your hands*. See 1 King. 13:33.

*{come near, and bring sacrifices, etc.}* He had before given directions and exhortations to the priests and Levites, now he calls upon the people to do their duty. *Sacrifices* were for expiation of their sins; *thank-offerings* to testify their grateful acknowledgment of God’s mercy to them.

*{And the congregation brought in sacrifices and thank offerings}* They were wrought upon by the king’s exhortation, and readily did what he required.

*{and as many as were of a free heart}* This is spoken comparatively; such as were of a more free and forward spirit than the rest.

*{burnt offerings}* There was more respect to God manifested in bringing burnt-offerings than in the rest. For in the other the offerers themselves had a part, but in these they had none; for all was burnt upon the altar. See 1 King. 3:15.

**V. 32. ***{And the number of the burnt offerings which the congregation brought}* This word *Congregation* is distinguished from *the King*, v. 23, but here it comprises king and all under it: but we may not think the king brought no free offerings.

*{was threescore and ten bullocks, etc.}* This was a very great offering, but not comparable to sundry others. See 1 King. 8:63. But so soon after the whole land had apostatized it was a wonder the people should be so forward.

**V. 33. ***{And the consecrated things}* i.e. Such gratulatory sacrifices as they had by voluntary vows consecrated to the Lord. Others take them to be such sacrifices as were now consecrated, but reserved to be offered at another time.

*{were six hundred oxen, and three thousand sheep}* This added much to the number mentioned v. 32, and much amplified their zeal.

**V. 34. ***{But the Priests were too few}* Some delayed to come, and others to use the rites of consecration, either out of shame for their former idolatry, or for want of thorough repentance.

*{so that they could not slay all the burnt offerings}* To slay and flay beasts, and to cut them in pieces, and lay them on the altar, were works proper to priests. See on Lev. 1:5, 6.

*{wherefore their brethren the Levites}* Priests and Levites came all from the same father, Levi, and in that respect are styled *brethren*, v. 15.

*{did help them}* Heb. *strengthened them*. The Levites by undertaking part of the work did enable those few priests that were there the better to hold out. This they did in this particular case, being somewhat extraordinary. For the Levites were given as an help to the priests.

*{till the work was ended}* i.e. The offering up of those sacrifices.

*{and until the other Priests had sanctified themselves}* This implies that the Levites continued at other times also to help the priests. Of *sanctifying themselves* see v. 5.

*{for the Levites were more upright in heart to sanctify themselves than the Priests}* Persons of lower rank may be more hearty, zealous and forward in the work of the Lord than their superiors.

**V. 35. ***{And also the burnt offerings were in abundance}* See v. 32. This is added as another reason why the Levites helped the priests, because the sacrifices of all sorts were very many.

*{with the fat of the peace offerings}* See 1 King. 8:64. The fat was everywhere to be pulled off and burnt, Ex. 29:13, which caused much more work.

*{and the drink offerings for every burnt offering}* Or, *with every burnt-offering*. See 1 King. 3:15.

*{So the service of the house of the LORD was set in order}* i.e. The daily services to be performed time after time. The doors of the temple were shut up in the days of Ahaz, and the services thereof clean omitted, but now settled in their due courses again.

**V. 36. ***{And Hezekiah rejoiced, and all the people}* So did David and his people, 1 Chr. 29:9.

*{that God had prepared the people}* That he had put such grace and zeal into their hearts as before set down. It is God that prepares the heart to good.

*{for the thing was done suddenly}* i.e. Very speedily, sooner than could have been expected. They had long been blinded and led aside to idolatry: therefore to have their hearts so soon and so thoroughly changed was an extraordinary work of God’s Spirit; and the consideration thereof did much quicken and revive their spirits.

## CHAPTER 30

**Verse 1. ***{And Hezekiah}* Here is set down a solemn celebration of the Passover in this good king’s time.

*{sent to all Israel and Judah}* i.e. To all under his command, namely the tribes of Judah and Benjamin, and also those who out of other tribes had come and joined themselves with the kingdom of Judah. See ch. 12:13, 16. To these Hezekiah sent messengers.

*{and wrote letters also to Ephraim and Manasseh}* Under these two tribes are comprised so many of the ten tribes as the king of Assyria had left in the land of Israel. For he had carried many of them away captives into other lands, 2 King. 15:29. These being the chief of the ten tribes, are put for all the rest. By letters he invites these that they might the better accept thereof.

*{that they should come to the house of the LORD at Jerusalem}* For there that great feast whereunto he invited them was to be kept, Deut. 16:5, 6.

*{to keep the Passover}* This was a solemn feast to be kept every year by the Israelites in memorial of God’s delivering their ancestors from bondage in Egypt, and of his passing over the Israelites when he destroyed all the firstborn in Egypt, as the notation of the word shows, Exod. 12:12, 13, 14. It was also one of their ordinary sacraments till the coming of Christ, of whose sacrifice the Paschal lamb was an especial type, 1 Cor. 5:7.

*{unto the LORD}* See 2 King. 23:21.

*{God of Israel}* See 1 King. 8:15. He here sets out God by this relation to give them to understand that they were the children of that father whose God the Lord was in special manner, and to whose seed he extended his promises, Gen. 28:14 and 35:10, 12.

**V. 2. ***{For the king had taken counsel, and his Princes, and all the Congregation in Jerusalem}* Under these are comprised that assembly which ordinarily met about matters of church and state, and was the representative body of the whole kingdom. At Jerusalem this assembly used to meet together.

*{to keep the Passover in the second month}* The first month was appointed Exod. 12:2, etc., yet in some extraordinary cases it was permitted to keep it in the second, Num. 9:11.

**V. 3. ***{For they could not keep it at that time}* Namely, in the first month.

*{because the Priests had not sanctified themselves}* See ch. 29:5.

*{sufficiently}* This may have reference either to the number of the priests: there were but few that had sanctified themselves, ch. 29:34, so as there were not enough to offer that great Passover which was intended. Or, *to the rites of sanctification: they had not fully observed all*. This was one reason of putting off the Passover.

*{neither had the people gathered themselves together to Jerusalem}* See v. 1. This was another reason of putting off the Passover. Two such reasons as are here set down are alleged where the dispensation is granted, Num. 9:10, 11.

**V. 4. ***{And the thing}* Or, *this thing*. Namely, that the Passover should be put off to the second month.

*{pleased the King}* Heb. *was right in the eyes of the king*. That which seems right in good men’s eyes, pleases them. A like phrase is applied to God, 1 King. 3:10.

*{and all the congregation}* Under this word *Congregation* the princes also are comprised, who are distinguished from it v. 2.

**V. 5. ***{So they established a decree to make a proclamation throughout all Israel}* All the twelve tribes are here meant. See 1 King. 15:22.

*{from Beersheba even to Dan}* From south to north, 1 King. 4:25.

*{that they should come to keep the Passover, etc.}* See v. 5.

*{for they had not done it of a long time in such sort as it was written}* According to the prescript of the law, Exod. 12:1, etc. The ten tribes after their revolt from Judah clean cast off the law of the Lord, and Judah had neglected it all the days of Ahaz. If they kept the Passover, they kept it not according to the law: for the doors of God’s house were shut up, ch. 28:24.

**V. 6. ***{So the Posts went with the letters}* For more speed.

*{from the king}* Heb. *from the hand of the king*. By his appointment.

*{and his Princes}* To show their consent with the king.

*{throughout all Israel and Judah}* See v. 1.

*{and according to the commandment of the king}* *The commandment* has reference to the posts whom the king sent, and commanded to make all speed. They of Israel were invited and entreated as the words following show.

*{saying, Ye children of Israel}* This is a friendly compellation and pithy insinuation.

*{turn again unto the LORD}* They had revolted from the Lord, therefore he advises them to repent, and turn to him.

*{God of Abraham, Isaac and Israel}* See 1 Chr. 29:18.

*{and he will return}* In grace and mercy, Ps. 90:13. Zech. 1:3. God deals with men according to their dealing with him, 1 Sam. 2:30. Jer. 18:8, 10.

*{to the remnant of you, that are escaped out of the hand of the kings of Assyria}* i.e. Such as remained in the land after it was subdued by Pul and Tilgath-pilneser, who carried many captives away, 1 Chr. 5:26. 2 King. 15:29. Shalmaneser’s invasion, 2 King. 17:3, 6, was after this: for this Passover was in the first year of Hezekiah, and that captivity in the sixth, 2 King. 18:10.

**V. 7. ***{And be not like your fathers}* By *fathers* are meant such Israelites as revolted from Judah, from the beginning of the apostasy till those present times. Hezekiah would not have those to whom he wrote to continue in the idolatry of their fathers, nor to be obstinate as they were, Zech. 1:4.

*{and like your brethren}* i.e. Such Israelites as lived in those times.

*{which trespassed against the LORD}* By continuing in their fathers’ steps.

*{God of their fathers}* See ch. 13:12 and 24:18.

*{who therefore gave them up to desolation}* 2 King. 15:29.

*{as ye see}* See ch. 29:8.

**V. 8. ***{Now be ye not stiff-necked, etc.}* Heb. *harden not your necks*. See 2 King. 17:14.

*{but yield yourselves}* Heb. *give the hand*. By giving the hand willing subjection is testified.

*{unto the LORD}* For they had forsaken him by their apostasy from the house of God and from the house of David, 2 King. 12:26, etc.

*{and enter into his Sanctuary}* See ch. 20:8.

*{which he hath sanctified for ever}* See ch. 7:16.

*{and serve the LORD your God}* Under this word *serve* all divine worship and obedience to God is comprised. That particular relation, *your God*, is a strong motive to enforce the duty.

*{that the fierceness of his wrath may turn away from you}* See ch. 29:10 and 28:11, 13. 2 King. 23:26.

**V. 9. ***{For if ye turn again unto the LORD}* See v. 6. 1 King. 8:33.

*{your brethren and your children}* True penitency of some may prove beneficial to others, Josh. 6:23.

*{shall find compassion before them that lead them captive}* See 1 King. 8:50.

*{so that they shall come again into this land}* i.e. The land of Israel. God can so work upon enemies as to move them to let their captives go free, ch. 28:14, 15.

*{for the LORD your God is gracious and merciful}* See Exod. 35:5.

*{and will not turn away his face from you}* This is attributed to God after the manner of man. When a man is angry with one, or scorns him, or will not grant his request, he uses to turn away his face. By *not turning away the face* is implied a gracious acceptance, and a readiness to hear and to do good.

*{if ye return unto him}* Repentance in a sinner is an evidence of God’s mercy to him, ch. 7:14.

**V. 10. ***{So the Posts passed from city to city}* Many messengers went several ways, and that with speed, leaving their message in every place they passed by.

*{through the country of Ephraim and Manasseh}* See v. 1.

*{even unto Zebulun}* Zebulun was by the seaside, Gen. 49:13, so as this was one of their utmost coasts.

*{but they}* Not all of them, v. 11, but it may be most of them.

*{laughed them to scorn and mocked them}* They accounted God’s ordinances but light and small matters, and therefore thought them but fools that invited them to go out of their own country thereunto. This caused them to laugh and mock.

**V. 11. ***{Nevertheless divers of Asher and Manasseh and of Zebulun humbled themselves and came to Jerusalem}* They whose hearts are broken for sin, are quickly drawn to the place where mercy may be found.

**V. 12. ***{Also in Judah}* As well as in the tribes of Israel.

*{the hand of God}* The powerful work of God’s Spirit.

*{was to give them one heart}* Or, *to make them be of one mind*.

*{to do the commandment of the king and of the Princes}* See v. 6.

*{by the word of the LORD}* The king’s commandment was grounded on God’s word, and thereupon the more readily obeyed.

**V. 13. ***{And there assembled at Jerusalem much people to keep the feast of unleavened bread}* i.e. The Passover. See ch. 8:13.

*{in the second month}* See v. 2.

*{a very great Congregation}* This shows that the hand of God was manifested herein, as is noted v. 12.

**V. 14. ***{And they arose}* i.e. The king, princes, and others. See ch. 29:20. 1 King. 14:2, 4.

*{and took away the altars that were in Jerusalem}* Those which Ahaz had made, ch. 28:24.

*{and all the altars for incense took they away}* God had one altar for burnt-offerings, and another for incense, but idolaters would exceed, and had many altars for both. See Jer. 11:13.

*{and cast them into the brook Kidron}* See ch. 29:16.

**V. 15. ***{Then they killed the Passover}* i.e. The Paschal lamb, ch. 35:11. Exod. 12:21. See on Exod. 12:6.

*{on the fourteenth day of the second month}* Num. 9:11.

*{and the Priests and the Levites were ashamed}* Such of them as had been backward, ch. 29:34, were now ashamed of that backwardness, and the more, because they observed the great forwardness not only of other Levites, but of many of the common people.

*{and sanctified themselves}* See ch. 29:5.

*{and brought in the burnt offerings into the house of the LORD}* Before they were sanctified they might not do this, ch. 29:34, but so soon as they were sanctified, they did what belonged to their office.

**V. 16. ***{And they stood in their place}* Heb. *their standing*. i.e. That proper place wherein each order was appointed to stand.

*{after their manner}* As they were accustomed before Ahaz his time, who put them all out of order. The porters stood in their places; the singers in theirs; the Levites that assisted the priests, in theirs.

*{according to the law of Moses}* i.e. Those ordinances about the Levites’ functions which God appointed Moses to deliver.

*{the man of God}* See 1 Chr. 23:14.

*{the Priests sprinkled the blood}* See ch. 29:22.

*{which they received of the hand of the Levites}* The Levites held the basins to receive the blood from the beasts that were slain, and having fitted it for sprinkling they gave it to the priests.

**V. 17. ***{For there were many in the Congregation}* Some take fathers of families to be here meant by *many*. Others apply it to the priests of whom mention is made ch. 23:34.

*{that were not sanctified}* See ch. 29:4, 5.

*{therefore the Levites had the charge}* See ch. 29:34.

*{of the killing of the Passovers}* i.e. Both the Paschal lambs and kids, which were the most proper offerings at that feast, v. 15, and other sacrifices also, which were then offered in great abundance, v. 24. When first the Passover was instituted, every house or family was to offer a lamb or a kid, Exod. 12:3, 5. But after the law enjoined all the families to go up to the house of the Lord, there to celebrate the Passover, Deut. 16:6, 16, many lambs or kids were to be offered. Hence the plural number, *passovers*, is used.

*{for every one that was not clean}* The Levites that were sanctified offered the Passover instead of them that were not sanctified, and should have done that work if they had been sanctified.

*{to sanctify them unto the LORD}* i.e. To keep the sacrifices from being polluted, as they would have been if unsanctified persons had offered them.

**V. 18. ***{For a multitude of the people}* That came to celebrate the Passover.

*{even many of Ephraim and Manasseh, Issachar and Zebulun}* These tribes are mentioned to show that they who came unprepared were of the kingdom of Israel, who had from the time of the division been without law and without priests to instruct them.

*{had not cleansed themselves}* According to those rites which were by the law enjoined to such as should eat the Passover.

*{yet did they eat the Passover}* Their ignorance made them overbold in adventuring upon sacred ordinances.

*{otherwise then it was written}* In the law of Moses.

*{But Hezekiah prayed for them}* God gave some visible evidence of displeasure against them, which Hezekiah observing prayed for them.

*{saying, The good LORD pardon every one}* Their want of due preparation was a sin; therefore he craves pardon: for obtaining which, he pleads God’s goodness.

**V. 19. ***{That prepareth his heart to seek God}* That in truth of heart sets himself to serve and please God. See 1 Chr. 16:11.

*{the LORD God of his fathers}* See ch. 15:12 and 17:4.

*{though he be not cleansed}* Though through ignorance or negligence he have failed in the use of the external rites of cleansing.

*{according to the purification of the Sanctuary}* i.e. Such means of purifying as are prescribed to such as come to God’s holy place.

**V. 20. ***{And the LORD hearkened to Hezekiah, and healed the people}* This may be taken either of an inward healing, or of an outward: he forgave their sin, and inwardly by his Spirit sanctified and cleansed them, or accepted them as cleansed; or, he removed some judgment that he had inflicted on them for transgressing his law.

**V. 21. ***{And the children of Israel that were present at Jerusalem}* Heb. *found at Jerusalem*. See ch. 29:29.

*{kept the feast of unleavened bread seven days}* Exod. 12:15.

*{with great gladness}* One special cause of their gladness was the evidence that was given of God’s accepting them. See 1 Chron. 29:22.

*{and the Levites and the Priests praised the LORD day by day}* i.e. Those days wherein this feast was celebrated.

*{singing with loud instruments}* Heb. *instruments of strength*. Instruments which make a loud sound must be strong, able to hold a strong blast. Some take this to be meant of setting forth the strength or power of the Lord.

*{unto the LORD}* To his praise and glory.

**V. 22. ***{And Hezekiah spake comfortably unto all the Levites}* Heb. *to the heart of all the Levites*. Gen. 34:3. Isa. 40:2. Hos. 2:14. He instructed them in the free grace and rich mercy of God, who accepts of those that are upright in heart, though they have some outward failings. And this could not but much comfort them.

*{that taught the good knowledge of the LORD}* Or, *instructed men in such things as made them understand the good will of God*, Rom. 12:2.

*{and they did eat throughout the feast, etc.}* i.e. Every day of the feast.

*{offering peace offerings}* See 1 King. 3:15.

*{and making confession, etc.}* Namely, of their sins; as Matt. 3:6.

**V. 23. ***{And the whole assembly}* King, priests, princes, and others then assembled at Jerusalem.

*{took counsel to keep other seven days}* They advised together and agreed about that point; because they observed people still to flock more and more to the house of God.

*{and they kept other seven days}* This was besides the first law: but because the case was extraordinary, the Lord accepted their holy zeal.

*{with gladness}* See v. 21.

**V. 24. ***{For Hezekiah king of Judah did give}* Heb. *lifted up*. Or, *offered*, or, *reserved*.

*{to the congregation}* That they might offer part to the Lord, and eat the remainder in those days of feasting.

*{a thousand bullocks, and seven thousand sheep}* A very bountiful gift, testifying his zeal of God’s glory, and good respect to his people.

*{and the Princes gave to the Congregation, etc.}* Piety in kings puts on subjects to imitate them.

*{and a great number of Priests sanctified themselves}* Though they were too backward before, v. 3 and ch. 29:34, yet seeing the great need of their pains, and beholding the zeal of others, they put on themselves to help forward the service of the Lord. See ch. 29:5.

**V. 25. ***{And all the Congregation of Judah, etc.}* The people which came from all parts of that kingdom to this feast.

*{and all the congregation that came out of Israel}* From sundry parts of that kingdom.

*{and the strangers, etc.}* i.e. Such of other nations as had been circumcised upon their conversion to the Jews’ religion. See Exod. 12:48.

*{rejoiced}* Outwardly testified their inward joy for God’s grace in them and blessing on them: ch. 24:10 and 29:36.

**V. 26. ***{So there was great joy in Jerusalem}* See 1 Chron. 12:40 and 29:9.

*{for since the time of Solomon}* Because Solomon was the last king of all the tribes united, he is by name mentioned.

*{the son of David}* See ch. 1:1.

*{king of Israel}* i.e. Of all the twelve tribes. David and Solomon were kings over them all; so as this title may be applied to either.

*{there was not the like in Jerusalem}* For since the division no such companies out of the ten tribes came to Jerusalem to solemnize that feast. Neither was there such an occasion before given of doubling the days of the feast.

**V. 27. ***{Then the Priests the Levites}* i.e. The priests that descended from Levi, not such as Jeroboam made, 1 King. 13:33.

*{arose}* See ch. 29:20.

*{and blessed the people}* According to divine institution, Num. 6:23.

*{and their voice was heard}* Of God. That blessing which they pronounced God accomplished.

*{and their prayer came up to his holy dwellingplace}* Heb. *to the habitation of his holiness*. The very presence of God makes the place holy where he is. And as other excellencies of God are most manifested in the place here meant, so is his holiness.

*{even unto heaven}* This is added to show what place is meant.

## CHAPTER 31

**Verse 1. ***{Now when all this was finished}* i.e. Hezekiah’s repairing and sanctifying the house and things of God, ch. 29:3, etc., and his solemn celebration of the Passover, ch. 30:1, etc. In this chapter his care in settling the courses of the priests and Levites, and providing maintenance for them, is related.

*{all Israel that were present}* Heb. *found*. See ch. 30:21.

*{went out to the cities of Judah}* i.e. The other cities of the kingdom of Judah, besides Jerusalem.

*{and brake the images in pieces}* Heb. *statues*. That which is ascribed to Hezekiah, 2 King. 18:4, is here ascribed to the people. The king was the principal author and commander of what was done; but the people were the immediate actors.

*{and cut down the groves}* See 1 King. 16:33.

*{and threw down the high places}* Idolatrous high places are here meant. See 1 King. 3:3, 4.

*{and the Altars}* Such as had been built by idolaters. See chap. 30:14.

*{out of all Judah and Benjamin}* These places were under his own dominion.

*{in Ephraim and Manasseh}* Such cities in those tribes are here meant as the kings of Judah had formerly taken from Israel, ch. 13:19 and 15:8, and such as of their own accord had joined themselves to the kingdom of Judah. And by reason of the many troubles in Israel many in that kingdom did submit themselves to the king of Judah.

*{until they had utterly destroyed them all}* Heb. *until to make an end*. Which Hebraism is well explained by our translators. The reformation was not partial, or by halves, as we speak, but a thorough reformation.

*{Then all the children of Israel}* All that came to the Passover both out of Judah and Benjamin, and also out of other tribes, ch. 30:11.

*{returned every man to his possession into their own cities}* They had done that at Jerusalem for which they came, and therefore returned home to their own callings.

**V. 2. ***{And Hezekiah appointed the courses, etc.}* See ch. 8:14. 1 Chr. 23:6 and 24:1.

*{the Priests and Levites for burnt offerings and for peace offerings}* See 1 King. 3:15. In offering these the Levites were helpful to the priests, and therefore are here joined with them.

*{to minister}* By doing the service which belonged to their places.

*{and to give thanks, and to praise}* See 1 Chr. 25:3.

*{in the gates}* The Levites used to stand with their musical instruments at the entrance into the priests’ court, here called *the gates*, that so the people might not only hear but also see their comely order in giving solemn praise to God.

*{of the tents of the LORD}* i.e. The temple, or house of the Lord. For the priests and Levites there kept watch and ward, as soldiers use to do in their camp. In this respect they which did the service in the temple are styled *an host*, Num. 4:3. Besides, all the people assembled thither at sundry times, as soldiers to their camp, or to their tents therein. Or this metaphor may be here used in allusion to the former house of God, which was a tent or tabernacle, Exod. 35:11. *Tents* are here mentioned in the plural number in regard of the several courts that appertained to the temple (see 1 King. 6:36), or in regard of the several chambers and other places that were as tents in a camp.

**V. 3. ***{He appointed also the king’s portion of his substance, for the burnt offerings}* The people were wont to bring burnt-offerings to the priests upon sundry occasions, Levit. 1:2, etc., and 22:18, etc., but by the inroads of enemies in the time of Ahaz they were so impoverished that now they could not. Therefore that the service of the Lord might not be neglected, the king took order that out of his own substance, even that which was proper to himself, there might be supply made from time to time of that which was requisite for the services of the temple. And this he did the rather, because the treasures of the temple were exhausted by his father, ch. 28:21, 24. Herein he showed himself like unto David, 1 Chr. 29:3.

*{to wit, for the morning and evening burnt offerings, etc.}* See Num. 28 and 29.

*{as it is written in the law of the LORD}* This was the good king’s rule, and he held close thereto.

**V. 4. ***{Moreover he commanded the people that dwelt in Jerusalem}* Those that dwelt in that royal city and the places adjacent might think themselves privileged and exempted from allowing anything to the services of the temple: wherefore to prevent such conceits, the king lays a strait command upon them. Or by *Jerusalem* may be meant all the kingdom of Judah, as *Samaria* is put for the kingdom of Israel, 1 King. 18:2.

*{to give the portion of the Priests and the Levites}* Such maintenance as belonged to them by law, Num. 18:8, etc.

*{that they might be encouraged in the law of the LORD}* Having their maintenance provided and brought in by the people, they were freed from care and pains about their livelihood, and so might more willingly and cheerfully spend their time about such services as the law of the Lord required of them.

**V. 5. ***{And as soon as the commandment came abroad}* Heb. *brought forth*, or *broke forth*. Was publicly made known.

*{the children of Israel}* See v. 1.

*{brought in abundance}* Heb. *multiplied*. Two circumstances note out the people’s holy zeal in maintaining the service of the Lord; 1. Their quick and speedy doing what they did, even so soon as they had any notice that they should do it. 2. Their plentiful and bountiful doing of it.

*{the firstfruits of corn, wine, and oil}* These were expressly commanded to be given to the priests and Levites, Deut. 18:1, 4.

*{and honey}* Or, *dates*. The Hebrew word is oft used for *honey*. It is also indefinitely put for *sweet fruits*. And we do not read that honey was brought to the house of the Lord for an offering: but on the contrary, the Jews were forbidden to offer any honey, Lev. 2:11. The Hebrews therefore understand here *dates*, or indefinitely *trees which did bear sweet fruits*, called *honey* by reason of their sweetness. And they comprise it under this phrase, *the fruit of the tree*, Lev. 27:30, the tithe whereof was holy unto the Lord.

*{and of all the increase of the field}* Many other commodities grew out of the earth besides those before mentioned, therefore this general phrase is here used, which comprises them all.

*{and the tithe of all things, etc.}* Which were by law enjoined. There were tithes of cattle, and sundry other things, besides the fruit of the ground. The tithes were the tenth of all their increase, and an offering distinct from the firstfruits.

**V. 6. ***{And concerning the children of Israel and Judah, that dwelt in the cities of Judah}* i.e. Here and there in the country. For these cities seem to be distinguished from Jerusalem, v. 4.

*{they also brought in the tithes of oxen and sheep}* Besides the tithes of the increase of the field, v. 5.

*{and the tithe of holy things}* Lev. 27:30. Deut. 14:28.

*{which were consecrated unto the LORD}* This shows the reason why they were called *holy things*. Such things are said to be consecrated unto God, as God ordained to be brought to his house, and given to the priests and Levites, or such as were by vow or other free gift given to the Lord.

*{their God}* This relation betwixt God and them was a strong bond to bind them to do what they did, Exod. 20:7.

*{and laid them by heaps}* Heb. *heaps, heaps*. i.e. Many heaps, great abundance. This much affected the king, v. 8.

**V. 7. ***{In the third month}* See ch. 15:10. Then began their first harvest.

*{they began to lay the foundation of the heaps}* They brought in the tithe of such corn as they first reaped or gathered in, and so began to make the heaps.

*{and finished them in the seventh month}* This answers in part to our September, and is the beginning of autumn. Then use the fruits of trees to be gathered: and it is counted the last of harvest. Thus they continued from the beginning of harvest to the end, from the reaping of the first corn, to the gathering in of the last fruit (which in that respect is styled *the end of the year*, Ex. 23:16), to bring in their tithes to the house of the Lord, and they brought in of all sorts.

**V. 8. ***{And when Hezekiah and the Princes came and saw the heaps}* Which were many, and those great ones.

*{they blessed the LORD}* For stirring up the people so cheerfully and abundantly to bring in their tithe. Thus did David, 1 Chron. 29:13, 14. It is probable that the Lord gave that year a plentiful increase of the land, which was the occasion of that great abundance, and that Hezekiah and the princes in that respect blessed God.

*{and his people Israel}* This phrase is here taken as *children of Israel*, v. 1. The king and princes commended these, and in that respect are said *to bless them*. The same word is oft applied to God and man, yet in different respects, Gen. 14:19, 20. 1 Sam. 25:32, 33. Exod. 14:31. 2 Chron. 20:20.

**V. 9. ***{Then Hezekiah questioned with the Priests and the Levites concerning the heaps}* Two things he might inquire thereabouts; 1. How there should be so many and so great heaps. 2. How so much should remain, seeing there were many priests and Levites to spend the same. The answer v. 10 seems directed to the latter question.

**V. 10. ***{And Azariah the chief Priest, of the house of Zadok}* Heb. *the head of the house of Zadok*. The like phrase is used 2 King. 25:18. And he is called *Ruler of the house of God*, v. 13. He is said to be *of the house of Zadok*, because Zadok was made high priest in the room of Abiathar, 1 King. 2:35. Some think that there still continued two high priests, one of the house of Eleazar, another of the house of Ithamar, and that Zadok, of the stock of Eleazar, is here mentioned to distinguish him from the other chief priest.

*{answered him, and said, Since the people began}* i.e. From the beginning of harvest in the third month, till this time, which was at least four months. See v. 7.

*{to bring the offerings into the house of the LORD}* By *offerings* are meant tithes, which were brought and offered to the Lord, in that they were given to the priests and Levites his ministers.

*{we have had enough to eat}* Therefore the abundance remaining was by reason not of their sparing of that which was provided for them, but of the extraordinary plenty that was brought in.

*{and have left plenty}* For future relief of ourselves, families, all sorts of Levites, poor widows, orphans, strangers, and others in need.

*{for the LORD hath blessed his people}* Both by the plentiful increase which he has given, and also by working on the people’s hearts.

*{and that which is left is this great store}* i.e. The remainder, over and above that which was eaten by them.

**V. 11. ***{Then Hezekiah commanded to prepare chambers}* Or, *storehouses*. Places wherein to lay up that remainder, for future uses.

*{in the house of the LORD}* i.e. About the temple. Such Solomon built, 1 King. 6:5.

*{and they prepared them}* They readily hearkened to good counsel.

**V. 12. ***{And brought in the offerings}* i.e. Such things as were to be offered for their sins, or in way of thanksgiving.

*{and the tithes}* See v. 5.

*{and the dedicate things}* Such things as were voluntarily consecrated unto the Lord.

*{faithfully}* Heb. *in truth*, or *with faithfulness*. Though no man could tell whether they brought the full due or no; yet they had such respect to God, as they would not defraud his priests of anything due to them.

*{over which Cononiah the Levite was ruler}* He was the chief treasurer, who was to see what was brought in, and how that which was taken away was used. See 1 Chr. 26:20.

*{and Shimei his brother was the next}* Heb. *second*. He was next under Cononiah, to give account to him; but over those mentioned v. 13, to take account of them.

**V. 13. ***{And Jehiel, etc.}* Here are ten set down by name who had a further charge given them.

*{were overseers under the hand, etc.}* Heb. *at the hand*. Both phrases may well stand together: they might be *at their hand*, to do what was enjoined them; and *under*, to give an account of what they did.

*{at the commandment of Hezekiah the King and Azariah}* The king in matters ecclesiastical would do nothing without the advice of the high priest.

*{the ruler of the house of God}* *Chief Priest*, v. 10. He had the rule over all persons that appertained to the house of God, in and about the services thereof.

**V. 14. ***{And Kore the son of Imnah the Levite, the Porter}* For such as were porters were also treasurers, 1 Chr. 26:20, etc.

*{toward the East}* This was the principal gate, which being opened they might look into the holy and most holy place. See 2 King. 11:6. 1 Chr. 26:14.

*{was over the freewill offerings of God}* See Ezra 3:5.

*{to distribute the oblations of the LORD}* To see them given out and distributed to priests and Levites for their use.

*{and the most holy things}* Heb. *sanctities of sanctities*. Of this Hebraism see 1 King. 8:13. These *most holy things* were the remainder of all manner of offerings which were for the priests to eat, as meat-offerings, Lev. 2:3, etc., sin-offerings, Lev. 6:18, and trespass-offerings, Lev. 7:6.

**V. 15. ***{And next him}* Heb. *at his hand*. See v. 13.

*{were Eden, etc.}* These six were under Kore, v. 14, as the ten mentioned v. 13 were under Cononiah and Shimei.

*{in the cities of the Priests}* Where they dwelt.

*{in their set office}* Or, *trust*. A man’s office makes trust to be reposed in him.

*{to give to their brethren}* i.e. All sorts of Levites, Deut. 18:7.

*{by courses}* As they were set in their several orders.

*{as well to the great as to the small}* Every one was to have what was sufficient for him.

**V. 16. ***{Besides their genealogy of males from three years old and upward}* These words being included in a parenthesis, the verb *to give* v. 15 may fitly be referred to that which follows in this verse; as if it were thus read, *Eden and Miniamin, etc. were to give to their brethren by courses, as well to great as to the small, (beside their genealogy of males from three years old and upward) even unto every one*, etc. The meaning is, that there was provision made for all that belonged to the Levites, not only to such as did service, but also to their young ones, even from three years old and upward. Thus this verse is an exposition of the last clause of the former, *as well to the great as to the small*. Mention is made of *males*, because none were admitted into the temple but such. And of these many were trained from three years old and upwards to the services of the house of God, 1 Sam. 1:24, 25 and 2:11, and therefore were to be maintained thereby. Other males of Levites under three years, and females also, were to be maintained by tithes and such other dues as belonged to the Levites, v. 18, but such as came into the house of the Lord might partake of such oblations as were offered there. See 1 Chr. 23:24.

*{even unto every one that entreth into the house of the LORD}* To bear any office, or do any service therein.

*{his daily portion}* Every one was to have his daily allowance.

*{for their service in their charges}* In recompense of their pains in the places where they were set.

*{according to their courses}* See 1 Chr. 23:6.

**V. 17. ***{Both to the genealogy of the Priests by the house of their fathers}* Here is particularly exemplified that which in general was said before. By *the genealogy of Priests* such priests were meant as were put into the genealogy, and that as they descended from such and such ancestors. See 1 Chr. 4:33.

*{and the Levites}* Such as were under the priests, but yet to be maintained as well as priests.

*{from twenty years old and upward}* Then they might enter into the house of God.

*{in their charges by their courses}* See v. 16.

**V. 18. ***{And to the genealogy of all their little ones, their wives, and their sons and their daughters}* Because the priests and Levites wholly attended the service of God, and had no time to provide for themselves, their wives and children, therefore not only they themselves but also theirs were maintained by the offerings of God.

*{through all the congregation}* i.e. Of priests and Levites.

*{for in their set office they sanctified themselves in holiness}* They having sanctified themselves in their distinct offices for the holy things of the temple, their wives and children were provided for. Or, *by reason of the faithfulness of such as were sanctified thereto, wives and children of priests and Levites were provided for*.

**V. 19. ***{Also of the sons of Aaron}* Or, *to the sons of Aaron*. This verse as well as the three former, depends on the verb *to give*, v. 15.

*{the Priests, which were in the fields of the suburbs of their cities}* Priests waited in course, so as while some served in the temple, others were abroad in their particular cities and suburbs.

*{in every several city}* Heb. *in every city*. No city was passed over. The men expressed by name v. 15 are here meant.

*{to give portions to all the males among the Priests, etc.}* See v. 16, 17.

**V. 20. ***{And thus did Hezekiah throughout all Judah}* Hezekiah showed himself the same man throughout his whole kingdom. As he did in one place, so he did in another. He was impartial.

*{and wrought that which was good}* i.e. That which was lawful, being warrantable by and agreeable to the word of God.

*{and right}* This has reference to his dealing with his subjects in general, and with priests and Levites in particular: he did that which was just, yielding to every one their due.

*{and truth before the LORD}* This sets out the sincerity of his heart; which because God alone discerns, this phrase *before the Lord* is added.

*{his God}* His apprehension that God was in a special manner *his God* put him on to do what he did.

**V. 21. ***{And in every work that he began in the service of the house of God}* For settling all things after a right manner.

*{and in the law}* i.e. The moral law.

*{and in the commandments}* i.e. Ordinances about divine worship.

*{to seek his God}* By taking care how he might be best honored.

*{he did it with all his heart}* This implies both sincerity and zeal.

*{and prospered}* See 1 Chr. 29:23. 2 King. 18:7.

## CHAPTER 32

**Verse 1. ***{After these things}* i.e. The things registered ch. 29, 30 and 31 about the worship of God. In this chapter such matters as concerned the civil state are recorded; particularly, the deliverances which God gave Hezekiah from a potent enemy and from a mortal sickness, together with his ingratitude and repentance.

*{and the establishment thereof}* Or, *and the truth*, or *faithfulness*. The former reading implies Hezekiah’s constancy about the service of God, till he had established all according to the law; the latter, his truth and faithfulness in what he did. Some apply this to the faithfulness of the penman of this history, who registered things as they were indeed done.

*{Sennacherib king of Assyria came}* 2 King. 18:13, etc. Isa. 36:1, etc.

*{and entered into Judah}* Into the tribe, or kingdom of Judah.

*{And encamped against the fenced cities}* See 2 King. 18:13.

*{and thought to win them for himself}* Heb. *to break them up*. To batter down their walls, enter into them, plunder them, and make them subject to him. This he supposed he should have done; and indeed he did take *some of the fenced cities of Judah*, 2 King. 18:13, but partly by a bribe, 2 King. 18:14, and partly by God’s restraining of him, v. 21, he did not accomplish all he thought to do.

**V. 2. ***{And when Hezekiah saw that Sennacherib was come}* That he was entered into his kingdom with a mighty army.

*{and that he was purposed to fight against Jerusalem}* Heb. *his face was to war against it*. A man’s turning his face to a thing, and going toward it, declares his purpose.

**V. 3. ***{He took counsel with his princes and his mighty men}* The case being of great concernment he prudently advises with counselors of state and men experienced in war thereabout.

*{to stop the waters of the fountains which were without the city}* The city stood on an hill, and there were sundry spring heads without the walls, whence abundance of water was brought into it. These the enemy might have refreshed his army with: thereupon it is agreed that those fountains should be stopped so as no water should flow forth for the enemy’s use, v. 4, and yet enough be conveyed into the city by water-pipes underground. See Isa. 22:9, 11. 2 King. 20:20.

*{and they did help him}* The work being great, the king, princes, commanders in war, and other subjects, all join together to bring it to pass. They were helpful one to another.

**V. 4. ***{So there was gathered much people together}* The danger was common, and concerned great and mean; therefore many assembled together to do what they could to prevent the enemy.

*{who stopped all the fountains}* Though those were very beneficial to all sorts in time of peace, yet to prevent the enemies’ use thereof they are content to suffer some damage themselves.

*{and the brook}* Gihon by name, v. 30.

*{that ran through the midst of the land}* Heb. *overflowed*. A sweet full current flowed from Gihon by many parts of that kingdom.

*{saying, Why should the Kings of Assyria}* There is mention made only of one king of Assyria, namely Sennacherib; but the plural number is used, because haply many kings took part with him, as we read of two and thirty kings which joined with the king of Syria against Israel, 1 King. 20:1. Or the princes of Sennacherib may be called *Kings*: so Sennacherib himself calls them, Isa. 10:8. See ch. 28:16.

*{come and find much water}* It is a point of prudence to cut off all conveniences from an enemy, and to leave nothing to refresh, or further him.

**V. 5. ***{Also he strengthened himself}* He thought it not enough to do what he could to the prejudice of his enemy, but he also did what he could for his own advantage.

*{and built up all the wall that was broken}* Joash king of Israel in Amaziah’s time broke down a great part of the wall of Jerusalem, ch. 25:23. And it may be that breach was not fully repaired and strongly fortified till now.

*{and raised it up to the towers}* He made the walls strong and high to keep the city more safe from the enemies’ shot.

*{and another wall without}* Namely, without the wall to be for outworks. Of these two walls that compassed Jerusalem see 2 King. 25:4.

*{and repaired Millo in the city of David}* See 1 King. 9:15.

*{and made darts}* Or, *swords*, or *weapons*.

*{and shields}* Under these two kinds are comprised all manner of offensive and defensive weapons. See ch. 26:14.

*{in abundance}* The enemies being many, he would have store of preparation against them.

**V. 6. ***{And he set captains of war over the people}* Commanders in war are as necessary as multitudes of soldiers.

*{and gathered them together to him}* That so he might direct and encourage them all together.

*{in the street of the gate of the city}* This was a fair broad street within the walls of the city, but at the very entrance thereinto.

*{and spake comfortably unto them}* Heb. *to their heart*. See ch. 30:22.

**V. 7. ***{Be strong and courageous}* See 1 Chr. 22:13.

*{be not afraid nor dismayed for the king of Assyria}* He was at that time the greatest monarch and the most formidable enemy in the world, yet God being with them, they need not be afraid of him. See 1 Chr. 22:13.

*{nor for all the multitude that is with him}* See 2 King. 19:35.

*{for there be more with us then with him}* More in multitude, 2 King. 6:17. Ps. 34:7 and 91:11. Heb. 12:22. 2 King. 19:35. Rom. 8:31.

**V. 8. ***{With him is an arm of flesh}* He means all his men and horses, which were of flesh, and weak and frail, Isa. 31:3. Jer. 17:5. *Arm* imports power; *flesh*, weakness.

*{but with us is the LORD our God}* *Lord* notes God’s sovereignty and almighty power; *our God*, his special grace and favor towards his people.

*{to help us, and to fight our battles}* To defend us, and to subdue our enemies.

*{and the people rested themselves}* Heb. *leaned*. They by faith so relied on God as a man uses to lean upon a strong staff when he leaps over a water.

*{upon the words of Hezekiah king of Judah}* By Hezekiah’s exhortation the people were moved so to believe in God as they were not afraid.

**V. 9. ***{After this did Sennacherib king of Assyria send his servants to Jerusalem}* This has reference to v. 1. See 2 King. 18:17.

*{but he himself laid siege against Lachish}* See 2 King. 18:14.

*{and all his power with him}* Heb. *dominion*. Hereby is meant that great host which he brought with him into Judah.

*{unto Hezekiah king of Judah, and unto all Judah that were at Jerusalem, saying}* He sent to the king, because he had the command over all; and to the people, to affright them and make them yield.

**V. 10. ***{Thus saith Sennacherib king of Assyria}* They deliver their message in their king’s name to make it the more regarded. In this message there were sundry persuasive motives as they thought.

*{Whereon do you trust}* He supposed they had none on whom they might place sure confidence, and thereupon propounded this question. This is his first motive.

*{that ye abide in the siege in Jerusalem}* The word signifies indefinitely any *strait* whereunto a man is brought; and because men besieged are oft brought to great straits, it is put for a *siege*, as Deut. 28:53, 55, 57. Further also, because men use to betake themselves to strongholds when they are in straits, or because strongholds are built to straiten them the more, it is put for *a strong hold*, or *fort*, ch. 8:5. Deut. 20:20. Ps. 60:9. Jerusalem was not yet besieged, but because the enemy had a purpose to besiege it, the word may be here translated *siege*.

**V. 11. ***{Doth not Hezekiah persuade you}* He supposed they would not be so foolish as to stand out against him if Hezekiah did not persuade them thereto. See 2 King. 18:29.

*{to give over yourselves to die by famine and by thirst}* He conceived they must needs perish through want of meat and drink if they continued to stand out against him. This is his second motive.

*{saying, The LORD our God shall deliver us out of the hand of the king of Assyria}* See 2 King. 18:30.

**V. 12. ***{Hath not the same Hezekiah taken away, etc.}* See 2 King. 18:22.

*{and burn incense upon it}* See 1 King. 9:25. This was the principal means of making atonement between God and men; therefore he mentions it. He perverts Hezekiah’s pious reformation of religion, and impeaches it of profaneness, and thence gathers a third motive.

**V. 13. ***{Know you not what I and my fathers have done unto all the people of other lands?}* See 2 King. 19:11. He supposes Hezekiah and his people could not but know his, and his predecessors’ former victories. This is his fourth motive.

*{were the gods of the nations of those lands any wise able to deliver their lands out of mine hand?}* See 2 King. 19:12. He takes it for granted that the idols of other nations were gods; and that if they could, they would have delivered their people: which because they did not, he supposes himself stronger than those gods. This is his fifth motive.

**V. 14. ***{Who was there among all the gods of those nations that my fathers utterly destroyed}* The nations are named 2 King. 18:34 and 19:12, 13.

*{that could deliver his people out of mine hand}* From an event he gathers an impossibility: they did not, therefore they could not. This is no good consequence.

*{that your God should be able to deliver you out of mine hand}* Most blasphemously he ranks the Lord with false gods, and makes him no better than they, v. 19, and so would take away that confidence which the men of Judah placed in him.

**V. 15. ***{Now therefore let not Hezekiah deceive you}* See 2 King. 18:29.

*{nor persuade you on this manner}* This may have reference to Hezekiah’s encouragement, v. 7, 8, whereof he might have notice by some intelligencer. Or he might guess that Hezekiah persuaded them not to yield, because they stood out against him.

*{neither yet believe him}* He dissuades them from believing their king, that they might yield to him.

*{for no god of any nation, etc.}* His own and his predecessors’ successes made him so confident as he presses it again. See v. 13.

*{how much less shall your God deliver you out of mine hand?}* He does not only equal false gods to the true God, but makes the true God inferior to the false gods, and thereby increases and aggravates his blasphemy.

**V. 16. ***{And his servants spake yet more}* Sennacherib sent his servants twice, 2 King. 18:17 and 19:9, and in that respect it may be said they *spake yet more*. Or they might add of their own to what their master appointed them to say, and so *speak yet more*.

*{against the LORD God}* To make the Lord like to false gods is *to speak against him*, which is *to blaspheme him*.

*{and against his servant Hezekiah}* This shows the reason why they spoke against Hezekiah, he was God’s *servant*; and it also aggravates their folly that they dared speak against a *servant of the Lord*.

**V. 17. ***{He wrote also letters}* See 2 King. 19:9, 14.

*{to rail on the LORD God of Israel, etc.}* The substance of the letters is set down 2 King. 19:10, etc. Thereby we may see that to impeach God’s power is *to rail upon him*. The like may be said of other divine attributes.

*{As the gods of the nations of other lands, etc.}* See v. 14.

*{so shall not the God of Hezekiah, etc.}* The Lord whom Hezekiah professed and worshiped, and on whom he trusted. See v. 14.

**V. 18. ***{Then they cried with a loud voice in the Jews’ speech}* 2 King. 18:28, that is attributed to Rabshakeh alone which is here applied to all the messengers, because they all stood together, and consented to what he said.

*{unto the people of Jerusalem that were on the wall}* See 2 King. 18:26.

*{to affright them, and to trouble them}* When men are affrighted with fear of danger, they use to be much troubled, not knowing what to say or think; and so are oft divided one against another, some being of one mind, and others of another.

*{that they might take the city}* No such way to surprise a besieged city as to make a division among the people that are in it.

**V. 19. ***{And they spake against the God of Jerusalem}* See v. 16. In Jerusalem was the house of God, and this was the chief city of the kingdom: in that respect the Lord is called *the God of Jerusalem*.

*{as against the gods of the people of the earth}* See v. 14 and 2 King. 18:30, 33, etc.

*{which were the work of the hands of man}* The gods of other nations were *the work of mens hands*, but the God of Israel was the creator of all things. See this difference largely exemplified Jer. 10:3, etc.

**V. 20. ***{For this cause Hezekiah the king}* He was first and most moved, and sent unto the prophet, 2 King. 19:2, etc.

*{and the Prophet Isaiah the son of Amoz}* See Isa. 1:1.

*{prayed}* This the king desired of the prophet, 2 King. 19:4, and without question the prophet did what was desired of him. Hezekiah’s prayer is expressly set down 2 King. 19:15, etc.

*{and cried to heaven}* The word *cried* implies their fervency in prayer. By *heaven* is meant the Lord, whose throne is in heaven. Thus are we taught to pray Matt. 6:9.

**V. 21. ***{And the LORD sent an Angel}* See 2 King. 19:35. This verse gives evidence of the power of prayer.

*{which cut off all the mighty men of valour}* See 2 King. 24:14. 1 Chron. 5:24. God can execute his judgments on the mightiest that be.

*{and the leaders and captains in the camp of the king of Assyria}* Had men executed the judgment, these commanders might haply have escaped; but there is no flying from God’s hand.

*{So he returned with shame of face to his own land}* Sennacherib was very high in his terms against God himself, v. 14, but by this hand of God upon him he was so confounded as he dared no longer abide in Judah, but returned without his host to his own home, 2 King. 19:36.

*{And when he was come into the house of his god}* See 2 King. 19:37.
