*{they that came forth}* Or, *some of them that came out*. Namely, two of them.

*{of his own bowels}* His sons begotten by him, 2 King. 19:37.

*{slew him there with the sword}* Heb. *made him fall*. See 2 King. 19:37.

**V. 22. ***{Thus the LORD saved Hezekiah}* By destroying his enemies.

*{and the inhabitants of Jerusalem}* For the enemy sought the destruction of king and people.

*{from the hand of Sennacherib king of Assyria}* See v. 1.

*{and from the hand of all other}* i.e. From those that assisted the king of Assyria. See v. 4. Or other nations that were enemies to the Jews were by this judgment on Sennacherib’s host restrained from annoying Hezekiah.

*{and guided them on every side}* The phrase is metaphorical, taken from a shepherd, who guides his sheep, by providing for them, protecting them, leading them in and out, and every way taking care for them.

**V. 23. ***{And many brought gifts unto the LORD to Jerusalem}* To wit, Hezekiah’s subjects, or some of the ten tribes, who by God’s goodness showed to Hezekiah were moved to bring offerings to the Lord. It may be also that some of other nations might be made proselytes thereby, and so bring offerings.

*{and presents, etc.}* Heb. *precious things*. See 2 Chron. 17:5, 11.

*{so that he was magnified, etc.}* Evidences of God’s special favor to a king make other nations hold him in high account.

**V. 24. ***{In those days Hezekiah was sick to the death}* See Isa. 38:1.

*{and prayed unto the LORD}* His prayer is set down 2 King. 20:3.

*{and he spake unto him}* The Lord returned him a gracious answer by his prophet Isaiah, 2 King. 20:4, etc.

*{and he gave him a sign}* Or, *wrought a miracle for him*. Isa. 38:8, etc.

**V. 25. ***{But Hezekiah rendered not again}* Was not so affected with the blessing, nor rendered praise unto God, nor carried himself in the after-course of his life as he ought. See on Exod. 12:42.

*{according to the benefit done unto him}* This word *benefit* comprises sundry blessings; his recovery from a deadly disease, confirmation of his faith by a great miracle, promise of fifteen years’ life, protection of himself and his kingdom, 2 King. 20:6.

*{for his heart was lifted up}* With pride and self-conceit, as if the blessings bestowed upon him had been merited by him. See v. 26.

*{therefore there was wrath upon him}* This was an occasion of the judgment that the Lord afterwards inflicted. His anger was first kindled by Hezekiah’s ingratitude.

*{and upon Judah and Jerusalem}* Sins of kings oft bring judgments upon all their people. See on Gen. 20:7 and 34:25.

**V. 26. ***{Notwithstanding Hezekiah humbled himself}* After the prophet had declared God’s displeasure against him, 2 King. 20:17, etc.

*{for the pride of his heart}* Heb. *the lifting up*. See v. 25. Pride puffed him up by reason of the destruction of his enemies, v. 21, his own recovery, v. 24, and his rich treasure, which too too boastingly he showed to the men of Babel, 2 King. 20:13.

*{both he, and the inhabitants of Jerusalem}* His subjects follow his good example, as ch. 12:6.

*{so that the wrath of the LORD came not upon them}* True humiliation for sin is an especial means to prevent or avert God’s wrath.

*{in the days of Hezekiah}* This was according to the king’s desire, 2 King. 20:19. After his days his son and the people turned to idolatry, and thereby again incensed the wrath of God, ch. 33:11.

**V. 27. ***{And Hezekiah had exceeding much riches and honour}* These are two great worldly blessings, which God sanctifies to his children. See 1 King. 3:13. 1 Chron. 29:12, 28. Chap. 18:1.

*{and he made himself treasuries, etc.}* Treasuries were set and safe places to lay up precious things in. There were *treasuries of the house of God*, 1 Chr. 9:26 and 28:11, 12. Kings also had their proper treasuries, Esth. 3:9 and 4:7. See 1 Chr. 29:3.

*{and for spices}* See 1 King. 10:2, 10. 2 King. 20:13.

*{and for shields}* These might be such as Solomon made, 1 King. 10:17. Or he might lay up things for defense as well as things of worth.

*{and for all manner of pleasant jewels}* Heb. *instruments of desire*. i.e. All outward things that might be desired.

**V. 28. ***{Storehouses also}* These were a kind of treasuries, but for meaner commodities.

*{for the increase of corn and wine and oil}* All very necessary commodities: corn for bread. Wine to refresh the heart of man, Ps. 104:15. Oil likewise for sustenance, and many other uses. See 1 King. 17:12 and 2 King. 4:2, 7.

*{and stalls for all manner of beasts}* Horses were wont to be kept in stalls, 1 King. 4:26, and oxen, Prov. 15:17, calves, Amos 6:4. Mal. 4:2, and all sorts of great cattle comprised under the word *herd*, Hab. 3:17.

*{and cotes for flocks}* i.e. Sheep, goats, and other small cattle. This description of Hezekiah’s wealth is set down to show what it was that lifted him up, v. 25.

**V. 29. ***{Moreover he provided him cities}* Fenced cities especially.

*{and possessions of flocks and herds in abundance}* *Possessions* implies that there was not only abundance of great and small cattle in the land, but that he had abundance of them proper and peculiar to himself.

*{for God had given him substance very much}* i.e. All kinds of this world’s commodities. Even the things of this life are given of God.

**V. 30. ***{This same Hezekiah also stopped the upper watercourse of Gihon}* See 1 King. 1:33. There was a passage made out of Gihon which brought water to the city, into a place called *the upper pool*, Isa. 7:3. This Hezekiah took away, and so is said *to have stopped the upper water-course*. But underground he brought water to the city. The reason hereof see v. 4.

*{and brought it straight down to the west side}* It seems before the water-course ran in a compass another way, but upon the approach of the enemy Hezekiah made it run straight into *the lower pool*, Isa. 22:9, which was new made by him, and distinguished from *the old pool*, Isa. 22:11.

*{of the city of David}* See 1 King. 2:10.

*{and Hezekiah prospered in all his works}* See 2 King. 18:7. 1 Chr. 29:23.

**V. 31. ***{Howbeit in the business of the Ambassadors}* Heb. *interpreters*. For ambassadors use to declare and interpret the mind of their master.

*{of the Princes of Babylon}* They were the king of Babylon’s ambassadors, 2 King. 20:12, 13, but sent with the consent of the princes.

*{who sent unto him to enquire of the wonder that was done in the land}* The wonder was done in heaven: it was the sun’s running back, 2 King. 20:11. And all nations must needs take notice thereof; among others, the astrologers of Babylon. But certainly it was divulged that the God of Israel upon Hezekiah’s recovery had done it; and thereupon Hezekiah is sent unto thereabouts.

*{God left him, to try him}* God oft tries his dearest servants, Gen. 22:1. Job 1:12 and 2:3, 6, to discover either their graces, or their weaknesses and corruptions.

*{that he might know}* Or, *to know*, or, *that it might be known*. That Hezekiah himself, and others also, might discern that frailty that was in him. See on Job 22:2.

*{all that was in his heart}* Corruption oft lies very close, and cannot be discovered, till there be some occasion to try a man thoroughly.

**V. 32. ***{Now the rest of the acts of Hezekiah}* See 1 King. 11:41.

*{and his goodness}* Heb. *kindnesses*. Such kindnesses especially are here meant as he showed to God’s people, and that in reforming religion, ch. 28, 29 and 30.

*{behold, they are written in the vision of Isaiah}* i.e. The prophecy of Isaiah, which by vision, revelation, and other ways was made known to him. See Isa. 36, 37, 38 and 39.

*{the Prophet, the son of Amoz}* See Isa. 1:1.

*{and in the book of the kings of Judah and Israel}* See 2 Chron. 16:11.

**V. 33. ***{And Hezekiah slept with his fathers}* See 1 King. 2:10.

*{and they buried him in the chiefest}* Heb. *highest*. i.e. The fairest and most excellent.

*{of the sepulchres of David}* Many kings of Judah descending from David had their sepulchers in that place. It may be David’s own sepulcher was fairer than all the rest: it remained to the apostles’ days, Acts 2:29. This of Hezekiah’s might be the next to David’s in excellency.

*{and all Judah and the inhabitants of Jerusalem did him honour at his death}* They accompanied him with much funeral solemnity to his grave. And many epitaphs might be made of him, and great mourning and lamentation for him. Much honor have such as fear God, restore his worship, and seek the good of his people in life and death.

*{and Manasseh his son reigned in his stead}* See 2 King. 20:21.

## CHAPTER 33

**Verse 1. ***{Manasseh was twelve years old, etc.}* See 2 King. 21:1. In this chapter we have the history of two kings, Manasseh, and Amon. Of Manasseh two points are registered; his very evil course, to v. 11, his repentance, from v. 11 to v. 21. He was the thirteenth king of Judah from the division.

**V. 2. ***{But he did that which was evil, etc.}* See 2 King. 21:2.

**V. 3. ***{For he built again, etc.}* Heb. *he returned and built*. His father had destroyed the high places, ch. 31:1. Now in that he reedified them, he is said *to return and build them*. Of this verse see 2 King. 21:3.

**V. 4. ***{Also he built Altars, etc.}* See 2 King. 21:4.

*{in Jerusalem shall be my name for ever}* 1 King. 8:29 and 9:3.

**V. 5. ***{And he built Altars for all the host, etc.}* See 2 King. 21:5.

**V. 6. ***{And he caused his children, etc.}* See 2 King. 21:6.

*{in the valley of the son of Hinnom}* See 2 King. 16:3.

*{also he observed times, and used enchantments}* See 2 King. 21:6.

*{and used witchcraft}* See 2 King. 9:22.

*{and dealt with a familiar spirit}* 2 King. 21:6, *familiar spirits*.

*{and with wizards, etc.}* See 2 King. 21:6.

**V. 7. ***{And he set a carved image}* Our translators, 2 King. 21:7, turn it *graven*, but the Hebrew word is the same in both places.

*{the idol which he had made, etc.}* Or, *of the grove that he had made*. See 2 King. 21:7.

**V. 8. ***{Neither will I any more remove, etc.}* See 2 King. 21:8.

*{by the hand of Moses}* See 1 King. 8:53, 56.

**V. 9. ***{So Manasseh made Judah and the inhabitants of Jerusalem to err}* This he did by example and by command. Herein he showed himself like to Jeroboam, 1 King. 14:16, and to Omri, Mic. 6:16.

*{and to do worse then the heathen, etc.}* See 2 King. 21:9.

**V. 10. ***{And the LORD spake to Manasseh and to his people}* This God did by his servants the prophets, 2 King. 21:10.

*{but they would not hearken}* See 2 King. 21:9.

**V. 11. ***{Wherefore the LORD brought upon them the captains of the host of the king of Assyria}* Heb. *which were the kings*. Many of Sennacherib’s captains were slain before Jerusalem, and after that, himself, ch. 32:21. So as this was another king, and these other captains. Now they might do what they did in revenge of the forementioned destruction of the host of the Assyrians; but God ordered it both to punish and also to reclaim Manasseh. By *the King of Assyria* the king of Babylon may be here meant. See 2 King. 20:12. Neh. 9:32. The story of Manasseh’s repentance is wholly omitted in the book of the Kings.

*{which took Manasseh among the thorns}* Some translate the word, *fetters*; others, *strong holds*; but it is most frequently used for *thorns*, as Cant. 2:2. 2 King. 14:9, it is translated *thistle*. It being here in the plural number, may be taken for *a place full of thistles*, or *thorns*; or for some *thicket* whither Manasseh fled to hide himself, as the Israelites did when they were afraid of the Philistines, 1 Sam. 13:6.

*{and bound him with fetters}* Or, *chains*. So they dealt with Zedekiah, Jer. 39:7, and other captives, Jer. 40:1.

*{and carried him to Babylon}* This shows that the king of Babylon was now king of Assyria.

**V. 12. ***{And when he was in affliction}* For a king to be in fetters in his enemies’ country must needs be a great affliction.

*{he besought the LORD}* What God’s word could not do, v. 10, his rod did. He *heard the rod*, Mic. 6:9. Hos. 5:15 and 6:1. Job 36:8, 9.

*{his God}* Though he had cast off God, yet God had not cast off him. These words *his God* may respect his present condition, being a convert.

*{and humbled himself greatly}* This is a ready way to find mercy with the high and mighty God, Isa. 57:15. See ch. 12:6.

*{before the God of his fathers}* See 2 Chr. 29:18. The consideration that he descended from those progenitors to whom and to whose seed God had made many gracious promises, moved him the rather to humble himself before the Lord.

**V. 13. ***{And prayed unto him}* When the soul is humbled for sin, the spirit will be quickened to pray for pardon.

*{and he was intreated of him}* God is ready to hearken to the prayers of the penitent.

*{and heard his supplication}* See 1 King. 8:28, 30, 45. God granted him what he desired.

*{and brought him again to Jerusalem}* No doubt but God so wrought upon the heart of his enemy, as he was moved to show mercy unto him, to loose his bonds, and to suffer him to return to his own country. Herein was that branch of Solomon’s prayer accomplished, 1 King. 8:46, 50.

*{into his kingdom}* He was settled again on his throne.

*{then Manasseh knew that the LORD he was God}* By his captivity and freedom from it he was in his judgment convinced that the Lord whom he had formerly despised (advancing other gods above him) was the only true God. See 1 King. 18:39. See also the like Dan. 4:34.

**V. 14. ***{Now after this}* After he was settled in his kingdom.

*{he built a wall without the city of David}* This wall his father began, ch. 32:5, and it might be broken down when Manasseh was taken: so as now he might repair or fortify it. In this sense the word *build* is oft used, as ch. 11:6 and 14:6.

*{on the west side of Gihon}* Of Gihon see 1 King. 1:33.

*{in the valley}* For Gihon was in a low place.

*{even to the entering in at the fish gate}* See Neh. 3:3.

*{and compassed about Ophel}* Or, *the tower*. See ch. 27:3.

*{and raised it up a very great height}* For it was made a fortress, and from thence they might shoot far off against their enemies.

*{and put captains of war in all the fenced cities of Judah}* In these cities he had garrisons, over whom he set captains.

**V. 15. ***{And he took away the strange gods}* Such as himself had before set up, and were called *Baalim*, v. 3. This is a fruit of true repentance, to put away those evils that formerly we have done.

*{and the idol out of the house of the LORD}* That mentioned v. 7.

*{and all the altars that he had built in the mount, etc.}* Mentioned v. 3, 4. This mount was called *Moriah*. See chap. 3:1.

*{and cast them out of the city}* An evidence of his detesting that idolatry which before he had so much doted on.

**V. 16. ***{And he repaired the Altar of the LORD}* Idolaters used to deface the Lord’s altar, 1 King. 18:10, and this Manasseh also in his idolatrous humor had done. But now being a convert, he thinks it not enough to destroy idolatrous monuments, unless also he fit and prepare the Lord’s holy things for their use. Or by *repairing* may be meant his cleansing and sanctifying the altar.

*{and sacrificed thereon peace offerings, etc.}* See ch. 29:31 and 1 King. 3:15.

*{and commanded Judah to serve the LORD, etc.}* He went as far in religious courses, now being converted, as he had formerly done in idolatrous. As by his example and command he had made others to err, v. 9, so now he brings them to serve the Lord. See Luke 22:32.

**V. 17. ***{Nevertheless the people did sacrifice still in the high places}* See 1 King. 3:2, 4 and 15:14.

*{yet unto the LORD their God only}* They were brought to the true religion, though there were still a mixture of superstition.

**V. 18. ***{Now the rest of the acts of Manasseh}* See 1 King. 11:41.

*{and his prayer unto his God}* There is a prayer of his set down among the Apocrypha, but great question may be made whether that be the prayer here intended.

*{and the words of the Seers}* See 2 King. 17:13.

*{that spake to him in the Name of the LORD, etc.}* True prophets delivered nothing in their own name, but what the Lord had given them in charge.

*{behold, they are written in the book of the kings of Israel}* i.e. 2 King. 21:10, etc. Or, some civil records not now extant may be here meant.

**V. 19. ***{His prayer also}* See v. 18.

*{and how God was intreated of him}* See v. 13.

*{and all his sin, and his trespass}* *Sin* may signify his transgressions against God; *trespass*, his wrongs done to men, 2 King. 21:16.

*{and the places wherein he built high places}* See v. 3, 4, 5.

*{and set up groves and graven-images}* See v. 3, 7.

*{before he was humbled}* See v. 12.

*{behold, they are written among the sayings of the Seers}* Or, *Hosai*. Some take the word appellatively, for *Seers* or *Prophets*; others, for a proper name. As a proper name we read nowhere else mention of it. But prophets or seers did use to register the facts of kings in their time, whether good or bad, that answerably others might make use thereof. See ch. 9:29. 1 Chr. 29:29.

**V. 20. ***{So Manasseh slept with his fathers}* See 1 King. 2:10.

*{and they buried him in his own house}* Namely, in a garden appertaining thereunto, 2 King. 21:18.

*{and Amon his son reigned in his stead}* A son that imitated his father’s impiety, but not his repentance.

**V. 21. ***{Amon was two and twenty years old, etc.}* See 2 King. 21:19. Amon was the fourteenth king of Judah from the division.

**V. 22. ***{But he did that which was evil, etc.}* See 2 King. 21:20.

*{for Amon sacrificed unto all the carved images}* Idolaters so doted upon their images as they would not only curiously carve them, but also do that to them which true worshipers of the Lord did to him, as sacrificing, etc.

*{which Manasseh his father had made}* It seems Manasseh had only laid aside his idols, and not utterly destroyed them, as he should have done, Deut. 7:5.

*{and served them}* See 1 King. 9:9 and 16:31.

**V. 23. ***{And humbled not himself before the LORD}* Men are more prone to imitate their fathers’ wickedness than their repentance.

*{as Manasseh his father had humbled himself}* See v. 12.

*{but Amon trespassed more and more}* Heb. *multiplied trespass*. This was a great aggravation that he added sin to sin.

**V. 24. ***{And his servants conspired, etc.}* See 2 King. 21:23.

**V. 25. ***{But the people of the land slew, etc.}* See 2 King. 21:24.

*{and the people of the land made Josiah, etc.}* See 2 King. 14:21 and 21:24.

## CHAPTER 34

**Verse 1. ***{Josiah was eight years old, etc.}* See 2 King. 22:1. He was the fifteenth king of Judah from the division.

**V. 2. ***{And he did that which was right, etc.}* See 2 King. 22:2.

**V. 3. ***{For in the eighth year of his reign, etc.}* This was the sixteenth year of his age. See v. 1. At this age kings use to reign without a protector, and by themselves alone govern the state. In his father’s time the whole land was given to idolatry; and he coming to the crown when he was a child could not reform matters as he would: but so soon as he had the reins in his own hands, he forthwith set upon the work. For him therefore in this eighth year of his reign to begin reformation was as great a commendation as for his great-grandfather to begin it in the first year of his reign, ch. 29:3.

*{while he was yet young}* For the sixteenth year of one’s age is within the compass of youth; and in relation to so great a work as reforming religion in a whole kingdom one of sixteen years may well be said to be young. See 1 Chron. 22:5. Herein was accomplished the prophecy of the man of God, 1 King. 13:2, etc.

*{he began to seek after the God}* Certainly his heart was seasoned with the fear of God in his childhood, when first he began to reign. Therefore this phrase *began to seek* has relation to his endeavor of settling the true religion, and to his public manifestation of his faith in God and his zeal of God’s glory.

*{of David his father}* See 2 King. 20:5. God had entered into a special covenant with David and with his seed, ch. 7:18, and therefore was he in succeeding ages styled *the God of David*, as formerly *the God of Abraham, Isaac, and Israel*, 1 Chr. 29:18.

*{and in the twelfth year he began to purge Judah and Jerusalem, etc.}* See v. 5. 2 King. 23:4, etc.

*{and the groves}* 2 Kings 23:6 is mentioned only one grove; but there might be more. For Manasseh, besides that in the house of the Lord, made other groves, ch. 33:3, 19, and Amon also might do the like.

*{and the carved images}* Of wood, or other like matter.

*{and the molten images}* Of gold, silver, or other metals.

**V. 4. ***{And they brake down}* i.e. Such subjects of Josiah as he set on work. This was according to the law, Deut. 7:5.

*{the altars of Baalim}* See ch. 33:3.

*{in his presence}* That his command might be accomplished to the full, he himself would be an eyewitness thereof.

*{and the images that were on high above them}* Or, *Sun-images*. They were wont to set such images on high above their altars.

*{he cut down}* They were fastened to the places where they stood, and therefore he cut them down.

*{and the groves, and the carved images, and the molten images}* See v. 3.

*{he brake in pieces}* See 2 King. 23:14.

*{and made dust of them}* He did beat or grind them to dust, in testimony of his great indignation against idolatry.

*{and strowed it upon the graves}* Heb. *face of the graves*. i.e. Upon the top of them. See 2 King. 23:6.

*{that had sacrificed unto them}* To their greater ignominy.

**V. 5. ***{And he burnt the bones of the Priests, etc.}* See 2 Kings 23:20.

*{and cleansed Judah and Jerusalem}* Country and city from idolatry.

**V. 6. ***{And so did he in the cities of Manasseh, and Ephraim, and Simeon, even unto Naphtali}* Under these tribes are comprised all others that submitted themselves to the king of Judah’s government. See ch. 31:1.

*{with their mattocks}* Or, *mauls*. Ezek. 26:9. Some think these were sharp iron instruments which being forced into a wall were a means of breaking it down. Or synecdochically the word may be put for axes, hatchets, pickaxes, spades, shovels, and other like instruments, whereby they beat down and cast out idolatrous monuments. Some translate it, *in their desolate places*, which had been wasted by enemies.

*{round about}* In all places where he could come.

**V. 7. ***{And when he had broken down the altars and the groves}* See v. 4.

*{and had beaten the graven images into powder}* Heb. *to make powder*. Or, *dust*. See v. 6.

*{and cut down all the idols}* As v. 4.

*{throughout all the land of Israel}* See v. 6.

*{He returned to Jerusalem}* Where was his royal palace, and the place of God’s public worship.

**V. 8. ***{Now in the eighteenth year of his reign}* See 2 King. 22:3. This good king still went on to do more and more good. In his eighth year he began to testify his piety, in his twelfth year to purge the land, v. 3, and in his eighteenth to repair God’s house.

*{when he had purged the land and the house}* For idolatry had been set up both abroad in the country everywhere, and in the holy house of God also.

*{he sent Shaphan the son of Azaliah}* See 2 King. 22:3.

*{and Maaseiah the governor of the city}* This man was a great officer, the chief of the city under the king.

*{and Joah the son of Joahaz the Recorder}* See 2 King. 4:3. Joah also was a great officer: so as all these were men of high account.

*{to repair the house of the LORD his God}* To take order thereabout. See the sum of their message 2 King. 22:4, etc.

**V. 9. ***{And when they came to Hilkiah, etc.}* See 2 King. 22:8.

*{they delivered the money that was brought into the house of God}* The messengers whom the king sent summed up the money, and ordered that it should be given to workmen, 2 King. 22:4. The like was done 2 King. 12:10, 11.

*{which the Levites that kept the doors}* These are called *porters*, 1 Chr. 26:1, etc. They had the charge of such money as was brought into the house of God.

*{had gathered of the hand}* As other Levites, so porters, served in courses; and when their course was ended, they went to their houses in the country: and here also they might go up and down from place to place to gather money.

*{of Manasseh, and Ephraim, and of all the remnant of Israel}* Here also, as v. 6, are meant such in the kingdom of Israel as had submitted themselves to the king of Judah and to the ordinances of God’s house.

*{and of all Judah and Benjamin}* These two tribes ever cleaved to the house of David and to the temple of the Lord.

*{and they returned to Jerusalem}* i.e. The Levites that gathered the money out of the foresaid tribes.

**V. 10. ***{And they put it in the hand of the workmen}* *They*, i.e. Hilkiah and others, that had the charge of procuring the house of God to be repaired.

*{that had the oversight of the house of the LORD, etc.}* See 2 Kings 22:5.

**V. 11. ***{Even to the artificers, etc.}* i.e. *Carpenters and masons*, 2 King. 22:6.

*{to buy hewn stone and timber}* See 2 King. 22:6.

*{for couplings}* Or, *for beams*, whereby the walls were coupled and joined together.

*{and to floor the houses}* Or, *to rafter them*. By *the houses* the chambers for the priests and Levites’ uses are especially meant.

*{which the kings of Judah had destroyed}* Idolatrous kings, such as Ahaz, Manasseh, Amon.

**V. 12. ***{And the men did the work faithfully}* Heb. *in truth*, or *faithfulness*.

*{and the overseers of them were Jahath, and Obadiah, etc.}* These four were they who *had the over-sight of the house of the Lord*, v. 10.

*{to set it forward}* Their office was, to see that the house were repaired with all convenient speed.

*{and other of the Levites}* Besides those before named.

*{all that could skill of instruments of musick}* This is added for honor’s sake. For it was then an high commendation to be skillful in music, Ps. 4, title; 2 Sam. 23:1. Besides these might by playing on their instruments quicken the spirits of the workmen, and sing praises to God as they saw the work go forward.

**V. 13. ***{Also they were over the bearers of burdens, etc.}* They were not only to have a care that carpenters, masons, and such like should go forward with their work, but that laborers also should be diligent in theirs.

*{in any manner of service}* Heb. *in service and service*. They had the oversight of all sorts of works.

*{and of the Levites there were Scribes}* See 1 King. 4:3. i.e. Scribes about such things as concerned the house of God.

*{and officers}* Such as had the oversight of others. See v. 10.

*{and porters}* See v. 9.

**V. 14. ***{And when they brought out the money, etc.}* See v. 9.

*{Hilkiah the Priest found a book, etc.}* See 2 King. 22:8.

*{given by Moses}* Heb. *by the hand of Moses*. See 1 King. 8:53, 56.

**V. 15. ***{And Hilkiah answered, and said to Shaphan, etc.}* 2 Kings 22:8.

**V. 16. ***{And Shaphan carried the book to the king}* See 2 Kings 22:10.

*{and brought the king word back again, saying, All that was committed to thy servants}* Heb. *to the hand of thy servants*.

*{they do it}* This gives instance of the faithfulness both of the messengers, and also of the overseers and workmen: all did their task faithfully.

**V. 17. ***{And they have gathered together, etc.}* Heb. *poured out*, or *melted*. See 2 King. 22:9.

**V. 18. ***{Then Shaphan the Scribe told the king, etc.}* See 2 Kings 22:10.

**V. 19. ***{And it came to pass when the king, etc.}* See 2 King. 22:11.

**V. 20. ***{And the King commanded Hilkiah, etc.}* See 2 Kings 22:12.

**V. 21. ***{Go, enquire of the LORD for me, and for them that are left in Israel and in Judah}* Or, *for the people, and for all Judah*, 2 Kings 22:13. He calls them a *remnant*, because most in the ten tribes were carried away captive, 2 King. 17:6, and also many in Judah. For questionless when Manasseh was bound and carried to Babylon, ch. 33:11, many of his subjects were carried with him.

*{concerning the words of the book that is found}* See 2 King. 12:13.

*{for great is the wrath of the LORD that is poured out upon us}* Or, *that is kindled against us*. The former phrase resembles God’s wrath to a water that overflows; the latter to a fire that burns; both metaphors imply great destruction.

*{because our fathers have not kept the word of the LORD}* Or, *have not hearkened unto the words of this book*, 2 King. 22:13. It was the word of the Lord that was written in that book.

*{to do all that was written in this book}* In 2 King. 22:13 is added *concerning us*. What Moses wrote concerning all the posterity of Israel.

**V. 22. ***{And Hilkiah and they that the king had appointed}* These are set down by name 2 King. 22:14. *Ahikam, and Achbor, and Shaphan, and Asaiah*.

*{went to Huldah the Prophetess, etc.}* See 2 King. 22:14.

*{and they spake to her to that effect}* Which the king had given them in charge, v. 20.

**V. 23. ***{And she answered them, etc.}* See 2 King. 22:15.

**V. 24. ***{Thus saith the LORD, Behold, etc.}* See 2 King. 22:16.

*{even all the curses that are written in the book}* Or, *all the words of the book*. There were blessings also as well as cursings in that book, but the things here especially intended were curses.

*{which they have read before the king of Judah}* Or, *which the king of Judah hath read*, 2 King. 22:16. Josiah so heeded what was read unto him as if he had read it himself. Or, *after others had read the book to him he himself read it over again*.

**V. 25. ***{Because they have forsaken me, etc.}* See 2 King. 22:17.

*{my wrath shall be poured out upon this place, etc.}* Or, *shall be kindled against this place*, 2 King. 22:17. See v. 21.

**V. 26. ***{And as for the king, etc.}* See 2 King. 22:18.

**V. 27. ***{Because thine heart was tender, etc.}* See 2 King. 22:19.

*{before God}* Or, *before the Lord*.

*{when thou heardest his words}* Or, *when thou heardest what I spake*. God speaks unto us out of his word.

*{and humbledst thy self before me}* Before this 2 King. 22:19 are these words, *that they should become a desolation and a curse*.

*{and didst rend, etc.}* See 2 King. 22:19.

**V. 28. ***{Behold, I will gather thee, etc.}* See 2 King. 22:20.

*{and upon the inhabitants of the same}* This clause is not expressed 2 King. 22:20, but it is implied: for under this word *place* is meant the inhabitants thereof.

*{So they brought, etc.}* See 2 King. 22:20.

**V. 29. ***{Then the king sent, etc.}* See 2 King. 23:1.

**V. 30. ***{And the king went up, etc.}* See 2 King. 23:2.

*{and the Levites}* These are not mentioned 2 King. 23:2, but comprised under the word *Priests*, and instead thereof *Prophets* are set down.

*{great and small}* Heb. *from great even to small*. See 2 Kings 23:2.

**V. 31. ***{And the king stood in his place}* *By a pillar*, 2 King. 23:3.

*{and made a covenant, etc.}* See 2 King. 23:3. In the end of that verse is added *and all the people stood to the covenant*.

**V. 32. ***{And he caused all that were present}* Heb. *found*. See 1 Chr. 29:17.

*{in Jerusalem and Benjamin to stand to it}* These were his own proper subjects, whom he kept in such awe by his regal authority and penal laws against idolatry as they dared not but stand to that covenant which he made. Though after his death many of them soon fell from it; yet questionless God by his Spirit so wrought upon some of them as willingly and heartily they consented to the king and his covenant.

*{and the inhabitants of Jerusalem}* They were most forward.

*{did according to the covenant of God}* That covenant which the Lord enjoined them in his law.

*{the God of their fathers}* See 1 Chr. 29:18.

**V. 33. ***{And Josiah took away all the abominations}* i.e. Idols especially. See 1 King. 11:5.

*{out of all the countries that pertained to the children of Israel}* *Israel* is here taken in a large extent, for Judah and Israel both: for many of the ten tribes were subject to him. See v. 6.

*{and made all that were present in Israel to serve}* See v. 32.

*{even to serve the LORD their God}* He thought it not enough to draw his people from idolatry, unless also he brought them to serve the true God aright.

*{and all his days they departed not from following the LORD, etc.}* Heb. *from after the Lord*. A good governor is a great means to keep people in a good course. See Josh. 24:31.

## CHAPTER 35

**Verse 1. ***{Moreover, Josiah kept a passover unto the LORD in Jerusalem}* See Exod. 12:3, etc. 2 King. 23:21.

*{and they killed the passover, etc.}* See ch. 30:15. Exod. 12:16.

**V. 2. ***{And he set the Priests in their charges}* i.e. Those which were enjoined by law, Num. 18:5, and distinctly set down by David, 1 Chr. 23:6.

*{and encouraged them to the service of the house of the LORD}* By pithy exhortations and by promises that all things belonging to their maintenance should be duly provided.

**V. 3. ***{And said unto the Levites that taught all Israel, which were holy unto the LORD}* Or, *that prepared the holy things of the Lord*. The former reading shows that it did in special belong to the Levites to teach and instruct the people in the law of the Lord. See ch. 17:8, 9. The latter, that they were in particular employed about such sacred things as belonged to the house of the Lord, to prepare and fit them for priests. See 1 Chr. 23:28 and 25:1.

*{Put the holy Ark in the house}* Or, *put them before the Ark*. i.e. Whereas while the doors of the temple were shut, ch. 28:24, or a free liberty thereto restrained, you were forced to keep the Passovers and other holy feasts in private, now that you have the liberty of the temple, where the ark abides, and is not, as of old, carried from place to place, prepare the holy things of the Lord before the ark; and there execute the holy functions. Or it may be the ark by some idolater in former times was removed out of his place, and therefore he now thus says, *Put the holy Ark into the house*, whereby he exhorts them to set it again in its proper place. The Hebrew styles it *Ark of holiness*, which implies that among other holy types this was a most holy one.

*{which Solomon the son of David king of Israel}* See ch. 30:26.

*{did build}* This has reference to the oracle or most holy place, where Solomon set the ark, 1 King. 16:19 and 8:21.

*{it shall not be a burden upon your shoulders}* Seeing of old it belonged to the priests to carry the ark, Exod. 25:14. Num. 10:35. Deut. 31:9. 1 King. 8:6, the Levites here mentioned were priests. See 1 Chr. 23:26.

*{serve now the LORD your God}* He makes this inference upon that liberty which was given them from bearing the ark up and down, that they should more diligently attend other services of the temple, as offering sacrifices, singing, sounding instruments, etc., and this was *to serve the Lord their God*.

*{and his people Israel}* These they *served* by taking such offerings as they brought, by cleansing such as were unclean, by instructing them, and doing other like things that tended to their spiritual good.

**V. 4. ***{And prepare yourselves}* Our translators have well inserted *your selves*, to show that they ought to sanctify themselves to the holy duties they were to perform.

*{by the houses of your fathers}* By the distinct families of your predecessors, ch. 25:5.

*{after your courses}* See 1 Chr. 23:6.

*{according to the writing of David, etc.}* 1 Chron. 23, 24, 25 and 26.

*{and according to the writing of Solomon his son}* Ch. 8:14.

**V. 5. ***{And stand in the holy place}* i.e. The priests’ court, where all manner of sacrifice was offered. There they were to stand to receive the lambs and such other offerings as were brought by the people.

*{according to the divisions of the families of the fathers}* Heb. *the house of the fathers*. i.e. The posterity of such and such fathers.

*{of your brethren the people}* Heb. *the sons of the people*. i.e. Such as were of other tribes, but all descended from one father.

*{and after the division of the families of the Levites}* The Levites were distinguished by their families, so that some might attend upon some families of the people, others upon others.

**V. 6. ***{So kill the Passover}* See ch. 20:15.

*{and sanctify yourselves}* See ch. 29:5.

*{and prepare your brethren}* Or, *for your brethren*. As ch. 31:15, 16. Some apply this to the Levites preparing the sacrifices for the priests to offer up, as v. 11, 12.

*{that they may do according to the word of the LORD}* That the priests may do their duty as is enjoined them by God.

*{by the hand of Moses}* See 1 King. 8:53.

**V. 7. ***{And Josiah gave to the people}* Heb. *offered to the people*. He gave them to offer.

*{of the flock, lambs and kids,} *(Heb. *sons of goats*)* {for all the Passover offerings}* Either of these kinds might be offered at the Passover, Exod. 12:5.

*{for all that were present}* Because the Passover was to be eaten only by them that were present.

*{to the number of thirty thousand}* i.e. Lambs and kids.

*{and three thousand bullocks}* For burnt-offerings.

*{these were of the king’s substance}* Of such herds and flocks as belonged to himself. See the like done by Hezekiah, ch. 30:24.

**V. 8. ***{And his princes gave}* Heb. *offered*, as v. 7.

*{willingly}* Heb. *to liberality*. A liberal gift implies a willing mind.

*{unto the people}* The king’s example v. 7 moved the princes.

*{to the Priests, and to the Levites}* They gave to these also, that they might rejoice with the people.

*{Hilkiah}* He was the high priest, ch. 34:9.

*{and Zechariah, and Jehiel, rulers of the house of God}* These were of the second order, and heads of two distinct families. See 2 King. 1:34. These together with the high priest were rulers over other priests and Levites in the house of God.

*{gave unto the Priests}* i.e. Inferior priests.

*{for the Passover offerings}* V. 7.

*{two thousand and six hundred small cattle, and three hundred oxen}* It seems the foresaid rulers were great men and wealthy.

**V. 9. ***{Conaniah also, and Shemaiah, and Nathaneel, his brethren}* These two latter were brethren to Conaniah, *q.d.* *Conaniah with Shemaiah and Nathaneel his brethren*.

*{and Hashabiah, and Jehiel, and Jozabad, chief of the Levites}* Or, *fathers and rulers over the Levites*. This title of honor has relation to all the six Levites before mentioned.

*{gave}* Heb. *offered*, as v. 7, 8.

*{unto the Levites}* i.e. Inferior Levites under those chief ones.

*{for passover offerings}* See v. 7.

*{five thousand small cattle, and five hundred oxen}* These Levites exceeded the priests v. 8. The total sum given by the king, princes, priests and Levites was three thousand eight hundred bullocks, and thirty-seven thousand and six hundred small cattle. See 1 Kings 8:63.

**V. 10. ***{So the service was prepared}* All things fit for a solemn Passover were provided and made ready.

*{and the Priests stood in their place}* See ch. 30:16.

*{and the Levites in their courses}* Every one in his order and rank.

*{according to the king’s commandment}* See v. 4. Herein this good king imitated his father David, and observed his direction.

**V. 11. ***{And they killed the Passover}* See ch. 30:15.

*{and the Priests sprinkled the blood from their hands}* See 2 King. 16:13. Ch. 19:22. The blood they sprinkled they received from the hands of the Levites.

*{and the Levites flayed them}* See ch. 29:34.

**V. 12. ***{And they removed the burnt offerings}* They separated such sacrifices as were to be eaten from the burnt-offerings, which were to be wholly consumed on the altar: or they took away the livers, kidneys, and other things which were to be consumed, Lev. 3:4, 10, 11, that the flesh might be fit to be eaten.

*{that they might give according to the divisions of the families of the people}* They set apart the burnt-offerings that so it might be known how much remained for the people to partake of according to their several families.

*{to offer unto the LORD}* This has reference unto the burnt-offerings which were consumed upon the altar, and in an especial manner were counted the Lord’s offering.

*{as it is written in the book of Moses}* They had an especial eye to the law of God in what they did.

*{and so did they with the oxen}* Even as they had done with the smaller cattle, as is set down in the former part of this verse.

**V. 13. ***{And they roasted the Passover with fire}* *Passover* is here put for the lambs or kids before mentioned. It was roasted to typify the tormenting death of Christ.

*{according to the ordinance}* The ordinance here meant is expressly set down Exod. 12:8, 9, and that both affirmatively, it must be eaten *rosted*; and negatively, *not raw, nor sodden*.

*{but the other holy offerings}* Such as were not consumed on the altar.

*{sod they}* Because they could not roast so much as should suffice for so great a multitude as were there. That of old they used to seethe some of their holy offerings, is evident, Num. 6:19. 1 Sam. 2:15.

*{in pots}* Vessels of brass bigger in the belly than the neck.

*{and in cauldrons}* Great vessels, of an even size from top to bottom, very deep.

*{and in pans}* Pans were shallow and broad, of the same size at top and bottom. They had pans to bake some of their meat-offerings in, Lev. 2:5. Num. 11:8. 1 Chr. 23:29.

*{and divided them speedily}* Heb. *made them run*.

*{among all the people}* This is the reason why they had so many vessels to seethe the meat; that there might be sufficient for all the people.

**V. 14. ***{And afterward they made ready for themselves}* Their greatest care was to provide for the people. That therefore they did first; yet they did not wholly neglect themselves.

*{and for the Priests}* The reason why they provided for the priests follows in the next words.

*{because the Priests the sons of Aaron were busied in offering of burnt offerings}* The number of the burnt-offerings mentioned v. 7, 8, 9, must needs take up a great deal of time.

*{and the fat}* That which was taken from the offerings that were eaten. See ch. 29:35.

*{until night}* For the burnt-offerings and fat of other offerings were one after another laid upon the altar, and not very quickly consumed, so as they must needs be busied thereabout till night.

*{therefore the Levites prepared for themselves, and for the Priests}* See the beginning of this verse.

*{the sons of Aaron}* See 1 Chr. 23:13.

**V. 15. ***{And the singers the sons of Asaph}* See 1 Chr. 25:1, 2.

*{were in their place}* Heb. *station*. For as the porters had their set place where they executed their office, so also had the singers.

*{according to the commandment of David}* 1 Chr. 25:1, etc.

*{and Asaph, and Heman, and Jeduthun}* These were prime masters of music. With them did David advise about the courses of the singers. Besides, these three did press upon their posterity a careful observation of those courses which David had set down.

*{the king’s Seer}* Heman also had this title, 1 Chr. 25:5, and Asaph likewise, ch. 39:30. Of its meaning see 2 King. 17:13.

*{and the porters waited at every gate}* Heb. *gate and gate*. See 1 Chr. 9:17, 18 and 26:14, etc.

*{they might not depart from their service}* During that solemnity.

*{for their brethren the Levites prepared for them}* As they had done for the priests, v. 14.

**V. 16. ***{So all the service of the LORD}* Such things as were enjoined for outward parts of God’s worship, wherein, and whereby he was served. See v. 10.

*{was prepared the same day}* This is noted to show the diligence of the priests and Levites, in doing what was to be done on the appointed time.

*{to keep the Passover}* That was at that time the most solemn part of God’s worship, therefore other things were fitted and prepared for it.

*{and to offer burnt offerings}* The burnt-offerings here mentioned made much to the solemnity of the Passover, therefore they are here added thereunto.

*{upon the altar of the LORD}* Upon this were all burnt-offerings to be offered, whence it was called *the Altar of burnt-offering*, Exod. 30:28. Ch. 29:18. By it the sacrifice offered thereon was sanctified, Matt. 23:29, and because the Lord appointed it to that end, it is called *the Altar of the Lord*.

*{according to the commandment of king Josiah}* See v. 10.

**V. 17. ***{And the children of Israel that were present}* Heb. *found*. See 1 Chr. 29:17.

*{kept the Passover at that time}* See v. 1.

*{and the feast of unleavened bread seven days}* See ch. 30:21.

**V. 18. ***{And there was no Passover like to that kept in Israel from the days of Samuel the Prophet}* 2 King. 23:22, *from the dayes of the Judges that judged Israel*. Samuel was the last judge: so as if the end of those judges’ government be there meant, that place well agrees with this, which mentions Samuel. Besides, it is probable that no judge kept a more solemn Passover than Samuel did; and in that respect, though the time be reckoned from the beginning of the judges, yet it may well stand with this of Samuel’s days. See on 1 Sam. 3:1.

*{neither did all the Kings of Israel, etc.}* See 2 King. 23:22.

*{and the Priests, and the Levites}* Some of these added much to the Passover by the abundance of offerings which they gave, v. 8, 9, and others by their diligence in preparing and acting all things that were to be done thereabout, v. 10, etc.

*{and all Judah and Israel that were present}* Among these the princes are to be reckoned, who gave many offerings, v. 8. The other sorts did what they did with great cheerfulness and gladness, as in Hezekiah’s time, ch. 21:30.

*{and the inhabitants of Jerusalem}* These being near the temple, were without question most forward about setting out the solemnity of this feast, ch. 34:3.

**V. 19. ***{In the eighteenth year, etc.}* See 2 King. 23:23.

**V. 20. ***{After all this}* This has reference both to the solemn Passover, and also to other good things noted ch. 34.

*{when Josiah had prepared the Temple}* Heb. *house*. i.e. The house of God, which was the temple. This shows that Josiah’s piety prevented not God’s judgments, which were inflicted, as for Manasseh’s sins, 2 King. 23:16, so also for the sins of the people, even in Josiah’s time, Jer. 3:6.

*{Necho king of Egypt came up to fight}* See 2 King. 23:29.

*{against Carchemish by Euphrates}* For Carchemish belonged to the king of Egypt, and the Assyrian had taken it from him.

*{and Josiah went out against him}* See 2 King. 23:29.

**V. 21. ***{But he sent Ambassadors to him, saying, What have I to do with thee}* Heb. *what to thee and me*. See 1 King. 17:18. 2 King. 3:13. John 2:4. This shows that the king of Egypt was not willing to fight with Josiah.

*{thou king of Judah}* This compellation he used to persuade him the more to cease.

*{I come not against thee this day}* I intend not to hurt you.

*{but against the house wherewith I have war}* Heb. *the house of my war*. Or, *the family, society or company of my enemy*.

*{for God commanded me to make haste}* By Jeremiah, or some other prophet. Or Pharaoh might pretend God’s warrant, to move Josiah the rather to desist, as 2 King. 18:25.

*{forbear thee from meddling with God}* This is justly inferred upon the forementioned ground: for if any hinder one to do what God commands, they oppose God therein.

*{who is with me}* God is with them whom he sets on work.

*{that he destroy thee not}* This also justly follows. For they who hinder God’s work provoke God to destroy them.

**V. 22. ***{Nevertheless Josiah would not turn away his face from him}* Would not forbear to fight with the king of Egypt.

*{but disguised himself}* That he might not be known to be the king. For enemies use to bend their greatest force against a king, if they espy him in a battle. On such a ground Ahab disguised himself, 1 King. 22:30.

*{that he might fight with him}* That he might the more boldly put himself into the battle, and fight with his enemies.

*{and hearkened not unto the words of Necho}* See v. 20.

*{from the mouth of God}* Which Necho said came from God. For Josiah believed it not.

*{and came to fight in the valley of Megiddo}* See 1 King. 9:15. Zech. 12:11.

**V. 23. ***{And the archers shot at king Josiah}* Either they discerned him to be the king, or by his valor in the battle he provoked them to aim at him.

*{and the king said to his servants, Have me away}* Carry me out of the battle. See the like, 1 King. 22:35.

*{for I am sore wounded}* Heb. *made sick*. See 1 King. 22:34.

**V. 24. ***{His servants therefore took him out of that chariot}* That wherein he was wounded, which must needs be full of blood. See 1 King. 22:35.

*{and put him in the second chariot that he had}* The former chariot might be the fitter for war; this, more close, and fitter for a dying man.

*{and they brought him to Jerusalem, and he died}* See 2 Kings 23:30.

*{and was buried in one of the sepulchres of his fathers}* Or, *among the Sepulchres*. This is called his *own Sepulchre*, 2 King. 23:30. See on Judg. 12:7.

*{and all Judah and Jerusalem mourned for Josiah}* Zech. 12:11. The whole land both city and country mourned for him. This was the greatest mourning we read of, and that not without cause, whether the worth of the man and the good that he did, or the evil that followed upon his death, be considered.

**V. 25. ***{And Jeremiah lamented for Josiah}* Jeremiah was a prophet that lived in his days, and knew the evil that would follow after his death, and could not but be much moved thereupon.

*{and all the singing men and the singing women spake of Josiah in their lamentations}* They used to have mourners both men and women, to make lamentations at the death of persons of worth. See Eccl. 12:5 and Jer. 9:17. In those lamentations they used to make mention of the parties deceased, and to mourn for them. But they mentioned Josiah in their mournings for others also.

*{to this day}* See 1 King. 9:13 and 10:12 and 12:7, 19.

*{and made them an ordinance in Israel}* Not the mourners only at Josiah’s funeral, but also at the funerals of others, time after time, made mention of his death; insomuch as it came to be a constant custom, and as it were a settled ordinance, to speak of Josiah in their doleful elegies. It may be that by reason of the great loss of so worthy a king a law was enacted that in all other solemn mournings there should be mourning for Josiah, and that common mourners observed the same.

*{and behold, they are written in the Lamentations}* Some conceive the Lamentations of Jeremy registered in sacred Scripture to be here meant; but there might be some other public lamentation remaining upon record, wherein the loss of Josiah was set down.

**V. 26. ***{Now the rest of the acts of Josiah}* See 1 King. 11:41.

*{and his goodness}* Heb. *kindness*. The good he did about reforming religion was a great kindness to those that were good in his land. See Neh. 13:14, 31.

*{according to that which was written, etc.}* See 2 King. 23:25.

**V. 27. ***{And his deeds, first and last, etc.}* See ch. 12:15 and 16:11 and 20:34.

## CHAPTER 36

**Verse 1. ***{Then the people of the land took Jehoahaz, etc.}* See 2 King. 23:30. This was the sixteenth king of Judah from the division. He was a wicked king, 2 King. 23:32.

**V. 2. ***{Jehoahaz was twenty and three years old, etc.}* See 2 Kings 23:31.

**V. 3. ***{And the king of Egypt}* Pharaoh-Necho, 2 King. 23:33.

*{put him down}* Heb. *removed him*. Namely, from his throne.

*{at Jerusalem}* This was the royal city where the king’s throne and palace was. It is not likely that Necho came into the land so far as Jerusalem, but that rather he sent for Jehoahaz to come to Riblah, and there put him in bands, 2 King. 23:33. For the men of Judah at this time stood in such a fear of the king of Egypt as they dared not deny anything he demanded, no not their king.

*{and condemned}* Heb. *mulcted*. i.e. Imposed a tax or fine.

*{the land in an hundred talents of silver and a talent of gold}* See 2 Chr. 22:14.

**V. 4. ***{And the king of Egypt made Eliakim, etc.}* See 2 Kings 23:34.

*{over Judah and Jerusalem}* Over the whole kingdom, both country and city.

*{and turned his name to Jehoiakim, etc.}* See 2 King. 23:34.

**V. 5. ***{Jehoiakim was twenty and five years old, etc.}* See 2 Kings 23:36. This was the seventeenth king of Judah from the division.

*{and he did that which was evil, etc.}* See 2 King. 23:37.

**V. 6. ***{Against him came up Nebuchadnezzar, etc.}* See 2 Kings 24:1.

*{and bound him in fetters}* Or, *chains*. This was because he had broken covenant with him, 2 King. 24:1.

*{to carry him to Babylon}* That was the king of Babylon’s purpose; but Jehoiakim died, even at Jerusalem, after he was taken, Jer. 22:19, etc.

**V. 7. ***{Nebuchadnezzar also carried of the vessels, etc.}* Namely, some of them; for some were left, and taken away in Jehoiachin’s time, 2 King. 24:13, and some in Zedekiah’s, Jer. 52:19. See Jer. 27:18.

*{and put them in his Temple at Babylon}* By divine providence they were there reserved to be carried back again to Jerusalem, Ezra 1:7, 8, etc. But Nebuchadnezzar intended them for the service of his idol, and Belshazzar his grandchild most profanely abused them, Dan. 5:2.

**V. 8. ***{Now the rest of the acts of Jehoiakim}* See 2 King. 24:5.

*{and his abominations which he did}* 2 King. 23:37, it is said that *he did evil according to all that his fathers had done*, which may have reference to the abominations committed by Ahaz, ch. 28, and Manasseh, ch. 33:2, etc.

*{and that which was found in him}* This may have especial reference to his breaking covenant with Nebuchadnezzar, and rebelling against him, 2 Kings 21:1, but the Jews say that idolaters were wont to imprint some marks upon their bodies; and that such were found upon Jehoiakim.

*{behold, they are written, etc.}* See ch. 35:27.

*{And Jehoiachin, etc.}* Or, *Jeconiah*, 1 Chr. 3:16, or *Coniah*, Jer. 22:24. See 2 King. 24:6.

**V. 9. ***{Jehoiachin was eight years old when he began to reign}* He must have reference to Jehoiakim, the father of this man, mentioned v. 8. For Jehoiachin was eighteen years old when he himself began to reign, 2 King. 24:8. See 2 King. 16:2.

*{and he reigned three months, etc.}* See 2 King. 24:8.

*{and he did that which was evil, etc.}* See 2 King. 24:9.

**V. 10. ***{And when the year was expired}* Heb. *at the return of the year*. When another year began, and it was seasonable to go into the field, 2 Sam. 11:1. 1 King. 20:22. For Jehoiachin reigned not a whole year, v. 9. When Nebuchadnezzar had surprised Jerusalem and taken Jehoiakim, the winter was come on; but when the next year began, he came again against Jerusalem.

*{king Nebuchadnezzar sent}* An army against Jerusalem, 2 Kings 24:10, 11.

*{and brought him to Babylon}* For the city being besieged, Jehoiachin yielded himself up to the king of Babylon.

*{with the goodly vessels, etc.}* Heb. *vessels of desire*. Such things as excel in worth, beauty, or any other ways, are much desired. And these vessels were every way so excellent as they made such as beheld them to desire them; better could not be desired.

*{and made Zedekiah, etc.}* Or, *Mattaniah*, 2 King. 24:17. Jer. 37:1.

*{his brother, etc.}* *Brother* is here taken for *uncle*; for Zedekiah was Jehoiachin’s father’s brother. See 1 Chr. 3:15. 2 King. 24:17.

**V. 11. ***{Zedekiah was one and twenty years old, etc.}* See 2 Kings 24:18.

**V. 12. ***{And he did that which was evil, etc.}* See 2 King. 24:19.

*{and humbled not himself before Jeremiah the Prophet}* Jeremiah beforehand denounced the judgments against him and his people, which afterwards fell out, but neither he nor they hearkened unto him: they repented not, but hardened their hearts, and continued obstinate in their sins, Jer. 27:2, 6, etc.

*{speaking from the mouth of the LORD}* This aggravates their obstinacy. It was not against a man only, but against the Lord. So Exod. 16:8.

**V. 13. ***{And he also rebelled, etc.}* See 2 King. 24:20. Jer. 52:3.

*{who had made him swear by God}* Namely, that he would acknowledge fealty and pay tribute to Nebuchadnezzar. See Ezek. 17:16, 18, 19. Though the oath were to a heathen king, yet it being made by God, Zedekiah’s breaking thereof tended much to God’s dishonor, and caused his name to be blasphemed.

*{but he stiffened his neck}* See ch. 30:8. Exod. 32:9. Acts 7:51.

*{and hardened his heart}* This is an interpretation of the former metaphor, Neh. 9:16, and is opposed to yielding or hearkening unto good counsel.

*{from turning unto the LORD}* He had turned from the Lord by his many sins, but would not by any admonitions or threatenings be brought to turn to him.

*{God of Israel}* See 1 King. 8:15.

**V. 14. ***{Moreover, all the chief of the Priests}* Such as had command over other priests and Levites, and in that respect should have been examples not only to the people, as *Priests*; but also to other priests and Levites, as *chief Priests*.

*{and the people}* Thus all sorts conspired to sin against the Lord, king, priests, and people.

*{transgressed very much}* Heb. *they multiplied to transgress by transgression*. They ceased not to add sin to sin, and that in an high manner, measure and degree.

*{after all the abominations of the heathen}* See 1 King. 14:24. 2 King. 16:3 and 21:2.

*{and polluted the house of the LORD}* By profaning God’s ordinances, and also by bringing into his house such things as he detested. See ch. 33:7.

*{which he had hallowed in Jerusalem}* See 1 King. 9:3, 7.

**V. 15. ***{And the LORD God of their fathers}* See 1 Chr. 29:18.

*{sent to them by his messengers}* Heb. *by the hand of his messengers*. See 1 King. 8:56. By messengers are meant priests, prophets, and other ministers of God’s word, Hag. 1:13.

*{rising up betimes, and sending}* i.e. Continually and carefully sending. This is a metaphor taken from careful householders, who with the soonest seek to redress mischiefs, causing their servants for that end to rise betimes, Jer. 11:7 and 25:3, 4 and 26:5 and 32:33.

*{because he had compassion on his people}* See 2 King. 13:23.

*{and on his dwellingplace}* Ps. 74:4. See 1 King. 8:13. The temple is hereby meant.

**V. 16. ***{But they mocked the messengers of God}* See ch. 30:10.

*{and despised his words}* In that they did not believe nor obey God’s word, but rather did the contrary, they are said *to despise it*.

*{and misused his Prophets}* This Christ complains of, Matt. 21:34, 35, 36. Some interpret the Hebrew phrase thus, *They erred in the Prophets*; or *They said they were seduced by the Prophets*.

*{until the wrath of the LORD arose against his people}* See 2 King. 22:13. God’s wrath is not easily incensed: we may here see I. A conspiracy of all sorts against the Lord, v. 14. II. A multiplication of transgressions, v. 14. III. Monstrous abominations, v. 14. IV. Great profaneness, v. 14. Contempt of God’s messengers. By all these and by many other things they provoked the Lord before his wrath was kindled.

*{till there was no remedy}* Heb. *healing*. The last means that is ordinarily used to reclaim men is God’s ministers, to tell them of their sins to their faces, and to denounce judgments against them for the same. If this prevail not, then remains nothing but expectation of judgment.

**V. 17. ***{Therefore he brought upon them the king of the Chaldees}* Called *the king of Babylon*, 2 King. 25:1. See 2 King. 24:1, 2.

*{who slew their young men with the sword}* See 1 King. 8:12.

*{in the house of their Sanctuary}* i.e. The temple, whither it may be the Jews fled for refuge when the Babylonians entered the city, and the enemy pursuing them thither, there destroyed them. This circumstance aggravates the impiety of the enemy, and the judgment on the Jews. Some take Jerusalem to be meant by *the house of their sanctuary*, because the house of God stood in that city.

*{and had no compassion upon young man, or maiden, old man}* It is a great degree of cruelty to spare no sex nor age. Some apply this to the enemy; others to God himself, and make it an aggravation of his just judgment, as Deut. 32:25.

*{or him that stooped for age}* Or, is, as we speak, *decrepit, very old, and infirm withal*.

*{he gave them all into his hand}* God gave all before mentioned into the power of the enemy; otherwise he could have had no power over them, John 19:11.

**V. 18. ***{And all the vessels of the house of God, etc.}* 2 King. 25:13, etc.

*{and the treasures of the house of the LORD, etc.}* See 1 King. 7:51 and 14:26. Whatsoever was therein precious he took away, that he might lay it up in his own treasures.

**V. 19. ***{And they burnt the house of God}* This was in regard of the very fabric and glorious ornaments, but much more of the holy use and typical signification, a great loss to the people of God. But what will not enemies spoil when they enter into another kingdom? And what will not God give over to be spoiled and consumed if it be profaned? Instance this house of God.

*{and brake down the wall of Jerusalem, etc.}* That it might not be a refuge to his enemies. See 2 King. 25:9, 10.

*{and destroyed all the goodly vessels thereof}* See v. 10.

**V. 20. ***{And them that had escaped from the sword}* Heb. *the remainder from the sword*. Such as were not slain in the siege, or at the sacking of the city.

*{carried he away to Babylon}* See 2 King. 25:11.

*{where they were servants to him, and his sons}* Namely, to Evil-merodach his own son, 2 King. 25:27, and Belshazzar his grandson, Dan. 5:1.

*{until the reign of the kingdom of Persia}* When Cyrus the first monarch of Persia had subdued the Babylonians, then was the monarchy accounted to be in Persia, and that to be the kingdom of the world; for it was the greatest, and had many other kingdoms under it. So soon as Cyrus had conquered the Babylonians, he presently set the Jews free. They were therefore servants until that time, and no longer.

**V. 21. ***{To fulfil the word of the LORD by the mouth of Jeremiah}* Jeremiah foretold that they should be in captivity seventy years, and at the end of seventy years be set free, Jer. 25:9, 11 and 29:10. Dan. 9:2.

*{until the land had enjoyed her Sabbaths}* *Sabbath* signifies *rest*; and so long as the people were kept out of her, the land rested; there were none to plow or dig up the land, or to cause any tumults, or to do any work at all therein. This phrase of *keeping Sabbaths* is the rather used, because the people of Judah, while they remained in the land, exceedingly profaned the holy Sabbaths: but all profaneness ceasing while the people were in captivity, the land is said *to keep her Sabbaths*.

*{for so long as she lay desolate, she kept Sabbath}* Or, *rested*, Lev. 23:32 and 26:34.

*{to fulfil threescore and ten years}* Some conceive these seventy years began at Zedekiah’s captivity, because then Jerusalem was utterly destroyed. And as a ground of this opinion they allege this phrase, *the seventy years in the desolations of Jerusalem*; and because in this place it is said *the land enjoyed her Sabbaths so long as she lay desolate*. But these phrases may be used in reference to the greater part, though eleven years of the seventy might be past before the utter desolation. Stronger arguments there be to prove that the seventy years began at the first captivity, in the time of Jehoiachim: for before the utter destruction of Jerusalem Jeremiah writes to those of the first captivity to comfort them with this, that after seventy years they should be delivered, Jer. 29:1, 10. Besides, Ezekiel expressly sets down the beginning of the captivity at Jehoiachin’s going into Babylon, Ezek. 1:2, and plainly distinguishes the beginning of the captivity from that time wherein Jerusalem was utterly destroyed, and makes the former to be eleven years before the latter, Ezek. 40:1. See on Jer. 25:11, 12.

**V. 22. ***{Now in the first year}* See Ezra 1:1. This was not the first year of his reign in Persia, but of his monarchy over a great part of the world, which began with the destruction of Babylon. See on v. 20. Dan. 2:37, 38.

*{of Cyrus}* In Hebrew he is called *Chores*: but the Latin, English, and other tongues call him *Cyrus*, after the Greek. See on Isa. 44:28 and 45:1, 2, 3.

*{King of Persia}* Persia was his proper inheritance, received from his grandfather Astyages. He conquered the Medes, and united that kingdom to Persia. After that, having vanquished Babylon he became monarch of the world. Which in honor of his hereditary kingdom was called *the Persian Monarchy*, and *the Kingdom of Persia*, v. 20. And Cyrus himself was styled *Cyrus the Persian*, Dan. 6:28.

*{that the word of the LORD spoken by the mouth of Jeremiah might be accomplished}* Or, *when the word of the Lord was accomplished*. To wit, that after seventy years God would cause his people to return out of their captivity to the land of Judah, Jer. 29:10.

*{the LORD stirred up the spirit of Cyrus king of Persia}* Either by sending some prophet to declare unto him the mind of God about the return of his people, or by giving him notice of prophecies tending to that end, as of Isa. 44:28 and 45:1, etc., or by some inward special instinct, Prov. 21:1.

*{that he made a proclamation}* Heb. *caused a voyce to pass*. Proclamations use to be published by the voice of an herald, or some other crier. See 1 King. 15:22.

*{throughout all his kingdom}* i.e. *All the kingdoms of the earth*, v. 23, his whole monarchy. For though most of the Jews were in the kingdom of Babylon, yet many were dispersed up and down in other kingdoms.

*{and put it also in writing, saying}* He did as kings and other governors now use to do, who cause their proclamations not only to be published by voice, but also to be printed, that they may be stuck upon posts, and sent up and down from city to city, from one place to another.

**V. 23. ***{Thus saith Cyrus king of Persia}* See v. 22.

*{All the Kingdoms of the earth}* All round about on every side. He had conquered all the nations that were near him, and brought them under his dominion; yea, his conquest extended far and near, so as he might be thought to have subdued *all the kingdoms of the earth*. Yet here is a synecdoche, *all kingdoms* put for *many*. Thus it is said that Augustus (who was another monarch) caused *all the world to be taxed*, Luke 2:1, i.e. A great part thereof, which was under his jurisdiction.

*{hath the LORD God of heaven given me}* This heathen monarch makes such an acknowledgment of God’s sovereignty, power and providence in disposing the great and weighty affairs of this world, as could not be but by divine instinct; John 19:11.

*{and he hath charged me}* Heb. *concerning me*. It may be that Daniel (who lived in his time, and was advanced to great place by Darius that took Babylon, Dan. 6:3) by showing to Cyrus the prophecy of Isaiah, Isa. 44:28 and 45:1, etc., informed him of the mind of God concerning this point. Or otherwise, Daniel, or some other prophet, might in God’s name tell him that which here follows.

*{to build him}* Or, *to cause to be built for him*.

*{an house}* A temple, or place for divine worship.

*{in Jerusalem}* The very place where the former temple was.

*{which is in Judah}* This is added more fully to declare the place where the temple should be built, even where the people of God of old were: and if there were in any place in the world another Jerusalem, to distinguish that from this.

*{Who is there among you of all his people?}* His proclamation is very large, excluding none of what condition soever, or in what place of the world soever he were. He implies that he would have them willing to go; and so much may be supplied to make up the sense, thus, *Who is there among you of all his people that are willing to build an house to God?* He acknowledges the Jews to be God’s peculiar people, and thereby excludes other nations from that privilege.

*{the LORD his God be with him}* He was not only ready to let them go, but also very desirous that they should prosper in what they went about; and therefore he prays for God’s blessing on them. This is the only way to obtain good success. Of this phrase *God be with him* see 2 Chr. 15:2, 9. It implies God’s prospering one in that which he undertakes, and giving him good success.

*{and let him go up}* Hereby a free liberty is given to all the Jews that were willing to go to their own land, and there to help forward the work of the Lord.

The two last verses of this book being word for word repeated in the beginning of the next book, called Ezra, do show that both the books are one continued history, and penned by the same author.
